| **Territory (oddplayground)** | A battle-family mode (`territory`, feature odd-territory) after oddplayground's pong-wars reels ("Vortex VS Bomber – One bounce can flip the board"): a tile map in the exported square – **Board Columns** 12–48 (`tyc`, default 24; the rows follow the board's shape) – split into **2 or 4 team regions** (`tyt`: top / bottom or quadrants), **Balls per Team** 1–8 (`tyb`). A ball passes through tiles of its own colour and bounces off any other, converting the tile it hit with a note pitched by team and row. A **power per team** (`typ`, e.g. `vortex,bomber,painter,ghost`): the **vortex** curves its path and drags a whirl whose spiral arms convert enemy tiles, the **bomber** arms and blows on its next bounce off an enemy tile (shock ring, debris, wall-break sound, the board jolts), taking every tile in its reach – one bounce can flip the board –, the **painter** dashes through enemy territory painting a trail, the **ghost** passes through enemy tiles and converts a 3×3 block on every wall bounce – **Power Every** 1–10 s (`tye`), **Power Reach** 1–8 tiles (`tyr`); the powers are tuned to win about as often as each other on the default board. The HUD band: the FLASHING LIGHTS badge (`tybg`), PICK A SIDE, the countdown (**Countdown** 10–120 s, `tyd`; the clip length follows it), "VORTEX 52% VS BOMBER 48%" and a live percentage bar (`tyh`); an optional dotted grid whose dots deflect the balls (`typg`). The most tiles at the end win (winner banner + confetti, DRAW on a level top); a team roster lends its colours and names, Find Simulation searches a winner, the Forced Winner rigs one. The Ball Size has no maximum: a ball wider than a tile takes the tiles under its contact edge – a dent the shape of its front on every hit –, big balls that crowd the board pass through each other (ARENA FULL) and a ball wider than the board eats the arena |
| **Maze Escape** | A battle-family mode (`maze`, feature odd-maze) after oddplayground's maze reels ("who else thought that was blood"): a seeded perfect maze (6–40 columns on the slider, `mzc`; the rows follow the portrait field) with glowing green walls on near-black, a red entrance slot in the top wall and an exit gap in the bottom wall. 1–8 balls (`mzn`; the Teams roster colours and names the first six) queue in a chute above the entrance and drop in a moment apart, then move physically – a downward pull (`mzg`), perfectly elastic walls, their own speed (`mzs`) – in micro-steps against a cell-indexed wall grid, so no ball ever tunnels through a wall at any speed. A **brain** steers them by nudging the velocity at every cell centre: **bounce** (pure physics), **wall follower** (left, right or alternating hand, `mzh`) or **explorer** (prefers the cells it has not visited – a depth-first search) (`mzb`). Every cell a ball visits is painted in blood red (opacity `mzt`, colour `mztc`, or each ball's own colour `mzto`), every wall hit is a note whose pitch follows the cell's BFS distance to the exit – the melody climbs as a ball closes in – and the first ball through the exit wins (wall-break sound, shake, slow motion as the leader reaches the exit cell, the winner banner with its deadpan caption); the others keep going until every ball is out or the clip limit (`mzd`, then the ball nearest the exit wins). A HUD with a distance-to-exit bar per ball (`mzhud`), the "FLASHING LIGHTS" badge (`mzbg`), fog over the unvisited cells (`mzf`) and the wall colour (`mzwc`). Deterministic: Find Simulation searches the run length or the winner; the rigged forced winner steers the shortest way and the exit stays closed to the others until it is out |
| **Conveyor Belt & timed respawns** | An escape-family mode (`conveyor`, feature gerald-conveyor) after the character-ball account's "conveyor belt loading" and "Gerald respawns every 3 seconds" clips: a conveyor belt across the top of the field (its tread moving, its rollers turning) carries a ball out of a hatch every **Drop Interval** seconds (0.5–10 on the slider, default 3; `cvi`) and drops it into the arena below, up to **Balls** (1–200 on the slider, default 8; `cvn`). The **Arena** (`cva`): **rings** – Classic's concentric rings with rotating gaps (Wall Count, Gap Size and Rotation apply) that every ball has to escape on its own (a note per ring passed, the wall-break sound and confetti per escape, the director's help for a ball stuck for long) while a second belt at the bottom carries the escaped ones away; **bowl** – a U-shaped container where the balls pile up and settle (about 30 of the Ball Size before the first one rolls over the rim), with an overflow counter; **pegs** – a Galton board of notes over bins. **Freeze on Landing** (`cvf`): a ball that lands freezes in place and becomes an obstacle, so the pile grows visibly (in the rings its freedom lasts four seconds); **Variety** (`cvv`) mixes the balls' sizes and colours – the first is always Gerald. The belt hums while it carries a ball and clicks when one drops, every bounce is a note through the ToneGenerator (melodies, instruments, hit samples, beat lock and the music bed apply), and a counter keeps score ("Loaded 17 / Escaped 4"). The schedule runs on the simulation clock and the run is seeded, so Find Simulation searches its length. Classic and Multiply get the same timer as **Respawn Every** (`respawnEvery`, URL `rse`, seconds, 0 = off): a new ball drops in from the top every N seconds |
| **Moving exits & splat barriers** | Two options of the ring modes (feature gerald-exit-splat) after the character-ball account's "solving the case of the moving exit" and "Splat Barrier Demo" clips. **Exit Behaviour** (the end of the Wall section; Classic, Accumulation and Multiply – the ring modes with one exit a ring; URL `exit`): **Rotate** (the exits turn with their rings – the default), **Jump** (every ring's exit teleports to a new seeded spot of its ring every `exj` seconds, 1–10, or 0.2 s after a ball comes within `exs` degrees of it – a ball that reaches the doorway first gets out – with a flash where it was and where it lands), **Flee** (the exit runs away from the ball along its ring at up to `exf` degrees a second while the ball is within its sense: the ball has to out-run it) and **Shrink** (the exit narrows over `exj` seconds until it shuts, then opens again at least a quarter turn away). With a moving exit the rings hold still, and the exits move at every physics sub-step, so a gap pass is judged where the exit is at that moment. **Splat Barrier** (Visual section, after Wobbly Walls; every ring mode but Shatter and Color Match; URL `splat`): every wall hit leaves a splat of paint in the ball's colour, with drips, at the impact point on the inside of the wall – `sps` × the ball's radius (0.5–2) – and every splat is a solid circle of the obstacle layer, so the ball builds its own barrier; past `spm` splats (10–300) the oldest fade out, a splat drips off when an exit moves over it or its ring breaks, and when the barrier has kept every ball off the wall for 2.5 s the oldest one falls, so a run never locks up (Grow's splats only paint its ring). A landing plays a wet splat – a noise burst through a falling low-pass over a low blop, or the hit sample an octave down in sample mode. Both off by default and deterministic (the engine's seeded RNG): Find Simulation, links, presets, the fast export and the split-screen arenas replay them |
| **Pricing & licences (Pro)** | The playground stays free – every mode, setting and sound, Find Simulation, presets and share links, the gallery, split-screen races, the obstacle editor, captions, the timeline and the daily challenge, with no account (feature paywall-gate). **Pro** – $6.99 a month or $49 a year (save 42 %), by card through Stripe Checkout (Apple Pay and Google Pay included), PayPal, or BTC, ETH, USDT and 300+ coins through NOWPayments (a prepaid period: crypto cannot renew by itself) – unlocked everything that creates a video file; --- free-watermark --- since the watermark gate everyone records and exports (Record Video, the fast export, the batch render, the viral bot's renders, the desktop app's render queue) and Pro removes the watermark from the videos and keeps Publish (its button carries a lock and a "Pro" tag and opens the Unlock dialog, now "Remove the watermark"); a `/pricing` page in every language holds the Free / Pro table, the plans, the purchase a checkout returns with, Manage subscription and Restore purchase, and an account row in the studio says Free or "Pro – <plan> until <date>". Licences are ES256-signed JWTs the backend (`billing/`) issues and the browser verifies with WebCrypto; without a configured key the site runs in a visible test mode (see [Pricing and licences](#pricing-and-licences)) |
| **Bouncing Orbs** | A rhythm-family mode (`orbGrid`, feature orb-grid) after an Instagram account's "Satisfying physics simulation – N varied bouncing orbs" clips: a perspective field of glossy orbs – a grid, a hex grid, a disc or octagon rings of **Columns × Rows** orbs (`ogC` × `ogR`; 33 × 33 = 1089 by default, the count drawn as "1089 bouncing orbs" at the top of the clip; 70 × 70 = 4900 and any count typed, a run building at most 250,000) – over a blue-grid slab, a round plate, a grid or nothing, every orb bouncing straight up and down on its own spot. One **varied property** (bounciness, drop height, release delay, size, gravity or period, `ogV`) follows a **distribution** over the field (varied, corner to corner, centre outwards, rows, columns, spiral, ripple bands, checker, `ogD`) with a **spread** (`ogS`), the orbs released together, outside in, inside out, row by row or at random (`ogL`, stagger `ogT`): the heights form a wave surface – a rising sheet, a funnel, rings – that falls into phase at the **resolve moment** (`ogRes`) and dissolves again, and – in the **Decay** model – the run ends when every orb is at rest (the default **Rhythm** model bounces forever: see *Bouncing Orbs: rhythm, polyrhythms & metronome*). Glossy, metallic, matte or glass orbs (`ogM`) coloured by height, ring, row, the Ball Colour or position (`ogP`) under a tilting (`ogE`), turning (`ogRot`) and auto-orbiting (`ogO`) camera; every landing a soft note of the scale (a row lands as a chord), or a sleep sound, a melody composed by the landings or metal clinks (`ogSnd`). Eight presets in the Presets group after the account's clips; Find Simulation searches a run length, a run still bouncing when the clip ends or the first in-phase moment at a chosen second; 1089 orbs run at ~55 fps on the page and ~29 fps while recording 1080×1920 (see the Bouncing Orbs section under How to extend it) |
| **Fight League** | An arena game in the rhythm family (`fightLeague`, feature fight-league, rebuilt by the seven-stage overhaul; next to Battle Royale and Capture the Flag) in which popular characters duel as balls, every matchup in its genre: **147 fighters in 19 divisions and 5 conferences** (Comics A, Comics B, platformers, MOBA, fighting games, game legends, shonen anime, space saga, fantasy, movie monsters, action movies, TV, pocket monsters, modern anime, sandbox & online games, horror movies, animated movies, cartoons, and Gerald alone in Wildcard), each with one of **21 weapon kinds** drawn as the site's own vector silhouette under a contract of reach, cadence and hit geometry, stats, a role, a look of our own and an ability built from **26 primitives** that charges from time and damage, telegraphs by its class and fires by itself – fair hits (per-source windows, clashes, guard, crowd-control immunity), sudden death and an HP-fraction verdict at the cap, seeded and deterministic at any frame rate. **1v1** (Thor vs Loki), 2v2, 3v3 and free-for-alls of 3 and 4, best-of-N series, brackets and boss rushes played as one run; a lilac stage with a VS card, 3-2-1, FIGHT!, first blood, a lead bar, callouts, a KO finale in slow motion and a winner card that loops, 9:16 plates; synthesized signature sounds, an announcer, match stings and custom clip slots for the user's own licensed audio (kept on the device); a story engine with the VS card's "win chance (simulated)"; Find Simulation outcomes (a winner, a length, a double KO, upset, clutch, perfect, comeback, ability finish, lead changes, a cliffhanger, a decider, sudden death, a champion, the boss falling); the honest rigged forced winner per format; 76 presets with a gallery and Surprise me, a fighter picker, Make a clip, a tier list card and the viral bot's fight recipes. Names, sources and move names are plain text labels of an unaffiliated fan simulation – no logos, costumes, faces, artwork or audio copied. In the Fight League block of the Mode row, shared via the URL (`fl1`–`fl6` and the other `fl*` keys of the section's settings table) |
| **Land Claim** | A battle-family mode (`landClaim`, feature land-claim) after the land-claim clips of an arena-battle account ("who will claim the most?", "1,144 blocks, 146 balls"): the arena – a **square**, a **hexagon** or a **circle** (`lca`) – is lined with **Block Columns** (`lcc`, default 24, spread over the sides) of **Blocks per Column** (`lcr`, default 12) standing on the inside of its wall; **Competitors** 2–12 on the slider, any number typed (`lct`, default 4; the Teams roster names, colours and flags the first six – its new **Country** picker fills a row with one of 47 countries –, a palette of RED, BLUE, GREEN, GOLD… the rest) bounce **Balls Each** (`lcb`, default 3) at a constant speed (gravity 0 by default, the Gravity slider still bends them), elastic off the walls, the columns and each other. The **Claim Rule** (`lcm`): **knock** – a hit takes the column's top block, which flies off in the hitter's colour and counts for it; **claim** – the block stays, recoloured; **steal** – a hit on a claimed column flips its top block that is not the hitter's, and the battle runs to its duration. Every **New Ball Every** blocks a competitor takes (`lce`, default 8; 0 = never) spawn one more ball of its colour at the knock point. The battle ends when the wall is taken or at its **Battle Duration** (`lcd`, default 60 s; the clip follows it) and the most blocks wins: DOMINATION past 60 % of the land, SUCH A CLOSE BATTLE when the top two are within 5 %, else "[name] CLAIMS THE MOST". A HUD in the exported square (`lch`): the **Battle Title** line (`lcti`, default LAND CLAIM), the counters and a live bar per competitor (its badge, name, blocks and balls in play); flags drawn as emoji inside a glowing ring in the team colour – the two-letter code on a disc where the fonts have no flag glyphs. A wooden knock pitched by the column, a spawn chime and a KO thump on a column's last block (the arena's last block accented). Five presets (4 countries, Domination: 1144 blocks, 146 balls, Steal, Hexagon: 6 countries, 2v2 claim), a "Who claims the most?" caption whose answer `[winner]` names the winner, Find Simulation's winner, run-length and **Close battle** outcomes, and an honest Forced Winner that aims the chosen colour's rebounds at land still to take. Every number is uncapped (a million blocks, a thousand competitors, 2,000 balls at most a run). See "Land Claim" below |
| **Obfuscation & proprietary licence** | The site is closed-source (feature code-obfuscation). `npm run build` obfuscates the shipped JavaScript as a build step (`scripts/obfuscate.mjs`, run from `scripts/postexport.mjs` before the service worker so its precache hashes see the final files): every `/_next/static/chunks` chunk but the Next loader chunks (webpack/framework/main/polyfills) gets hexadecimal identifier renaming (every local, function and class name, the engine's included) and a one-line proprietary banner, source maps are off and any `.map` is deleted. Settings are tuned for speed so the 60 fps budget, the smoke-test frame-rate floors and the seed finder hold (the string array is off – its wrapper de-optimised the physics hot loop, measured ~28x on the finder); `OBFUSCATE=0` skips it for debugging. A `LICENSE` file, `"license": "UNLICENSED"` in every package and a line on the terms page make the software proprietary. It deters casual copying but is not DRM – a determined person can still read it, so the repository must stay private (see [Obfuscation and the licence](#obfuscation-and-the-licence)) |
| **String Circle** | A third style of the String Battle (`sbStyle` "circle", URL `sbst=circle`; feature string-circle) after the STRING CIRCLE country fights of an arena-battle account (the clips of Land Claim's account): flag balls fly round a thin white **circle** or a **hexagon** (`sba`) on black, each curling as it flies, and every ball anchors **Strings per Second** (`sbrt`, default 30) from itself to the rim straight ahead of it – a dense fan in its colour that sweeps the rim. A ball crossing a rival's string on its outer half cuts it. Under the strings rule (the battle's default, feature string-battle-alive) a flag is alive while one of its strings holds and is out when its last one is cut (a fan keeps at most 256 strings, halved every second once 4 s pass without a knockout, so four flags fight it out in 30–50 s); under the lives rule (`sblf=lives`, what the five presets play) every **Strings per Life** strings of one owner cut (`sbc`, default 200) take a life and shield its fan for 2.5 s and a ball out of lives is out; its fan dissolves, and the last flag standing wins (at a clip limit: the most lives, then the most rim). The last two flags fight the String Battle's final duel. The fans' share of the rim is the live score – thin arcs just outside the wall and a **Standings Strip** along the bottom (`sbh`) – under a **Title Line** (`sbti`, default STRING CIRCLE). The Teams roster (its Country picker – Land Claim's, now 47 countries – fills a row with a flag) names, colours and flags the first balls; the balls past it play the clips' countries by their two-letter codes in colours apart (TR, IN, US, IR, DE, CA, JP, CN…), so a mega fight has any number of flags. A soft plucked twang for the strings anchored (at most three team notes at a time, twenty times a second), a snap for the strings cut, the battle's shatter at a KO. Five presets (Country fight: India vs USA, Battle of countries: Turkey / India / USA / Iran, Mega country fight (12), Hexagon arena (6), String arena classic (5)), a "Which flag wins?" caption, Find Simulation's winner outcome and the Forced Winner by flag; every number uncapped (a ball keeps up to 1,024 live strings – a memory-safety ceiling). Measured by the smoke test on a shared 4-core machine with software rendering (the obfuscated build too): the four-flag default and the 12-flag mega preset at 60 fps on the page, the mega preset recorded at 1080×1920 at 46–55 fps; fans of ~400 strings on 5 and 12 balls at 60 fps on the page and 43–50 / 29–40 fps recorded. See "String Circle" below |
| **Free-video watermark** | Every video made without a verified Pro licence carries the watermark, drawn into its pixels by the compositor of every output path (feature free-watermark, `src/lib/watermark/`): a badge – the logo mark, the site's name and domain at 70 % opacity with a soft shadow – in a bottom corner of the exported square inside the platforms' safe zone, changing corner every 6 s, plus the domain tiled diagonally over the whole frame at 4 %. Recording, the fast export, the batch render, the viral bot and the desktop render queue now work for everyone; the decision is sealed when a recording or export starts by verifying the stored licence again, in module-private state no DOM, storage, URL or console edit reaches (see [Free-video watermark](#free-video-watermark)) |
| **Bouncing Orbs: rhythm, polyrhythms & metronome** | The Bouncing Orbs rework (feature orb-rhythm) after the owner's "bouncing orbs is clunky and not fluid like the instagram given, add metronome and polyrythms to it": the new default **Rhythm** model (`ogModel`; **Decay** keeps the settling field) makes every orb an ideal bouncer on its spot forever – its height the parabola of an elastic bounce, h(t) = A · 4u(1 − u) with u = frac((t − φ) / T), its apex A = g · T² / 8 under the Gravity slider (faster orbs bounce lower) or all alike (**Equal Heights**, `ogEq`) – drawn at every frame's own simulation time, so the field moves smoothly at 60 or 120 Hz and in the fast export's exact frame times, with a 10 % **Landing Squash** easing out over 60 ms (`ogSquash`). **Polyrhythm** (`ogPoly`): **Groups** of rows, columns, rings, diagonals, a checkerboard or every orb (`ogGroup`), group k bouncing n_k times a **Cycle** (`ogCycle`, 30 s), so every orb lands together at every multiple of the cycle – the **IN PHASE** moment, with its banner on a dark backdrop and a chord – and the wave surface morphs in between; ten **Rhythm Presets** (`ogRhythm`): Pendulum wave (51 + k), 3 against 2, 4 against 3, 5 against 4, 7 against 5, 3:4:5, Euclidean (`ogSteps`), Corner to corner, Centre outwards and Varied. The **Metronome**: a **Tempo** (`ogBpm`, 120; the beat lock's while it is on) and **Beats per Bar** (`ogBeats`), a swinging bar at the top of the square, a ring pulsing around the field or a dot hopping along a beat strip (`ogMetro`), a woodblock **Click** with an accented downbeat (`ogClick`), the cycle snapped to whole **Bars** (`ogBars`, 16) so IN PHASE lands on a downbeat, and **Melody** (`ogMelody`): a pitch of the scale per group, the polyrhythm playing a tune. A ninth preset, Metronome 3:2; Find Simulation's In phase at answered at once by the cycle maths; a caption's question answered at the IN PHASE moment. See the Bouncing Orbs section under How to extend it |
| **Windows app 1.0.3: the AI fixed, an AI status panel** | The owner's "the exe ai doesnt work" (feature desktop-ai-fix): the AI studio of the Windows app works with the small local models now and says why when it does not. Each job normalises its answer before the checks (hashtags split, given their "#", cleaned, de-duplicated and clamped to 3–15; clip names slugified; platforms mapped – `src/lib/desktop/ai/normalise.ts`), the reply grammar bounds what it can (a hashtag ≤ 61 characters, a name ≤ 60, the copy's platforms the requested ones, one item each), **Make videos** offers its answer only after `plan_clips` has made plans and then only with their ids (the reply envelope is rebuilt every turn), a retry comes as the app's note with a hint at a higher temperature, and the prompts are shorter (the settings that matter for the request, recipe hooks without placeholders). The local model keeps one chat session per load (the evaluated prefix is reused: no re-reading 3–4k tokens every turn), gets a context of at least 4,096 tokens and room for prompt + reply, falls back to fewer GPU layers and then the CPU when memory is short (then: "Not enough free memory for this model: close other apps or pick a smaller model (Qwen2.5 1.5B is the smallest)"), and unloads only after the reply in progress (also when quitting). Every download and cloud call goes through Electron's **net.fetch** (the system proxy and certificate store – antivirus HTTPS scanning and company proxies broke Node's fetch, which stays as the fallback), errors carry their cause codes explained, and everything lands in main.log (llama.cpp's own messages, every request with its timings, every failed IPC handler with its cause chain). An **AI status** panel tops the AI tab: Runtime, Model, Cloud provider, Network and Last error, green / amber / red with the reason, **Run checks** (llama.cpp started, the model loaded with an 8-token grammar test, the endpoint and Hugging Face reached), **Copy report** (JSON: versions, RAM, GPU, backend, model, provider – never the key –, the last errors, main.log's tail), **Open logs** and **Run the model on: Auto (GPU) / CPU only**. A first-run card offers the 2 GB model or a cloud key, a finished download is selected when the selected model is missing (before the page hears it is ready, so Run is enabled at once), a run shows "Reading the request… N s" and a timing line per step. Version **1.0.3**: unsigned builds no longer carry a publisher name, so 1.0.3 → 1.0.4 updates install (1.0.2 users install 1.0.3 by hand once – the release notes said so), and CI's packaged smoke runs a real Settings and Captions job on a cached GGUF (see [Windows app: AI fix and status panel](#windows-app-ai-fix-and-status-panel)) |
| **Mode thumbnails** | Every mode card – the modes wall, the studio's mode picker, the daily card – shows its mode at its **hero moment**, mid-action with the payoff on screen (feature mode-thumbnails): an entry per mode in `HERO_MOMENTS` (`src/lib/thumbnails/heroMoments.ts` – settings, pinned seed, second, camera and the mode's colour; glow and trails on, bigger balls where a 240 px card needs them, only the HUD element that is the point), rendered by the real simulator's deterministic still camera and framed the same way for every card: a 480 × 480 square (2× the ~240 px card), the same inset and vignette, a subtle edge glow in the mode's colour, WebP under 60 KB, no captions or watermark. `npm run previews` regenerates them, printing each file's size, and stops when a mode has no hero moment; the viral bot's covers use the same idea – the payoff frame of each clip, not its first frame (see [Mode thumbnails](#mode-thumbnails)) |
| **Watermark on every free simulation** | The free-video watermark on the live canvas too (feature watermark-everywhere, `src/lib/watermark/live.ts`, `liveLayout.ts`): every frame a visitor without a verified Pro licence sees – the studio's canvas in every mode (and so share links, the gallery's presets, the daily challenge and the Windows app, which runs the same page), a split-screen race's composed frame (once, not per arena) and the landing page's live preview – carries the badge and the faint domain tiles, drawn into the canvas' own pixels as the frame's last pass: 4.5 % of the exported square tall, 1.5 % from its edges, on the other side every 6 s, clear of the mode's HUD. The decision is free-watermark's sealed gate, so a Pro licence removes it at once – mid-run too – and removing the licence brings it back; a page recording copies each canvas frame just before that pass and stamps the video's own badge at export resolution – one badge per frame in the video layout's place, clear of the HUD, the tiles over the letterbox bars too (--- low-findings ---; see [Watermark on every free simulation](#watermark-on-every-free-simulation)) |
| **Loop foundation & Grow: fill and loop** | The first family of the loop-clip account's simulations (feature loop-foundation). An original synthesized sound library (`src/lib/audio/loopTones.ts`, `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`: pentatonic plucks, tuned bars, a progress ladder, a groove bed, impact accents, a completion chord, reset glides and washes, a field drone – every voice scheduled on the simulation clock under a per-tick voice budget, with optional clip slots for your own sounds that never leave the device), the offline mix of the fast export and the batch render normalised to −14 LUFS with its true peak at most −1 dBTP (BS.1770), the loop contract (a mode reports its seamless cycle; **Export whole loops** cuts recordings, fast exports and batch renders to a whole number of cycles), a lowercase **loop HUD** drawn into the frames, a **Loop style** caption preset in Publish, and Grow's fill-and-loop upgrade: growth law approach / multiply / add, when it fills stay / loop / finish, hold and shrink, colour by size, contact markers, pitch by size, the presets Fill and loop, Slow burn, Instant and Classic grow, and Find Simulation's "Fills within" and "Fill on a bar line". See [Grow: fill and loop](#grow-fill-and-loop) and [Loop foundation](#loop-foundation-sounds-whole-loops-and-the-loop-hud). |
| **Sharded smoke test** | The browser smoke test (`scripts/smoke-test.mjs`, some 750 checks, ~45 minutes on one runner) runs in CI as **four shards on four runners at once**, about 12 minutes each (feature smoke-sharding): the suite is a list of blocks – `await smokeBlock("title", async () => { … })`, each setting up its own page – split at block level, never at check level, by a greedy longest-first balance over the blocks' wall times in `scripts/smoke-timing.json` (a block the file does not know weighs the median, so a new block gets a shard by itself). `--shard i/N` / `SMOKE_SHARD=i/N` runs one part, `SMOKE_ONLY=<title>` single blocks, `--list` prints the split, `--write-timing` refreshes the baseline, every run prints its 15 slowest blocks, and a narrowed run starts every block from a clean page. The `smoke` job of deploy.yml and smoke.yml is a matrix of the four shards; `smoke-summary` merges their screenshots and slowest blocks and gates the deploy (see [Smoke test shards](#smoke-test-shards)) |
| **Chord Stars** | A rhythm-family mode of the loop family (`starChords`, feature chord-stars) after the loop-clip account's "five stars, all closing at once" clip: 1–12 balls (any number typed) ride inside one thin lavender circle on navy, each drawing a star polygon n/k – every bounce keeps its angle, so the contact point steps k of n points round the rim and every chord touches one inner circle of radius R·cos(πk/n). The motion is analytic (no solver: at cycle time t ball i has made ⌊t·nᵢ/T⌋ bounces), every ball moves at nᵢ·chordᵢ/T so **every star closes on the same frame** at any frame rate, then the drawing holds, fades and loops (the loop contract: whole loops of drawing + hold + fade). Stars as typed `n/k` pairs or a random coprime set, drawing time, hold, fade, start spread, line width, inner circles (when closed / always / off), pastel / rainbow / ball colours, a pentatonic pluck, chime or bar per bounce pitched by the ball's speed, a completion chord with a reset glide, the loop HUD's "stars closed 3/5" counter, the presets Five stars, Seven-point bloom and Heptagram duel, and a Find Simulation outcome that picks the best-looking star set and fits the loop to the clip. See [Chord Stars](#chord-stars). |
| **Spinning Hoops** | A rhythm-family mode without rings (`hoops`, feature bead-hoops) after the loop-clip account's bifurcation reel ("the biggest hoop's bead lifts first"): 1–16 concentric rainbow hoops (eight by default, red outside, violet inside) spin together about their vertical diameter, drawn face-on and squashed sideways by the cosine of their turn so they read as 3D, each with one bead sliding on it (θ″ = sin θ (ω² cos θ − g/R) − c θ′, RK4 in 8+ sub-steps a 60 Hz step). The spin ramps from 0.13 to 1.05 turns a second – linear, eased or one step a hoop – and past each hoop's critical spin √(g/R) its bead climbs to arccos(g/(Rω²)), the biggest hoop's first: every lift a tuned bar note pitched by its hoop, a glock chime every turn, a chord when all are up. The return ramps the spin back down until every bead rests again – a seamless loop of whole turns for **Export whole loops**. The presets **8 rainbow hoops**, **One hoop, slow** and **Twin hoops** bring the clip's navy page and the loop HUD ("spin faster, and the beads climb", the amber counter "0.13 turns a second · 0 of 8 beads up"); Find Simulation searches **Lifts in size order** and **All up within**. See [Spinning Hoops](#spinning-hoops-the-loop-clip-family). |
| **Chaos fan (Double Pendulum)** | The Double Pendulum's chaos fan (feature chaos-fan) after the loop-clip account's "they start less than a thousandth of a degree apart" reel: a **Chaos fan** switch at the top of the Double Pendulum block swings 1–500 double pendulums on the slider (any count typed, at most 5,000 built – what one step's budget integrates) from one pivot, their lower rods spread over a total **Start spread** of 0.001° (0–1° on the slider, quick picks from a millionth to a tenth of a degree): one thin white line with a pink bob for a few seconds, then a rainbow fan by index (or the Ball Color). **Run backwards** records the **Forward run** (10 s) and plays it back, so every pendulum retraces its path home and the clip loops on a seam (Export whole loops), under the loop HUD's "200 pendulums, a hair apart" and a grey "running forwards" / "running backwards" label. Original synthesized sounds: a pentatonic pluck as the bobs swing through the bottom – one voice while the fan is together, a widening cloud as it spreads, mirrored on the way back –, a ding at the split, a wash at the turn, the completion chord as the fan comes home and a reset glide onto the seam. Find Simulation's **Splits at** finds a seed whose split lands at a chosen second; presets **200 pendulums, a hair apart**, **1,000 threads** and **Two that drift apart**. See [Chaos fan](#chaos-fan-the-double-pendulums-palindrome). |
| **Phase Spiral** | The Metronomes & Polyrhythms rings as the loop-clip account's "watch the spiral straighten into one line" reel (feature phase-spiral, `src/lib/physics/phaseSpiral.ts`): **Arms** put 1–8 dots on every ring (any whole number typed; rings and spiral layouts), evenly spaced on as many spokes, and a dot steps one spoke a tick – ring i still ticks i + 1 times a cycle, so the polyrhythm, its chord and its finish stay the classic ones while the picture winds into a k-armed spiral, passes through interference patterns and straightens into the spokes **exactly once a cycle**, on the alignment. The **in-line meter** shows "in line N%" over a thin amber bar – the arms-fold resultant \|mean e^{i·k·θ}\| of the dot angles (100 % at every alignment, near 0 in between) – under the disc or as the loop HUD's counter; **comet tails** (0–1 s of motion, the classic 0.18 s by default) fade behind every dot; the **spiral colours** go blue inside to red outside on a dark disc; **loop sounds** play a pentatonic pluck a tick (inner rings low), risers as the meter crosses 50, 75 and 90 % that stop on the alignment's open chord and bell. An endless run (Cycles 0) is a seamless loop for **Export whole loops**. The **Phase spiral, 20 rings × 3 arms** preset brings the clip's navy page and loop HUD. See [Phase Spiral](#phase-spiral-the-loop-clip-family). |
| **Focus Shot** | A rhythm-family mode of the loop family (`ellipseFoci`, feature ellipse-foci) after the loop-clip account's whispering-gallery clip: an elliptical pool – a pool table with a wooden rim, a copper rail and green felt, or a neon or thin outline – with its two foci marked, and a volley of balls (72 by default, 1–720 on the slider, any whole number typed) fired at once from one focus every way, coloured around the hue wheel by launch angle, with tapering comet trails. Every focus–wall–focus path is 2a long, so at the speed 2a / T the whole volley converges on the other focus every transit T – a white bloom – alternating foci, while every pass collapses the swarm onto the long axis. The motion is analytic (the ray–ellipse quadratic and the reflection about the ellipse's normal, no drift: a seed replays exactly at any frame rate and in the fast export). At a convergence: **Refire** (a fresh volley every Passes per loop – a seamless loop of passes × T that Export whole loops cuts to), **Collapse** or **Every pass**; eccentricity, size, transit time, spread and start angle, launch focus, orientation, trail length, a paths overlay. Sound: a descending pentatonic pluck per wall hit, an impact (kick, noise crack, chord) on every convergence after a 0.4 s pre-drop, a groove bed at 8 · 60 / T BPM; the lowercase loop HUD ("fire from one dot, hit the other") and an optional pass counter; Find Simulation's **Converges on the beat** and **Longest collapse**; five presets. See [Focus Shot](#focus-shot). |
| **Colour Sort** | An escape-family mode (`colourSort`, feature colour-sort) after the loop family's colour-sorting rings ("360 mixed balls, sorted by four rings"): **Colours** × **Balls per Colour** (`csc` 4, `csn` 90 – 360 balls) start packed in the core of K concentric rings – K − 1 gated rings and a closed outer wall, at (k + 1) / K of its radius – and move as a constant-speed gas (**Ball Speed** `csv`, 300 px/s of a 720-wide frame; no gravity). Every gated ring is **Gates per Ring** arcs and gates (`csg` 4, **Gate Width** `csw` 35°) with a white pin at each arc end, turning at the **Inner Ring Spin** (`css`, 31°/s) times the **Spin Falloff** per ring outward (`csf`, ×0.8), in **Alternate Directions** (`csa`). A gate is a one-way colour filter – the **Gate Rule** (`csu`): outward and never the ring's own colour (the reference), outward and only its own colour, or both ways but never its own – and refuses every other ball like an arc, reflected in the ring's turning frame: red never leaves the core, orange stops at the orange ring, green at the green one and blue reaches the outer band – a bullseye. (Both ways lets sorted balls wander back in, so that sort never completes: it settles at an equilibrium – about 200 / 360 – and the cycle ends once the count stops rising, on the minor chord, with no Find Simulation outcomes.) The loop HUD's amber counter "n / 360 sorted" (a ball in its own band) climbs with a long tail to the completion chord (A2 with its 1.7 Hz tremolo, minor when the **Time Limit** `csl` ran out first), then reads "mixing again": a critically damped spring pulls every ball back to its exact frame-0 place over the **Remix** (`csm`, 2.8 s) under a noise wash, and the next cycle replays the first – the loop contract reports every seam, so Export whole loops cuts a recording into seamless cycles. Every gate pass plucks its colour's note of A major pentatonic (at most six a tick). **Ball Contacts** (`csx`) are on by default: elastic contacts, at a speed that keeps the reference clip's pace; off, a ghost gas, cheap for thousands of balls; **Ball Size** (`csr`, 4.5 px), **Refusal Flashes** (`csh`). The page's own Ball Speed, Ball Size and Ball Color (with Rainbow) are not shown in this mode: its balls, their speed, size and colours are its own (`ballControlsOf()` in `panelKeys.ts`). Five presets (Bullseye sort, Rainbow 7, One-gate torture, Ten thousand, Reverse rule) and Find Simulation's **All sorted within**, **Last colour** and **Sorted on a bar line** (±20 ms) outcomes. Every number is uncapped (20,000 balls a run at most). See "Colour Sort" below |
| **Beat Machines** | A rhythm-family mode of the loop family (`beatMachine`, feature beat-machine) after the loop-clip account's "every note is a marble" and "eight steps. eight notes. one phrase" clips: kinetic music machines in which every note is a ball landing on a tuned target exactly on the beat, with a mechanical return path so the machine loops forever. **Marble xylophone** – 10 rainbow bars (2–48, any number typed) low to high, a tube of marbles in each bar's colour above it, a brass floor, a ladder lift with moving rungs and a top rail: each marble leaves its tube at t_note − √(2h/g) so it strikes its bar on the beat, rebounds through the gap to the floor, rolls to the lift, waits for the next free rung, rides up and rolls back along the rail into its own tube; the tubes are first in, first out and hold as many marbles as the tune needs (a marble that cannot be back in time gives its tube a spare); 32 beats at 100 BPM loop in 19.2 s. **Staircase** – one cream ball hops down 8 steps (1–64) in identical arcs of one beat (80 BPM: 749 ms), every landing a note, then drops into a hook, rolls round the U-shaped return track and is tipped back onto step 1 exactly `returnBeats` (4) beats later – (steps + return) beats a loop, progress dots under the panel. The notes: a seeded original phrase, a scale run, the melody loaded under Sound or a typed list (bar numbers or note names); the Sound section's scale and root tune the bars and steps. Sound (original synthesis through `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`, scheduled from the plan): tuned-bar strikes and a phrase bass every two bars, pentatonic plucks and a reset glide that ends at the loop point. Four presets (Eight steps, Scale staircase, Marble xylophone, Load your melody), the loop HUD ("every note is a marble", "each step plays a note") with an optional note counter, the loop contract for **Export whole loops**, and Find Simulation's **No tube conflict** and **Most marbles in flight**. See [Beat Machines](#beat-machines). |
| **Funnel Spin-up (Sound Vortex)** | The Sound Vortex's ring waves and perspective view (feature funnel-spin) after the loop family's "watch the ring of 24 spin up as it drops" clip: **Release** one by one (the Sound Vortex as it was) or as **ring waves** – 6, 12 and 24 marbles released every 4.5 s, evenly spaced on the rim with the same speed and spin, each wave one polar state (`src/lib/physics/funnelSpin.ts`, `funnelRun.ts`) so it stays a perfect ring all the way down. The spin friction acts on the angular momentum and the radial motion separately: L is nearly kept while friction lets the ring slip lower, so its lap climbs from the 5 s first lap to about 2.3 laps a second by the hole (about 11×, 12.5 s down with the defaults; without friction a ring orbits the rim for ever). After the last ring and a refill gap the first wave comes back: a seamless loop the whole-loop export cuts on. **View** from above or in **perspective** – a translucent navy gravity well seen from the camera elevation (25° by default, 90° is the top view), a mesh of rings and spokes, the spout narrowing to a dark hole, glossy rainbow marbles depth-sorted behind the pale front lip (`components/simulator/funnelRenderer.ts`). Original sounds through the loop library: a glock chime a lap pitched by the ring's spin, a rolling hiss following the marbles' speed (`src/lib/audio/funnelTones.ts`), a groove bed at the page's tempo and a noise wash before every refill. Presets Ring of 24, Galaxy (360 from above), Coin funnel and Doubling (1 … 256); the loop HUD's "why marbles speed up in a funnel" with an optional lap readout; Find Simulation's **Last ring on a bar** fits the wave interval and the friction; a loop-style caption. See [Sound Vortex](#sound-vortex) |
| **Land Claim pyramid** | A second layout of Land Claim (`lcLayout` "pyramid", URL `lcl=pyramid`; feature land-claim-pyramid) after the loop-clip account's "1,144 blocks, 146 balls, and the summit goes last" clip: instead of the arena's wall, a stepped pyramid – a **Pyramid Size** × Pyramid Size grid of columns (`lcg`, 11; 3–40 on the slider, any whole number typed) whose heights are **Steps** (`lcs`, 6) minus each column's Chebyshev distance to the centre, scaled to exactly **Blocks** (`lcbk`, 1,144; 0 = the natural Σ of the steps) – stands on a square floor, drawn isometrically on a navy page: teal, green and yellow layers up to an orange summit, light tops and darker sides, the balls rolling on the floor with soft shadows. The physics is top down: every column with a block is a square obstacle, a hit knocks its top block off (it flies away in the hitter's colour) and a bare column is floor again; Land Claim's rule stays – every **New Ball Every** blocks (8) adds a ball – so the outer steps go first and the summit falls last, with 3 + 1,144 ÷ 8 = 146 balls on the floor. One colour (yellow) or any number of competitors (the wall's verdicts then apply). **Rebuild and loop** (`lcrb`): after the summit's payoff the columns grow back over the **Rebuild Time** (`lcrs`, 2.5 s), the added balls melt into the pyramid and the starting balls glide to their corners, and the seam restores the first frame exactly – the loop contract, so Export whole loops cuts seamless cycles. Original synthesized sounds from the loop library: a pentatonic pluck per block pitched by its layer (low blocks low, at most six new notes a tick), a chime on every eighth block, an impact accent and a major chord as the summit falls, the completion chord when the floor is clear, and the reset glide, wash and cut into the seam. The loop HUD reads "a pyramid, one block at a time" over a grey "N blocks" counter. Find Simulation's **The summit falls last**; preset **Pyramid, 1,144 blocks**. Every number uncapped (a 1,000-wide grid and a million blocks are the memory-safety ceilings). See "Land Claim" below |
| **Ring Escape (Classic)** | --- ring-escape --- The loop family's oldest reel ("it takes three seconds to get through the first one 🔊") as options of Classic's own rings (no new mode; `src/lib/physics/ringEscape.ts`; Wall Settings in Classic): a **gap layout** – even (as always), random (seeded), aligned or a **spiral** whose gaps step round by **Spiral step** degrees (`regl`, `ress`; 0–90° on the slider, any angle typed) –, the rings turning **together** so the spiral stays a spiral (`resp`), a **rainbow by ring** (red outside to cyan inside, `rerb`), **Time in ring** – "ring k: t s" in amber under the rings, "out in t s" once out, recorded with the clip (`retm`) – and original **ring sounds** – a pentatonic pluck per bounce pitched by the ring, a rising step per ring escaped, a chord at the way out (`resn`). **Find Simulation** gets **First ring at** (the first ring escaped within ±0.5 s of a chosen second) and the preset **Rainbow spiral, 11 rings** is the poster's look. See [Ring Escape](#ring-escape-classic-the-loop-family) |
| **Go-live kit** | Everything between "it works" and "strangers pay" (feature go-live-kit, owner request 8): [`docs/GO-LIVE.md`](docs/GO-LIVE.md), a plain-language launch guide with sixteen sections – costs, hosting and a custom domain, the four accounts (Cloudflare, Stripe, PayPal, NOWPayments with your own payout wallets), test first then live, payouts, licence keys, GitHub variables, legal pages, taxes, a launch-day and a monthly checklist, the fee table and what to do when something breaks; **`npm run go-live`**, a wizard that asks for every key once (hidden, checked by its shape and mode), finds or creates the KV storage, deploys the Worker, makes the licence key pair, finds or creates the Stripe prices and PayPal plans (never twice), stores every secret with `wrangler secret put` on standard input only and sets the GitHub variables and secrets (`--dry-run`, `--skip`, `--mode`); **`npm run go-live:check`**, 17 PASS/FAIL checks of the Worker (`/health`, mode, providers, webhooks, CORS, `/config`, storage), the site (online, not in test mode, the same public key as the Worker, pointing at it, the legal sections), Stripe/PayPal/NOWPayments (prices, plans, webhook endpoints, the customer portal) and GitHub. The Worker gains `GET /health`, live/test mode safety (mixed keys switch a provider off: `503 provider_misconfigured`), refunds, disputes and reversals that end a subscription's access, per-connection rate limits (`429 rate_limited`), several site origins and one price file, [`billing/billing-config.json`](billing/billing-config.json); the terms gain Delivery, Cancellation and refunds and Who you are buying from (`SUPPORT_EMAIL`, `LEGAL_NAME`, `LEGAL_ADDRESS` repository variables); `deploy.yml` refuses a half-configured or test-key production deploy (`node scripts/go-live-check.mjs --deploy-guard`) |
| **Legal copy (legal-copy)** | The legal, privacy, claims and download-page copy says only what the code does (`tests/legalCopy.test.ts` pins it). The privacy policy (`privacy/page.tsx`) opens with a *Who is responsible* block (the operator's name, address and support address from `src/lib/operator.ts`, else the feedback form), states that the site sets no cookies, names Cloudflare Web Analytics (the beacon Cloudflare injects at the edge) and, only when the build has `NEXT_PUBLIC_ANALYTICS_SCRIPT_URL`, the action-counting service by its host, lists who else handles data with their policies, gives the visitor's rights, says the product is advertised on other platforms and loads no ad pixels, and lists what the Windows app sends where. Its section numbers come from one list, and `LEGAL_LAST_UPDATED` moves with any legal copy change. PayPal is named only in the provider-specific strings (its pay button shows only once the backend lists it). The pricing row, lede and terms say *quick share … with the share sheet or a download* instead of publishing, a checkout line under the pay buttons gives the currency, the tax and a Terms link, and the Account page shows *Channel connections are not open yet* when the relay's `/api/health` lists no platform. FAQ q1 takes its prices from the plans, the Disclaimer carries the Fight League fan-made notice, and the download page says what is free, how to check an unsigned installer, and the ffmpeg, Electron, llama.cpp and Llama notices. --- legal-audit --- The terms number their sections from one list (`ORDER` in `terms/page.tsx`) and gain the EU and UK right of withdrawal with a model form, Age, AI features, Reporting content (notice and takedown, a copyright agent line when the build names one), Ending the service and Law and disputes (`NEXT_PUBLIC_LEGAL_COUNTRY`), consumer-safe liability, warranty and change clauses, the relay licence and the YouTube ToS sentence; the privacy policy gains the legal bases, Where your data goes, retention periods, the one-month answer, the ages, an opt-out switch for the action counts (`jbl.stats.off`, `src/components/site/StatsOptOut.tsx`, read by `trackingAllowed()`) and never routes a rights request to a public GitHub issue form; the Connect YouTube control carries the YouTube ToS and Google privacy links; the launch check gains A12 (operator identity) |
| **Analytics events (analytics)** | The conversion events an ad campaign is judged by, counted by an optional cookie-free statistics service (Plausible, Umami or Rybbit): `cta_click`, `sim_start`, `clip_export`, `checkout_start`, `purchase` and ten more, each with a fixed list of short props (never an email, reference, licence, seed or address). Off until `NEXT_PUBLIC_ANALYTICS_SCRIPT_URL` is set, and then still off in the Windows app and for Do Not Track and Global Privacy Control visitors (no request is made). The studio keeps an ad link's `utm_*` and click ids in the address bar. See "Analytics events" (`src/lib/analytics/`, `src/components/site/Analytics.tsx`, `tests/analytics.test.ts`). |
| **Press page, ad kit and launch check (press-ads)** | What a writer and an ad reviewer need, and a test that the ads stay true. A **/press page** in every language (`src/app/[locale]/press/page.tsx`, a server page with no client component, in the sitemap and the footer): a fact sheet read from the code – the number of modes, the Pro prices of the billing config, the Windows app version, the website build label, the operator and the support e-mail (or the feedback page while none is set) –, the description in three lengths with their word counts measured from the text, logo files that already ship with the site, the brand colours, rules for using the name, a note that Fight League names and footage are not part of the kit, and a link to the changelog. **[docs/ADS.md](docs/ADS.md)**, the ad kit for this product: the one promise, the banned claims, audiences, destinations and the UTM naming, the copy of Google, Meta, TikTok, X, Reddit and YouTube in English, Polish and Spanish within each field's limit (`docs/ads/copy.json`), creative specs and safe zones, the viral bot commands for the clips that sell best, the measurement plan without pixels, KPIs, a test plan, and the owner's steps. **`npm run launch:check`** reads the live site from outside (build identity, every page in every language, social cards, claims against the billing and relay health, the downloads, redirects, headers, and with `--browser` that campaign names survive) and prints PASS, WARN or FAIL. See "Press page, ad kit and launch check" below |
| **Auto-release (desktop-autorelease)** | One push updates everything: the website deploys from the product branch only (`CUSTOM_DOMAIN` switches it to a domain root; optional Cloudflare Pages), the billing Worker deploys on changes to `billing/` with a health check, and a version bump in `desktop/package.json` (`npm run release:desktop -- 1.0.4`) builds, smoke-runs and publishes the Windows release that electron-updater serves; the app checks every 4 hours, shows a Restart / Later banner and a notification, never interrupts a render. [docs/RELEASING.md](docs/RELEASING.md) |
| **Wave Tank** | --- wave-tank --- A rhythm-family mode of the loop family (`waveTank`) after the loop-clip account's "the double slit, in water 🔊" reel: a ripple tank in perspective, its water drawn as 40 pale ridge lines back to front with the hidden lines removed (Joy Division style). A golden wave-maker along the back sends straight crests at a slate barrier with **1, 2, then 7 gaps** (the sections; the barrier swaps on the exact frame and the old pattern travels on and leaves): one gap fans out, two cross into stripes with still nodal lines, seven leave as a grating's tall near-field spikes and a few sharp beams. The water is solved, not faked: the wave equation on a 192 × 256 grid (leapfrog sub-steps under a Courant number of 0.5, an absorbing sponge on the sides and the front) or a **Huygens sum** of wavelets from every gap; no randomness at all. Eleven brass bells along the front glow amber as high as the water under them, and a cursor sweeps them left-right-left at 8 strikes a second, each strike (original synthesis through `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`) as loud as (|u| / max)^1.5 – the beams ring, the nodal lines stay silent – over a root bass that crossfades at every section. The loop: 30 s of whole frames and whole wave cycles, the 1-gap section wrapping across the loop point so the clip closes on its first frame (**Export whole loops**). Five presets (Double slit, Single-slit width sweep, Grating 15, Two sources, Talbot carpet), the loop HUD ("the double slit, in water" and the amber "2 gaps"), every setting a `wt…` URL key. See [Wave Tank](#wave-tank-ripple-tank). |
| **Brick Smash** | An escape-family mode of the loop family (`brickRebuild`, feature brick-rebuild) after the loop-clip account's Breakout picture smash ("we smashed our own logo"), with its gold-capsule ("from 3 balls to 280") and kaleidoscope ("the other fourteen are reflections") variants: a box whose top 78 % is a 30 × 30 picture of one-hit bricks – the built-in emblem (an original navy field, teal ring and amber square), **your own uploaded logo** (cover-fitted, quantised to ≤ 8 colours by seeded k-means; it stays in the page – no link, preset or project file carries it) or typed text in a pixel font – smashed by hollow balls that sweep through the grid (no tunnelling at any speed or size) and add a ball every 4th break; at 0 bricks gravity drops them, the bricks fly back colour layer by colour layer while the counter climbs back, and the start balls reappear on the seam, so Export whole loops records a clip that repeats forever. Pentatonic plucks by row, a groove that drops on a bar line, a wash and a ding over the rebuild; Find Simulation searches a smash cleared by a time, a last brick on the downbeat or a final ball count. See [Brick Smash](#brick-smash). |
| **Drawing Machines** | A rhythm-family mode of the loop family (`drawingMachine`, feature drawing-machine) after the loop-clip account's "three pendulums, one pen" harmonograph and "earth and venus draw this every 8 years" orbit flower, both analytic in the simulation clock and both looping on frame 0: the **harmonograph**'s three damped pendulums (left-right, up-down and rotary; 1.5 Hz, the second 2 % faster so the figure keeps twisting, friction τ = 18 s shrinking it toward the middle) drive one pen that inks a typed-array trace on cream paper between two metal rollers for 22.5 s, then the paper scrolls a fresh sheet in (1.5 s); the **orbits** draw a line between two planets every 8 days (Venus and Earth at exactly 13 : 8) for 8 years at 1.8 s a year, mark the five conjunctions 144° apart and join them into a pentagram, hold, and rewind – the year counter running back to 0.0 and the lines un-drawing newest first. Six presets (Three pendulums, one pen; Lissajous 3:2; Decay rose; Earth and Venus; Earth and Mars at their real periods; Jupiter and Saturn at 5 : 2), every number an uncapped field (`dm…` link keys), original synthesized sound on the loop foundation (a drone that breathes with the pendulums' energy, the pen's scratch, the paper's ratchet; the groove bed, a rising pluck at every conjunction, a wash on the rewind), the loop HUD ("three pendulums, one pen" in cream serif, or "earth and venus draw a flower" and the amber "year 8.0") and Export whole loops. See [Drawing Machines](#drawing-machines). |
| **Perfect Fit** | --- perfect-fit --- A rhythm-family mode of the loop family (`perfectFit`) after the loop-clip account's packing reels ("same 12 pieces, three perfect rectangles", "70 pieces, not one out of place"): pieces dropped straight down into a container that a solver guarantees ends exactly full, with an audible progress ladder. **Pentominoes** – the 12 pieces lift out of a tray one by one, glide above their columns already in their final orientation and fall straight into a 10 × 6 box (a small squash on landing), then the box flashes, its 60 unit squares burst out and fade and the same pieces fill 12 × 5, then 15 × 4 (`pfBx`, any W × H list; 0.55 s a piece, 24.2 s a loop); under the box "6 × 10" and "one of 2,339 ways" (1,010 and 368 for the others). The tiling is an exact cover (Algorithm X with dancing links, a seeded column order) whose drop order lets every piece fall vertically: a tiling with no such order is rejected, so 3 × 20 – two tilings, neither droppable – shows "can't be built by dropping". **Tetris** – 70 tetrominoes fall at a constant speed into a 10 × 16 well in their final column and rotation: four four-line perfect clears (a vertical I last, under a gold "four lines" banner, each section faster) and a held twelve-line finale that flashes cream-white (`pfSc` 4,4,4,4,12, `pfW` × `pfH`, `pfHd`), "N lines cleared" under the well, an empty well at the 18 s loop point. Every piece plays the next degree up the pentatonic (the chip voice in Tetris) over a soft landing thump, a rectangle the completion chord and a crack, a clear the chord and a 0.3 s hard silence – original synthesis through the loop sounds. Seconds per piece, Fall feel, Land on the beat (every landing on the Sound section's tempo), Solution seed (`pfS`, `pfF`, `pfB`, `pfSd`), four presets, the loop HUD ("all 12 pentominoes", "every piece fits"), the loop contract and Find Simulation's **Most pieces on the floor** and **Every landing on the beat**. See [Perfect Fit](#perfect-fit). |
| **String Battle: alive while a string holds** | The String Battle's new default life rule and its final duel (feature string-battle-alive): a ball stays in the fight while one of its threads is attached and shatters only when its last one goes (`sblf`, the old four lives one click away as **Lives**), and the moment two balls are left they speed up to 2× over 1.5 s into a staged **Final Duel** (`sbdl`): speed surges while nobody cuts, homing at the bounces, a FINAL DUEL banner, comet trails, slow-motion cuts with a camera punch and a ring flash, and an original tension soundtrack (riser, heartbeat, drone, swells) that sounds the same live, in the fast export, the batch and the bot. Panel: the String Battle block's **Life Rule** and **Final Duel**; en / pl / es. See "String Battle (oddplayground)" below |

## Design

The site is built as **the stage and the desk**: the simulation is the stage – the one bright, moving thing on the
screen – and everything else is a quiet desk around it. Near-black surfaces, hairlines instead of boxes, one accent,
no gradients or glows in the chrome, and emoji only where they are content (ball faces, the emoji picker, team emoji,
caption text, the particle previews).

**Tokens** (`src/app/globals.css`, `@theme`): `bg` and three surface steps (`surface-1/2/3`), two hairlines (`line`,
`line-strong`), three inks (`ink`, `ink-2`, `ink-3`), the accent (`accent`, `accent-strong`, `accent-dim`, `accent-ink`
for text on it) and three states (`ok`, `warn`, `danger`). Radii are 6 px for controls, 10 px for panels and 16 px for
the stage frame only; elevation comes from the surface steps plus one soft shadow for dialogs and sheets. Components use
these names (`bg-surface-2`, `text-ink-3`, `border-line`) – never the stock palette or hex values.

**Type roles** (`src/app/fonts.ts`, `next/font/local`, self-hosted from `src/fonts/` with their OFL licences, each in a
latin and a latin-ext subset so Polish and Spanish render in the same faces): **Space Grotesk** for display headings,
**Hanken Grotesk** for the interface, **JetBrains Mono** for readouts – the stage strip, slider values, number fields,
keyboard hints and the uppercase `eyebrow` labels. The `num` utility gives tabular, slashed-zero figures.

**Layout zones and breakpoints**

| Width | Studio | Site |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ≥ 1536 px | rail with icons and labels (240 px) · stage · panel (380 px) | 1200 px container, reading column 680 px with a sticky table of contents on the legal pages |
| 1280–1535 px | icon rail (64 px, labels as tooltips) · stage · panel | as above |
| 768–1279 px | stage, then the rail as a sticky tab strip, then the panel | the reading column alone |
| < 768 px | stage with the transport bar pinned to the bottom; the tab strip is the peek of a bottom sheet that opens to 70 % of the height (a press on the open tab, or on the dimmed stage above it, puts it away and keeps its blocks) | a menu sheet from the header |

The canvas frame is sized from the viewport only (the stage column's width and the screen height), so it changes size
exactly when the window does – the moment the canvas re-measures itself. The world keeps its shapes (square below
640 px, 16:9 above); the export format and its ratio (9:16 by default) are shown in the stage strip.

**Components** (`src/components/ui/`): `Button` / `buttonClass` (primary, secondary, ghost, danger), `IconButton`,
`Chip`, `Segmented`, `Field` with `inputClass` / `selectClass` / `textareaClass`, `Switch`, `Panel`, `Tooltip`, `Dialog`
and `Sheet` (native `<dialog>`: focus stays inside, Esc and the scrim close, the focus returns to the opener), `TabStrip`
(the rail: toggle buttons with roving focus), `Kbd` and the icon set (`icons.tsx`, 20 px grid, 1.5 px strokes). The
studio's own pieces are in `src/components/simulator/studio/` (stage strip, mode picker, command palette); the reading
pages use `src/components/site/ProseLayout.tsx`.

**Motion and access**: 150–200 ms ease-out for panels, sheets and dialogs, nothing bouncy, and none of it with
`prefers-reduced-motion` (the landing preview then shows a still picture). Every interactive element gets the accent
focus ring; hit targets are 32 px at least and grow to 44 px on coarse pointers (the kit's buttons, fields and segments
carry the sizes, and `globals.css` gives every button of the panel the floor – the choice rows of the feature sections
included); the icon rail's labels are drawn in a fixed layer beside the icon (the rail's scroll box would clip them) and
every rail item has its label as a title; the rail and the panel keep the names, `aria-pressed` / `aria-expanded`
semantics and test ids of the old panel headers, and the panel's switches are `role="switch"` named by their labels.
A scroll to the studio (a mode picked, Play today's seed, a bot clip opened) stops below the sticky header
(`scroll-mt-14` on `main#simulator`).

**Adding to it**: a new control group goes into the `sections` list of `Controls.tsx` with an icon from `icons.tsx` – it
appears on the rail, in the tab strip, in the command palette and in the search; a new reading page wraps its text in
`ProseLayout` (an eyebrow, the title, an optional lede and table of contents).

**The command palette** offers what the panel shows in the current mode (`paletteKeyGroups()` in `Controls.tsx`,
`src/components/simulator/panelKeys.ts`): the mode's own block under the Mode group (`MODE_BLOCK_KEYS` – a new mode's
block registers its search keys there), the other groups' controls without the other modes' blocks and without what the
mode or a setting hides (`sectionKeyShown()`: Wall Count outside the ring modes, the arena editor with one arena, a
caption's form without captions …), one entry per label in a group. Choosing a control opens its group and focuses it;
one that its group does not show right now (an advanced option, the collapsed Project file block) is shown through the
search box at once, and a press on the rail always wins over a jump still under way.

## Getting started

```bash
npm install
npm run dev        # http://localhost:3000 (plus the base path, if set) → redirects to your browser's language, /en by default
npm run build      # static export into ./out (+ 404.html and .nojekyll for GitHub Pages)
npm start          # serves ./out the way GitHub Pages does (base path, 404 page, trailing slashes)
```

Copy `.env.example` to `.env.local` to configure the build. Everything is optional for local development:

- `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH` – sub-folder the site is served from (`/Balls` on `https://user.github.io/Balls`, empty for a root deployment).
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` – public URL used for canonical links, hreflang, sitemap and Open Graph, including the base path. Defaults to `http://localhost:3000` plus the base path.
- Feedback channel, first one set wins: `NEXT_PUBLIC_FEEDBACK_ENDPOINT` (an endpoint that accepts a cross-origin JSON POST, e.g. Formspree, Basin or your own worker with CORS enabled), `NEXT_PUBLIC_FEEDBACK_EMAIL` (opens the visitor's email app) or `NEXT_PUBLIC_GITHUB_REPO` (opens a prefilled GitHub issue; needs a public repository with Issues on, and visitors need a GitHub account).
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_ANALYTICS_SCRIPT_URL` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_ANALYTICS_SITE_ID` – --- analytics --- load a privacy-friendly analytics script (Plausible, Umami, Rybbit…) and count a few actions (see "Analytics events"). The address must be an absolute http(s) URL; the script is never loaded in the Windows app or for a browser that sends Do Not Track or Global Privacy Control, and without the address nothing is requested.
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_YOUTUBE_CLIENT_ID` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_PUBLISH_RELAY_URL` – --- social-publish --- the Publish block's Google OAuth client ID for direct YouTube uploads and the relay URL it suggests (both public; see "Publish").
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_API` – --- paywall-gate --- the licence public key (base64url SPKI; without it the site runs in test mode) and the billing Worker's origin (without it the pay buttons say payments are not configured); both public, see "Pricing and licences".
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPPORT_EMAIL` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_LEGAL_NAME` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_LEGAL_ADDRESS` – --- go-live-kit --- the operator's public details: the terms name who runs the site, and the terms, the privacy policy and the pricing page show the support address (also the feedback form's fallback channel). On GitHub Pages the repository variables `SUPPORT_EMAIL`, `LEGAL_NAME`, `LEGAL_ADDRESS`; see [docs/GO-LIVE.md](docs/GO-LIVE.md).
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_LEGAL_VAT_ID` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_LEGAL_REGISTRATION` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_LEGAL_COUNTRY` – --- legal-audit --- the VAT number ("VAT number: …" on the terms, the privacy policy and the footer imprint), the register entry ("Registered as …") and the operator's country as visitors should read it (the governing law in the terms' "Law and disputes" section). Repository variables `LEGAL_VAT_ID`, `LEGAL_REGISTRATION`, `LEGAL_COUNTRY`; on the VPS `JBL_LEGAL_VAT_ID`, `JBL_LEGAL_REGISTRATION`, `JBL_LEGAL_COUNTRY`. `npm run launch:check` (A12) fails a live site whose English legal pages show no operator name and postal address.

`next build` reads `.env.local` (and `.env.production*`, `.env`); `npm start`, `npm run smoke` and `npm run previews` read them with
Next's own loader (`@next/env`, in `scripts/dotenv.mjs`), so they see the same `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH` and a base-path build previews correctly at `http://localhost:3000/<base path>/`. You can also pass it explicitly:
`npm start -- --base /Balls` and `BASE_URL=http://localhost:3000/Balls npm run smoke`.

Other scripts:

```bash
npm run typecheck                          # tsc --noEmit
npm run lint                               # eslint
npm test                                   # vitest unit tests (engine, physics extras, ball interactions, obstacles, Ball Drop, Bouncing Shapes, MIDI parser, settings, hit samples, song slicer, scales, instruments, music bed, beat detection, beat clock, Picture Paint)
npm run test:ci                            # the same tests the way CI runs them: scripts/test-ci.mjs does not fail a run in which every test passed and the only error is vitest's own "Timeout calling onTaskUpdate" (a busy machine)
npm run smoke                              # headless-browser end-to-end checks; build and `npm start` first (see scripts/smoke-test.mjs)
npm run smoke -- --shard 2/4               # --- smoke-sharding --- one of four balanced parts (SMOKE_SHARD=2/4 too); --list prints the split
SMOKE_ONLY=fight-league npm run smoke      # --- smoke-sharding --- single blocks by title (comma-separated, * for any characters)
npm run previews                           # regenerate public/modes/*.webp from the real simulator (build and `npm start` first); MODES=drop npm run previews renders one mode
GALLERY=1 npm run previews                 # --- daily-gallery --- only the gallery cards (public/gallery/*.webp); GALLERY=neon-escape renders one
MODES=drop SWEEP=2:14:1 npm run previews   # --- mode-thumbnails --- contact sheets for picking a hero moment (SEEDS, EXTRA, CAMERA, RAW=1, OUT_DIR)
npm run bot -- --count 3 --platform reels   # --- viral-bot --- plan, render and caption today's clips (build and `npm start` first; --dry-run needs neither)
python3 scripts/generate-midi.py           # regenerate the built-in melodies in public/notes
python3 scripts/generate-sounds.py         # regenerate the wall-break and hit sound effects
node scripts/generate-icons.mjs            # --- pwa --- rasterise public/icon.svg into the app icons in public/icons, and (--- seo ---) icon-48.png and public/favicon.ico (Playwright's Chromium)
node scripts/generate-og.mjs               # regenerate public/og.png, the default social preview image: the pages without a card of their own and a missing card show it (build and `npm start` first)
npm run og:cards                           # --- seo --- redraw the 27 social cards, public/og/<locale>/<page>.png, from the Seo.card messages (Playwright's Chromium, no server); --locale pl --page home draws one
node relay/server.mjs                      # --- social-publish --- the self-hosted publish relay for TikTok, Instagram and YouTube (relay/README.md)
node scripts/billing-keygen.mjs            # --- paywall-gate --- a production licence key pair: the Worker's secret, then the site's public key
node scripts/lib/test-license.mjs          # --- paywall-gate --- prints a Pro licence signed with the committed TEST key (test-mode builds)
node scripts/owner-license.mjs --email you@example.com --key private.json  # --- paywall-gate --- the owner's Pro licence, signed locally with the production key (live site)
npm run go-live                            # --- go-live-kit --- the launch wizard: pastes the payment keys into the Worker and the public settings into GitHub (docs/GO-LIVE.md)
npm run go-live:check                      # --- go-live-kit --- proves the site, the billing Worker, the providers and GitHub agree (PASS/FAIL per check)
```

## Deploying (Cloudflare, jumpingballslive.com)

GitHub only keeps the code and runs the builds, so the repository can be private. The live site at
**https://jumpingballslive.com** (and `www`) is the static export served as the static assets of the `jumpingballslive`
Cloudflare Worker (`wrangler.toml` at the repository root attaches both addresses to it). The repository ships
`.github/workflows/deploy.yml`:

1. **Add two repository secrets once** (*Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions → Secrets*): `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` (the
   token the billing Worker's workflow uses: *Edit Cloudflare Workers*) and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`. Until they exist the
   *deploy* job stops with a message that names the missing one.
2. **Push to the product branch** (`claude/optimistic-johnson-46x3qg`, or `main` once renamed; both are listed in
   `deploy.yml`). Every push to any branch is linted and type-checked (a private repository has 2,000 free Actions minutes a
   month, so nothing more runs on other branches); pushes to the product branch also run the unit tests (`npm run test:ci`),
   build the site for the root of the domain (no base path) and run the browser smoke test (four shards), and are published
   only once it passes. A manual run from another branch checks only. (--- desktop-autorelease ---)
3. The last step asks `https://jumpingballslive.com/build.txt` (a file the build stamps with the commit) until it names the
   pushed commit, and fails if it does not: a deploy that did not reach the domain is red, not silent. `/version.json` (written by
   `scripts/postexport.mjs`, see [Build identity](#build-identity)) says more about what is live: the release version, the full commit,
   its date and the build id (`curl -s https://jumpingballslive.com/version.json`).

By hand: `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL=https://jumpingballslive.com NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH= npm run build`, then
`npx --prefix billing wrangler deploy` (Wrangler comes with `cd billing && npm ci`).

**Feedback form**: on a public repository with Issues enabled the deployed form opens a prefilled GitHub issue
(visitors need a GitHub account). On a private repository, or to use another channel, set a repository
*Variable* (Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions → Variables): `FEEDBACK_ENDPOINT` (a form endpoint that
accepts a cross-origin JSON POST, e.g. Formspree), `FEEDBACK_EMAIL` or `SUPPORT_EMAIL`. Until one of these applies the form
tells visitors that no channel is configured. `ANALYTICS_SCRIPT_URL` and `ANALYTICS_SITE_ID` variables are passed
to the build the same way.

**Another domain**: the repository variable `CUSTOM_DOMAIN` (the bare domain, e.g. `example.com`; empty: `jumpingballslive.com`)
makes the next run build for that domain (`.github/actions/site-url` resolves the address for both workflows), the Windows
app's share links follow, and `billing-deploy.yml` checks that the Worker's `SITE_ORIGIN` lists it (the website run warns
until it does). `wrangler.toml`'s two `routes` name the domains the Worker answers on.

**The Windows app's downloads and updates** come from the Releases of a second, public GitHub repository that holds the installers and no source code
(`CronusAztec/jumpingballslive-windows`, see *Release workflow* below), so nothing a visitor needs depends on this repository being public.

Shipping an update, step by step, for the owner: [docs/RELEASING.md](docs/RELEASING.md).

**Other static hosts** (Netlify, Cloudflare Pages, GitHub Pages, S3, nginx…): run `npm run build` with `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`
(and `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH` for a sub-folder) set and upload `out/`. Point the host's "not found" page at `404.html`.

The browser smoke test (`scripts/smoke-test.mjs`, ~45 minutes on one runner) gates every deploy: the `smoke` job of
`deploy.yml` – --- smoke-sharding --- a matrix of four shards that run at once, about 12 minutes each (see
[Smoke test shards](#smoke-test-shards)) – builds the site under a base path, serves it with `scripts/serve-static.mjs` and
runs its part of the suite, and the `deploy` job may publish once the `smoke-summary` job has seen every shard pass.
`.github/workflows/smoke.yml` runs the same four shards on pull requests and on demand, with
`SMOKE_STRICT_TIMING=1`. Frame-rate and timing checks retry once; when they still fail while the page itself, with the run
paused, gets below 50 fps – an idle machine gives it 60 – (the machine, not the run, is short of CPU), they are reported as *inconclusive* and listed apart
instead of failing the run – unless `SMOKE_STRICT_TIMING=1`. The suite also fails on any same-origin request that answers 4xx/5xx (a
missing asset, a URL without the base path).

### Smoke test shards

--- smoke-sharding --- The suite (some 750 checks) is a list of over 80 **blocks**, the sections of related checks that share a
page, a recording or a state. Each one is

```js
await smokeBlock("feature-key", async () => {
// --- feature-key --- what the block checks
…its checks, starting with its own page.goto() / browser.newContext()…
});
```

(the body left unindented, so a block's diff stays the lines it changes), and `scripts/smoke/blocks.mjs` decides which blocks a
run runs:

| Command | What runs |
| --- | --- |
| `npm run smoke` (no flag) | every block, in order – the whole suite exactly as before, with the same output (the ✅ / ❌ / ⚠️ lines, the inconclusive list, the final `N/M checks passed` line) |
| `npm run smoke -- --shard 2/4` or `SMOKE_SHARD=2/4 npm run smoke` | shard 2 of 4 (1-based; the flag wins over the variable) |
| `SMOKE_ONLY=fight-league npm run smoke` | the named blocks – comma-separated titles, `*` for any characters (`SMOKE_ONLY='*watermark*'`); with a shard, the named blocks of that shard. A name no block has stops the run |
| `npm run smoke -- --list` | every block with its shard (N from `--shard`, else 4 – the CI matrix) and weight, one line each and nothing else; no browser starts (--- low-findings --- the shards' totals and the balance, max ÷ mean, go to stderr) |
| `npm run smoke -- --write-timing` | the suite (or the shard), then `scripts/smoke-timing.json` rewritten with this run's wall times (blocks it did not run keep theirs) |

- **The split** (`scripts/smoke/shards.mjs`, pure functions, proven by `tests/smokeShards.test.ts`): a block runs in exactly one
  shard, the shards together are the whole suite, and checks are never split from their block. The blocks' weights are their wall
  times in `scripts/smoke-timing.json` (block title → seconds; a block the file does not know weighs the median of the ones it
  does), and the blocks, sorted by weight (heaviest first) and title, each go to the shard with the smallest total so far (the
  lowest index on a tie) – a greedy longest-processing-time split. No list of blocks is kept anywhere: a new block gets a shard as
  soon as it is in the suite, and the split is the same on every machine for a given baseline and N. A shard runs its blocks in
  the suite's order. The test checks for N = 1…8 that the shards are disjoint, complete, order-stable and balanced (the heaviest
  at most 1.6 × the mean with the checked-in baseline), and that the suite has the shape the split relies on.
- **Every run reports** its blocks: `OUT_DIR/smoke-timing.json` (title → seconds of the blocks it ran) and
  `OUT_DIR/smoke-blocks.json` (each block's shard, seconds, checks, failed checks and inconclusive timing checks), rewritten
  after every block, and the 15 slowest blocks printed just before the final count; a shard or a `SMOKE_ONLY` run also says
  which blocks it ran.
- **Blocks are self-contained**: a block starts with its own navigation (or its own browser context) and needs nothing an
  earlier block did. A narrowed run (a shard, `SMOKE_ONLY`) makes sure of it: before every block but its first it closes the
  pages and contexts the previous block left open, empties the site's localStorage and sessionStorage (the test licence comes
  back with the next page), takes back the clipboard permissions, restores the 1400 × 900 viewport and leaves the page on
  `about:blank` – so a block passes or fails the same whichever blocks share its shard. The full suite runs as before, without
  it. The checks after `smoke.endBlocks()` (failed same-origin requests, console and page errors) run in every shard, over that
  shard's pages.
