<!-- /fl-primitives -->

- **Charge** – the classes (`abilityClass()`): an ultimate 13–14 s, an area or freeze ability – freezing every foe, slow time, arena cuts, lightning, a shockwave of 4.5 radii or more, a fire ring of 3.5 or more, a volley of 8 or more, a beam 1.2 radii wide or more – 11–12 s, any other 8–10 s. The meter fills from time – full after the charge time ÷ the cast speed (`meterAfter()`, `chargeSeconds()`) – and **from damage**: 0.6 % per point dealt (at most 6 % a hit) and 0.4 % per point taken (at most 5 %), × the cast speed (`hitCharge()`).
- **Telegraph classes** (`telegraphMs()`, `FL_TELEGRAPH_CLASS_MS`): quick 300 ms (bursts, invulnerability, heals, reflection, giant hits, decoys, summons, transforms, drains), standard 450 ms (beams, volleys, pulls, chokes, disarming, confusion, blink strikes, lightning, traps, walls), area 650 ms (shockwaves, fire rings, arena cuts, freezes, slow time) and ultimate 850 ms (a charge of 13 s or more). Full, the ability's name flashes in its box with a rising swell, a **preview** shows where it will land (every primitive has one), the **aim locks** at the telegraph's start, and then it fires by itself; a range-limited ability waits up to 2.5 s for a foe in range.
- **Counterplay** – a foe inside an area telegraph is nudged out of it (the **evade nudge**; DODGE! when it escapes); hard crowd control – a freeze, a hold, a pin – **interrupts** a telegraph (INTERRUPTED!, the meter back to half, the next telegraph uninterruptible) and leaves its target **immune** to more for 1 s (IMMUNE).
- **Ultimates** – the fight-enders (`ultimate`: The Snap, Serious Punch, Falcon Punch, Raging Demon, Omega Effect…): the ultimate charge and telegraph, READY with a marching-ants border in the box, the super flash at the cast (fl-heavy: white at alpha 0.06 on noir, where the ink veil was invisible, ink at 0.35 elsewhere; a heavy ability's name was slammed into the arena's top through the wind-up, so its flash shows no ribbon, and the flash ends at the caster's first heavy blow after the cast); Serious Punch's and Falcon Punch's giant hits take **half** the foe's maximum HP (`maxHpFrac` 0.5: exactly 50 vs a 100 HP foe at the HP 100 setting, 57.5 vs Hulk's 115, 60 vs Saitama's 120).
- **Heavy damage** (fl-heavy, "Stop The World": the hard-hitting abilities hit hard) – a hard-hitting ability takes a **share of the foe's maximum HP**, in three bands by charge class, and every rising row pays in the same row. A one-blow giant hit uses `maxHpFrac` (`giantDamage()`: max(the plain blow, frac × the foe's maximum HP) – never a flat field and never a multiplier, so the number reads the same at every HP setting and on tanks and the tuner cannot shrink it); shockwaves, lightning, beams and blink strikes keep `damage × the damage stat`, aimed at the band (section 3) and re-aimed when a tune moves the stat (section 15). **The bands** (HP at the 100 HP setting vs a 100 HP row): the **standard class** (charge 8–10 s, the 300 ms telegraph) at most 0.30 of a bar in one blow – 0.25 where a freeze, a launch of 5 or a second effect rides along – or 0.30 over a multi-hit cast: Shoryuken 30 (+ a launch of 3), Electric Wind God Fist 30 (+ a 0.6 s stun), Detroit Smash 30, Karate Chop Action 30, Star Punch 25 (+ a 1 s freeze), Dragon's Rage 25 (+ a launch of 5), Mouse Hole and Night Shroud 25 (a second effect), Shoryureppa 3 × 10, Omnislash 5 × 5.4 (10 → 9 paid in the row: at 5 × 6 Cloud Strife sat on the 75 % edge of the circle gate), Teleport 3 × 7, Spirit Rush 3 × 8.8 (kept under the band's top: at 3 × 10 Ahri wins 82 % in the circle), Chidori 4 × 6, Spinning Slash 5 × 5; the **area class** (charge 11–12 s, the 650 ms telegraph with the evade nudge) at most 25 per foe for a shockwave of 4.5 radii or more (Hulk Smash 25.0, TNT 25.0 behind its 1 s fuse), 20–25 for a radius-4 or spin shockwave (Howitzer Impact 25.0, Spin Attack 20.0, Hand Slap 20.0), lightning – every foe, undodgeable – at most 20 per foe (Zeus' Fury 20.0, Thunderbolt 20.0; Thunder Strike 18.76 untouched), a fused shockwave may keep more (Explode 47 behind 1.5 s), and a beam ticks every 300 ms (`FL_IFRAME_MS`) at most 8 a tick and 35 over a full ray (Kamehameha 5.8 a tick, 29 over its 1.2 s); the **ultimate class** (`ultimate`, charge 13–14 s, the 850 ms telegraph, READY, the super flash) 0.50 in one blow (Falcon Punch, Serious Punch), 40 for a never-miss blink (Raging Demon: one blink, `ignoreIframes`), 25 per foe for lightning (The Snap 25.2), 35 over an ultimate beam (Final Flash 7.0 a tick, 5 ticks over 1.4 s). A **blow** is what one attacker lands on one foe within one 60 Hz step, summed (`FlFighter.blowSum`: a volley's projectile and its burst, a shotgun's pellets, two weapons at once), as EV_HEAVY measures it. Nothing standard class exceeds 30 in one blow (Plasma Blast 16.6 → 2 × 14.94 = 29.9: Toothless' bolt and its blast both land on the foe it hits, 32.4 before), nothing reaches 50 outside the ultimate class, a division holds at most one ability of 40 or more (Shonen anime excepted: Death Ball's orb and its blast both land on the foe it hits, 2 × 20.2 = 40.4, beside Serious Punch), and an ability's HP per cast ÷ its charge seconds stays at or under 4.0 HP/s (Falcon Punch 50/13 = 3.85, Serious Punch 50/14 = 3.57, Raging Demon 40/13 = 3.08, Shoryuken 30/10 = 3.0, Hulk Smash 25/12 = 2.1 per foe, Final Flash 35/13 = 2.7). **The payment order** inside a row: class promotion where the blow is 40 or more (`ultimate: true`, the only way to 13 s – Falcon Punch and Raging Demon), charge +1 within its class (Star Punch, Shoryuken, Shoryureppa, Electric Wind God Fist, Dragon's Rage, Detroit Smash, Teleport 9 → 10; Hulk Smash 11 → 12), the weapon's base damage down (Hulk's and Thanos' fists), then `stats.damage` through the tuner (section 15). A **loaded share punch weighs × 4 in a clash** (section 5), so a jab never parries a Falcon Punch. `flHeavyAbility()` (`fightLeagueRoster.ts`, beside `abilityClass()`) names the rows whose blow reaches the heavy tier – an ultimate, or a giant hit of `maxHpFrac` ≥ `FL_HEAVY_FRAC` 0.25 – whose wind-up is drawn differently (section 7). The unchanged heavy hitters: Electric Fly, Demacian Justice, Explode, Solar Flare, Smoke Bomb, Death Ball (40.4 on its direct target: the orb's hit and its explosion's shockwave, `ignoreIframes`, land on the same foe), Avada Kedavra, Fulminated Mercury, Laser Sweep, the multi-shot homing giants and every standard beam; Thor and Loki are never touched, so the default duel's goldens and `BASELINE.fightLeague` hold without a re-record.

#### 5. Match rules

- **The arena** – a square or a circle drawn at 74 % of the square the recorder exports (`FL_ARENA_FRAC` under the names band of `FL_ARENA_TOP` 0.10; the physics field keeps the first release's 70 % and is drawn scaled, so the replays hold – `buildFightField()`); the fighters are engine balls (6.5 % of the arena side × their size) cruising at 0.7 arena sides a second × their speed, bouncing off the walls – a circle's rebound turned a seeded 5–12° – and off each other; the director turns a wall rebound a little toward the nearest foe, fully after 3 s without a hit; no gravity by default.
- **Launch at the foe** – a VS card holds everyone still for 1.5 s (`FL_INTRO_MS`), the fighters spawn half way to the walls, then launch at the nearest foe (± a seeded 20°).
- **Seek sweeps, lunges and intent steering** – a sword sweeps 120° at a foe in reach on its cadence (the arc follows the foe; also on every bounce and clash of bodies); a melee fighter whose weapon is ready lunges at a foe within its reach + 3 radii; in flight the **intent steering** turns the velocity – never the speed – toward the gap its first weapon wants (`intentBand()`): **Seek Turn** (`FL_SEEK_TURN` 1.1 rad/s, URL `flSk`; **0 = the bounce look** of the first release).
- **Per-source invulnerability windows** – one touch is one hit, and each source (an attacker's weapon, or its abilities, summons and contact together) has its own window on the foe (`flSourceWindowMs()`: light kinds by their cadence, 120–300 ms; heavy kinds and abilities 300 ms, `FL_IFRAME_MS`; the pellets of one volley land together), so two weapons never block each other; a shot refused by a window **grazes**.
- **Clash and cadence jitter** – melee blows traded in one sub-step resolve fairly (`resolveMelee()`): the one 1.25× stronger (`FL_CLASH_RATIO`) lands at half, else a **CLASH!** (no damage, both knocked apart), and a pair's repeat clash within 2 s goes to a seeded coin (fl-heavy: a loaded share punch – a `maxHpFrac` giant hit with `giantLeft` – weighs × 4 in `impactOf()`, so a jab never parries a Falcon Punch); a seeded **cadence jitter** at the spawn keeps two fighters – a mirror match above all – out of step.
- **Step-parity order** – the fighters act in an order that flips every step; damage taken within one 60 Hz step is applied together and the KOs are decided at its end (a fighter whose last HP went strikes no more that step), so no fighter wins by its slot.
- **Guard** – a shield facing a hit (40° either side) blocks it and spends its guard (section 3).
- **Meter from damage** (section 4) and **knockback by weight** (× size^−1.5 between 0.55 and 1.6, `knockbackWeight()`), fading in about 0.3 s.
- **The verdict** – the last fighter (a team's last) with HP wins; sides that go down in the same step – or to a projectile landing within 250 ms of the last KO (`FL_KO_GRACE_MS`) – make a **double KO**; at the **time cap** (`flT`, 90 s counted from FIGHT!, 0 = none) **sudden death** (`flSD`, on by default) shrinks the arena to 60 % of its side over 8 s for at most 10 s – a KO still ends it – and then the side with the larger **HP fraction** wins (Σ HP ÷ Σ maximum HP, a KO counting 0), sides within 0.5 % drawing (`capVerdict()`).
- **The finish freeze** – at the verdict every fighter stops where it stands (then the KO finale plays in simulation time, section 7).
- **The heavy hits' events** (fl-heavy; visual state only) – `hit()` pushes **EV_HEAVY** (kind 24) after a landed blow of `FL_HEAVY_FRAC` 0.25 of the target's maximum HP or more – at most once per target every `FL_IMPACT_GAP_MS` 400 ms – and after every lethal blow: `aux` 0 heavy, 1 critical (`FL_CRIT_FRAC` 0.40), 2 lethal; (x2, y2) the hit's unit direction, `value` the damage dealt (post-stat, post-giant, before the HP clamp), `color` the attacker's accent, `src` the hit's source. `stepLead()` pushes **EV_TURN** (kind 25) once per 60 Hz step when the lead changes sides past the hysteresis to the team of the step's largest hit – one of `FL_TURN_FRAC` 0.15 of its target's maximum HP or more, at most once per attacker every `FL_TURN_GAP_MS` 2.5 s – with the lead swing as `value`, the target's position as (x2, y2) and `aux` 1 for a clutch comeback (the attacker under a quarter of its own HP); the first lead of a match is no change. EV_KO now carries the finishing blow's amount (`value`) and the killer's source (`src`), and EV_DAMAGE's `x2` is 0 for a `maxHpFrac` giant (no "×N" beside a share-based number). The new fighter fields (`heavyMs`, `lastHitAmount`, `turnMs`) and the view's scratch (`turnSlot`, `turnAmount`, `turnTarget`) are read by no rule, clock or random draw, so the fingerprints (positions, velocities, HP, hits, casts, meters, counters) and the Thor vs Loki goldens hold; the 8-bracket, boss rush and 3v3 goldens were re-recorded because they draw retuned rows.
- **Determinism** – every random number comes from `ctx.random()` in the order the mode's header documents: at the init the entrants (one draw per random slot or token in slot order; a bracket's and a boss rush's drawn entrants, then a random seeding's shuffle), then per match the spawns – each fighter's spawn jitter (x, y), its launch heading, its cadence jitter and its meter's head start, fighter by fighter in slot order –, then every step the casts in the processing order (an arena cut one draw, a decoy or a summon two, a blink strike's side one), per sub-step a circle rebound's turn, the weapons' spread (one draw per projectile, a burst's later rounds included) and a repeated clash's coin. The interludes, the sound cues, the story and the renderer draw nothing. A seed replays **exactly at 30, 60 and 144 fps** (golden fingerprints of a 1v1, a Bo3, an 8-bracket, a boss rush and a 3v3).

#### 6. Match types and shows

- **One match** (`flM`): **1v1** (the default, Thor vs Loki), **2v2** (A + B against C + D, every ball ringed in its team's colour – the Teams roster's when one is set – with no friendly fire), **3v3** (A + B + C against D + E + F at 0.85× the size from two columns, team rings solid and dashed, compact boxes – two rows of three, a team a row – and name rows) and **free-for-alls of 3 and 4**. Six slots (`FL_MAX_SLOTS`, A–F: `fl1`–`fl6`, with a handicap each). A "random" slot is drawn by the seed, from the first chosen fighter's division while Random Stays In Division is on; **random tokens** keep a slot in a division (`random:pokemon`) or a conference (`random:anime`); a random slot next to Gerald draws from every other division, and a fully random match draws a division first (all equally likely), then a fighter in it.
- **Best-of-N** (`flBo`, on 1v1, 2v2 and 3v3 and a bracket's final): the first to ⌈n/2⌉ wins (an even n rounds up), a draw or a double KO counts for nobody and adds a game, after n + 2 games the higher summed HP share takes it; GAME n, SERIES POINT, the gold decider and "[name] wins the series 2–1!".
- **Brackets** (`flM=bracket`) – the entrants (`flE`: ids and tokens – `thor`, `random`, `random:<division|conference>`, `division:<id>`, `conference:<id>` – comma separated) fill up to `flBr` (8 by default; a run builds at most `FL_ENTRANT_CEILING` 256, `MEMORY_CEILINGS.flBracketSize`): the named slots A–F first (the **pinned** entrants – the finder's and the rig's sides), then the list, then draws from the list's last random pool, by default `random:<division of slot A>`; a division that runs out fills from its own conference (the **conference pools**), never beyond. Seeding (`flSd`): by rating (the tuner's strengths), random or as listed, placed the standard way (1v8, 4v5, 3v6, 2v7), the **byes** of a size that is not a power of two going to the top seeds. Every bout is capped at min(`flT`, 45 s) and then sudden death. **Expected clip lengths** (the panel's estimate): an 8-bracket about 2:30, a 16 about 5:30.
- **Boss rush** (`flM=boss`) – slot A is the boss (Gerald when slot A is random; the Boss button puts Gerald there; our own vector crown): HP × `flBoss` (2.4), 1.5× the size, 1.25× the damage; the challengers come one at a time from `flE` (by default `random:<the boss's division>` – Gerald, alone in Wildcard, takes on Marvel) × `flBw` (6); the boss keeps its HP between waves and heals `flBh` (0.15) of its maximum in each interlude; at 50 % **PHASE 2** (speed ×1.15, cast speed ×1.2); THE BOSS FALLS – [challenger] at its wave, or BOSS SURVIVES n/n. At the defaults Gerald clears 9 of 20 Marvel rushes (a test holds it in 30–70 %).
- **One deterministic run per show** – the mode owns `view.show` (single / series / bracket / boss, the match in play and its phase – intro, fight, finale, card, done –, wins, rematches, entrants, rounds, the boss's HP, the wave, the champion). A match's verdict sets `view.matchOver`; `view.finished` comes only at the show's end. Between two matches an **interlude**: 800 ms of finale, then a card of `flCard` seconds (2.5) – the bracket card, the series score or the boss's queue – and `startMatch()` clears the arena and spawns again (the sides swap every match; `view.matchSerial` grows). A draw or a double KO inside a show is a **REMATCH!** (the pairing again, up to 2 times), then more damage dealt over the pairing's matches, the HP share, the better seed.
- **How the finder, the recorder and the rig see a show** – the recorder and the fast export record the whole show (the page holds its end like a match's); the finder follows a show 90 s a match (`flShowHorizonMs()`) and stops a seed whose chosen side is out of the show (a knocked-out entrant, a fallen boss – not a lost game); the rig follows the pinned entrants (section 11); `RunSummary.fl` carries the show's deciding match, its bouts and its champion (`championEntrant`).

#### 7. The clip

- **The VS card** (1.5 s): panels sliding in – a duel's in its fighters' colours, a team's in its team colours with each member's chip in its own colours, a free-for-all's chips each in the corner its fighter starts in –, VS slamming in at 0.3 s, **3-2-1** at 0.45 / 0.8 / 1.15 s, **FIGHT!** at 1.5 s, the win chance under the names (section 8).
- **During the fight**: **first blood** (once a match), the **lead bar** under the names band, the HP bars with a ghost of the HP just lost, the timer pill (red and popping in the last 5 s), name tags under the balls with three fighters or more (`flTg`), the ability boxes (a mini portrait, a role glyph, the ability's name, a glowing meter, READY, the stats – one row of boxes for two and three fighters, two rows of slim boxes for four and six: a 2v2's teams in columns, a 3v3's in rows, a free-for-all of 4 in its corners, each name running the box's width with the stats stacked at its right edge), side **callouts** (BLOCK, DODGE!, IMMUNE, INTERRUPTED!, CLASH!, CRITICAL!, COMEBACK!, the ability's name at its cast – on noir at 0.6 unit in the fighter's name colour, fl-heavy, since 0.36 unit in a dark raw accent was unreadable on black) and **combos** (3+ hits in a row: "N HITS"), damage numbers merged per attacker within 150 ms (`flDn`), hit flashes, squash and stretch, impact frames (`flIf`), knockback ribbons, wall-slam dust, the mode's own screen shake (`flSh`, 0.6; off while the camera's Screen Shake is on, × 0.3 under reduced motion) and telegraph previews. **Banners one at a time** by priority (first blood, KO, FINAL KO, TIME!, sudden death, the verdicts, the shows' GAME n / SERIES POINT / ROUND OF 16 / QUARTER-FINAL / SEMI-FINAL / FINAL / CHAMPION / WAVE n/m / PHASE 2).
- **The KO finale** (`flSm`, on by default; `FL_FINALE_MS`) – after a KO verdict (not at the time cap, not in a double KO) 1.2 s in **simulation time**: slow motion 1 → 0.25 in 80 ms, held to 0.8 s and back by 1.2 s, the KO'd ball flying on as a ghost, a 1 → 1.12 push-in, 12 speed lines and a spotlight; the survivors drift at ≤ 0.3× their cruise. The final KO's big **KO!** banner sits in the arena's half away from the KO point (at 0.68 of the arena's height when the KO was in its upper half, else 0.32: fl-heavy), clear of the lethal blow's huge number and the survivor the push-in centres on.
- **The winner card** (0.9 s into the finale, held until 3 s after its end – 3.3 s on screen; `FL_WIN_HOLD_SEC` 3 s after TIME! or without the finale): "[name] wins!" with at most two tags (PERFECT! > UPSET! > COMEBACK! > CLUTCH!, "[ability] finish!") and HP left · hits · time, **crossfading toward the VS split in its last 0.4 s so the clip loops**. A found fight records its length + 1.2 s + 3 s.
- **Portrait plates** (`flPp`, on): in a 9:16 export's bars only (never over the square or the watermark) – the names in their colours with the division above, WHO WINS? below during the fight, "[name] WINS" after.
- **The stage** (`flSt`): **noir** by default (feature fl-noir, after the "Painting vs Mace" reel family: a pitch-black page and arena, the arena a thin white square outline and nothing else on the field; the arena drawn as a 0.6 S square lower in the frame (`FL_NOIR_ARENA_TOP` 0.205, `FL_NOIR_ARENA_FRAC` 0.6: a render-only scale and shift of the display mapping in `applyWorld()` – the physics field keeps its size and place, so every replay and fingerprint holds); above it the fighters' weapons as big icons (0.12 S tall) side by side – our own held-weapon sprites – and under them the matchup title (0.06 S letters over at most 0.78 S, clear of the timer at the right) "THOR   VS   LOKI" in a chunky pixel face of our own, each name in its fighter's colour, VS white, the timer small at the right; the fighters plain solid discs with the HP number big and white inside – no pattern, crest, face or HP bar –; under the arena a small monospace stats block of four lines: the names with their HP in the fighters' colours, `DMG: 12 | DMG: 27` (damage dealt), the state tags `[NORMAL] | [KNOCKBACK]` (dead, stunned, casting, shielded, knockback, slowed, buffed, normal – `flStateOf()` from the fighter's statuses) and the ability's name with its meter – the ability boxes folded into that line, READY over the meter; no names band, no lead bar, **no VS card** – the fight is on from the first frame, only FIGHT! flashes at the launch; the arena style is ignored – always clean; a show's column takes the icons' place; `fightLeague/noir.ts`), **lilac** (a #d9cef7 → #c4b4ee backdrop with faint stripes around a white floor with an ink-violet rim, the HUD in white cards with ink borders), **night** (dark, a vignette) or **theme** (the first release's look: the page's theme around the arena). The stage continues into an export's letterbox bars, under the watermark, which keeps its place, its opacity and its order. **Arena styles** (`flAs`): clean, grid, division (the genre's rim colour and a generic motif – a dark floor with a red rim for horror, a pale green field for Pokémon, star dots for Star Wars …; a cross-division match uses clean) and neon.
- **Hit particles** (fl-noir, every stage – white on noir, the stage's palette elsewhere – always on, about half in the live lite tier): every hit bursts round dots of mixed sizes (big slow puffs and small fast dots), grey square debris chips and a few red and orange sparks along the hit; a heavy impact, a bomb's burst and a KO add a smoke cloud of soft circles that expands and fades, a wall slam its dust; on noir the knockback ribbon and speed lines are white with short white streaks beside the trail, and a struck disc flashes white with a one-frame scale punch (1.14 → 1 over 60 ms). `FlParticles` in `fightLeague/fx.ts`: a fixed pool of `FL_PARTICLE_CAP` (512) typed-array slots filled from the event ring as its events appear – the analytic hash seeded by the event's time, so the same fight spawns the same particles at any frame rate (`data-fl-particles` counts them) – each particle's position a function of its age, drawn as one path a kind (the dots, the chips, each spark colour) and the puffs from the cached glow sprite; the stage never changes the simulation.
- **Heavy hits: Stop The World** (feature fl-heavy; every stage) – the frame a heavy blow lands (section 4's tier: `FL_HEAVY_FRAC` 0.25 of the foe's maximum HP, EV_HEAVY) **holds**: the page camera dips to factor 0.05 (`FL_HITSTOP_FACTOR`) for `FL_HITSTOP_MS` 90 real ms – `FL_HITSTOP_KO_MS` 150 on the lethal blow – through `cam.dip()` beside the String Battle duel's cue (`FightLeagueLayer.cameraCue()`, once a frame in Canvas.tsx after the physics steps; the dip starts already slowed, with no ease-in (the cue's `rampInMs` 0), so the frame after the cue feeds 0.05 × its own length to the steps, runs no fixed step and holds the impact frame; measured in the page's loop, the frames inside the 90 ms feed about 21 ms of simulation at 60 fps, one fixed step (two after a carried remainder over 12.5 ms), and 9 ms at 30 fps, none or one, and the 150 ms about 23 and 32 ms, one or two steps: a freeze, not a judder; the engine's clock and its fixed steps are untouched, the headless tests never see it, and a recording grows by the dips through `cam.getSlowLagMs()`: about 80 to 90 ms per heavy hit and 125 to 135 per KO blow at 60 or 30 fps). Under it the arena **washes white** (alpha `FL_HEAVY_WASH_NOIR` 0.55 on noir, 0.45 elsewhere, × (1 − t)² over `FL_HEAVY_WASH_REAL_MS` 50 ms of the page's real clock from the camera's cue (`FightLeagueRenderOptions.heavyStopMs`): the effects' clock all but stops in the dip, and a wash on it held the whole frozen frame a flat grey, so now the frozen frame flashes and ends on a dark arena under the white ring, the cone and the number; a render with no page camera, so no dip, runs it over `FL_HEAVY_FLASH_MS` 90 on the effects' clock; drawn under the HP numbers and before the zoom punch, so it fills the arena's shape and never spills past the rim) and the **zoom punch** draws the bodies and effects inside the arena clip 7 % larger – 10 % on the lethal blow – about the impact point, easing out over 400 ms (`flHeavyZoom()`: the plate, the clip, the shake and the HUD stay still; beside the finale's push-in never past 1.2; because the effects' clock nearly stops during the dip, the punch is held at its peak on the frozen frame and eases out after release). A **directional shake** along the hit (`FL_SHAKES.heavy` 0.024, `heavyKo` 0.028, 320 ms, at `FL_SHAKE_HEAVY_HZ` 10: one push along the hit for about 3 frames, then the spring back, where the shared 22 Hz flipped its sign every 1 to 2 frames, a jitter; summed under the `FL_SHAKE_CLAMP` 0.03 × side clamp before the strength, which both stay under, so the lethal blow shakes harder than a heavy hit, where 0.02 cut both to it: about 16 and 18 px at the 1080 square at the default 0.6, where the spec's 0.014 moved the arena 6 px); a white **shock ring** 1.6 → 4.2 R over 420 ms (0.26 unit tapering to 0, open already on the held frame) chased 60 ms later by a thin ring in the attacker's accent to 3.6 R; a **cone of 12 white speed lines** 3 R long and 0.14 unit wide within ± 0.35 rad of the hit direction over 240 ms (not on the lite tier, nor under the ink flip, where white on the white arena it only cut through the ink number); the arena **rim stroked in the attacker's accent** for `FL_HEAVY_EDGE_MS` 300 (0.04 side wide inside the edge, fading, drawn without the zoom punch so it hugs the rim, not under the ink flip: the one colour pop); the giant hit's gold crit star is skipped on a heavy blow (the ring and the cone are its payoff, and the victim's HP digits stay readable on the held frame); and on top of the hit's own burst 44 dots, 14 chips and 12 sparks along the hit with an 8-puff cloud (the lethal blow's cloud is the KO's; half on the lite tier), leaving from the victim's rim (the puffs from 1.5 R) already `FL_HEAVY_BURST_AGE_MS` 40 ms old, so the frozen frame shows them spreading off the disc, not a blob over the victim – the blow's own hit and impact bursts start 40 ms old too, the impact's from the rim with its puffs from 1.5 R, the hit's puffs give way to the heavy hit's, and the giant hit's accent ring, which started inside the victim's disc, gives way to the white shock ring –; every hit's particles draw under the HP numbers; and **the victim stays visible**: its white hit flash lasts `FL_HEAVY_FLASH_CAP_MS` 30 ms of the page's real clock from the camera's cue (the plain 70 ms on the simulation's clock, which the dip stops, held it white for the whole freeze) and then it shows its own colour and its new HP, with no tint. **The huge number**: every damage number's base size is divided by the noir scale, so noir matches the other stages (`flNumberFs()`: 30 px at the 1080 square from about 15 damage up, where noir drew 0.81× too small); the heavy tier is 2.4 × the base (`FL_NUMBER_HEAVY_SCALE`: 72 px on screen at rest for a 50 on noir, where the arena is 0.6 of the square), white digits with the attacker's heavy colour as the stroke (0.18), **slammed** `FL_NUMBER_SLAM` 1.8 → 0.94 at 70 ms → 1 at 140 ms (`flNumberSlam()`: about 130 px tall at its peak at the 1080 square; at 2.6 the held frame's number hid both fighters), a 22 Hz jitter of 0.06 unit dying over 120 ms, its glyphs a radius clear above the victim's disc (below it when the arena's top leaves no room for the number at its peak and CRITICAL!, and for the lethal blow on the KO point's side away from the arena's middle, since the final KO! takes the other half), rising only 0.5 unit over `FL_NUMBER_HEAVY_MS` 1400 (`FL_NUMBER_HEAVY_CROWD_MS` 900 with three fighters or more, so a four-way's heavy numbers do not pile up; the plain tier keeps 750 ms and 0.9 unit), held at full alpha to 75 %, and clamped so the zoom punch never pushes it, or a callout, past the rim; the tier reads the largest one-step blow merged into the number (EV_DAMAGE's y2, `flNumberBlow()`), never the merged value, so two sub-heavy blows a few steps apart that merge into one number (Thunder Strike 18.76 and the hammer's 14.74, 133 ms apart, read 33.50) keep the plain tier: the huge number always has a heavy blow behind it; no gold "×N" beside a share-based giant (kept at 0.6 fs for a multiplier giant). At the **critical** tier (`FL_CRIT_FRAC` 0.40 of the bar) **CRITICAL!** (`FightLeague.canvasCritical`: KRYTYCZNY!, ¡CRÍTICO!) pops on the huge number's far side from the disc (above it, below a number under the disc) once its slam has landed, from 140 ms to 700 ms, at 1.3 unit (0.9 read as small print on a phone), white for its first 50 ms then the heavy colour, pop 1.5 → 1 (the callout `critical` in `data-fl-callouts`). The **lethal blow on noir** adds the **ink flip** for `FL_KO_FLIP_MS` 100 real ms, timed on the page's own clock from the camera's lethal cue (`FightLeagueRenderOptions.koStopMs`: 6 frames at 60 fps, 3 at 30, since the simulation's clock all but stands still through the 150 ms dip; a render with no page camera has no dip either and times it on the simulation's clock): the arena fills white, its rim and every disc fill ink (no white flash on them), the HP digits draw white with no stroke, the huge number ink with no stroke, no cone; the rim pop waits for it, and the flip is that KO's flash (no white KO flash after it: it kept the black arena mid grey for about 400 ms under the KO's white ring). Every heavy effect on noir (the chaser ring, the rim pop, the number's stroke, CRITICAL!, COMEBACK!, the rim sweep, the wind-up's rings and name, ARMED) is drawn in the fighter's **heavy colour** (`flNoirHeavyColor()`): its name colour (`flNameColor()`, the colour of its title, stats line and disc, so the hit reads as that fighter's: Saitama's yellow, Little Mac's green), or, for a near-white one (luminance above 0.8, Ryu's #f3f4f6) that would vanish on the white rim, its accent through `flNoirAccent()` (lightened in 0.05 steps until its luminance is 0.3 or more – 77 of 147 accents, Little Mac's #111827 and Hulk's #6b21a8 among them – or it would vanish on black). **Impact Frames off** (`flIf=0`) or `prefers-reduced-motion` disable the dip, the zoom punch, the flip and the number's jitter and slam together and drop the wash to 0.3 (the number keeps the plain 1.35 pop); the existing EV_IMPACT render hold (50 / 80 ms) and 60 ms catch-up stay – physics keeps moving under a render hold. Photosensitivity: the full ink flip is noir-only and KO-blow-only (100 real ms on the page's clock, once a match plus once per non-final KO); a heavy wash fires at most once per target per 400 ms. Every heavy effect takes its age from the effects' clock (`fr.fxNow`); the flip, the opener and the lead flash use the frame's clock by design.
- **The heavy wind-up** (fl-heavy) – a fighter whose ability passes `flHeavyAbility()` (an ultimate, or a giant hit of `maxHpFrac` ≥ 0.25) telegraphs differently: at the telegraph's start the ability's **name slams into the arena's top centre** (0.5 unit, 0.6 for an ultimate, at least 48 px at the 1080 square, its centre line 1.6 font sizes below the arena's top edge, clear of the heavy rim pop; white with the heavy colour's stroke 0.16, pop 1.4 → 1 over 110 ms, held through the telegraph and, for a loaded punch, through its ARMED phase for up to 1200 ms after the cast (a 300 ms quick telegraph alone showed it for under half a second), gone the moment its blow lands (a heavy blow or a giant hit by the caster after the cast: the held frame is the blow's, and a huge number pinned to the arena's top would meet it), else fading 150 ms after the cast; one name at a time, the newest wind-up's, when two heavies wind up at once in a four-way or a 3v3 (they printed over each other); none while a huge number's top sits within 2.5 name heights of the arena's top (drawn over it, the name hid the number's slam in a four-way); drawn without the zoom punch; it registers the `cast` callout and the rising name over the caster is skipped); **three rings in the caster's accent converge** on the disc from 3.5 R to 1.05 R, a third of the telegraph apart, their line growing 0.04 → 0.12 R (one ring on the lite tier); the disc **swells** 1 → 1.2 and **trembles** ± 0.08 R at 12 Hz along its locked aim (no tremble on lite; 1.12 and ± 0.03 R were under a pixel at phone size); the arena rim fades toward the accent (alpha 0.6 × progress) and snaps back to white at the cast; the giant hit's "×N" telegraph badge is skipped for a share-based blow. **ARMED**: after the cast, while the giant hit is loaded (`giantLeft` > 0), a solid accent ring at 1.3 R pulses to 1.45 R at 2 Hz (0.14 R wide, 3 px at least) with the ability's name in small caps at 0.8 unit above the disc (about 32 px at the 1080 square; 0.45 read 17 px, about 6 CSS px on a phone, and past the wind-up's hold it is the only name on screen), until the blow lands or the fighter is KO'd (`fx.drawArmed()`, after the shields). No darkness veil, no spotlight, no aura below a full meter. The sounds are the existing ones: `charge` at 0.85, the `telegraph` stretched to the class (2.125× for the 850 ms ultimate class), `fire` at the cast. An ultimate's super flash shows no ribbon (the name is on screen already) and, on noir, no edge tint (a raw accent at 0.45 on black read as a muddy second frame) and ends at the caster's first heavy blow after the cast with a 60 ms fade, so the frozen frame and the huge number own the arena (section 4).
- **The KO blowout** (fl-heavy) – from the KO point a white shock ring expands **past the arena's edge** (R + 1.6 × the arena side × ease-out over the 1200 ms KO, crawling through the finale's hold and completing as time returns; 0.05 side wide, tapering), a second ring in the victim's body colour at 0.8 of the radius, **24 shards** alternating white and the body colour under the existing gravity (12 before; 12 on the lite tier), 8 long white streaks 0.4 side × t from the KO point (one stroke path; none on lite), and the KO flash at alpha 0.5 for the final KO (0.3 for a KO inside a show). The finale is visible on noir: its 12 speed lines draw in the stage's streak colour (white on noir, ink elsewhere) at max(1.5 px, 0.008 side) and alpha 0.7 (1 − t), and on noir a **white iris ring** closing from 1.6 to 1.0 × the arena's half-side over 600 ms replaces the vignette's drawImage. The engine's finale – 1 → 0.25 in 80 ms, held to 800, back at 1200, the ghost, the 1.12 push-in, the winner card at 900 ms – is unchanged.
- **The lead turn** (fl-heavy, EV_TURN) – when a blow of 15 % of the foe's bar or more flips who is winning, the attacker's accent **runs once round the white rim**: a dash a quarter of the perimeter long sweeps clockwise from the top-left corner over `FL_TURN_SWEEP_MS` 600 (`setLineDash`, 0.012 side wide, fading over the last 200 ms); **COMEBACK!** (the existing `canvasComeback`) pops in the heavy colour 1.6 R above the attacker where it is now, at 1.2 unit (0.8 read as small print on a phone), for 900 ms (pop 1.5 → 1; the callout `comeback`); when the turn is a heavy blow's (an EV_HEAVY by the same attacker within 50 ms) the sweep starts when the rim pop ends (300 ms later) and COMEBACK! waits 700 ms, starting as CRITICAL! ends (at 500 ms over the blow's place, beside the victim, it was drawn across CRITICAL! and the number), and the sweep is drawn without the zoom punch, on the rim; and the noir stats block's name line of the new leader flashes white for 300 ms (`FL_NOIR_TURN_FLASH_MS`; the other stages already flash the lead bar). No shake, no particles. Independent of any event, on noir an alive fighter **under 25 % HP** (`FL_HEARTBEAT_FRAC`) wears a **heartbeat**: a white ring at R × (1.15 + 0.12 sin 2π · 1.6 Hz), 0.2 R wide at alpha 0.85, its HP digits scaling 1 + 0.15 × max(0, sin) with it (0.08 R at 0.5 read as a 1 px grey outline) (not on the lite tier; nothing on the other stages, whose HP bar already blinks). The `lowHp` sting (once per fighter under 25 %) is its audio.
- **The noir opener** (fl-heavy; noir only, the match's first 1.5 s) – frame one moves: each disc **slides in from outside the arena** along the vector from the arena's centre through its spawn point, from 1.3 × the half-side out (clipped by the arena, so it enters through the rim), easing in over `FL_OPENER_MS` 450 (easeInOutCubic, so most of the motion ends on the arrival's squash, where easeOutCubic stopped the discs about 150 ms before it: a render-only offset of where the disc is drawn, the HP number riding it and fading in with how far the disc's centre is inside the rim, so no digits float over the black margin) and arriving on the first tick cue with a squash 1.18 → 1 over 120 ms and a white arrival ring 0.4 → 1.6 R over 200 ms; from 450 ms to FIGHT! the discs breathe 1 → 1.04 at 2 Hz in opposite phase by slot parity (not on lite) and a white dash three rim widths wide **grows clockwise round the rim** from 600 to 1400 ms, so the square completes right before FIGHT!, between the second and third tick; at FIGHT! a 60 ms white wash of the arena (0.35 → 0) under the banner. Analytic in the frame's clock – no event, no particle, nothing read back by the physics (the fighters are frozen before `introMs` as before) – so the clip has motion at 0 s, a beat at 0.45 s, the reveal at 1.5 s and first blood before 2 s.
- **The spectacle switches** (the Spectacle group: Stage – Noir, Lilac, Night, Theme –, Arena Style, Arena Shake, KO Slow Motion, Impact Frames, Damage Numbers, Portrait Plates, Name Tags) are presentational: applied live without a restart, never one of the engine's keys, written into the URL only when they differ from the default.
- **Readability minimums at the 1080 px square** (a test): names ≥ 54 px, the HP number ≥ 34 px, damage ≥ 30 px, box names ≥ 26 px **as drawn** (`flFitBoxName()`: a long ability name is set smaller down to that floor, then condensed to no less than 75 % of its width – the letters keep their height –, then ellipsised at a word's end, 'Super Mega Death…'; the test draws the roster's longest names in every match type, square and 9:16, and measures them), stat lines ≥ 20 px, tags ≥ 22 px, banners ≥ 90 px; every name holds 4.5:1 against its ink stroke and a dark body gets an ink rim; in a portrait export the boxes end above the watermark badge and the right 10 % of the square stays free of text.

#### 8. Story and win chance

- **The story** (`view.story`, visual state only – no rule reads it): first blood, the lead changes of the HP share (with 5 % hysteresis), each side's deepest deficit, the cliffhanger moment (every side standing at or under 25 %), the best combo, and at the verdict the finisher (`weapon:<kind>[:<style>]`, `ability:<name>`, reflect, summon, trap, burn, contact), an ability finish, the winner's HP share, CLUTCH (≤ 10 %), PERFECT (no HP lost and nothing absorbed by the rig), UPSET (a win chance under 40 % at the bout's start) and COMEBACK (the winner trailed by 40 % of the share). Read by the winner card's tags, the finder (`RunSummary.fl`), `getProgress()` and the data attributes.
- **'win chance (simulated)'** – `flWinChance()`: ΣsA ÷ (ΣsA + ΣsB) from the tuner's strengths (`FL_STRENGTH` in the ratings file, section 15); Gerald, a pairing across divisions and teams count 1 a fighter and are marked **"est."** (an unmeasured pair); a free-for-all s_i ÷ Σs. The VS card draws a bar of the two shares ('62 %' / '38 %', UNDERDOG below 40 %) with the caption "win chance (simulated)"; it is **hidden whenever a handicap is not 1**, where **BUFFED** (lime, 'DMG ×1.25 ▲') / **NERFED** (red, 'SPD ×0.80 ▼') tags show instead; `flOd=0` turns it off. No betting words, odds formats or wagers anywhere.

#### 9. Sound

- **Synthesis only** – every sound is synthesised in the browser from recipes written as data (`src/lib/audio/flRecipes.ts` in the notation of `flSynth.ts`: `role(ms) layer + layer …`, a layer a source – sine, triangle, square, saw, 25 % / 12.5 % pulses as cached PeriodicWaves, seeded white / pink / brown noise, a Karplus–Strong pluck, a struck bar, a three-band formant voice – with its glide, vibrato, FM, AM, filters, drive, envelope, repeats and stereo position; an echo after `|`). Every one-shot lasts at most 1.4 s; `scripts/fl-sound-trims.mjs` renders every row in Chromium and writes its gain (`flRecipeTrims.ts`: one-shot peak 0.40, charge 0.30, held 0.25).
- **The tables** – `FL_WEAPON_SOUNDS` (the 21 kinds: swing, shoot, hit, block, return, ricochet, clash, blink, snag, fuse), `FL_PRIMITIVE_SOUNDS` (the 26 primitives: charge, telegraph, fire, sustain, impact, end …), 26 family sets around a pitch (`FL_FAMILY_SETS`: energy blade, blaster, katana, greatsword, slasher, chainsaw, brawler, martial, speedster, arcane, pyro, storm, gunslinger, tech, beast, cartoon, bomber, aqua, frost, dark lord, frontier, ghost, psychic, retro, blocky, symbiote), **a signature set for each of the 147 fighters** (`FL_SIGNATURES`: roles of its own over a family set – Thor's anvil hit and thunder, a web shot, a shield clang…), a **tint** per division layered on its roles (`FL_DIVISION_TINTS`), the **match stings** (`FL_MATCH_SOUNDS`: the VS slam, the count's ticks, the cap's clock, KO, the final KO with the finale's time warp, the winner's own two-chord fanfare, double KO, TIME!, draw, low HP, sudden death and the shows' stings) and the **announcer** (`FL_ANNOUNCER`: a deep synthetic vowel shape or an instrumental sting for every banner kind – never a word of anyone; `FL_BANNER_SOUNDS` maps each banner to its call and sting).
- **The resolve order** (`flSoundResolve.ts`, pure): the fighter's custom clip, else its signature set with its division's tint, else its weapon kind's or primitive's row, else the legacy kind; stings and calls resolve directly. **The mixer** (`flMixer.ts`): seven buses (swing, shoot, hit, ability, ambient – the blade hums –, match, announcer) with levels and voice limits (the oldest of the lowest priority fades out in 15 ms past a limit), the same recipe of a fighter once within 40 ms, the weapons ducked under a sting (−6 dB, the abilities −3 dB) and the music bed under a sting, a cast or a big hit. **The per-tick budget** (`modes/fightLeagueSound.ts`): each 60 Hz simulation tick keeps its strongest 3 hits, 2 shots, 1 swing, 1 block and 1 wall note – at most 6, hits first – while the urgent cues (abilities, match stings, calls, KOs, low HP) always pass, so every frame rate, the fast export and every split-screen arena pick the same cues. `flDispatch.ts` `playFightEvent()` is the one dispatch; the ToneGenerator's `playFightCue()` resolves and mixes (also in the offline twin).
- **The heavy hits' sound** (fl-heavy) – every landed blow of `FL_HEAVY_FRAC` of the foe's bar or more plays the giant hit's **thump** (`FL_PRIMITIVE_SOUNDS.giantHit.impact`: a 100 → 35 Hz driven sub, a 50 ms crack, a brown rumble, 602 ms) as an urgent `impact` cue at `flLevel` 1 – a shockwave foe's otherwise silent hit included – and because the mixer runs in real time its tail fills the 90 ms hit stop; the urgent key keeps one thump per attacker per tick when a shockwave hits three foes. A **critical** blow (`FL_CRIT_FRAC` 0.40 of the bar; never the lethal one, whose KO, final KO and time-warp stings own the moment) adds the one new row, the match sting **`FL_MATCH_SOUNDS.critical`** (640 ms, one-shot on the match bus, which ducks the weapons and the music bed: a driven sub drop 70 → 24 Hz, a 35 ms band-passed crack, a short mid thwack that still reads on a phone speaker, a brown body thud and three fast 1320 Hz taps from 120 ms; it passes the avoid-list, lands within 8 % of its declared length and has its trim in `flRecipeTrims.ts`). A **lead turn** (EV_TURN) stamps the existing `comeback` sting in the same tick. Both are stamped in `hitCue()` and `stepLead()` – never from frame time – so 30 and 144 fps pick the same cues; a lethal blow adds nothing of its own.
- **The audio rules** – the header of `flRecipes.ts` carries the **avoid-list** every row was reviewed against: no sample, rip, re-recording or re-synthesis of a source cue, no voice line or imitation, no melody or jingle of three notes or more and no iconic two-note figure in its original interval, register and timbre. The gestures a row could reproduce by its shape alone – a chip tone swept up like the plumber's jump, climbing revs like the spin-dash, the coin's rising fourth – are checked by machine on every row of every table (`src/lib/audio/flAvoidList.ts`, `flAvoidListProblems()`); Mario's intro is a chip "boing" and Sonic's telegraph a smooth spool-up of our own.
- **Custom clip slots – your own licensed audio, on your device only.** The Custom Sound Slots panel under the pickers (`sections/FlCustomSoundsPanel.tsx`) gives every fighter of the match six slots – attack, hit, ability, KO, intro and win – with Add, Play and Remove, and Export Set / Import Set (`<name>.jumpingballslive.zip`: WAV files and a `clips.json` manifest). A file is **validated** before it is kept (`lib/audio/flClips.ts`): at most 2 MB, audio by its magic bytes (WAV, OGG, FLAC, MP3, M4A/AAC, WebM), decodable, trimmed to its first 3 s; a refused file says why. The clips live in this browser's **IndexedDB** (`jbl-fl-clips`) – never uploaded, never in a share link or a project file, never shipped with the site (`.gitignore` keeps `fight-sounds/` and `*.jumpingballslive.zip` out; a test allows tracked audio only under `public/hitSounds/` and `public/wallBreak/`). **The rights note**: add only audio you own or have a licence to use – a video that uses it can still draw a content claim.
- **Settings** – **Sound Set** (`flSnd`: signature – the default –, kinds – the kinds' and primitives' rows only – or legacy, the first release's sounds through `fightTones.ts` as the **legacy fallback**), **Announcer** (`flAnn`, on) and **Custom Sounds** (`flCS`, on); all presentational.

#### 10. Find Simulation

- **Kept from the first release**: a winner ("A wins", "B wins" … each side by its letter and name, a team by its team), a fight that ends at a chosen second (the duration search) and a **double KO**.
- **The story's outcomes** (judged on the run's deciding match, `RunSummary.fl`): **upset**, **clutch**, **perfect**, **comeback**, **ability-finish**, **lead-changes** (at least a count), **both-low-at** (the cliffhanger: every side ≤ 25 % with the fight still on within ±0.5 s of a second – the clip is cut there), **decider** (a series that goes the distance), **sudden** (decided in sudden death), **champion** (a bracket won by one of its pinned entrants), **boss-falls-at** (the boss falling at a wave) and **close** (the winner's HP share ≤ 0.10). PERFECT is rare at the default 100 HP (Thor vs Loki: none in 1,000 seeds); at 40 HP about 1 seed in 20.
- **Batch sizes per format** – the duration search simulates 4 single matches, 2 series, or 1 bracket or boss rush a frame; a show is followed 90 s a match and its progress names the furthest bout ("Furthest: bout 3/7"). Make a clip (section 12) runs through the same search with a finish window (`FinderOutcome.finishWithin`) and the cliffhanger's `cutWhileOn`.

#### 11. Rigged forced winner

- **Per format** (Rigged Outcomes, registered like String Battle's: a fighter in 1v1 and the free-for-alls, a team in 2v2 and 3v3): every game of a series (engaging only in a game the rival could clinch and in the decider, so a rigged series can come back 2–1), only the **pinned entrant's** bouts of a bracket, the **boss or the challengers** of a boss rush.
- **The honest-rig guarantees** – the chosen side's hits land a little more (a longer reach, homing shots, rebounds turned toward a rival – more the further it trails) and the rivals' a little less (it stays invulnerable longer after a hit), all inside the same deterministic replay; as a backstop the chosen side's last fighter never drops below 1 HP until the verdict (`absorbLethal()`, `guardChosenSide()` before every step's KOs – against hits, reflected damage, a shared KO step and a shot in the KO grace alike), sudden death waits while it trails, a rigged show never ends a match in a double KO, and PERFECT never shows for a winner whose lethal hits were absorbed. The chosen side always wins – often with very little HP left.
- **The panel note** names the format ("Rigged (bracket): A · Thor wins"); nothing of the rig shows in the recording.

#### 12. Settings and URL keys

- **The block** – the Fight League block of the Mode row (`sections/FightLeagueSection.tsx`, found by the search box): Matchup (the presets), Match Type, Fighter A–F (**the picker**), Random Stays In Division, HP, Time Cap, Sudden Death, Arena, Seek Turn, HUD, Handicaps (speed, damage, attack-speed and cast-speed multipliers per slot); the Shows group (`sections/FightLeagueShowsSection.tsx`: Best Of, Entrants, Bracket Size, Seeding, Boss Waves, Boss HP, Boss Heal, Card, Win chance, the clip estimate); the Spectacle group (section 7); the Sound group (section 9); the Custom Sound Slots panel; the Creator tools (`sections/FightLeagueCreatorSection.tsx`).
- **Every fl\* key** – old keys are unchanged; every number is an uncapped NumberField from its minimum (`atLeastMin`; `tests/uncap.test.ts`), the comfort range only bounds its slider; a show's entrants are bounded by memory only (`FL_ENTRANT_CEILING` 256 in `MEMORY_CEILINGS`). The table between the `fl-settings` markers is `flSettingsTableMarkdown()` (`FIGHT_LEAGUE_URL_KEYS` with each field, its default as the URL writes it and its comfort range); the mode's defaults are no gravity and a 60 s clip, and a value is written into the URL only when it differs from its default.

- **The picker** (`sections/FighterPicker.tsx`): a chip per slot – a mini ball in the fighter's colours, its name, its division and a role glyph – opening a dialog (a bottom sheet on phones): a **search** over names, short names, ids, sources, abilities, weapons and divisions, case- and diacritic-insensitive (`fightLeagueSearch.ts`: name prefix > name > ability > source > weapon); **conference tabs** of division tabs with their counts; a grid of tiles with a NEW badge; **random tokens** ('Random · any', 'Random · <division>', 'Random · <conference>'); the last eight picks; arrows, Enter, Esc and type-ahead; and the native selects grouped by division behind a **List view** toggle.
- **Presets and 'Surprise me'** – 76 presets (`FL_PRESETS`, Thor vs Loki the default, every fighter of a preset in one division): 50 duels, team fights and free-for-alls (Captain Falcon vs Little Mac, Yuumi vs Katarina, Batman vs Joker, Goku vs Vegeta, Pikachu vs Charizard, Gojo vs Sukuna, Tom vs Jerry, a Comics A free-for-all, Naruto + Sasuke vs Goku + Vegeta …), 10 best-of-3 rivalries, 3 in-division 3v3s, 5 brackets (Comics A 8, Shonen 8, Games 16, Anime 16, Movies 16), 4 boss rushes (Gerald against Comics A, Space saga, Horror movies, Cartoons) and 4 reworks with handicaps. The gallery shows them as a scrolling row of cards filtered by format chips; **Surprise me** picks a division and two of its fighters.
- **Make a clip** (`fl-make-clip`, `lib/physics/modes/fightLeagueClip.ts`, `components/simulator/fightLeague/makeClip.ts`): a length (short 15 s, standard 25 s, long 75 s) and a story (any KO, upset, clutch, comeback, ability finish, cliffhanger, a bracket's champion, the boss falling). The page measures the matchup's median KO over 8 seeds headless, paces the HP (`flPacedHp()`: the median KO at 85 % of the clip), and searches a seed whose final KO lands at 82–88 % of the clip (a cliffhanger is cut while both are low; a show ends with its verdict); the found clip gets the question caption "Who wins?" (0–2.5 s, answered "[winner] wins!") and a Top Text ("Thor vs Loki", "Can Gerald beat all 6?", "8 fighters. 1 champion.").
- **The tier list** (`fl-tier-list`, `lib/physics/modes/fightLeagueTier.ts`, `tierCard()` in `fightLeague/cards.ts`): a division or a genre ranked S–D by the tuner's round-robin win shares (15 / 20 / 30 / 20 / 15 %), every fighter once as a mini ball with its short name, downloaded as a 1080 × 1350 or 1080 × 1920 PNG with the disclaimer line; no logo, image or reference branding.
- **The viral bot's recipes** (`lib/bot/fightRecipes.ts`, the battle family): `fight-duel` (resolved and cliffhanger alternating), `fight-upset` (kept only when the underdog wins), `fight-bracket` (an 8-bracket of one division) and `fight-boss` (Gerald against a division); the HP bars are the countdown, the night stage with the neon arena the look, the matchup's running episode the series label ("Thor vs Loki #3"), copy in `ViralBot.recipes.fight*` with genre hashtags only.

#### 13. Canvas data

`data-fl-*` on the canvas (`FIGHT_LEAGUE_DATA_KEYS`, written by `FightLeagueDataset` – the strings rebuilt only when their values change): the match and the arena; names, ids, abilities, HP and maximum HP, hits, casts, alive; KOs, shots, blocks, clashes, sounds; the winner (names, `draw` or `double-ko`), the winning side, finished and its second, decided by time, double KO, forced; the HUD with its names and boxes drawn and where the boxes start; the VS card, the banner, fighters, weapons and projectiles drawn, the clock; the fair hit pipeline's counters (grazes, `clashes2`, immunes, interrupts, dodges, sudden); the spectacle's (stage, arena style, `render-ms`, banners, first blood, perfect, clutch, finale, shakes – a heavy hit counts –, trails, telegraphs, plates, debug, callouts – `critical` and `comeback` among them – and `data-fl-hitstops`, the heavy hits' camera dips cued this run, fl-heavy); the shows' and the story's (show, bout '5/7', round, series '2-1', champion, boss '3/6', odds, handicap tags, story, lead changes, finisher, combos, match index, scores, bracket, wave, boss HP, bracket / champion / boss bar shown); the ground layer's peaks (`data-fl-walls`: the most walls standing at once this run; `data-fl-traps`: the most ground hazards marked at once – traps lying in wait and the landing zones of lobbed bombs); and the ability boxes' rows and names as drawn (`data-fl-box-rows` 1 or 2, `data-fl-box-name-fs` the smallest name size scaled to the 1080 px square, `data-fl-box-name-squeeze` the narrowest condensing, `data-fl-box-names-cut` how many were ellipsised). The creator tools add `data-fl-clip-plan` / `-state` / `-ko` / `-sec` on the clip block and `data-fl-tier` / `-tier-size` on the tier block; the custom sounds panel `data-fl-clips` and `data-fl-clip-played`. **The debug overlay**: `flDbg=1` (URL only) draws every live hit shape in translucent red (`data-fl-debug=1`).

#### 14. Performance

- **The render contract** (`tests/fightLeagueFx.test.ts` on a fake canvas, `tests/fakeCanvas.ts`): after 60 warm-up frames no gradient or pattern is made, no shadowBlur is set on the page's canvas, save/restore stay balanced, at most 450 drawImage and 40 fillText a frame (with the heavy hits of fl-heavy: the four shooters measure 167 and 0, the Stage 3 heavy kit – Charizard, Walter White, Superman, Naruto – 159 and 0, the final stage's heavy kit – Venom, Walter White, Jonesy, Jinx, 20 s – 155 and 0, the noir 3v3 of six 176; a synthetic **heavy-hit storm** – a heavy, critical or lethal EV_HEAVY with its EV_DAMAGE every 40 frames and an EV_TURN every 150 – 158 on the noir heavy kit and 188 on the noir 3v3) – on every stage and arena style, in a portrait layout and with the hit shapes on. Glows, halos and the drop shadow are baked into **sprite caches** (bodies, weapons, shots and glows per radius bucket and device scale, an LRU of 160 text sprites with their ink stroke, a digit atlas for the numbers (fl-heavy: one entry per accent stroke of a huge number, within its cap of 40), the arena as one pre-rendered plate, the stage's backdrop rendered once and blitted, the flame cone as 3 cached frames at 20 Hz plus 8 embers, the bracket card cached offscreen); sparks are batched into one path per colour, the hit particles (fl-noir) into one path per kind from a pool of 512; fixed **pools** (projectiles 144, events 192 – a heavy blow pushes EV_DAMAGE, EV_HIT, EV_SHOCK, EV_IMPACT and EV_HEAVY, 5 of the 192 slots, 15 for lightning on three foes; EV_HEAVY is gated per target by 400 ms and EV_TURN per attacker by 2.5 s, so a busy free-for-all may lose an older particle or shake event but never a trigger that matters –, minions 24, tasks 32, decals 24, walls 8, particles 512). The heavy hits draw paths and sprites only (fl-heavy): two ring strokes, one cone path and one rim stroke per live heavy hit (at most two live at once), 3 or 4 glyph drawImage per huge number, one callout sprite, 8 puffs; the KO blowout two rings, one shard fill and one streak stroke, and on noir one drawImage fewer (the vignette); the wind-up three arcs, a rim stroke and one text sprite per casting heavy fighter; the opener one ring and one dashed stroke; 0 fillText anywhere. Every pixel is a function of the view, its simulation clock and the options (no wall clock or random number in the renderer – a grep test); the page drops to a **lite tier** (fewer embers and ghosts) only on the live preview while not recording – every recording and the fast export draw in full.
- **The budget** – `data-fl-render-ms` is the page's moving average of the layer's drawBodies + drawOverlay; the budget is ≤ 4 ms a frame for the four-shooter FFA (the smoke test logs it for the four shooters, the heavy kit and the heavy kit's 1080 × 1920 recording, and checks it as a timing check).
- **The fps floors** (the smoke test, 3.5 s windows): 30+ fps live for four shooters (Iron Man, Doom Slayer, Legolas, Jinx at 300 HP), for the **heavy kit** (a four-way of Venom's whip, Walter White's bombs, Jonesy's wall and shotgun and Jinx's minigun) and for a 3v3 of six shooters; 20+ fps for the 1080 × 1920 recording of the default duel and for the heavy kit's 10 s recording.
- **Measured** (the final stage, the obfuscated export on a shared 4-core machine at load 6–13): the four shooters 57 fps live at 1.3–1.7 ms of render a frame, the heavy kit 60 fps live at 0.9 ms (1.5 ms while recording 1080 × 1920), the 3v3 of shooters 59 fps, the default duel's recording 46 fps; the heavy kit's recording 32 fps. The first 1–2 s of the first page in a fresh browser context run slower (the moving average peaks at 6–22 ms during the VS card, frames up to 100 ms) – mostly the browser compiling the page's code: a second load in the same context, with every sprite cache empty again, starts at 60 fps and about 1 ms – so a recording started on a cold context (the watermark block's free recording, the heavy kit's first try at load 6+) can miss its floor in its first window, which the checks' retry and the busy-machine baseline account for. No budget failed, so the measures held in reserve for a failing one (pre-warming a bout's sprites at its start, walls and traps as one path per owner) were not needed – the sprite caches and the live-only lite tier of Stage 3 already hold the floors.

#### 15. Balance

- **The gate** (`tests/fightLeagueBalance{ComicsAnimeShows,Games,Movies}.test.ts` with `tests/flBalance.ts`: one test per division, 18 in three files run in parallel): every pair of a division over the 6 balance seeds of `tests/flProbes.ts` (three with the sides swapped; headless, fixed 60 Hz steps, a 60 s cap from FIGHT! and then sudden death, a draw counting half) in the square and in the circle; it fails if a fighter wins more than 75 % or fewer than 25 % of its matches in either arena, if the division's median fight after FIGHT! (the **TTK band**) is outside 12–25 s or if more than 2 % of its duels reach the cap. The whole gate takes well under the 120 s budget of the Fight League tests on CI.
- **The tuner** (`node scripts/fl-balance.mjs`, `--apply` to write the damage stats into the rows and the ratings file; one child per division running `tests/tools/flBalanceTune.test.ts` with `FL_TUNE=1 FL_TUNE_DIVISIONS=<division>` – skipped without `FL_TUNE`, which CI never sets): on 16 **train seeds** disjoint from the gate's (50000 + k·104729), in both arenas, every fighter's damage stat is multiplied by clamp((0.5 / its win share)^(0.4 · 0.85^iteration + 0.12), 0.75, 1.33) × clamp((median fight / 17 s)^0.7, 0.85, 1.18) – the exponent halved for a fighter whose share crossed 50 % since its last update, down to an eighth (the **update rule**) – kept in 0.3–2.6, for at most 18 iterations, until every fighter wins 40–60 % with a median fight of 12–25 s. A **pinned fighter** is one at a clamp or with a train share outside 40–60 % (none); the lopsided ones (a train share outside 35–65 % in one arena: Vader, Legolas, Homelander) are a diagnosis only the kit can fix – nine kits were adjusted by hand that way (Bugs Bunny's dynamite, Neo, Spider-Man, Aquaman, Luffy, Kylo Ren, Levi, Homelander, Yuumi). `--apply` writes **the ratings file** `src/lib/physics/modes/fightLeagueRatings.ts` (`FL_STRENGTH`: every fighter's test-seed win share and a Bradley–Terry strength, geometric mean 1 per division, with the roster's fingerprint – a test warns, without failing, once the rows change after it). Run on the tuned roster it converges at iteration 0.
- **The tuner's table** – between the `fl-balance` markers, written by `node scripts/fl-balance.mjs --reuse --readme` from the last reports (`scripts/out/fl-balance/<division>.json`, git-ignored; `--apply` writes it too):
- **The heavy-hit retune** (fl-heavy) – six divisions were tuned one at a time (`FL_TUNE=1 FL_TUNE_DIVISIONS=<division> npx vitest run --maxWorkers=1 tests/tools/flBalanceTune.test.ts`: platformers 2 iterations, fighting games 5, MOBA 4, shonen anime 4, modern anime 2, animated movies 3) and their patch values copied into the row files by hand to two decimals; four stats were set by hand inside the gate – Link 0.88 (the tuner's 0.83 left most Spin Attacks missing in QA 9), Yuumi 1.12 (her lopsided kit: the tuner's 1.05 left her at 21 % in the circle), Lee Sin 0.6 (the tuner's 0.55 left him at 24 % in the square beside Yuumi's nudge) and Raiden 1.11 with Electric Fly's charge 9 → 10 (from 1.32: once his loaded spark zap landed the giant hit, 5 × 1.11 × 2.5 = 13.9, he won 99 % of the square at 1.32) – and the rows above are the tuner's reports as written. After the tunes, six abilities' pre-stat damage was re-aimed at the band under the tuned stats (Hand Slap 30.8 and Spin Attack 22.7 for 20.0 per foe, Spinning Slash 7.58, Chidori 11.1, Final Flash 12.1, Kamehameha 9.35: Platformers, Modern anime and Shonen anime hold), Omnislash paid 10 → 9 in the row (Cloud Strife sat on the 75 % edge of the circle gate), Spirit Rush kept 9.6 (3 × 8.8: at 3 × 10 Ahri wins 82 % in the circle) and Plasma Blast went 18 → 16.6 (its bolt and blast now land on the foe they hit as one blow: 2 × 16.6 × 0.9 = 29.9, the standard class's 30). The ratings file was **not** rewritten (`FL_RATINGS_HASH` is stale: the test warns without failing, and the VS card's win chance keeps the pre-change shares until a full `node scripts/fl-balance.mjs --apply --jobs=1`, 20 to 90 min). Thor and Loki were never touched.

#### 16. Files, tests and smoke checks; release notes

- **Files** – the mode `src/lib/physics/modes/fightLeague.ts` with its pure helpers `fightLeagueRoster.ts` (+ `fightLeagueRows/<division>.ts`, `fightLeagueRows/kit.ts`), `fightLeagueSearch.ts`, `fightLeagueRatings.ts` (generated), `fightLeagueFx.ts` (the visual maths), `fightLeagueSound.ts` (the per-tick budget), `fightLeagueShow.ts` (series, entrants, pools, seeding, the win chance, the story's thresholds, the clip estimate), `fightLeagueClip.ts`, `fightLeagueTier.ts` and `fightLeagueDocs.ts` (this section's tables); the renderer `src/components/simulator/fightLeagueRenderer.ts` (the facade: `FightLeagueLayer`, `FightLeagueDataset`, `FIGHT_LEAGUE_DATA_KEYS`) over `components/simulator/fightLeague/` (palette, text, stage, bodies, weapons, projectiles, fx, hud, noir – the noir stage's HUD –, cards, plates, shows, debug, sprites, frame, makeClip); the panel `sections/FightLeagueSection.tsx`, `FighterPicker.tsx`, `FightLeagueShowsSection.tsx`, `fightLeaguePresetMatch.ts`, `FlCustomSoundsPanel.tsx`, `FightLeagueCreatorSection.tsx`; the sound `src/lib/audio/fightTones.ts` (legacy), `flSynth.ts`, `flRecipes.ts`, `flRecipeTrims.ts` (generated), `flSoundResolve.ts`, `flMixer.ts`, `flClips.ts`, `flDispatch.ts`, `src/lib/recording/unzip.ts`; the bot's `src/lib/bot/fightRecipes.ts`; the scripts `scripts/fl-balance.mjs`, `scripts/fl-sound-trims.mjs`, `scripts/fl-readme.mjs`; `public/modes/fightLeague.webp`; small blocks marked `// --- fight-league ---` and `// --- fl-overhaul ---` in the shared files (the types, the engine and the registry, `lib/modes.ts`, settings, the finder and its outcomes, teams, the rig, uncap, Canvas, Controls, the panel keys, the finder's outcome fields, Simulator, the ToneGenerator, the fast export, split screen, the bot's finder request, the desktop AI and the messages).
- **Tests** – `tests/fightLeague.test.ts` (the roster, settings, URL and presets, every kind's geometry, windows, charge and telegraphs, every primitive, the verdicts, the match types, the rig, replay fingerprints at 30 / 60 / 144 fps, the finder, the data attributes, the ratings' staleness, one regression test per QA finding of Stage 1 and, fl-heavy, the heavy damage table – Falcon Punch exactly 50, every band, the Hadouken-carried Shoryuken –, the classes, EV_HEAVY's tiers, direction and gap, EV_TURN, EV_KO's payload, the cues in their ticks and a 60 / 144 / 30 fps replay with the heavy events), `tests/fightLeagueWeapons.test.ts` with `tests/flProbes.ts` (the contract probes against an inert dummy, mirrors, crowd control, telegraphs, sudden death, the Stage 2 kinds and primitives, the first hits and the probe table), the balance gate (section 15), `tests/fightLeagueFx.test.ts` (the render contract, contrast, every shape and look, the finale, merging, banners, trails, layout, plates, the readability floors; fl-heavy: the camera cue and its dip, `flHeavyZoom` / `flNumberSlam` / `flNumberFs` / `flHeavyShare` / `flShakeAt` / `flHeavyAbility` / `flNoirAccent`, the opener's offsets, every new event kind drawn by fake-canvas call counts, the callouts in en/pl/es and a synthetic heavy-hit storm under the contract), `tests/fightLeagueSound.test.ts` (every recipe row, the signatures and tints, the avoid-list, the cues of four matches, the budget at 30 and 144 fps, the resolver, the mixer, the clips and their ZIP, the repository's audio; fl-heavy: the `critical` row, Hulk Smash's thump and Falcon Punch's critical in their ticks, a 60 s heavy fight through the budget, the mixer's dedupe), `tests/fightLeagueMatches.test.ts` (3v3, series, brackets, boss rushes, the story, the win chance, the outcomes, the rig per format, the presets and the shows' goldens), `tests/fightLeagueClip.test.ts`, `tests/fightLeagueTier.test.ts`, the bot tests, `tests/uncap.test.ts` (every fl* number an uncapped NumberField) and `tests/docsConsistency.test.ts` (the README's Fight League tables equal the code; the smoke section numbers resolve). Determinism and naming greps run over the mode, its rows and its renderer: no `Math.random`, `Date.now` or `performance.now`, no name of the reference account or a game in the messages, nothing named after the old character name.
- **Smoke checks** – the fight-league block of `scripts/smoke-test.mjs` (`SMOKE_ONLY=fight-league npm run smoke` runs it alone, with the request and console checks; without it the full suite runs unchanged), lean – every fought-to-the-end check at 8× playback, 3.5 s fps windows, one added recording: the card and preview (`/modes/fightLeague.webp`), the URL ↔ panel round trip through the List view's selects, the search box, the default Thor vs Loki with its VS card on the lilac stage, the noir stage (the default: a black margin, no VS card) and night, the 3-2-1 and FIGHT! banners on the intro's clock, a 1v1 fought to its winner with the hits and abilities heard and a question caption answered, first blood, callouts, shakes and the KO blow's hit stop (`data-fl-hitstops`, fl-heavy), the KO finale and the card's ≥ 3 s hold, a four-way to its end, the forced winner and its backstop on pinned seeds, four shooters at 30+ fps with their render cost, the default duel's 1080 × 1920 recording at 20+ fps with its plates, an "A wins" find, the overhaul's counters and sudden death, the picker, `flDbg=1`, the custom clip slots, a Bo3, an 8-bracket (its final seen), a boss rush (its wave), a 3v3 of six shooters at 30+ fps (six boxes), the win chance and BUFFED, the gallery, a clutch find, the rigged bracket's note, Make a clip, the tier list PNG, the heavy kit at 30+ fps with its walls, ground hazards and shots, the heavy kit's 10 s 1080 × 1920 recording at 20+ fps, the render-ms budget, a `random:pokemon` slot over three seeds, the rigged bracket's forced entrant and the block's screenshots (`sim-fight-league-{vs,finale,bracket,3v3,shooters,win,ffa4,rigged,rig-backstop}.png`, plus sudden, champion, boss, buffed and heavy).
- **Release notes for old links** – a link from before the overhaul still opens the same matchup, but plays by the new rules: **sudden death is on by default** (`flSD=0` restores the plain HP verdict at the cap) and the time cap counts from FIGHT!; **the noir stage is the default look** (fl-noir; `flSt=lilac` restores the lilac stage of the overhaul, `flSt=theme` the page's theme around the arena – a link that names its stage keeps its look); and because rows were retuned and some fighters moved division (Pikachu, Charizard, Steve), **an old found seed replays a different fight** – the link opens the same fighters, not the same outcome (find it again). **The heavy hits** (fl-heavy, "Stop The World"): a hard-hitting ability takes a share of the foe's maximum HP – Falcon Punch and Serious Punch half the bar, Raging Demon 40, Shoryuken, Electric Wind God Fist, Detroit Smash and Karate Chop Action 30 %, Star Punch, Dragon's Rage, Mouse Hole, Night Shroud, Hulk Smash, The Snap, TNT and Howitzer Impact a quarter… (section 4) –, Falcon Punch and Raging Demon became ultimates (13 s), twelve divisions' rows were retuned and twelve descriptions rewritten, so **a found seed from before fl-heavy replays a different fight** in those divisions; Thor vs Loki, the default duel, plays exactly as before. Every heavy hit freezes the frame (section 7): `flIf=0` turns the hit stop, the zoom punch, the wash and the ink flip off together and keeps the plain number pop, and a recording grows by about 80 to 90 ms per heavy hit and 125 to 135 ms per KO blow.

#### 17. How to make a Fight League clip

1. Open Fight League and pick a matchup: a preset card from the gallery (a duel, a best-of-3, a 3v3, a bracket, a boss rush or a rework), **Surprise me**, or two fighters from the picker.
2. In the Creator tools choose a length and a story (any KO, upset, clutch, comeback, ability finish, cliffhanger, champion, boss falls) and press **Make a clip**: it paces the HP, finds a seed whose KO lands at 82–88 % of the clip and adds the "Who wins?" caption and the Top Text.
3. Record (real time) or use the fast export; a 9:16 export draws the portrait plates in its bars, and the winner card's crossfade back to the VS split makes the clip loop.
4. Optionally make the matching tier list card for a carousel post, or let the viral bot's fight recipes plan the week.

### Beat Runner and Paddle Keep-Up (rhythm runner)
Feature jdm-rhythm-runner: two modes without rings that own their playfields (the `"none"` ring layout; each moves one ordinary engine ball – `gravityScale` 0, `ballsMayRest`, `ballsPassThrough` – so pause, playback speed, restart, the recorder and the fast export work unchanged; only a run played by hand – a Beat Runner without Auto Jump, a Paddle Keep-Up without Auto Platform, `jdmRhythmPlayedByHand()` – is left to Record Video, since the export's fresh engine has no player: the ⚡ Fast export is off for it with a note (`FastExport.handPlayNote`) and a batch job of one fails as "played by hand" (`BatchRender.errorHandPlay`)) and draw their own entities through a small renderer module and a few `Canvas.tsx` hooks, like the race and the arena games. `src/lib/physics/modes/jdmRhythm.ts` holds what they share (`isJdmRhythmMode()`, the scale-degree notes `rhythmPitch()` / `rhythmChord()`); `jdmRhythmFields.ts` glues their SimulatorSettings fields, ranges, URL keys and validation into `settings.ts` and their finder settings into `finder.ts`.

- **Beat schedule** – `src/lib/simulation/beatSchedule.ts` inverts the beat clock of Picture Paint (`lib/simulation/beatClock.ts`): `beatTimeSec(config, i)` is the simulation time of beat `i`, numbered exactly like `BeatClock.sample().index` (a song grid mapped through the music bed's start offset and loop, past a non-looping song's end extrapolated at its tempo; the manual BPM from 0), and `firstBeatAtOrAfter(config, t)` the first beat at or after a time. `tests/runner.test.ts` checks both against `BeatClock` for several offsets, looping or not.
- **Jump-timing solver** (`runner.ts`) – `runnerPhysics()`: gravity `RUNNER_GRAVITY` cube lengths/s² × the Gravity setting (0.5×–2×), take-off speed √(2·g·H) from the Jump Height H. `jumpFlightTime(v0, g, rise)` is the descending root of v0·t − g·t²/2 = rise (onto a block `rise` 1 or 2, flat 0), `takeOffSec(land, …)` = land − flight time, `ascendTime()` when the rising jump passes a height (the block's corner), `dropTime(g, depth)` the free fall off an edge.
- **Course** – `buildRunnerCourse(settings, gravity, random)` (pure; four draws per obstacle whatever it becomes) walks the beats: for each obstacle a kind from the mix and the level (`pickKind()`), the earliest beat the jump fits (`LEAD_IN_SEC` before the first, `MIN_RUN_SEC` of running after every landing; below Density 1 a beat or two is skipped now and then), the take-off from the solver, and the geometry from the times (x = speed × time): a row of spikes centred under the apex, as many as the arc clears with the hitbox margins (`runnerMaxSpikes()`), a pit between the take-off and the landing (`GAP_MARGIN`), a block face the rising square clears by `FACE_MARGIN` (`runnerCanStepUp()`), an edge placed so the fall lands on the beat; floors and blocks are disjoint spans, a closing drop brings the square back to the floor, checkpoints every `SECTION_EVENTS` obstacles, the finish a second after the last landing. `courseIsClear()` in the tests flies every planned arc against the hitboxes.
- **Runner physics** – `RunnerMode.advance()` moves the square event by event inside every engine sub-step, on its own track clock (simulation time minus a shift): take-offs (the plan's times with Auto Jump, a buffered Space press – `requestJump()`, `JUMP_BUFFER_SEC` – without), running off an edge, landings (the descending crossing of a surface top with ground under it, solved analytically) and crashes (a block face or spikes ahead, `runnerFrontHitSec()`; the inset hitbox against blocks, pit walls and spikes, `runnerCollides()`; falling below `PIT_DEATH_Y`). A crash waits `RESPAWN_SEC`, rounded up to whole beats, and restarts the section at its checkpoint (the shift grows by whole beats, so the course stays on the music's beat). The rotation turns a whole number of quarter turns over a planned flight (half a turn per flat flight otherwise, snapped on landing). A landing queues a `"hit"` with the event's scale degree (`runnerDegree()`, a contour lifted by the level), the finish a chord and the `"multiplier"` arpeggio, a crash the wall-break sound and a low note that only accompanies the tune (`SoundEvent.melody` false: a loaded melody or slicer song moves on with the landings alone); dust, debris and finish sparks are analytic particles from the mode's own generator.
- **Paddle** (`paddle.ts`) – the field is in field heights (a portrait column, `PD_ASPECT`, the ceiling at `PD_CEILING`, the platform at `PD_PLATFORM`), so a seed plays the same on any canvas. `PaddleMode.step()` runs `PADDLE_SUBSTEPS` fixed sub-steps per 60 Hz step: the platform moves (the controller, or the hand input of `setInput()`), then the ball flies with walls, ceiling and the platform line met at their exact times. A catch launches it with `launchOf()` (the apex from `APEX_EDGE` to `APEX_CENTRE` of the room by how central the hit was, sideways `VX_KEEP` of its speed + `KICK` × spin × offset + `PLATFORM_CARRY` of the platform's) and speeds the tempo up (`paddleTempo()`: gravity × k², speeds × k, at most `MAX_TEMPO`); a ball past the platform is a miss, served again after `RESPAWN_SEC`, or GAME OVER with `GAME_OVER_HOLD_SEC` of banner. A catch is the next note of the tune (a loaded melody's next note); the walls, the ceiling, the streak chime, a miss and the game over only accompany it (`SoundEvent.melody` false – the ToneGenerator plays them with the bounce instrument and lets them use up no melody note, slicer slice, melody cooldown or beat-lock slot), so the song advances on the catches alone. The controller (`planCatch()`, pure): `predictLanding()` solves the flight to the platform line exactly (ceiling bounce, walls folded in with `foldBetween()`), a drawn sideways speed to send the ball off with sets the off-centre hit it aims for, and below skill 1 a timing error (±`TIMING_ERROR_SEC` × (1 − skill): it plans early or late), a heavy-tailed aim error (`AIM_JITTER`: P(error > d) = 1/(1 + (d/scale)²), so the miss rate grows with (1 − skill)²), a reaction delay and a slower platform (`controllerTopSpeed()`). The serves and the plans draw from the game's own generator seeded from the engine's at init – the engine's stream is shared with the cinematic director – so `runLengthMs()`, the finder's fast path, replays a game exactly without the engine loop (`tests/paddle.test.ts` compares it with engine runs).
- **Page** – `Simulator.tsx` hands the runner the song's beat grid (`rhythmBeatRef`, the same grid Picture Paint follows) and re-plans the Beat Runner only when what its run is planned from changes (`runnerPlanOf()` / `sameRunnerPlan()`: the course settings, Auto Jump, the Gravity's factor and the beat the course follows, compared by `sameBeatSchedule()` – so the BPM of a run on a song's grid, or a song finishing its analysis under a run on the BPM, changes nothing). The course lands on the music bed's beats, so a re-plan restarts the whole run (`restartRun(true)`, the R key's restart: the bed from its start offset, the melody, the slicer and the beat grid with the course – a paused run stays paused and its bed starts over when it resumes); a change of the beat it follows drops a found seed there, and neither the BPM nor a song touches a seed in any other mode (the runner's and the paddle's own settings drop it whatever the mode, like every mode's). Paddle Keep-Up restarts when its game (or the Gravity, the Ball Size) changes; while a runner or a paddle is played by hand, a capture-phase key listener turns Space / ↑ / W into jumps (instead of pausing; Escape pauses) and ← → / A / D and the pointer over the canvas into the platform's input. `components/simulator/jdmRhythmRenderer.ts` (`RunnerLayer`, `PaddleLayer`) draws both and writes `data-rr-*` / `data-pd-*` onto the canvas; `sections/JdmRhythmSection.tsx` is the Mode-row block of each (search keys `JDM_RHYTHM_KEYS`); the canvas words live in the `JdmRhythm` namespace of `messages/*.json`.
