- **Tests and checks** – `tests/runner.test.ts` (the solver, the schedule, the course, settings, whole runs: every landing on the beat and a note in its step, song grids, determinism and resizes, crashes and beat-aligned restarts, a perfect hand-played run, the finder, a loaded melody that only the landings advance – two crashes use up no note –, `sameBeatSchedule()` and when the page re-plans, `jdmRhythmPlayedByHand()`) and `tests/paddle.test.ts` (the predictor against brute force, the controller, the launch, skill 1 never missing, deterministic misses and GAME OVER, sounds, only the catches as notes of the tune – also through a real ToneGenerator with a melody on a fake audio graph (`tests/fakeAudio.ts`) –, bounds, hand input, the fast path and the finder); `tests/toneGenerator.test.ts` covers accompaniment hits (`melody` false) under a melody, the slicer, the beat lock and a hit sample; `tests/extras.test.ts` holds both fingerprints; the smoke test (section 31: also the melody on a paddle's catches, a runner that re-plans on a song's beat only when it follows the song – bed and course restarted together, landings on the song's clicks –, the BPM leaving a found seed in Classic, the Fast export off for hand play) and `scripts/generate-mode-previews.mjs` (`public/modes/runner.webp`, `paddle.webp`).

### Batch render
Feature batch-render: many fast exports in a row, built on the fast export without touching its renderer.

- **Pure parts** – `src/lib/recording/batch.ts` (covered by `tests/batch.test.ts`): the `BatchDefinition` (source `random` / `list`, the seed count, the pasted list, the variant `none` / `modes` / `sweep` with its modes or its setting, ends and steps, "download each clip"), validated by `parseBatchDefinition()` and kept in localStorage under `BATCH_STORAGE_KEY` (`loadBatchDefinition()` / `saveBatchDefinition()`, wrapped in try/catch – storage is a convenience, never required); `parseBatchList()` (one job per line: seeds, several seeds on a line, a share link, a link followed by seeds, `#` comments; `linkParams()` accepts a link only with `mode` or a `c` share code; unreadable lines are reported by number); --- low-findings --- the seeds of links and lists (`parseSeed()`, `seed=`) are the engine's signed 32-bit ones, −2³¹ … 2³¹ − 1 (`MIN_SEED` … `MAX_SEED`): the seed finder's order (`seedAt(i)` from `Date.now() | 0`) hands out negative seeds too, and `seedParam()` writes a link's `seed=` as the engine keeps it, so every found simulation replays from its link (the bot's share links, the desktop queue's job links and its journal keep them); `batchVariants()` (the picked modes in card order, or `sweepValues()`: K values from A to B snapped to the setting's `RANGES` step, both ends included, no repeats – add a numeric setting with a range to `SWEEP_KEYS` and a `BatchRender.sweep.<key>` label to sweep it); `planBatch()` (every seed in every variant, seed by seed, at most `MAX_BATCH_JOBS`, random seeds from an injectable generator); `resolveLinkSettings()` (a long link through `settingsFromSearchParams()`, a short one through `decodeShareCode()` + `mergeShareParams()`, exactly as the page opens them; the batch then keeps its own resolution and frame rate with `keepExportFormat()`, and `linkJobSettings()` gives the clip the page's wall-break sound too – a link never carries one, neither a built-in clip nor an upload); `sameSettings()`; the names (`batchFileBase()`: `mode-seed-duration` plus `-key-value` in a sweep, `uniqueFileBase()` against repeats, `batchZipBase()`).
- **ZIP** – `src/lib/recording/zip.ts` (covered by `tests/zip.test.ts`, which reads the archive back header by header): `crc32()` (table-driven, chainable over chunks), `dosDateTime()`, `zipHeaders()` (the local headers, central directory and end record from each file's name, size and CRC-32 alone: method 0, UTF-8 names via flag bit 11, no ZIP64 – `ZipTooLargeError` above 65 535 files or 4 GiB), `zipArchiveSize()` (that check from the names and sizes), `zipStore()` (byte arrays between the headers, referenced, not copied) and, for the clips, `zipBlobs()` / `zipBlobParts()`: the archive's size is checked from `blob.size` before anything is read, each clip is read once, one `ZIP_READ_CHUNK_BYTES` (4 MiB) slice at a time, for its CRC (`blobCrc32()`), and the archive is a Blob of the headers and the clips' own Blobs – referenced, not copied, so a ZIP of any size needs one slice of memory beyond the clips the page already holds.
- **Page** – `components/simulator/useBatchRender.ts` runs a batch. The fast export renders what the page shows (the hidden canvas copies the page's props, the export engine the page engine's config), so each job first puts its settings on the page – a link's or a swept value through `loadPresetSettings()` (which keeps the page's live uploads selected: `presetToLiveSettings()` in `settings.ts` puts back the uploaded wall-break sound and the custom hit sample that `presetToSettings()` drops), a mode through `changeMode()` (the mode card's change, so the carry-overs of a new feature apply without work here; the batch's clip length is put back afterwards, as a new mode starts from its defaults) – and waits for the page to commit them (an effect after every commit resolves the waiters, plus `SETTLE_MS` for asynchronous set-up such as a sample decoding); then it hands `startFastExport()` a `BatchExportRequest` through `batchExportRef` in Simulator.tsx: the export renders the job's seed instead of the page run's, scores a race under a key of its own, and gives its result to `settle()` instead of downloading `jumpingballslive-export.*`. The page run is paused for the batch and the page gets its settings back at the end – and a run Find Simulation had found: the preset loader clears the result and the physics effects drop its seed on the way, so the batch takes a restore from `keepFound` (Simulator.tsx) as it starts – the page engine's seed and the result – and, once the page's effects have run on its own settings again (`settle()`), sets the found run up as Find Simulation left it (seed, mode set up again, the Found panel, paused at its start), so Start, Record Video and the fast export play the found run. While a batch runs (the bot's renders through it too) nothing else may put settings on the page – Import project (button and drop: refused with a status line, `projectImportLocked`), saved presets, the mode cards and a share code are locked (`onRunningChange` → `batchActive` in Simulator.tsx) – and should anything else change them (a slider), the batch stops before its next job and keeps them instead of rolling them back (`BatchRender.settingsChanged`). A job whose export did not start (a recording or a search was running) fails as "busy", one whose run is played by hand (a Beat Runner or Paddle Keep-Up without its auto player: nobody would play it) as "played by hand" (`handPlay`); one without an encoder for its format stops the batch before the page would fall back to Record Video.
- **UI** – `components/simulator/sections/BatchSection.tsx` (search key `BATCH_KEYS`, rendered by Controls.tsx at the end of the Recording section): source and variant pickers, the list with its read-out, the mode chips, the sweep's setting / from / to / steps and its values, the summary (clips × resolution × fps × the longest clip of the plan – a swept clip length, a link's own `dur`: `batchLongestClipSec()`), **Render batch**, the queue (`data-batch`, `data-batch-done`, `data-batch-total` on the block, `data-batch-job` / `data-batch-file` / `data-batch-seed` per row, a progress bar on the clip being rendered), **Stop after this clip**, **Download all as ZIP** and **Clear list**. Strings live in the `BatchRender` namespace of `messages/*.json` (the search label and the download switch in `Controls`). The smoke test renders a two-seed list (names, both downloads, the ZIP read back entry by entry against them, the definition after a reload), a mode variant stopped after its first clip (the rest skipped, the page back in its own mode), an uploaded wall-break sound through a sweep of a seed and of a share link and through a mode variant (the select's value while every clip renders, the upload played in every clip, still selected afterwards) and a found run through a gravity sweep (the Found panel, the clip length, the fast export's digest and the page run's first escape are the same afterwards).

### Sound Vortex
Feature gerald-vortex: a Gerald-family mode without rings that owns its playfield and its motion like Pendulum Wave (the `"none"` ring layout, `ballsMayRest` + `ballsPassThrough`, balls with `gravityScale` 0 whose positions the mode overwrites in `onBallStep()`), so pause, playback speed, restart, the recorder, fast export, faces, emoji and trails work unchanged; the physics extras, the obstacle layer and the ring walls do not apply (the funnel is the mode's own).

- **Physics** – `src/lib/physics/modes/vortex.ts`: `buildVortexField()` lays the funnel out in the centred square the recorder crops to (rim `RIM_AT`, entry `ENTRY_AT` × rim, hole `HOLE_AT` × rim) and `ringRadii()` spaces the rings geometrically between the entry and the hole. Every ball is integrated in polar coordinates by `stepSpiral()` – L′ = −k·L (exact), r″ = L²/r³ − v_c²/r − k·r′, θ′ = ±L/r², semi-implicit Euler cut into turns of at most `MAX_TURN_PER_STEP` – under the attraction v_c²/r (it grows toward the centre; a circular orbit has the same speed v_c = rim · `BASE_OMEGA` · √pull everywhere, so the whirl speeds up as the ball sinks). A ball entering on v_c with r′ = −k·r follows r₀·e^(−k·t) exactly, so `dragRate()` = ln(entry / hole) / duration brings it to the hole on time and crosses the geometric rings at a steady tempo. The seed draws the whirl direction, the first entry angle, the run tempo (1 ± `TEMPO_SPREAD` / 2, scaling the drag) and per ball an entry speed of `ENTRY_SPEED_MIN`–1 × v_c (the weave); the entry angles step by the golden angle. `crossRings()` advances a ball's next ring past every ring it is inside (inward, once); the step's distinct rings become one `"hit"` event – a chord of at most `MAX_RING_NOTES_PER_STEP` when several – pitched by `ringPitch()` (degree i of the scale, a diatonic major while chromatic, starting at C4 and an octave lower while the innermost would pass C7). Reaching the hole records a splash, queues a `"hit"` with `pew: true` and `frequency = pewPitch()` (an octave above the innermost ring) and removes the ball at the end of the step; with `loop` it re-enters `RESPAWN_DELAY_SEC` later. The run finishes `SWALLOW_HOLD_SEC` after the last swallow (never with the loop: `runNeverFinishes()`); `vortexNominalRunSec()` / `vortexRunRangeSec()` give its length and the band the seed's tempo spreads it over, which is why the finder searches it. A resize before the first step places the entered balls from their seeded draws exactly as an init at the new size would (the finder builds its engine at the page's size); later it scales every ball's state with the funnel. `depthRadiusScale()` shrinks a ball with its depth (`radiusScale`, so a Ball Size change keeps it).
- **Sound** – `src/lib/audio/pewTone.ts`: `schedulePewTone()` glides a main oscillator (`pewWaveform()` of the bounce instrument) and a sine sub an octave below exponentially down `PEW_TONE.drop` times over `PEW_TONE.duration`; `ToneGenerator.playPew()` snaps the start to the scale, places it on the beat grid without taking a bounce's slot, ducks the music bed, and plays the chosen wall-break clip instead when there is one. The page's sound loop and the fast export's `playSoundEvent()` route `ev.pew` to it; the ring notes are ordinary hits (melody, instrument, hit sample, beat lock, slicer).
- **UI** – `components/simulator/vortexRenderer.ts` (`VortexLayer`): the funnel's depth gradient, four logarithmic whirlpool arms turning with the balls (their dashes flow inward), the rings (cyan at the rim to magenta by the hole with the rainbow walls, else the wall colour; a ring flashes and glows when a ball sinks past), the rim and the hole's lime lip under the balls; the throat's shade, note pulses and splashes (a ripple and droplets in the ball's colour, analytic on the simulation clock) over them; the title and the swallowed / pew counter at the top of the square, and a "PEW!" banner from the last swallow (`allSwallowed`) through the `SWALLOW_HOLD_SEC` hold before the end screen, so a recording ends on it. The canvas mirrors `data-vortex-*` (balls, entered, swallowed, in flight, notes, chords, rings, deepest ring, loop, tempo, depth, all swallowed, finished) for the smoke test. The panel block is `sections/VortexSection.tsx` (balls, stagger, rings, spiral time, pull, depth cue, loop and a run summary – or the endless note); a change of the funnel or the flight restarts the run and drops a found seed, the depth cue and the scale follow live. Settings `vxBalls` (URL `vxn`), `vxStagger` (`vxs`), `vxRings` (`vxr`), `vxDuration` (`vxd`), `vxGravity` (`vxg`), `vxLoop` (`vxl`), `vxDepthScale` (`vxds`), validated by `resolveVortexFields()`; the canvas words live in the `Vortex` namespace of `messages/*.json`.
- **Tests** – `tests/vortex.test.ts`: settings / URL / presets, the funnel geometry, the matched spiral and the integrator's determinism, ring-crossing detection, the notes rising with depth, the pew's place in the event stream, its synthesis and the ToneGenerator's dispatch (scale, waveform, beat grid, wall-break clip), the banner flag and the hold before the end, the loop, resizes and the finder landing a 30 s seed; `tests/extras.test.ts` holds the fingerprint of the default run. The smoke test checks the card, the URL ↔ panel round trip, a run's notes and pews and its banner hold, the finder and the frame rate at 1080×1920.
- **Funnel Spin-up: ring waves and the perspective view** (--- funnel-spin --- the loop family's "watch the ring of 24 spin up as
  it drops" clip; `src/lib/physics/funnelSpin.ts` holds the maths, the settings, the URL keys and the presets,
  `src/lib/physics/funnelRun.ts` the ring release the mode delegates to, `components/simulator/funnelRenderer.ts` the perspective,
  `sections/FunnelSpinSection.tsx` the controls at the end of the Vortex block, its words in the `Vortex` namespace of
  `messages/*.json`). Everything is off by default: the stagger release and the top view replay every existing run exactly
  (`tests/extras.test.ts`' fingerprint).
  - **Release** (`vxRelease`, URL `vxrl`): `stagger` – the Sound Vortex as it was – or `rings`: **Waves** (`vxWaves`, URL `vxw`,
    "6, 12, 24": any whole numbers from 1, ×2 adds a wave twice the last; a run builds at most the engine's ball ceiling,
    `MEMORY_CEILINGS.vxBalls`, the typed list kept) released every **Wave Interval** (`vxWaveInterval`, `vxwi`, 4.5 s [0–120]),
    each wave's marbles evenly spaced on the rim at the entry radius with the same (r, r′, L). A wave is one polar state, in rim
    units (the run is the same at every canvas size), stepped once a step by `stepFunnel()`, and marble i of n sits at the wave's
    angle + 2π·i/n: a perfect ring (tested to 1e-9 rad). Each marble wears the hue of its index around the ring.
  - **The physics**: L′ = −k_L·L, r″ = L²/r³ − C·r^(−p) − k_r·r′, θ′ = ±L/r² – the **Spin Friction** k_L (`vxSpinFriction`,
    `vxsf`, 0.02 [0–1]) on L and the radial drag k_r = `FS_SLIP_RATIO` · k_L on r′ separately (the Sound Vortex had one k for both).
    The pull is shaped to the release (p = 3 − 2·k_L / k_r, C = L₀²·r₀^(p−3)), so a ring released with r′ = −k_r·r₀ and the
    **First Lap** (`vxFirstLap`, `vxfl`, 5 s [0.1–120]; L₀ = 2π·r₀² / lap) rolls the matched spiral r = r₀·e^(−k_r·t),
    L = L₀·e^(−k_L·t) to the **Hole** (`vxHoleSize`, `vxh`, 25 % of the rim [1–50], the ring release's own hole) in
    ln(r₀ / r_h) / k_r – 12.5 s with the defaults – its lap rate ω = L / r² climbing (r₀ / r_h)^(2 − 1/FS_SLIP_RATIO) times (about
    11×: from the 5 s first lap to about 2.3 laps a second). Without friction the rings keep L to the bit and orbit the rim for
    ever (the run never ends: `runNeverFinishes()`).
  - **The loop**: the last ring vanishes (K − 1)·interval + the descent into the cycle; the funnel stays empty for the **Refill
    Gap** (`vxRefillGap`, `vxrg`, 1 s [0–30]); with the Sound Vortex's loop on (`vxLoop`) the seam then restores the run's first
    frame – the first wave on the rim again – so every cycle replays the first (the loop contract: `cycleSeconds()`,
    `loopSeams()`, the cycle on the engine's step grid, `funnelStepTiming()`), and Export whole loops cuts the recordings, the
    fast export and the batch render on it. Without the loop the run ends at that moment (a fixed length: the finder says so).
  - **View** (`vxView`, `vxv`): `top`, or `perspective` – the surface z(r) = −depth · 0.2 · R · (R / r − 1) (**Funnel Depth**
    `vxFunnelDepth`, `vxfd`, 1 [0–5], the picture only) seen from the **Camera Elevation** (`vxElevation`, `vxe`, 25° [0–90]; 90°
    is the top view exactly), fitted into the recorder's square (`funnelFrame()`): a translucent navy fill (#1a2a55 at 40 %),
    faint spokes and the sound rings as its mesh (flashing as a ring of marbles sinks past), the spout's dark elliptical hole, the
    pale 1.5 px rim (#9fb3e0); the marbles are drawn by the layer as glossy spheres, projected at draw time from their (r, θ)
    and depth-sorted far to near (`funnelDepthKey()`, `funnelDrawOrder()`), every one behind the front lip, which is stroked last
    (`lipDepth()`). A stagger run can be seen in perspective too (its balls at their projections). From above, the ring release's
    marbles are the layer's too (`FunnelLayer.drawTopBodies()`: the same glossy spheres at their top-view places, sized by the
    depth cue): each wears its own hue, more colours than the page's ball sprites are cached for (`SPRITE_CACHE_CAP`, 64), so the
    page's ball pass would re-create most sprites every frame (Galaxy's 360). The stagger release from above keeps the page's
    balls (faces, emoji, trails, glow).
  - **Sound** (the loop library's `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`, on the simulation clock: live, the fast export and the batch render
    alike): every lap of a ring (its first marble crossing θ = 0) a glock chime on C major pentatonic from C5, degree
    floor(speedNorm · 8) of its lap rate between the first lap's and the hole's (`chimeDegree()`), so the chimes climb and crowd as
    the ring spins up; a rolling hiss (`src/lib/audio/funnelTones.ts`, the `hiss` / `hissStop` loop kinds: the seeded noise
    through a 1.2 kHz band-pass, 4 nodes) whose level follows the marbles' total speed, 15 updates a second; the groove bed
    (pad and sub on a seeded 4-chord progression in C, a chord every bar of the Sound section's BPM, a kick on every beat after the
    first bar); a noise wash the last second before every refill and the loop voices' hard cut on the seam. The seeded choices, in
    order: the Sound Vortex's three (direction, tempo – unused by a ring run –, the first angle), then the bed's progression.
  - **HUD and presets**: the loop HUD reads "why marbles speed up in a funnel" over "falling in, a marble keeps its spin, / so
    every tighter circle is a faster lap" (`Vortex.canvasTitleFunnel`, `canvasSubtitleFunnel`) and, with the **Lap Readout**
    (`vxLapReadout`, `vxlr`), the newest ring's "lap 1.23 s" in amber (`canvasLapTime`); without the HUD the title and the readout
    sit in the square's corner. Presets: **Ring of 24** (the clip: 6, 12, 24 in perspective at 25°, a 22.5 s loop), **Galaxy** (one
    ring of 360, top view), **Coin funnel** (one marble, about a minute down a funnel with a 10 % hole) and **Doubling** (1, 2, 4 …
    256, the readout on), each with the loop, the navy page (#060914) and the loop HUD. The Publish caption editor's Loop style:
    "watch the ring of 24 spin up as it drops 🔊", the figure-skater fact and #satisfying #oddlysatisfying #simulation #physics
    #marbles.
  - **Find Simulation** – **Last ring on a bar** (`ring-on-bar`, `src/lib/simulation/funnelSpinFinder.ts`): the last ring's vanish
    within ±20 ms of a bar line at the Sound section's BPM. A ring run does not depend on the seed, so no seed is simulated: the
    wave interval is solved for the nearest bar lines (the friction kept), or the friction searched around its value (one ring, or
    no interval reaches), every candidate's descent integrated as the run integrates it; the clip is whole cycles.
  - **For tools**: the canvas mirrors `data-vortex-release`, `-view`, `-elevation`, `-waves`, `-wave-sizes`, `-cycle`, `-seams`,
    `-next-seam-ms`, `-refills`, `-last-refill-ms`, `-cycle-swallowed`, `-first-cycle-swallowed`, `-lap-chimes`, `-washes`,
    `-last-wash-ms`, `-kicks`, `-bed-chords`, `-hiss`, `-lap-sec`, `-descent`, `-lap-gain`, `-frame-scale`, `-sounds` and
    `-time-ms`; the section `data-fs-release`, `-view`, `-marbles`, `-cycle` and `-descent`.
  - **Tests** – `tests/vortex.test.ts` (the funnel spin-up blocks): a ring of N stays evenly spaced to 1e-9 rad, the lap rate rises
    as r falls, L is kept to 1e-9 without friction, the depth order at the lip (and the renderer strokes the lip after the last
    marble), the stagger / top fingerprint unchanged, the perspective placement (90° = the top view), the ring release drawn by the
    layer from above too (Galaxy, Doubling: no sprite or gradient, each body on its ball, size and colour), the Ring of 24 loop (42
    marbles swallowed in the first cycle, the refill on the seam, the next cycle replaying the first), replays at 30 / 60 / 120 fps
    (pinned), the sounds (chimes, hiss, bed, kicks, wash, cut) and their playback on `tests/fakeAudio.ts`, the run without the
    loop, the finder, the HUD's readout, the caption, the settings, URL and presets. The smoke test (section 44, block
    `funnel-spin`) runs the Ring of 24 preset for 24 s at 2× (42 marbles swallowed, the refill on the loop point), checks the
    controls, the URL round trip, the perspective's drawing, the HUD, the sounds, Galaxy's 30+ fps from above (no sprite made a
    frame), the bar-line search and a free visitor's watermark, and fast-exports two whole loops.

### Viral video bot
Feature viral-bot: the owner asked for "a bot that makes videos like project.jdm and the other accounts that go viral". The
research is [docs/virality-playbook.md](docs/virality-playbook.md); the bot is that playbook as code, in three layers around
one planner. Everything is client-side or runs on your own machine / runner – there is no server.

- **Playbook** – `src/lib/bot/playbook.ts` turns the playbook's recipe (§3) into data: the families (`escape`, `rhythm`,
  `battle`), platforms and their length buckets (short 8–15 s, standard 15–30 s – the Reels default –, long 60–90 s), the safe
  zone (`SAFE_ZONE`: clear of the bottom 20 % and the right 12 % of the 1080×1920 frame), the payoff band (80–90 % of the clip,
  accepted up to 97 % for runs that end a second after their payoff; a cut clip ends 0.5–1 s before it), the look (the Neon
  theme on black, glow, trails), the voices (piano: triangle bounces + plucked melody on a major scale; xylophone: marimba on a
  pentatonic one), the climbing public-domain melodies, the recurring cast (`SERIES_ROSTER`: Blaze, Wave, Volt, Moss, Nova,
  Tang; `MASCOT` Gerald, the ball with a face), the checklist the score reads (`CHECKLIST`, 100 points) and the **recipes**:

  | Recipe | Family | Mode | Countdown | Payoff | Where it comes from (playbook) |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `ring-escape` – “Can it escape in 24 seconds?” | escape | Classic / Portal / Accumulation | wall counter | escape | the rule-in-one-second + countdown pattern (§1, §3.2–3.3) |
  | `pip-escape` – “Gerald always escapes. Day 12.” | escape | Portal / Classic, face + name | wall counter | escape | geraldbounces' character and daily struggle |
  | `grow-fill` – “It grows every bounce…” | escape | Grow | percentage bar | fill | oddplayground's “It starts tiny and gets out of control” |
  | `clone-per-pass` – “Every escape spawns 3 more balls.” | escape | Multiply | timer | fill | the multiply / retention loop (§1) |
  | `multipliers-board` – “x2 x4 x8… until it breaks.” | escape | Multipliers board | percentage bar | fill | geraldbounces' multipliers |
  | `power-layers` – “Every hit doubles the power.” (the rule of the clip's sequence: `ViralBot.plRules`) | escape | Power Layers | the stack itself | escape | oddplayground's doubling layer breakers |
  | `pendulum-wave` – “20 pendulums. Wait for the chord.” | rhythm | Pendulum Wave | percentage bar | flip (back in line) | project.jdm's pendulum waves (music-first) |
  | `polyrhythm` – “12 rhythms. When do they all meet?” | rhythm | Polyrhythm | percentage bar | flip (all meet) | project.jdm's “Rhythm Theory” formats |
  | `drop-symphony` – “18 balls. Every peg is a note.” | rhythm | Ball Drop | timer | fill | project.jdm's sound-first drops |
  | `glass-smash` – “Gerald vs 4 floors of glass.” | rhythm | Glass Smash, face + name | timer | shatter | geraldbounces' “smash all of the glass” |
  | `string-battle` – “4 lives each. Cut a string, cost a life.” | battle | String Battle | lives (HUD) | winner | oddplayground's Web Dominion hit |
  | `territory` – “Pick a side: which colour takes the board?” | battle | Capture the Flag | timer | flip | oddplayground's pong-wars territory battles |
  | `battle-royale` – “10 squares, one survivor. Pick yours now.” | battle | Battle Royale | hit points (HUD) | winner | project.jdm's square deathmatch (1,099 comments) |
  | `square-race` – “6 squares. Which one finishes first?” | battle | Square Race | standings (HUD) | winner | project.jdm's races and recurring contestants |
  | `maze-race` – “Which one finds the exit first?” | battle | Classic with 3–4 team balls | wall counter | winner | oddplayground's maze escapes |
  | `fight-duel` – “Thor vs Loki: who wins?” | battle | Fight League (an in-division duel; HP paced to the clip) | HP bars (HUD) | winner (resolved, or a cliffhanger cut with both fighters low) | the Fight League's matchups (--- fl-overhaul --- Stage 6) |
  | `fight-upset` – “Yuumi has a 33% chance.” | battle | Fight League (an underdog pairing; only an upset is accepted) | HP bars (HUD) | winner | the simulated win chance (Stage 5) |
  | `fight-bracket` – “8 fighters. 1 champion.” | battle | Fight League 8-bracket of one division (long) | HP bars (HUD) | winner (the champion) | the shows (Stage 5) |
  | `fight-boss` – “Can Gerald beat all 6 Comics A fighters?” | battle | Fight League boss rush (standard 3 waves, long 5–6) | HP bars (HUD) | winner (the boss falls or survives) | the shows (Stage 5) |

  Territory and the maze play in the closest existing modes (Capture the Flag, a Classic ring maze); when dedicated modes
  land, point the recipe's `modes` and `settings()` at them.
- **Planner** – `src/lib/bot/planner.ts`, pure and deterministic (seeded generators keyed by the inputs; the only date is the
  one passed in). `planClip(recipe, planSeed, platform, options)` builds the settings (the mode's defaults, the look, the
  voices, 1080×1920 at 60 fps, the recipe's seeded numbers, the series label – the Top Text, or the Bottom Text in the modes
  whose own scoreboard band covers the top of the square, `TOP_HUD_MODES`: battle royale and capture the flag, where the canvas
  also starts the top captions below that band), then searches physics seeds with the
  seed finder's engine (`createEngineForSettings()` – `finderRequest.ts` rebuilds the page's physics config and mode settings
  from the settings and the page canvas's world size, so a planned seed replays exactly in the fast export) and its
  predicates (`outcomes.ts`: escapes-at for a resolved escape inside the last 20 %, never-escapes + escapes-at for a cut
  0.5–1 s before it, winner for the races; the finder's duration search for the modes that end on their own – a square race's
  payoff is its first finisher, when the mode names the winner, and a resolved race is cut at 85 % instead of at the podium; the fixed length of
  Power Layers; the analytic cycle of the Pendulum Wave and Polyrhythm; a measured fill for Grow and Multiply). It models the
  clip's real end (the run's own finish, the page's banner holds, the recorder's 0.5 s), fills the captions (the hook split into
  one-line pills that fit the safe zone, the countdown, the payoff text or the closing question; a recipe's `copyVars` fill
  words from the copy, e.g. Power Layers' `{rule}` names the sequence the clip plays by; --- low-findings --- a String Battle whose last
  two balls fell in the same step – a double KO, `RunSummary.doubleKo` in `simulateFacts()` as in the finder – names no winner:
  its payoff is the recipe's `payoffDoubleKo`, "Double KO – the last two fell together!", although the mode's own verdict still
  shares the win, `payoffKeyOf()`), the share link (with `seed=`, which
  the page reads in its first render and pins on its first run) and the post (`copy.ts`: caption, specific question, keywords, 5–10 hashtags, posting note, in the
  locale's `ViralBot` messages). Every accepted candidate is scored by `scoreClip()` (motion in the first second, hook present
  and safe, visible countdown, payoff timing, length bucket vs platform, sound, ending, series, look – each with a reason) and
  the best is kept. `planDay(date, platform, count)` rotates the leading family daily and splits every family into two halves
  that alternate by day, so no recipe runs two days in a row whatever the count; endings alternate clip by clip. The search is a
  generator (`planClipSteps`, `planDaySteps`) so the page runs it in slices.
- **Bot section** – `components/simulator/sections/BotSection.tsx` (search key `BOT_KEYS`, after the Batch block) and
  `components/simulator/useViralBot.ts`: platform, count 1–20, series, length and ending; **Plan clips** (a fresh set with these
  filters) and **Today's plan** (the day's own, the one the CLI and the job make); per clip the recipe, hook, mode, seed, length,
  ending, score and **why this clip**, **Open in simulator** (the settings through the preset loader, the melody, the seed pinned
  like a found simulation), **Re-roll** and **Copy caption**; **Render all** hands the plan to the batch renderer
  (`useBatchRender`'s `runJobs()` – a small hook: jobs with their own settings, seed, name and a `prepare()` that loads the
  melody) and downloads `jumpingballslive-bot-<date>.zip` with the videos (`<episode>-<recipe>-<seed>.mp4|webm`), a caption
  `.txt` each, `manifest.json` and `posting-schedule.md` (`output.ts`). The last plan is kept in localStorage (`store.ts`,
  `jumpingballslive_viral_bot`). Plans are timed for the canvas size they were made in: after a resize the block asks to plan again.
- **CLI** – `node scripts/viral-bot.mjs --count 5 --platform reels --out bot-output [--date YYYY-MM-DD] [--family escape]
  [--bucket standard] [--ending resolved] [--locale pl] [--dry-run] [--post | --post-only]` (also `npm run bot -- …`). Without `--family` the plan holds escape and rhythm clips only (`--family default`); `--family all` and `--family battle` are the only ways to plan Fight League clips (the legal audit).
  --- fl-overhaul --- (Stage 6) `--plan --date 2026-10-05` prints the recipe rotation of the 7 days from the date (the
  planner's `daySlots()`: no simulation, nothing written). With the
  exported site served (`BASE_URL`), it opens the simulator in headless Chromium, plans through `window.__jumpingBallsBot` in
  the page's world, renders every clip with the fast export and saves the videos, caption files, `manifest.json` and
  `posting-schedule.md`. `--dry-run` plans in Node instead (the same planner bundled from `src/lib/bot/node.ts` with esbuild)
  and writes only the text files. It never posts without `--post` (render and post) or `--post-only` (post what `--out` holds,
  after you uploaded it); both need `IG_USER_ID`, `IG_ACCESS_TOKEN` (a long-lived token with `instagram_basic` and
  `instagram_content_publish`) and `BOT_VIDEO_BASE_URL` (the public HTTPS folder Instagram downloads the MP4s from), optionally
  `IG_GRAPH_VERSION` / `IG_GRAPH_HOST` – see `.env.example`; keep them in `.env.local` or secrets, never in the repository.
  Publishing (`src/lib/bot/instagram.ts`, pure request builders and a flow with an injected `fetch`) creates a `REELS` media
  container from the video URL, polls its `status_code` until `FINISHED` and publishes it; a failure names its step, hides the
  token and prints the manual upload steps. Instagram takes MP4 (H.264 + AAC): a Chromium without H.264 + AAC encoders renders
  WebM (Playwright's Chromium on Linux has neither; Google Chrome on Linux has no AAC encoder), so after rendering the CLI
  converts every WebM clip with ffmpeg (on `PATH`, or `FFMPEG_PATH`: `-c:v libx264 -pix_fmt yuv420p -r 60 -c:a aac -b:a 192k
  -movflags +faststart`), removes the WebM and points `manifest.json` and `posting-schedule.md` at the `.mp4`; without ffmpeg
  the WebM stays, with a warning, and is never posted. --- paywall-gate --- The CLI puts `BOT_LICENSE` (your licence key –
  pricing page → Copy licence key) into the page before it loads, or, for a test-mode build, a licence it signs with the
  committed TEST key. --- free-watermark --- Without a licence the page takes (no `BOT_LICENSE` on a production build) it
  renders anyway and says that the clips carry the watermark; a `BOT_LICENSE` the page refuses stops it with a clear message.
- **Scheduled job** – `.github/workflows/bot.yml`: every day at 05:47 UTC and by hand (inputs `count`, `platform`, `post`) it
  installs (with ffmpeg, when the runner lacks it, checked for its libx264 and aac encoders), builds for the root of jumpingballslive.com,
  serves `out/`, runs the CLI (which converts the WebM clips to MP4) and uploads `bot-output` as an artifact kept 14 days.
  It posts only when run by hand with `post` ticked and the `IG_USER_ID`, `IG_ACCESS_TOKEN` and `BOT_VIDEO_BASE_URL` secrets
  plus a `BOT_UPLOAD_COMMAND` repository variable (the command that uploads `bot-output` to that public folder, e.g.
  `aws s3 sync bot-output s3://my-bucket/bot --acl public-read` with `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` / `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` secrets) exist;
  otherwise it warns and the clips wait in the artifact with their posting schedule.
- **Add a recipe** – append a `BotRecipe` to `RECIPES` in `playbook.ts`: `id`, `copyKey`, `family`, `modes`, `payoff`,
  `countdown` (`caption` or the mode's `hud`), `strategy` (how the payoff is timed), optionally `teams`, `character`,
  `musicFirst`, `endings` and `buckets` (what the mode can fill), a `settings(ctx)` generator that keeps every value on its
  `RANGES` step (use `ctx.rng`, `ctx.bucket` and `ctx.payoffTarget`), `hookVars()` and, for words that depend on the settings,
  `copyVars()` (variable → path under `ViralBot`); a mode whose own HUD covers the top of the square goes in `TOP_HUD_MODES`;
  then add its copy under
  `ViralBot.recipes.<copyKey>` in the three messages files (`name`, `series`, `hook`, `payoff`, `cliffQuestion`, `postQuestion`,
  `cliffPostQuestion`, `keywords`, `hashtags`, optionally `cliffHook`). `tests/bot.test.ts` checks every recipe against `RANGES`
  for many seeds, the copy in three languages and the safe zone; update the recipe table above and the playbook's §5.
- **Tests and checks** – `tests/bot.test.ts` (recipes vs RANGES, determinism of planClip / planDay, the square race cut
  before its first finisher, Power Layers' hook vs its sequence, the series label and the arena scoreboard, the daily rotation,
  the score item by item, the safe zone, captions and hashtags per locale, the manifest, caption files and schedule),
  `tests/instagram.test.ts` (the request builders and the publishing flow with a mocked `fetch`), `tests/botCli.test.ts` (the
  options, a real `--dry-run` in Node and the WebM → MP4 conversion with a stand-in ffmpeg); the smoke test plans three clips
  in the Bot block, opens their share links (the canvas' `data-seed` is the planned seed), reloads, opens one, keeps an arena
  game's captions below its scoreboard band, and renders a one-clip short plan into a ZIP it reads back.

### Journey
Feature gerald-journey: a Gerald-family mode in a family of its own (`MODE_CATEGORIES.journey`, the "Journey modes" heading) that strings the other modes' mechanics into one run. The mode is `src/lib/physics/modes/journey.ts`; the stages live in `src/lib/physics/journey/`, one adapter per kind around code that already exists – nothing is simulated twice.

- **Stage interface** (`journey/stage.ts`): `init(bounds, random, ballRadius)` lays a stage out inside its band of the column from its own generator (`stageRandom()`, seeded with one number of the engine's, so a resize before the run lays the same journey out again and one stage never shifts the next), `enter` / `onBallStep` / `update` / `onObstacleHit` / `onBallExit` run it, `render(painter)` describes it to a `StagePainter` – the canvas side (`components/simulator/journeyRenderer.ts`) implements the drawing, so the physics stays DOM-free. `shift()` / `rescale()` move it; `obstacles` are its engine obstacles (drawn by the canvas like every obstacle); `ownWalls` / `reach` tell the journey about a stage that brings its own side walls.
- **Adapters**: `rings.ts` sets the engine's own ring walls (`ctx.setCircularWalls()`) while it is the active stage and delegates the ring hooks to `ClassicMode` – bounces, gap passes that break a ring, the wall tones, the wall-break effects, the cinematic director and breathing walls work as in Classic; the ball drops down a chute into the core, flies under Classic's gravity with the slow-ball boost (`ballsMayRest` follows the active stage), and a medium or large stage bulges out of the column into a chamber (six obstacle walls) so up to nine rings fit (`RING_OUTER`, `MIN_RING_SPACING_RADII`; fewer on a small canvas); after `PATIENCE_SEC` the gaps widen slowly so the stage always ends. `glass.ts` builds panes with Glass Smash's `paneThickness()`, `updatePaneSegments()` and `glassPitch()` and plays them by Glass Smash's own rules, exported from `modes/glass.ts` as pure functions over a `GlassView` that `GlassMode` calls too – `touchGlassPane()` (the contact rule: a touch from above is always a landing), `hitGlassPane()` (the crack and the hop, or the shatter), `spawnGlassShards()` / `stepGlassShards()`, `mapGlassPane()` / `mapGlassShards()` for a resize – so a later change to Glass Smash's rules reaches the journey as well (`glassBits.ts` only gives each stage a Glass Smash view of its own, so `drawGlassWorld()` draws it, and moves it with the floating origin); `pegs.ts` maps `buildDropLayout()`'s rows into the band (bars tilted toward the middle, a C-major degree per row; the outermost peg of a full row sits on the wall as a half-peg and the outermost bar reaches out to it, so no corner at a wall can hold a ball) and reports the narrowest opening of the board – between two pegs, two bar tips (the tightest), or between rows – as its `maxBallRadius()`; `gates.ts` builds rows with `buildGlassGates()` / `gateSlotAt()` and stacks them through the run's `MultiplierRuntime` (a size gate only as far as `maxBallRadius()` of the stages below allows); `funnel.ts` and `bullseye.ts` are built from the obstacle layer (the bullseye's platform is two hinged bars that swing open like a trapdoor, `bullseyeScore()` scores the first landing); `home.ts` is Glass Smash's HOME, played by its `landedOnGround()`, `walkToDoor()`, `reachDoor()` (the chord) and `stepHomeCelebration()` (three confetti bursts, `CELEBRATION_MS`).
- **The mode** keeps one engine ball with `gravityScale` 0 and integrates `journeyGravity()` itself (Glass Smash's gravity scaled to `JOURNEY_GRAVITY`, the speed multiplier squared), so the audio-reactive engine gravity never touches it. Entering a stage shifts the whole world – stages, column walls, the ball and its trail, the engine's particles, shockwaves and obstacle glows (`ModeContext.shiftWorld()`, a floating origin) and the camera – so the active stage's centre is the canvas centre, where the engine centres its rings. Every transition raises the banner (`bannerStage`, `JOURNEY_BANNER_MS`) and queues `{ swoosh: true }`, which the page, the fast export and the split-screen arenas' `playArenaSound()` route to `ToneGenerator.playSwoosh()` (`src/lib/audio/swooshTone.ts`: band-passed noise sweeping up with a 180 Hz → 720 Hz sine glide, never snapped, on the beat grid, ducking the music bed). A ball that rests where no stage holds it on purpose gets a seeded hop after `STUCK_MS` (after `STUCK_SQUEEZE_AFTER` of them in one stage it is set down just below the stage at a seeded spot off the centre line, drifting, so it never lands dead-centre on the next stage's middle peg); the camera eases toward the active stage and never lets the ball out of the field. `JourneyView` (the same object every call) carries the stages, the route, the camera, the banner, the progress, the score and HOME; the canvas mirrors `data-journey-*`.
- **Settings and UI**: `journeyStages` (URL `js`) and `journeyAutoStages` (`jsa`) – `journey/sequence.ts` parses, formats and edits the list (`parseJourneyStages()`, `sanitizeJourneyStages()`, `moveJourneyStage()` …) and draws the seeded route (`generateJourneyStages()`); `resolveJourneyFields()` validates URLs and presets alike; a change restarts the run and drops a found seed. The panel block is `components/simulator/sections/JourneySection.tsx` (Mode row; its keys are pushed into `SECTION_KEYS.ball`); the stage names and canvas words live in the `Journey` namespace of `messages/*.json`. To add a stage kind: add it to `JOURNEY_STAGE_KINDS` (and `JOURNEY_TRAVEL_KINDS`), give it a height in `STAGE_HEIGHTS` and a colour in `STAGE_COLORS`, write the adapter (extend `BaseStage`), create it in `journey/stages.ts`, draw it in `journeyRenderer.ts` (a `StagePainter` method) and name it (`Journey.stage_<kind>`).
- **Tests**: `tests/journey.test.ts` covers the stage list and its edits, the seeded route, the settings / URL / presets, every adapter's layout, the run through the stages in order (banners, swooshes, the floating origin, the camera, HOME), the rings as engine walls, the gates, the glass, determinism, resizes, the finder and the swoosh; `tests/extras.test.ts` holds the fingerprint of the default run. The default route (`rings,pegs,glass,multipliers,rings-s,funnel,bullseye,home`) runs about 30 s, the default clip length. The smoke test checks the card, the panel ↔ URL round trip, a short route at 1× with its swooshes, a found 30 s seed, a 1080×1920 recording and a fast export.
### Bullseye
Feature gerald-bullseye: a Gerald-family mode without rings built on the obstacle layer like Ball Drop (the `"none"` ring layout, the side walls, pegs and bumpers are `obstacles.ts` pieces the engine resolves; `ballsMayRest` + `ballsPassThrough`), so pause, playback speed, restart, the recorder, fast export, faces, emoji and trails work unchanged; the physics extras apply to the flights.

- **Physics** – `src/lib/physics/modes/bullseye.ts`: `buildBullseyeLayout()` lays the portrait field out in the centred square the recorder crops to (the launcher at `LAUNCH_AT`, the deflector band `BAND_TOP_AT`–`BAND_BOTTOM_AT`, the target's centre line – the landing line – at the bottom, its radius `TARGET_AT` × the field width, `MOVING_TARGET_AT` when it moves, its ellipse squashed by `TARGET_TILT`) with `DEFLECTOR_ROWS` staggered rows of candidate pegs and bumpers spaced for the ball size; `chaos` keeps the share with the lowest `hash01(salt, row, column)`, so a seed picks its own field, nested as the chaos grows, and a rebuild at another size keeps the choice. `init()` draws everything from the seed up front (the salt, the moving target's phase, per shot a launch jitter and a triangular aim of ± `AIM_SPREAD` × the field width); shot k leaves at k × `interval` of the world clock, aimed ballistically (`timeToFloor()`). Scoring: `ringAt()` (the ring of a landing by its distance from the target's centre, the bull `bullHalfWidth()` wide, the rim still on the target) and `ringScore()` (10 for the bull down to about 1, `round(10 · (rings − ring) / rings)`). A ball sticks when it reaches the landing line, when it drops onto a stuck ball slower than `STICK_SPEED` (from above) or when it has come to rest on stuck balls for `REST_MS`; one perched on a peg or a bumper is nudged off sideways instead (`PERCH_KICK`, at most `MAX_PERCHES` times – the perfect shot always), a rally longer than `TIRED_MS` tires (`TIRED_DAMPING` per deflector hit) and a gravity below `MIN_GRAVITY` is topped up, so no ball sticks in mid-air; the mode resolves the flying balls against the stuck ones itself (`resolveBallCircle()` against its own circle obstacles, so a pinned ball never pushes itself) and pins the stuck engine balls (`gravityScale` 0) – on the target they ride along with the moving target (`targetOffset()`, a sine of period `TARGET_PERIOD_SEC`). Slow motion: a bullseye starts a `SLOW_MO_MS` window (`slowMoScale()`: a ramp down to `SLOW_MO_FACTOR`, a hold, a ramp back) during which the mode's world clock runs slower and every flying ball's velocity is scaled by the time scale and its gravity by its square – the same path, slower; the launch schedule, the moving target and every animation follow the world clock. The rigging hook: the perfect shot (`perfectShotIndex()`) is aimed at a free spot of the bull (`PERFECT_SPOTS`), steered with at most `STEER_ACCEL` × g sideways among the deflectors and exactly below them (`steerVx()`), flies past the stuck balls (stacking on them when the bull is full) and is held in the bull at the landing – a hard constraint. The run finishes `FINAL_HOLD_MS` after the last landing (`allLanded` raises the final banner); `bullseyeNominalRunSec()` is the summary the panel shows, and the seed moves the length (the last flight, the slow motions), which is what the finder searches.
- **Sound** – pegs return `pegPitch()` from `onObstacleHit()` (degree = the note slot across the field, a diatonic major from C4 while the Sound section is chromatic), bumpers queue the same note accented (and kick the ball sideways, `BUMPER_KICK`), a stuck ball being hit ticks (`stuckPitch()`, at most `MAX_NOTES_PER_STEP` notes a step) – ordinary hits, so melodies, instruments, hit samples, beat lock and the slicer apply. A landing queues a `"hit"` with `thud: true`, `frequency = thudPitch(score)` and a level: `src/lib/audio/thudTone.ts` (`scheduleThudTone()`: a sine body dropping from `bend` × the pitch onto it and a triangle knock above it) through `ToneGenerator.playThud()` – snapped to the scale, on the beat grid without taking a bounce's slot, ducking the music bed, the hit sample two octaves above the thud in sample mode; the page's sound loop, the fast export's `playSoundEvent()` and the split-screen arenas' `playArenaSound()` route `ev.thud` to it. A bullseye queues the race fanfare (`race: "fanfare"` on `fanfareRoot()`), which already goes the way of a bounce.
- **UI** – `components/simulator/bullseyeRenderer.ts` (`BullseyeLayer`): under the balls the bumpers' lime rings, the landing line, the target (dartboard colours `RING_COLORS`, a ring lighting up when a ball lands in it) and the launcher turning toward the next shot and recoiling; over them the score popups and the bullseye's starburst; in screen space the title, `SHOT n/total`, the running total and the big BULLSEYE!; the canvas adds the final banner (the total and the best shot) from the last landing through the hold. The canvas mirrors `data-bullseye-*` (shots, launched, landed, total, best, best shot, bullseyes, scores, notes, thuds, slow motions, time scale, rings, deflectors, moving, target x, perfect shot, all landed, finished) through `BullseyeDataset`. The panel block is `sections/BullseyeSection.tsx` (shots, interval, chaos, rings, moving target, perfect shot and a run summary); a change restarts the run and drops a found seed, the scale and root follow live. Settings `byShots` (URL `bys`), `byInterval` (`byi`), `byChaos` (`byc`), `byRings` (`byr`), `byTargetMoving` (`bym`), `byPerfect` (`byp`), validated by `resolveBullseyeFields()`; the canvas words live in the `Bullseye` namespace of `messages/*.json`.
- **Tests** – `tests/bullseye.test.ts`: settings / URL / presets and the registration, the scoring geometry and the flight maths, the layout (the recorder's square, the chaos selection), a run's launches, landings, sticking, thuds, peg notes and fanfares, deflection off and stacking on stuck balls, the moving target and the balls riding it, the slow-motion profile and the world clock, the perfect shot on every seed (a full field, a moving target), determinism, resizes, the end of the run and the finder landing 30 s, the thud's synthesis and the ToneGenerator's dispatch; `tests/extras.test.ts` holds the fingerprint of the default run. The smoke test checks the card and the preview, the URL ↔ panel round trip and the search, a short rigged run (scores, thuds, the bullseye's slow motion and fanfare, the final banner), the moving target with pause and restart, the finder at 8×, the frame rate of the default run and of a 1080×1920 recording, and a fast export. The card image is `public/modes/bullseye.webp` (`MODES=bullseye npm run previews`; the page's seed is random, so render until the frame catches a BULLSEYE!).

### Merge Ring
Feature merge-ring: a rhythm-family mode without engine rings, after the "Will the ball merge into its final form?" reels (a rainbow ring, a chute of queued balls, balls fusing tier by tier). The chute walls are `obstacles.ts` capsule segments the engine resolves (like Glass Smash's shaft walls), the rim is the mode's own circle (`ballsMayRest`, the pair pass on), so pause, playback speed, restart, the recorder, fast export, faces, emoji and trails work unchanged; the physics extras apply to the drops.

- **Physics** – `src/lib/physics/modes/mergeRing.ts`: `buildMergeRingLayout()` lays the arena out in the centred square the recorder crops to (the ring's radius `RING_AT` × the side, its centre `RING_DROP_AT` × the side below the square's centre, the chute `CHUTE_WIDTHS` tier-1 diameters wide from far above the top edge down to `openY`, where its walls meet the ring). `tierRadius()` sizes tier k at 1 + `RADIUS_STEP` · (k − 1) times the Ball Size (the ball's `radiusScale`, so a live Ball Size change keeps the tiers), `tierColor()` / `tierEmblem()` give it its colour and mark, `spawnTierFor()` draws the chute's tier (tier k weighs 2^(spawnTiers − k)). A ball leaves the chute every `interval` seconds while the top of the chute is free (`spawnBlocked()`), with a seeded jitter (`LAUNCH_JITTER`); `onBallStep()` tops the gravity up to `MIN_GRAVITY`, keeps every ball inside the rim except where the chute's opening clears it (`inChute()`: above the opening, or under it and narrow enough to pass) and rebounds it with `rebound()` of obstacles.ts (`RIM_RESTITUTION`, the wall bounciness extra, bounce math's bounciness; a hit faster than `THUD_SPEED` thuds and reports a bounce); `onPostSubStep()` resolves the balls against each other itself (`ballsPassThrough`: not the engine's elastic, equal-mass pair pass) – `resolvePairs()` (`PAIR_ITERATIONS` passes a sub-step, so a jammed pile settles) pushes every overlapping pair apart by inverse mass (mass by area, `PAIR_CORRECTION`, `PAIR_SLOP`), takes their approach speed with a soft `PAIR_RESTITUTION` and `PAIR_FRICTION` along the touch, and thuds a hard landing on the pile –, damps a ball touching the pile or the rim (`CONTACT_DAMPING`, so the pile settles), puts back a ball pushed across the rim and fuses every touching pair of one tier (`MERGE_TOUCH`, both below the opening) into the next tier – `mergeBalls()` of interactions.ts for the mass-weighted centre and the momentum, the next tier's own radius, pushed inside the rim when it would overlap it. The top tier never merges: `fuse()` marks the FINAL FORM (`finalMs`), queues the chord (`finalChord()`), confetti and an impact, and `onPostUpdate()` finishes the run `FINAL_HOLD_MS` later; a ball inside the chute above the opening for `OVERFLOW_MS` while slower than `REST_SPEED` or standing on the ball under it (a ring full to the opening, whose jammed pile may still creep) is the OVERFLOW, which finishes it at once. Everything the seed draws goes through `ctx.random()`.
- **Sound** – every merge queues the engine's `"merge"` event (the fuse) and a `"hit"` at `mergePitch()` (the pentatonic from C4, a step a tier, an octave every five), every hard rim contact a thud (`thud: true`, `thudPitch()`: the same ladder from C2, `MAX_THUDS_PER_STEP` a step) through `ToneGenerator.playThud()`, the final form an accented chord; the chute walls are silent (`onObstacleHit()` suppresses their note). Ordinary hits, so instruments, scales, melodies and the beat grid apply.
- **UI** – `components/simulator/mergeRingRenderer.ts` (`MergeRingLayer`): under the balls the rim (`RIM_SEGMENTS` arcs of `rimHue()`, turning `RAINBOW_DEG_PER_SEC`, open at the chute, the wall colour with the rainbow off, a white flash at the final form); over the balls the dark bodies with a rim and an emblem in the tier's colour (`drawEmblem()`: the ten original marks; over a face, an emoji or an image only the emblem), the final form pulsing (`pulseScale()`), and the halos of the merges (`haloAt()`); in screen space the title, `MERGES n`, the top tier's name, the tier ladder along the right side (the reached tiers lit) and the caption under the ring; the canvas adds the FINAL FORM / OVERFLOW banner. The canvas mirrors `data-merge-*` (tiers, spawned, balls, merges, top, notes, thuds, final, overflow, finished, finished ms) through `MergeRingDataset`. The panel block is `sections/MergeRingSection.tsx` (tiers, drop interval, spawn tiers, gravity, rainbow rim and a run summary); a change restarts the run and drops a found seed, the rainbow follows live. Settings `mrTiers` (URL `mrt`), `mrInterval` (`mri`), `mrSpawnTiers` (`mrs`), `mrGravity` (`mrg`), `mrRainbow` (`mrr`), validated by `resolveMergeRingFields()`; the mode's own Ball Size default is `MERGE_RING_BALL_RADIUS` (`mergeRingModeDefaults()`); the canvas words and the tier names live in the `MergeRing` namespace of `messages/*.json`.
- **Tests** – `tests/mergeRing.test.ts`: settings / URL / presets and the registration, the tier geometry (sizes, colours, emblems, the spawn draw), the layout (the ring in the recorder's square, the chute, the opening), the pitches, a run's spawn cadence, a forced same-tier pair merging into the next tier with its momentum kept (and never beyond the top tier), the final form ending with its chord and hold, the overflow ending, balls never leaving the ring or the chute, determinism, a resize mid-run, the finder building the mode and the renderer's pure helpers; `tests/extras.test.ts` holds the fingerprint of the default run. The smoke test (section 49) checks the URL ↔ panel round trip and the search box, a fast run at 8× that merges up the ladder, and the canvas data. The card image is `public/modes/mergeRing.webp` (`MODES=mergeRing npm run previews`).

### Spiral Glass
Feature spiral-glass: a rhythm-family mode without engine rings, after the "will the ball multiply?" reels (a glass spiral, balls running its channel, the glass eaten along it from the entrance in, a badge counting the laps). The spiral, the glass and the balls' motion are the mode's own (the `"none"` ring layout, `ballsMayRest` + `ballsPassThrough`, engine balls with `gravityScale` 0 whose positions the mode resolves every sub-step), so pause, playback speed, restart, the recorder, fast export, faces, emoji and trails work unchanged; the physics extras do not apply (the mode writes every ball's velocity).

- **Physics** – `src/lib/physics/modes/spiralGlass.ts`: `buildSpiralGlassLayout()` lays the spiral out in the centred square the recorder crops to (its centre `DROP_AT` × the side below the square's centre, the outermost radius `OUTER_AT` × the side, the pitch dividing it over `turns` + `TAIL_TURNS` turns, the ball radius at most `BALL_FIT` × the pitch). The polar frame: `polarOf()` / `pointOf()` (the angle clockwise from straight down), the line `linePoint(t)` (rho = pitch · t), the bands `bandOf()` / `bandInner()` / `bandOuter()` (band c between walls c and c + 1 on a ray, the walls spiralling a pitch inward per turn), the tiles `tileOf()` / `tileIndex()` (`TILES_PER_TURN` a band), the outer wall `outerBoundary()` (the ramp's in the tail sector). The channel: a ball's place is its channel coordinate q (`channelPhi()`, `channelBand()`: band c at angle phi is q = c − phi / 2π, one less a turn inward, a whole number on the entrance ray), drawn at `channelPoint()` / `channelRadius()` (the middle of the channel; the innermost band narrows to the centre, the deepest a ball fits is `channelEnd()`), from `spawnQ()` on the ramp; `channelSpeedScale()` turns px into q. The glass is eaten in channel order (`tileAlong()`, `tileFaceQ()`: band turns − 1 from the entrance, then a band inward every turn), so it stands from a single frontier on. Every sub-step `onBallStep()` runs the ball down the channel at its own speed, never past `glassFront()` (touching the frontier tile's radial face: `frontReach()`, the least q whose distance covers the reach, by bisection – a quarter turn once the ball touches the centre) nor the channel's end; `onPostSubStep()` lines the balls up front to back a diameter apart (`queueGap()`: widened until the chord, not the arc, is a diameter), writes every ball's point and its velocity (the move) back, credits the laps past the entrance ray (`crossingsBetween()`; the ramp's end is not one) and lets the front ball, when it presses on the glass (or sits at the channel's end with glass left), grind the frontier tile with every touching ball behind it (`PUSH_CAP` at most): `chip()` takes a hit point every `chipEvery` seconds of pushing (shards from the face, a note, on the last point the shatter and the wall-break event, the frontier moves on; the last tile is GLASS CLEARED). `multiply()` counts a lap (`MULTIPLY_COOLDOWN_MS` apart), queues a ball for the mouth (one per step while the mouth is free, up to `maxBalls`) and pops. `onPostUpdate()` flies the shards (a pool of `MAX_SHARDS`), finishes the run `CLEAR_HOLD_MS` after the clear or at `TIMEOUT_MS`. Everything the seed draws (the per-ball speed, the grind's phase, the shards) goes through `ctx.random()`.
- **Sound** – a chip queues a `"hit"` at `chipPitch()` (the pentatonic from C4, a step a band inward, `NOTE_COOLDOWN_MS` apart, the shatter accented), a shatter the `"gap"` event (the glass clip, `SHATTER_COOLDOWN_MS` apart) and a bounce-math break, a lap the `"multiplier"` event (the rising arpeggio with the count), the clear an accented chord (`clearChord()`). Ordinary hits, so instruments, scales, melodies and the beat grid apply.
- **UI** – `components/simulator/spiralGlassRenderer.ts` (`SpiralGlassLayer`): under the balls the tiles (sectors of the spiral band, `tileFill()`: a frost tinted after the line's hue, fading with the hit points, a white flash `TILE_FLASH_MS` after a hit) and the line (`LINE_POINTS_PER_TURN`, `lineHue()` cycling `HUE_DEG_PER_SEC`, the wall colour with the rainbow off, a white flash at the clear); over the balls the shards (`shardFill()`), the pops of the laps (`popAt()`) and the badge on the entrance ray with "x n"; in screen space the title, `TILES n/N`, the progress ring (`glassLeft()`) and the caption over the spiral; the canvas adds the GLASS CLEARED / TIME'S UP banner. The canvas mirrors `data-spiral-*` (count, balls, tiles, tiles total, hp, chips, shatters, notes, cleared, timeout, finished, finished ms) through `SpiralGlassDataset`. The panel block is `sections/SpiralGlassSection.tsx` (turns, glass strength, ball speed, max balls, chip time, rainbow line and a run summary); a change restarts the run and drops a found seed, the rainbow follows live. Settings `sgTurns` (URL `sgt`), `sgGlassHp` (`sgh`), `sgSpeed` (`sgv`), `sgMaxBalls` (`sgm`), `sgRainbow` (`sgr`), `sgChipEvery` (`sgc`), validated by `resolveSpiralGlassFields()`; the mode's own Ball Size default is `SPIRAL_GLASS_BALL_RADIUS` (`spiralGlassModeDefaults()`); the canvas words live in the `SpiralGlass` namespace of `messages/*.json`.
- **Tests** – `tests/spiralGlass.test.ts`: settings / URL / presets and the registration, the spiral geometry (the layout, the polar frame, bands, tiles, the outer wall, the mouth), the colours and pitches, the mode in the engine (the outer band bored open from the entrance, a ball pushed out of glass and its chip with shards and a note, a default run eroding the bands outside in to GLASS CLEARED with the chord, confetti and the hold, the multiply on a lap with the cap and the cooldown, a crowd never inside glass nor outside the outer wall, the timeout, determinism, a resize), the finder building the mode and the renderer's pure helpers; `tests/extras.test.ts` holds the fingerprint of the default run. The smoke test (section 50) checks the URL ↔ panel round trip and the search box, a fast run at 8× that clears a three-turn spiral, and the canvas data. The card image is `public/modes/spiralGlass.webp` (`MODES=spiralGlass npm run previews`).

### Beat Drop
Feature beat-drop: a rhythm mode without rings that owns its scene and its motion like Pendulum Wave and the Sound Vortex (the `"none"` ring layout, `ballsMayRest` + `ballsPassThrough`, one engine ball with `gravityScale` 0 whose position the mode sets from planned arcs in `onBallStep()`), so pause, playback speed, restart, faces, emoji, the recorder, the fast export and the batch render work unchanged; the physics extras, the obstacle layer and the ring walls do not apply.

- **Planner** – `src/lib/simulation/beatDropPlan.ts` (pure, no DOM): `planBeatDrop({ beats, beatIndices, period, kinds, drift, scroll, bounceHeight, anticipation, random })` turns the beat times into landings (the beats from `LEAD_IN_SEC` on, each at least `MIN_FLIGHT_SEC` after the one before – a double tap is skipped) in view units (the exported square's side is 1; x from its centre, y down from the first landing), so the plan never depends on the canvas size. The base gravity lifts a flight of one period the Bounce Height (`baseGravity()`: g = 8·h / P²); `flightGravity()` boosts it off a spring (×1.75) or a drum pad (×1.25) and caps it so the apex stays on screen (g ≤ 8·H_MAX / T², a long gap floats); `takeOffSpeed()` solves D = v·T + g·T²/2 for v, with |D| ≤ `DROP_FRACTION`·g·T²/2 so every flight leaves upward and lands descending. Sideways a seeded walk (keep the direction with p = 0.65, Drift × `DX_MAX` × 0.4…1, reflected into ±`X_SAFE`, at most `DX_SPEED_MAX`·T), a wedge sends the ball the way it faces (towards the middle near an edge, at least `WEDGE_DX_MIN`); vertically *endless* drops every landing `ENDLESS_DROP_MIN`–`MAX` lower (past the pad's body when straight below), *arena* steps inside ±`ARENA_BAND`. Each landing's obstruction: a kind from the mix (redrawn with p = `REPEAT_REDRAW` when it repeats), an entry from `allowedEntries()` (never the ball's horizontal side, not the top when it falls straight down), an arrival window of Anticipation × the incoming interval ending `settle` (≤ 50 ms) before the beat – when the landing is higher than the take-off it starts only once the ball has risen past its level –, and a leaving window: it holds while the ball is over it (`clear` = when the ball is past its edge) and is gone before the ball could fall back to its level (`back` = −2v/g), sliding away from the ball (`leaveSide`). Every landing draws `DRAWS_PER_LANDING` random numbers whatever they decide. `sampleBall()` (the exact arc at any time), `padPoseAt()` (offset, angle, alpha and the impact squash – `springDecay()` – of a pad at any time: cubic or back ease-out in, cubic ease-in out with a fade, a spinner's half turns ending flat), `ballDeformAt()` (the impact squash and the speed stretch), `cameraTargetAt()` and `beatEnergyAt()` are what the mode and the renderer evaluate. Drums by bar position: `drumForBeat()` (kick on 1 and 3, snare on 2 and 4), `isDownbeat()`, and a hat half-way through every flight of at least `MIN_HAT_INTERVAL`.
- **Easing** – `src/lib/anim/easing.ts` (shared): clamped cubic / quad / back in and out, smoothstep, `springDecay()` (a damped spring's response) and `criticallyDampedStep()` (the exact critically damped step, independent of how the time is cut).
- **Mode** – `src/lib/physics/modes/beatDrop.ts`: the settings (`BeatDropSettings`, `resolveBeatDropSettings()`, the SimulatorSettings fields and URL keys `bdk`, `bdd`, `bds`, `bdh`, `bda`, `bdsn`, `bdc`, `bdt` through `defaultBeatDropFields()` / `readBeatDropParams()` / `writeBeatDropParams()` / `resolveBeatDropFields()`, and the mode's own 14 px ball through `beatDropModeDefaults()`); **the beat source adapter** `beatDropBeatConfig()` – the one place the mode reads its grid: the beat clock's (the grid it is handed with its offset and loop, else the BPM, through `lib/simulation/beatSchedule.ts`). The page hands it the beat source in effect (feature video-beats: the song, an imported video's beats or the hand-placed markers, resolved by `useVideoBeats.ts` into the `rhythmBeat` the Beat Runner follows; the section's beat line names the source), and the headless finder requests take the markers (`markerBeatInputOf()`). `BeatDropMode` plans the run at init up to `PLAN_HORIZON_SEC` (the seed: `ctx.random()`), places the ball on the arc at every sub-step's time, measures each landing from the ball's state one sub-step before it (`maxErrorMs`), queues the landing's sounds in the step that holds its beat (`landingSounds()`: a `bdDrum` / `bdPad` accompaniment event and/or an ordinary hit for the melody note; `hatSound()` between), kicks the camera and calls `ctx.noteImpact()` on downbeats, steps the critically damped camera toward `cameraTargetAt()` (endless) or the middle (arena), and ends `END_HOLD_SEC` after the clip's last landing (`beatDropFinishSec()`, the clip = the Recording length, live). A resize only relays the view out (`layoutBeatDropField()`), so every landing stays on its beat.
- **Sound** – `src/lib/audio/beatDropTones.ts`: the kick (a sine sweeping 150 → 44 Hz with a click), the snare (high-passed noise and a triangle body), the hat and the six pad accents (a plank's knock, a block's thud, a spring's rising boing with a wobble, a wedge's metallic tick, a spinner's zip, a drum pad's tom) as data and pure scheduling functions; `beatDropVoices()` decides the levels (with a music bed only the downbeat's kick and the accents). `ToneGenerator.playBeatDrop()` plays them on the beat grid when the beat lock is on, snaps the accent to the scale, ducks the bed and never uses up a melody note or a slicer slice; the page's sound loop and the fast export's `playSoundEvent()` route `ev.bdDrum` to it.
- **UI** – `components/simulator/beatDropRenderer.ts` (`BeatDropLayer`): `beginFrame()` takes the engine's clock plus the canvas' leftover accumulator, so the ball, the pads, the camera (its state extrapolated between steps), the trail and the effects are evaluated at the frame's exact time – smooth at any playback speed; `drawBackdrop()` (the dark gradient, parallax beat lines and dust, the focus glow on the beat), `applyCamera()` (the mode's camera plus the Camera group's screen shake, `CinematicCamera.shakeNow()`), `drawWorld()` (every pad alive – drawn per kind with its squash, glow and fade – and the analytic trail), `frameBalls()` + `pushBallSquash()` (the canvas' ordinary ball pass draws the ball – faces, emoji, glow – where the plan has it, squashed and stretched), `drawEffects()` (ripple rings and particle puffs, hashed per landing), `drawOverlay()` (title, tempo, landings, the bar's beats) and the ON BEAT! banner from the clip's last landing through the hold. Nothing is allocated per frame. The canvas mirrors `data-bd-*` (landed, planned, the measured landing times and their beats, the largest error, tempo, pads alive, drums, notes, camera, finish). The panel block is `sections/BeatDropSection.tsx` (the mix chips, drift, scrolling, bounce height, fly-in time, landing sound, colours, trail, the beat it lands on and a run summary that says it cannot fail); a change of what the run is planned from (`beatDropPlanKeyOf()` / `sameBeatDropPlan()`: the mix, drift, scroll, bounce, fly-in and the beat) restarts the run with its music like the Beat Runner, sound / colours / trail / clip / scale follow live. Find Simulation resolves at once (`findBeatDropRun()`): the first seed, the run covering the target's beats. The canvas words live in the `BeatDrop` namespace of `messages/*.json`.
- **Tests** – `tests/beatDrop.test.ts`: the easing curves, the planner landing every beat within 1 ms from 50 to 220 BPM and on irregular grids (tapped, swung, gaps, double taps), flights up and down within the headroom, the safe area, endless / arena, the mix and the fixed draws, spring and drum boosts, obstructions arriving before and leaving after their beat, never from the ball's side and never through the ball, spinners flat at the beat, the squash and stretch, the camera, sounds by beat position and their queueing in the beat's step, the voices, the drum kit through a real ToneGenerator on the fake audio graph and the fast export's dispatch, a 20 s run at 120 BPM whose bounces (measured from the ball's sampled states) are on the beat, determinism, resizes, the clip's end, song grids and re-planning, the renderer's sub-frame ball, settings / URL / presets, the registration and the finder; `tests/extras.test.ts` holds the fingerprint of the default run. The smoke test checks the card and the preview image, the URL ↔ panel round trip and the search box, a 20 s run at 120 BPM landing on the beats (`data-bd-landing-times`), the frame rate at 1080×1920 scaled by the machine's load, a recording that downloads, the finder and a fast export that plays the same drums.

### Rebrand
Change `SITE_NAME` and the accent colours in `src/lib/site.ts`, the theme tokens in `src/app/globals.css`, and `public/icon.svg`
(then `node scripts/generate-icons.mjs`), and regenerate the social preview image `public/og.png` with `node scripts/generate-og.mjs`
against the new build; the 27 social cards in `public/og/` print the name and the address, so redraw them too with `npm run og:cards` (`OG_DOMAIN` sets the address they print).
The pages and messages read the name through `{siteName}`, and the downloads – clip exports, batch and bot ZIPs,
project files – are named after `SITE_SLUG`, its lower-case slug. The accent hex `#93d119` is also written out in Tailwind
arbitrary classes (`text-[#93d119]`, `bg-[#93d119]/10`…) and a few canvas colours across `src/` (globals.css maps it to the
`cyan-600` / `blue-600` / `purple-600` tokens), so a new accent replaces that hex across `src/` too.
These stay fixed on purpose, so a rebrand neither loses visitors' data nor breaks old files: the browser storage keys
(`*_STORAGE_KEY` in settings.ts, daily.ts, raceCup.ts, batch.ts, publish/store.ts, bot/store.ts), the file formats
(`PROJECT_FORMAT` / `PROJECT_EXTENSION` of project files, the bot's `MANIFEST_FORMAT`), the daily challenge's seed salts
(`jumpingballslive:daily…` in daily.ts – new salts would change every day's challenge) and internal ids nobody sees (the
`jumpingballslive:select-mode` event of the mode cards, the publish relay's `RELAY_NAME` message source).
The Windows app (`desktop/`) carries the name on its own: `productName` / `appId` in `desktop/electron-builder.config.cjs`, the
window, tray and menu titles (`desktop/src/main.ts`, `desktop/src/menu.ts`), the default output folder `Videos\<name>` (`main.ts`;
the Render queue names it through `{siteName}`) and the portable data folder (`desktop/src/paths.ts`).
--- code-obfuscation --- The licence names the product in plain text too: the banner the build prepends to every obfuscated chunk
(`BANNER` in `scripts/obfuscate.mjs` – `tests/obfuscate.test.ts` repeats it and the smoke test's code-obfuscation check its start, so
change all three together), `LICENSE` and the README's [Licence](#licence) section (the terms page reads `{siteName}`).
`SITE_DOMAIN` – the address the legal pages name the site by – is not hard-coded: it is `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` without the scheme
(`jumpingballslive.com`), so a domain set with the variable `CUSTOM_DOMAIN` (which `deploy.yml` turns into
`NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`) shows up there on the next deploy; `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_DOMAIN` overrides it. The footer shows the site name;
--- low-findings --- the videos and live simulations of a visitor without a Pro licence carry the site's watermark – its name and
`SITE_DOMAIN` (see [Free-video watermark](#free-video-watermark) and [Watermark on every free simulation](#watermark-on-every-free-simulation)) –
and the Watermark text control adds the user's own on top.

### Beats from a video
Feature video-beats: "make it understand a video and have the ball land on the beat – or let me put the beats myself – and do it automatically". One beat-source layer that every rhythm feature consumes, two new sources (a media file and hand-placed markers) and On beat for the ring modes.

- **The beat source** – `src/lib/simulation/beatSource.ts` (pure, `tests/beatSource.test.ts`). One small interface other features build on (documented in the file header): `BeatSourceGrid { kind: "bpm" | "song" | "media" | "manual", beats, downbeats, bpm, offset, energy, duration, loop }`. `bpmBeatSource()`, `analysisBeatSource()` (the detector's grid of `lib/audio/beats.ts`, for the song or the media) and `manualBeatSource()` build it; `beatClockConfigOf()` turns any grid into the `BeatClockConfig` that `beatClock.ts` and `beatSchedule.ts` already read (song time = the music bed's start offset + simulation time, wrapped when the bed loops), so "when is the next beat" has one answer everywhere. `nextGridPointSec()` / `previousGridPointSec()` / `gridErrorSec()` add subdivisions (1/4 beats, 1/8, 1/16), `beatEnergies()` + `downbeatPhase()` guess the downbeat from the loudest bar phase, `waveformPeaks()` feeds the strip. The markers are milliseconds: `serializeMarkers()` / `parseMarkers()` write them delta-encoded with runs (`250.500*63` is a steady 120 BPM grid; URL `bm`), `addMarker` / `removeMarker` / `moveMarker` / `nudgeMarkers` / `snapMarkersToOnsets` / `halveMarkers` / `doubleMarkers` / `steadyMarkers` edit them, `estimateTapTempo()` fits taps (median interval, then least squares; a skipped beat counts as two, a pause of 2.5 s starts over).
- **Who follows it** – `components/simulator/useVideoBeats.ts` resolves the picker into the page's `activeBeats` (the grid Picture Paint and the Beat Runner / Paddle Keep-Up were already reading): exactly the old song grid for **Song**, the media's or the markers' grid for **Media** / **Manual**, none for **BPM** (they follow the BPM then). A picked source without its input (no media, fewer than two markers) falls back to the song, else the BPM, and the panel says so. The beat lock follows a media or manual grid too: `ToneGenerator.setBeatSourceClock()` delays a sound to the grid's next point on the simulation clock (subdivided by the grid setting); with the BPM or a song it keeps its old BPM grid. Split-screen arenas get the page's On beat grid too, and the bot's headless finder requests carry On beat from the settings (`onBeatConfigOfSettings()`: the hand-placed markers or the BPM – a page's song is not there). **Beat Drop** follows the same `rhythmBeat` as the Beat Runner, so its obstructions land on the media's beats or the markers (its beat line says which), and the headless requests give the Beat Runner and Beat Drop the markers too (`markerBeatInputOf()`); the video background is drawn over Beat Drop's own backdrop.
- **Media import** – `src/lib/audio/mediaImport.ts`: `decodeMediaFile()` reads the bytes with `decodeAudioData` (Chromium, Firefox and Safari demux the audio of MP4 / MOV / WebM this way); a container the decoder refuses plays through a hidden media element into a `MediaElementAudioSourceNode` and a ScriptProcessor copies the samples out – at 2× with `preservesPitch = false` where the browser allows it (the track is then read back at half the context rate: fine for beats and a background bed, the top octave is lost), else in real time – with a progress bar and Cancel. Limits: files up to 100 MB (the project files' cap); a codec the browser cannot play (or DRM) fails with a message; a file without sound has no beat. `analyzeMedia()` runs the detector in idle slices, then the beat energies, the downbeat and the waveform. The decoded track becomes the **music bed** (volume, ducking, loop, start offset all apply, and the recorder and the fast export carry it); loading another track in Background music, or removing it, drops the media. The file is kept for **project files** (asset kind `beatMedia`), never in links.
- **Video background** – `components/simulator/videoBeatsRenderer.ts`: a hidden muted `<video>` kept on the song time (it plays along while the run goes, re-seeks past 0.2 s of drift, rests on the exact frame while paused) and drawn cover-fitted over the theme background at `videoBgOpacity`. The fast export seeks it before every exported frame (`FastRenderOptions.beforeFrame`); `prepareExport()` times four seeks first and leaves the layer out with a note when a seek takes longer than 150 ms (Record Video then includes it).
- **Markers editor** – `components/simulator/sections/VideoBeatsSection.tsx`: the waveform is drawn once per file into an offscreen canvas and every redraw copies the visible window (zoom and scroll) and draws the detected beats, the onsets, the markers (the downbeats amber) and the playhead. Click to add, click a marker to remove it, drag to move it; ▶ previews the file; **Tap** (or **T** while the preview or the run plays) collects taps, **Use taps** writes a steady grid; **Use detected beats**, **Snap to onsets**, ½× / 2×, ±10 ms, the downbeat (auto / 1–4) and **Clear** edit the markers, which become the **Manual** source.
- **On beat** – `src/lib/physics/onBeat.ts` (`OnBeatController`, wired into `engine.ts` in delimited `--- video-beats ---` blocks: `setOnBeat()` / `getOnBeatStats()`, a contact note at every rebound and gap pass, one call after each 60 Hz step, a reset per run). After a contact it predicts the next one by replaying the engine's own sub-step integration for that ball (gravity and its direction, wind, drag, the cruise boost) against the intact rings, picks the grid point to land on – a whole beat when one is reachable, else the grid setting's subdivision – and retimes the flight: velocity × c, the ball's gravity × c², which keeps the path and changes only the speed along it, within the **On beat range** (×1.5 … ÷1.5 by default); a secant iteration on the prediction makes c exact, the flight is re-checked every few steps (every step near the target, at once after another ball knocks it), and a fresh rebound resets the ball to its natural speed. Contacts are stamped with their step's time and aimed at the step's middle, so a timed hit lands within half a step (8 ms) of its grid point; a hop no grid point can reach within the range flies free. No position changes, no random numbers: a run is fully determined by the seed, the settings and the grid, so the page, the fast export and the finder replay it identically (`ModeSettings.onBeat`), and a found run reports the beats its hits cover (`beatCoverage()`).
