The rigged constraints act on the retimed flights like on any other. It applies to the ten ring modes (not while Picture Paint's own beat sync moves the ball) and stands aside without a tempo. The canvas mirrors the statistics onto `data-onbeat-*` (timed hits, on beat, worst error of the last 16, beats covered, free flights).
- **Settings** (`src/lib/simulation/videoBeatsSettings.ts`): `beatSource` (URL `bsrc`, default `song` – the old behaviour), `beatMarkers` (`bm`), `beatDownbeat` (`bdb`, −1 = auto), `onBeat` (`onbeat`), `onBeatRange` (`obr`, 0.1–0.8), `videoBackground` (`vbg`) and `videoBgOpacity` (`vbgo`); they carry over to another mode like the music bed and reset with the Sound section.
- **Tests** – `tests/beatSource.test.ts` (a click-track WAV decoded to a grid whose beats sit within 5 ms of the clicks, the accented downbeat, the waveform, tap tempo, the marker text round trip and editing, switching sources keeps the schedule, subdivisions and errors, the settings round trip) and `tests/onBeat.test.ts` (timed hits on the grid, an irregular hand-placed grid, determinism, the untouched default path, path-preserving retiming and release, ring modes only, the finder's engine and coverage, the beat lock on a media grid); the smoke test imports a generated click track, checks the detected BPM and the markers, turns On beat on and reads the hits' error off the canvas, and records a clip with its audio.

### Split-screen races
Feature split-screen: 2 or 4 arenas on one canvas and one recording, without touching the single-arena code paths.

- **Settings** – `src/lib/splitScreen.ts` (pure): the fields `arenaCount` (1 | 2 | 4, URL `ac`), `arenaLayout` (row | grid, `al`), `soundArena` (first | all, `sa`) and `arenas` (per-arena `{ label, seed?, gravity?, ballSpeed?, ballColor?, mode? }`, the compact URL key `ar` – arenas split by `|`, fields by `~`: `s` seed, `g` gravity, `v` ball speed, `c` colour, `m` mode) with defaults, `SPLIT_SCREEN_RANGES`, validation (`resolveSplitScreenFields()`, URLs, presets and project files alike) and `splitScreenCarryOver()` for a mode change; the override merge (`mergeArenaSettings()`: the shared settings with an arena's gravity, speed, colour and – not for the first arena, which always plays the Mode picker's mode – its mode on top; `resolvedArenas()` fills labels and missing arenas; `patchArena()` / `withArenaSeeds()` edit them); the viewport maths (`arenaViewports()`: the arenas tile the centred square the recorder crops, in reading order; each arena's engine simulates a world of its viewport's shape whose shorter side is that square's side – a single arena's world – drawn scaled by `scale`); `splitRestartKey()` (a new count, seed or mode restarts every arena; gravity, speed and colour follow live) and the race (`raceStandings()`: each arena timed by its first escape, else its finish, on its own simulation clock; ties on the same step).
- **Engines** – `src/lib/simulation/multi.ts` (`MultiArenaRunner`): the first arena is the page's own engine (Simulator.tsx lays the first arena's gravity / speed / colour / seed over the settings); the runner keeps one more `PhysicsEngine` per arena, set up through the page's hooks exactly like the page's engine and the fast export's (`arenaPhysicsConfig()` + `initEngineForMode()`), follows every settings change (from scratch before the run starts, only what changed during it), notices the page's engine restarting (its clock going back or another mode) and restarts the others on the same frame, shares the particle budget out (`engine.setParticleBudget()`, `arenaParticleBudget()`), drains the other arenas' sound queues every frame (played through `playArenaSound()` like the page plays its own – bounces at `EXTRA_ARENA_LEVEL`, `melody: false` accompaniment kept – only with "every arena"; their wall breaks are counted either way for the faces, `takeWallBreaks()`), marks each arena's first escape (`engine.getFirstEscapeMs()`) and finish (`engine.getFinishedAtMs()`: the step the run finished on, which the engine records in `update()` – exactly the finder's run length, however many steps an 8× frame runs) and holds the finished race `SPLIT_FINISH_HOLD_MS` before the end screen. The page's hooks (`ArenaHooks` in Simulator.tsx) let the other arenas play a hand-played Beat Runner / Paddle Keep-Up by themselves (the player's keys and pointer drive the first arena). `findArenaSeeds()` wraps the finder: the first arena is searched as always, then every other arena with its overrides and world (`arenaFinderRequest()`, progress tagged with the arena searched – `data-search-arena` on the progress bar); the seeds go into `arenas[i].seed`, the clip lasts the longest run. A search aborted at any point – Cancel, a mode change, a preset load – is never found, even when the first arena's search already was, and `runFinder` applies nothing of an aborted search (its settings may be stale).
- **Canvas** – `src/components/simulator/splitScreenCanvas.tsx` wraps the canvas component (`withSplitScreen()`, around `withFastRender()` in `Canvas.tsx`): with one arena it renders the canvas as before; in a race every arena is drawn by its own hidden instance of the canvas – the unchanged single-arena draw routine, with every prop the page gives the canvas – in the fast export's offline mode (a driver with the arena's world, its viewport's scale in device px and a clock from its first frame), and one animation loop composes them into the visible canvas (`drawImage()` into each viewport) with borders, the arena labels (time and medal once an arena is done), the race banner, the watermark, the Top / Bottom Text and the captions (on the first arena's run); the Grand Prix cup table is left to the first arena, whose race the page scores. It implements the canvas handle (recording, end-screen holds, background of the export's letterbox) and mirrors `data-split-*` for the smoke test.
- **Panel** – `src/components/simulator/sections/ArenasSection.tsx` (the "Arenas & Split Screen" section: count, layout, sound, one editor per arena); messages under `Controls.split*` and the `SplitScreen` namespace (the banner).
- **Tests** – `tests/splitScreen.test.ts` (viewport maths, the override merge, URL / presets, the standings, the runner with real engines – an arena replays the finder's run of its seed exactly and its finish mark is the finder's run length at 8× –, the sounds and wall breaks of the other arenas, and the finder wrapper); the smoke test races four arenas, records a race, finds seeds for two and cancels a search – or changes the mode – while the second arena is searched.

### Daily challenge and preset gallery
Feature daily-gallery: a challenge a day and a page of curated presets, both built on the share links the simulator already reads.

- **Daily challenge** – `src/lib/daily.ts` (pure, `tests/daily.test.ts`): `dailyChallenge(date)` → `{ date, number, seed, mode, query }`. The
  seed is `1 + hash32("jumpingballslive:daily:<date>") mod (2³¹ − 2)`; the mode comes from `DAILY_POOL` (modes whose run ends by itself and depends
  on the seed, each with a curated look as link parameters), dealt in blocks of `DAILY_POOL.length` days from `DAILY_EPOCH` – every block a seeded
  shuffle, a block that would start with the previous block's last mode swaps its first two days. **Shared `daily=` links depend on this
  derivation**: appending a mode to the pool reshuffles the blocks from the one it lands in, and changing the hash, the epoch or a look changes
  every challenge (the test freezes a few days). `dailyFromParam()` reads the link parameter (`1` = today, a date from #1 to tomorrow), history
  and streak helpers keep the first result of each day in localStorage (`jumpingballslive_daily`).
- **Simulator** – `src/components/simulator/useDailyChallenge.ts`: a `daily=` link's settings are the page's first settings (Simulator.tsx's
  initial state) and its seed is pinned once the engine is up (`pinSeed`, after the page's own seed effects); the button loads the settings
  through the preset loader, waits for the commit (whose effects drop any pinned seed) and pins the seed. The challenge counts as on while the
  engine still has its seed pinned in its mode (`engine.getPinnedSeed()`) – every physics change unpins a seed, so tweaking the physics ends it
  and a new colour does not; while on, the address carries `daily=<date>` on top of the mirrored settings. `DailyChallengeUi.tsx` holds the bar
  under the canvas and the end-of-run panel (outside the canvas drawing, so recordings never show it); `formatRunSeconds()` of the engine's
  finish step is the result.
- **Landing card** – `src/components/site/DailyChallengeCard.tsx`, below the hero; it reads the visitor's clock after mount (the page is static).
- **Gallery** – `src/content/gallery.ts` (`tests/gallery.test.ts` checks every query is canonical, has a seed, a preview image and copy in every
  language) and `src/app/[locale]/gallery/page.tsx` (cards with `data-gallery-*` attributes, an ItemList JSON-LD). To add a preset: build it in
  the simulator, copy the address bar's query, add `seed=` (the canvas' `data-seed` or a found simulation), append an entry with names and
  descriptions in every language, build, serve and run `GALLERY=<id> npm run previews` – the script reads the cards from the served
  `/en/gallery/` page and renders each card's query at its `previewAt` second.
- **Copy** – the `Daily` and `Gallery` namespaces in `messages/*.json` (the navbar and footer links use `Gallery.navLabel`); the smoke test opens
  the gallery, Try it, the landing card, `daily=` links, the button, a finished daily run and its share link.
### Changelog
Feature changelog: what changed, newest first, on a static page in every language, with a generated `CHANGELOG.md`, release notes for the Windows app's GitHub releases and a "What's new" notice inside the app.

- **Data** – `src/content/changelog.json` (typed by `src/content/changelog.ts`, anchors by `src/content/changelogId.ts`): `{ "releases": [ { "id", "product", "version", "date", "headline", "items" } ] }`, newest first. `product` is `site` (the website; its version is the root `package.json` one) or `app` (the Windows app; `desktop/package.json`'s, `DESKTOP_VERSION`); `id` is the product and the version with dashes (`site-1-10-0`, `app-1-3-0`), which is also the page anchor; `date` is `YYYY-MM-DD`; `headline` (at most 60 characters) says what the release is about; an item is `{ "kind": "new" | "improved" | "fixed", "text" }`. A headline and a text are `{ "en", "pl", "es" }`, plain text (the only placeholder is `{siteName}`), an item 16 to 220 characters, one to three sentences, ending in `.`, `!` or `?`. It is JSON so a Node script can read it too. `tests/changelog.test.ts` checks the shape, the ids, the order (dates never rise, each product's versions always fall), that the newest app release is `DESKTOP_VERSION` and the newest website release the `package.json` version, every language, the site-name, domain and mode-count rules and that `CHANGELOG.md` is up to date; `tests/copyStyle.test.ts` holds the text to `docs/COPY-STYLE.md`.
- **Page** – `src/app/[locale]/changelog/page.tsx` (in the sitemap and the footer): a line with the newest website and Windows app versions, then a section per release, newest first, every release and item on the page with no script. A section has the product and version as its heading ("Website 1.10.0", "Windows app 1.3.0"), the headline, the date as a `<time>`, the items tagged New, Improved or Fixed, and the anchor (`#app-1-3-0`) that the app and the release notes link to. The filter chips are radio buttons, so they work with scripts off. A CollectionPage JSON-LD lists the releases. Inside the Windows app `ChangelogYourVersion.tsx` names the running version and links its release (the `ChangelogLive` namespace, `PAGE_CLIENT_NAMESPACES["/changelog"]`); the page hands it the version numbers only, never the data.
- **CHANGELOG.md and release notes** – `node scripts/changelog.mjs --write` (`npm run changelog`) writes `CHANGELOG.md` from the JSON, in English (`## Website 1.10.0 · 2026-10-14`, the headline, then New, Improved and Fixed lists); `--check` (`npm run changelog:check`, run by the test) exits 1 when the file differs; `--notes <site|app> <version>` prints a release's notes as Markdown for a release body. `scripts/release/publish-downloads.mjs` makes the body of the public Windows release from the app's notes followed by the download and checksum lines (a version without an entry keeps the old one-line text), and `.github/workflows/desktop.yml` uses the same notes for the GitHub release in this repository. The notes name no repository.
- **What's new in the app** – `src/components/simulator/desktop/WhatsNew.tsx`, mounted in `DesktopSection.tsx`: when the version `window.desktop.info()` reports is higher than the last one it saw (`localStorage` key `jumpingballslive_whatsnew`, `src/lib/desktop/whatsNew.ts`), a small notice says "What's new in x.y.z" once, with a link to the bundled `/changelog` page at that release and a Dismiss button. A run with no stored version (a fresh install, or an update from a version that had no notice, such as 1.2.0 to 1.3.0) only stores the version, so the notice first shows at the update after 1.3.0. Nothing shows on the website.
- **To release** – add the entry in the same commit as the version bump: a website release is an entry at the top of `releases`, `npm version <x.y.z> --no-git-tag-version` and `npm run changelog`; a Windows app release is its entry, then `npm run release:desktop -- <x.y.z>` (the app carries the site, so the app's own changelog page must list its version). Then `npx vitest run tests/changelog.test.ts tests/copyStyle.test.ts`. The steps for the owner: `docs/RELEASING.md`.
- **Copy** – the `Changelog`, `ChangelogLive` and `WhatsNew` namespaces in `messages/*.json`; the smoke test's `changelog` block opens the page in every language, the version line, the anchors, the filter without scripts, a phone, the footer link, the sitemap and, with a stand-in `window.desktop`, the app's note and its "What's new" notice.

### Build identity
Which build is this? The website, the host and the Windows app answer with the same three facts: the **release version** (the root `package.json` `version`, which equals the newest `site` entry of the changelog), the **commit** (shown as 7 characters, 40 in the details) and the **commit date** (UTC). Compact id `1.10.0+8fa8dbb`, label `v1.10.0 · 8fa8dbb · 2026-10-14`.

- **Collector** – `scripts/lib/build-info.cjs` (plain CommonJS: `next.config.ts` is compiled to CommonJS, `postexport.mjs` and the tests import it). `resolveBuildInfo()` takes the commit from `JBL_BUILD_SHA` (40 lowercase hex), else `git rev-parse HEAD`, else `GITHUB_SHA`, else none; the date from `JBL_BUILD_DATE`, else `git log -1 --format=%cI <commit>`, else `SITEMAP_LASTMOD` (an ISO date), always as UTC; the version from `package.json`. The VPS and Actions check out one commit, detached, so there are no tags, branch names or commit counts to read, and nothing is: the deployers need no change (an installed `jbl-deploy.sh` of any age works) because the build runs this repository's own `next.config.ts` and `postexport.mjs`. A missing git, a timeout or a non-repository never fails a build: the label then reads `v1.10.0 · dev` and no `X-Build` rule is written. The build time is never used as a commit date, and no commit subject, branch or environment value is ever read or written.
- **In the pages** – `next.config.ts` passes `env: nextEnv(resolveBuildInfo())`, which inlines `NEXT_PUBLIC_BUILD_VERSION`, `_COMMIT` and `_COMMIT_DATE`; `src/lib/buildInfo.ts` reads them (literal `process.env` reads, with `0.0.0-dev` fallbacks for Vitest) and formats the label and id exactly like the collector. The footer (`Footer.tsx`) shows the label in its own element (`data-testid="build-stamp"`, next to but not inside the © line, a link to `/changelog` whose tooltip names the full commit and date); when `NEXT_PUBLIC_LEGAL_NAME` is set a separate "Operated by …" line joins it. `layout.tsx` adds `<meta name="jbl-build" content="1.10.0+8fa8dbb">` to every localised page. `generateBuildId` is deliberately left alone: Next re-rolls its random build id until it has no "ad" in it (ad blockers match those URLs), but not an id we supply, and a hash of 12 hex characters has one in about 4 cases in 100.
- **On the host** – `scripts/postexport.mjs` writes `out/version.json` last: `schema`, `name`, `siteVersion`, `build`, `commit`, `commitShort`, `commitDate`, `builtAt`, `channel` (`production` for a known commit and a public address, else `development`), `desktopVersion` (from `desktop/package.json`), `siteUrl`, `changelogUrl`. `curl -s https://jumpingballslive.com/version.json` is what is live (the live host answers its files with `max-age=0, must-revalidate`, so it is not served stale). It also adds `X-Build: <id>` to `out/_headers` (Cloudflare's static assets read that file, the existing rules of `public/_headers` stay), so `curl -sI https://jumpingballslive.com/en/ | grep -i x-build` works; the first deploy shows whether the host applies the rule. `/build.txt` (the full commit, written by the deployers) is unchanged.
- **The stamp is the build the page was loaded from.** It is part of the HTML and the scripts, so it also shows offline and in view-source; a tab left open keeps its stamp until it is reloaded (nothing reloads a page by itself), while `/version.json` is what is live now. The service worker's `version` in `sw.js` is a content hash of the export, not a release: do not call it the version. Nothing reads `/version.json` at run time.
- **Why the files come last** – `buildPwa()` precaches every file in `out/` that it does not know to skip. A `_headers` file is never served (the host reads it), so one present at that moment would be precached, the worker's install fetch would get a 404 and the install would fail for every visitor. `version.json` holds a build time. Both are written after the worker and `scripts/pwa/build.mjs` also lists `version.json`, `build.txt`, `_headers` and `_redirects` as not precached (a second guard).
- **Windows app** – the title bar reads `JumpingBallsLive 1.3.0` (`· portable`, `· safe mode`; the page's own `<title>` no longer replaces it); Help → About lists the app version and install type, the website build inside the app (read from the bundled `version.json`), Electron, how updates work (a portable EXE does not update itself: new versions come from the Download page), the data folder and the log, with **Copy details** (English text, the Windows user name hidden), **What's new** (the changelog page of the public site in the menu's language) and Close; Help → What's new does the same; the Desktop group's GPU tab (App card) shows the version, the website build, the install type and the same two actions; the log's first line, the tray tooltip and the diagnostics report carry the version too, and the `--smoke` result has `appVersion`, `windowTitle` and `siteBuild` (a packaged run fails when the bundled site has no readable `version.json`). The pure parts are `desktop/src/about.ts` and `src/lib/desktop/aboutText.ts`.
- **Release number** – the website's is the root `package.json` `version`: bump it with the changelog entry in the same commit (`npm version <x.y.z> --no-git-tag-version`, which also changes the lock file's two root fields). The Windows app keeps its own track (`npm run release:desktop`).
- **Tests** – `tests/buildInfo.test.ts` (the collector's order of sources and fallbacks, the client twin, `version.json` and `_headers`, the worker unharmed, `next.config.ts`, the footer render, package.json and the lock), `desktop/tests/about.test.ts` (title, About text, version.json reader, smoke identity, the menu command); the smoke test's `pwa` block checks `/version.json`, the footer stamp, the `jbl-build` meta tag and that the precache lists none of the identity files, its `desktop-exe` block the App card's rows and Copy details.

### Search and social sharing

What a search engine or a link preview reads is built in one place and held to its limits by a test (feature seo).

- **One helper for every head** – `src/lib/seo.ts`: `pageMetadata({ locale, path, title, description, social?, noindex? })` returns the title, the description, the canonical address with the hreflang set (en, pl, es, x-default), a complete Open Graph card (`og:url` = the canonical address, site name, type, `og:locale` with the other two as alternates, the picture with its size, type and alt text) and the Twitter card (`summary_large_image`, the same picture and alt). Next merges metadata one level deep, so a page that sets only part of `openGraph` replaces the layout's whole object and published the home page's address (or no picture) before: every page's `generateMetadata` and the locale layout (the home page) now go through the helper, and a new page needs one line: `return pageMetadata({ locale, path: "/name", title, description })`. A page can give a shorter `social` title and line (About does), and `noindex: true` keeps the feedback form and the account page out of search results.
- **Social cards** – a page's picture is `public/og/<locale>/<page>.png`, 1200×630, for home, simulator, gallery, download, pricing, about, tiktok-ball-videos, changelog and press in every language: 27 files, each under 150 KB. `scripts/generate-og-cards.mjs` (`npm run og:cards`) draws them with Playwright's Chromium and needs no server: the brand mark of `public/icon.svg`, the site's own type (`src/fonts`, with latin-ext for Polish and Spanish) and the headline and line from `Seo.card.<page>.title` and `.sub` in `messages/*.json` (at most 42 and 80 characters, `{siteName}` filled in). The cards show no number of modes and no version: a committed picture would go stale. A page that has no card (the legal pages, the feedback form, the account page) and a missing card show `public/og.png`, the simulator screenshot of `scripts/generate-og.mjs`. To add a card page, add it to `OG_CARD_PAGES` and `OG_CARD_PATHS` in `src/lib/seo.ts`, to `PAGES` in the script and a `Seo.card.<page>` to every catalog, run `npm run og:cards` and commit the PNGs; `tests/seo.test.ts` fails until all four agree.
- **Titles and descriptions** – a page title is at most 60 characters rendered with " – {siteName}" (about where a search result cuts it off), a description 70 to 160 characters rendered (`tests/copyStyle.test.ts` checks every `metaTitle` and `metaDescription`, `tests/seo.test.ts` the rendered head of every page in every language, and that no two pages share a title or a description). The pricing description says "card or crypto" and names no payment provider that is switched off.
- **Sitemap** – `src/app/sitemap.ts` lists every indexable page in every language with the hreflang set, the changelog and the press page included. `lastmod` is the date of the thing itself where there is one (the changelog: its newest release; the download page: the newest Windows app release; the legal pages: `LEGAL_LAST_UPDATED`) and the deploy date (`SITEMAP_LASTMOD`) for the rest. `changefreq` and `priority` are left out: Google ignores them.
- **Structured data** – JSON-LD builders in `src/lib/seo.ts`, no ratings or reviews (there is no real data for them): Organization (name, url, logo, and `contactPoint` and `legalName` only when `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPPORT_EMAIL` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_LEGAL_NAME` are set; no `sameAs` until the social profiles exist), WebSite (the language's entry page, `inLanguage`), WebApplication (with `inLanguage` and the card as `image`), SoftwareApplication on the download page (with `releaseNotes` = the changelog and `dateModified` = the newest app release), Product with the two Pro offers, ItemList of the gallery and a BreadcrumbList on every inner page. `tests/seo.test.ts` parses each block for each language and checks its required keys.
- **Icons** – `scripts/generate-icons.mjs` also writes `public/icons/icon-48.png` and `public/favicon.ico` (a 32 and a 48 px PNG image in one ICO file); the locale layout links the `.ico`, the SVG, the 48 and the 192 px PNG with `type` and `sizes`, besides the apple-touch-icon.
- **Service worker** – `og/` is kept out of the precache (`NOT_PRECACHED_PREFIXES` in `scripts/pwa/build.mjs`): a card is for crawlers, not for the offline app.
- **After a deploy** – Search Console, Bing Webmaster Tools and the two sharing debuggers are owner steps: [docs/GO-LIVE.md](docs/GO-LIVE.md), section 14.1.

### Installable offline app (PWA)
Feature pwa: a web app manifest, icons and a hand-written service worker (no library), all static.

- **Manifest** – `src/app/manifest.ts` renders `out/manifest.webmanifest` from `buildWebAppManifest()` in `src/lib/pwa.ts`: name (`SITE_NAME`), the English description, `start_url` `<base>/en/`, `scope` `<base>/`, `display: standalone`, the theme and background colour (`PWA_THEME_COLOR`, `PWA_BACKGROUND_COLOR` – the page background) and the icons: `public/icon.svg` plus the PNGs in `public/icons/` (192, 512, a 512 maskable one with the artwork inside the safe zone and a 180 apple-touch-icon), rendered from `icon.svg` by `node scripts/generate-icons.mjs` with Playwright's Chromium – run it again after changing the icon and commit the PNGs. Both root layouts link the manifest, the apple-touch-icon, the iOS web-app title and `theme-color` through their `metadata` / `viewport`.
- **Service worker** – `scripts/pwa/sw.template.js` is plain JavaScript; `scripts/postexport.mjs` calls `buildPwa()` (`scripts/pwa/build.mjs`), which writes `out/offline.html` (a self-contained page: inline styles and icon, the `Pwa` strings of every `messages/*.json`, in the language of the URL it answers for) and `out/sw.js` = the template + its configuration: the **precache list** (the root, the offline page, the landing and simulator pages of every locale with their RSC payloads, every `/_next/static` file and every public asset except the names in `NOT_PRECACHED` (`og.png`, `robots.txt`, `sitemap.xml`, `404.html`, `sw.js`, `version.json`, `build.txt`, `_headers`, `_redirects`), the folders in `NOT_PRECACHED_PREFIXES` (`og/`, `press/`) and files over 4 MB; `selectPrecache()`), which `splitPrecache()` divides into the **critical** files and the **deferred** rest (see Install diet below), the **version** (a hash of every exported file and the template, `hashExport()`) and the cache prefix (the `package.json` name). Cache names are `<prefix>:<scope path>:<version>`, so sites sharing a `github.io` origin never touch each other's caches.
- **Strategies** – navigations, `*.html`, the RSC payloads (`*.txt`), the manifest and other data: network first (the answer – also a 404 – is returned and, when OK, cached; the cache answers only when the network fails, then `offline.html` for a page never cached; navigation preload on); `/_next/static` and the public assets: cache first. Cross-origin requests, non-GET requests, byte ranges and `sw.js` itself are left to the network. Cache keys drop the query (`?mode=…&seed=…` share links open the cached simulator offline) and percent-encode every path segment one way (`[locale]` = `%5Blocale%5D`).
- **Updates** – install fetches the critical files past the HTTP cache (`cache: "reload"`; content-hashed `/_next/static` files are copied from the previous version's cache or fetched through the browser's own cache, which holds what the page just loaded), carries the previous version's deferred hashed files over, fails as a whole if a critical file is missing (the next page load retries) and calls `skipWaiting()`; activate deletes this scope's caches of other versions and `clients.claim()`s the open pages. `src/components/site/PwaRegister.tsx` (rendered in both root layouts) registers `<base>/sw.js` with scope `<base>/` and `updateViaCache: "none"` after the page has loaded – only in production builds and only over HTTPS or on localhost (`canRegisterServiceWorker()`); in development it unregisters a worker a production build left on the same scope. A tab that stays open checks for a new version when it becomes visible again (at most every 30 minutes); nothing ever reloads the page by itself, so a running simulation or recording is never interrupted.
- **Build identity** – `scripts/postexport.mjs` writes `out/version.json` and the `X-Build` rule in `out/_headers` after `buildPwa()`, so the worker neither hashes nor precaches them (a `_headers` file is never served, so precaching it would fail the install for everybody); `NOT_PRECACHED` also names `version.json`, `build.txt`, `_headers` and `_redirects` as a second guard. The footer's stamp is the build the page was loaded from, embedded in the page (it shows offline too, and does not make the worker change more often: every build is already a new worker, because Next's random build id is in every page); `/version.json` is network first, so online it is what is live. The `version` in `sw.js` is a hash of the export, not the release. See [Build identity](#build-identity).
- **Install button** – `src/components/site/InstallAppButton.tsx` in the footer keeps Chrome's `beforeinstallprompt` (`installPrompt.ts`) and shows **Install app** while the browser offers the install. Strings: the `Pwa` namespace of `messages/*.json`.
- **Install diet** (precache-split) – a first visitor on a phone used to download every file of the app (about 4.5 MB on the wire, 9 MB unpacked) right after the page loaded, competing with the page and with the studio they were about to open, on a connection they may pay for. Now `splitPrecache()` (`scripts/pwa/build.mjs`) divides the list: the **critical** files – the root, the offline page, the manifest, the icons, the stylesheets, the fonts and the landing page of every locale, 22 files, about 0.8 MB unpacked – are what the worker's install downloads; the **deferred** rest (the studio pages and chunks, the mode and gallery pictures, the sounds and melodies, 136 files) is fetched when the page posts `{ type: "precache-rest" }` to the worker. `PwaRegister.tsx` posts it `PRECACHE_REST_DELAY_MS` (8 s) after the worker is ready – and again when a newer worker takes over – and only if `shouldPrecacheRest()` (`src/lib/pwa.ts`) says so: not on Save-Data, not on a cellular link, not on a link that measures as 3g or slower; a browser without the Network Information API (Safari, Firefox) cannot be told from a metered phone, so there only a returning visitor (the worker already controlled the page when it loaded) gets the full precache. The worker answers by fetching what is not in its cache yet, two at a time at low priority, in one run however many tabs ask; a file that fails is skipped (the next request tries again) and the run ends when a newer worker has deleted the version's cache. What is not precached is cached the first time it is used (cache first for assets, network first for pages, as before), so on a metered connection the app still works offline for what the visitor opened, until the next deploy: a new worker carries over only the content-hashed files, and the pages and pictures come back when they are opened again or when the page asks for the full copy. Content-hashed `/_next/static` files are fetched through the browser's HTTP cache (`public/_headers` makes them `immutable`, so what the page just loaded costs nothing) and carried over from the previous version's cache, so a deploy downloads only what changed. `buildPwa()` stops the build when the critical files outgrow `MAX_CRITICAL_PRECACHE_BYTES` (1.5 MB unpacked). Counted at the server (brotli, an idle first visit to `/en/` in a phone-sized Chromium), the browser downloaded 8.65 MB in the first 5 seconds before (3.15 MB of it the social cards under `og/`, which the old list would have swallowed, and 5.5 MB the app), now 1.34 MB flat on a metered connection (the page itself is about 0.74 MB) and 4.85 MB in all after 10 seconds on an unmetered one.
- **Offline page behind a redirect** (offline-redirect) – Cloudflare's Workers assets answer `/offline.html` with a 307 to `/offline`, so the response the worker cached carried the "redirected" flag, and a browser refuses to answer a navigation with such a response: a never-visited page offline showed Chrome's "This site can't be reached" instead of the offline page. The worker now rebuilds every response without the flag when it stores it and when it serves it from the cache (`clean()` in `sw.template.js`; the smoke test's proxy makes the same 307 and checks the offline page appears).
- `localeAlternates()` lives in `src/i18n/alternates.ts`: a layout may only export Next's own fields, and the build type-checks the layout whenever webpack keeps it in a page's chunk.
- **Tests** – `tests/pwa.test.ts` (the manifest, the registration rules, the precache list, the version hash, the offline page, `buildPwa()` on a fake export, and the worker itself in a fake worker scope: the critical install, the deferred files on request (once, in one run, skipping what is cached, what fails and what a newer worker made obsolete), the caches of other versions and scopes, network first / cache first, the offline fallbacks, the offline page behind a redirect, a failed install and a new deploy replacing the old cache and carrying its hashed files over; `shouldPrecacheRest()` and the build's critical budget are tested there too); the smoke test checks the served files and head tags, the registration and the precache, then, through a local proxy that can drop every connection, runs the simulator offline, opens the offline page in Polish and simulates a new deploy (every page marked, a new `sw.js` version): the page comes from the network, the new worker replaces the old cache and serves the new pages offline.
### Speed of a cold mobile visit (perf-pass)
A visitor who clicks an ad on a phone loads one page on a slow link and decides within seconds. The pass below made that page lighter without touching the studio; numbers are from a built export served with brotli, in a 390 px phone profile (4x CPU, 1.6 Mbit/s, 150 ms, no cache, no service worker), two runs each.

- **Links never prefetch** (link-no-prefetch) – `src/i18n/navigation.ts` exports next-intl's `Link` with `prefetch={false}` unless a link passes `prefetch` itself. Next's default prefetched the whole studio (an RSC payload, the studio's page chunk and more) as soon as any link to `/simulator` scrolled into view, so every page, the pricing and download pages included, downloaded about 1 MB it never used. A click still opens the page, one request later. `/en/pricing/` 1,343 to 268 KB, main-thread blocking time (long tasks after first paint) 906–1,075 to 167–244 ms; `/en/` 1,391 to 739 KB; no `_rsc` request fires on any of them. `tests/perfGuards.test.ts` keeps the default and fails when a file imports `next/link` or mentions `prefetch`.
- **The landing page's card no longer loads the engine** (daily-core) – `src/lib/dailyCore.ts` holds the daily challenge without the simulator's settings (the rules, the pool, the dates, the streak); `src/lib/daily.ts` re-exports it and adds the settings query (`poolEntryQuery`, `DailyChallenge.query`, `dailySettings`), so every existing import works. `DailyChallengeCard.tsx` imports the core, which removed the 1.16 MB (unpacked) chunk with every mode's settings, the physics engine and the seed finder from the first scripts of `/en/`, `/pl/`, `/es/`, `/en/download/`, `/en/account/` and `/en/feedback/` (initial scripts of `/en/` in a readable build: 1,726 to 579 KB unpacked, 518 to 172 KB brotli, the largest two 169 KB framework chunks; in the obfuscated production build 953 KB unpacked, 248 KB brotli, the largest 330 KB). On top of the first fix, `/en/download/` went from 603 to 257 KB, blocking time from 410–423 to 137–159 ms and the load event from 3.6 s to 1.7 s. The hero's live preview still imports the engine after the page is interactive (a dynamic import), so `/en/` loads it a moment later, as before. `tests/perfGuards.test.ts` walks the static imports of every client component under `components/site/` and fails if one reaches `lib/settings`, a physics mode, the engine or the finder.
- **Headers** (static-headers) – `public/_headers` is read by Cloudflare Workers static assets (it is never served: `GET /_headers` is a 404): `/_next/static/*` is `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (content-hashed, so a repeat visit makes no request for it), the fixed-name pictures and sounds (`/modes`, `/gallery`, `/icons`, `/notes`, `/hitSounds`, `/wallBreak`) a day, and every URL gets `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`, `Permissions-Policy: camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=(), display-capture=()` (the source uses none of them: a test greps for it) and `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=15552000` (six months, no `includeSubDomains` or `preload`: the billing and relay hosts are other subdomains). Pages, `sw.js`, `offline.html`, `manifest.webmanifest`, `version.json` and the `og/` cards keep the host's revalidate-every-time default, so a deploy shows up at once. There is no Content-Security-Policy (the static export's inline scripts, the billing Worker and the analytics beacon need an audit first) and no `X-Robots-Tag` rule for the `workers.dev` preview host (its pattern could not be proven locally). Checked with the Wrangler the deploy pins (3.114.17): `wrangler dev` on a built export with a copy of `wrangler.toml` that points its assets at it, and `curl -I` on a page, a hashed chunk, a picture, `sw.js`, `/_headers` and an unknown URL. That check found a trap: Wrangler turns the file into a table keyed by path, so a second `/*` block replaces the first – the `X-Build` block that `postexport.mjs` appends removed the security headers. `scripts/lib/headers-file.mjs` therefore starts each run from the committed file and folds the appended block into the `/*` block (`mergeHeaderFile`), and `tests/staticHeaders.test.ts` fails if `public/_headers` names a path twice. The two canonical redirects that no `_headers` file can make, http → https and `www.` → the bare domain, are made by the Worker's own script since website 1.11.1 (`worker/redirects.mjs`: `main` in `wrangler.toml`, `run_worker_first = true`, the assets through the `ASSETS` binding; `tests/workerRedirects.test.ts`), so the dashboard's Always Use HTTPS and Redirect Rule are optional. The same build writes `/.well-known/security.txt` (RFC 9116: Contact, Expires within a year, Canonical, Policy) from the support address, `scripts/lib/security-txt.mjs` in `postexport.mjs`.
- **Small fixes** – the pricing table's scroller is `relative`, so its screen-reader-only labels no longer widen the page to 449 px on a 390 px phone (the smoke test checks `/pricing/`, `/download/` and the landing in all three languages); the header's logo link is named by its visible text (a `title` says where it goes), the hero preview's "Live" and "9:16" badges sit outside its link so the link's name is its label (axe-core's label-in-name rule passes on every page), and the footer's links are 29 px high on touch screens instead of 17 (`[@media(pointer:coarse)]:py-1.5`).
- **Guard rails** – `tests/perfGuards.test.ts` (source level, always: the `Link` default, the landing page's import graph, the pricing scroller, the touch targets and badges) and, when an export built by this tree is at `./out` (or at `PERF_GUARD_OUT=<folder>`; skipped with a message otherwise), the initial scripts of every marketing page (none over 600 KB, 1.5 MB together), `out/_headers` (immutable rule, one block per path) and `out/sw.js` (every listed file exists, none of `_headers`, `_redirects`, `version.json`, `build.txt`, `og/`, `press/`, the critical files within budget). The precache budget is also enforced by every build: `buildPwa()` throws when the critical files outgrow `MAX_CRITICAL_PRECACHE_BYTES`, and `postexport.mjs` prints the critical and deferred sizes.

### Analytics events
Feature analytics: the few actions an ad campaign is judged by, counted by an optional statistics service. Nothing is counted, loaded or requested until the build is given `NEXT_PUBLIC_ANALYTICS_SCRIPT_URL`; with it unset every call below does nothing (`track()` returns at once) and the page makes no request to any service.

- **Loader and gates** – `src/components/site/Analytics.tsx` (client component, mounted in the locale layout) adds the service's script after the page has loaded, and only when every gate in `trackingAllowed()` (`src/lib/analytics/track.ts`) holds: the build has a script address that is an absolute http(s) URL; the page is served over http or https (not `app://`, not `file://`); it is not the Windows app (`window.desktop` is absent); the browser sends neither Do Not Track (`navigator.doNotTrack`, `window.doNotTrack`, `navigator.msDoNotTrack`) nor Global Privacy Control (`navigator.globalPrivacyControl`). Behind a gate the script is never added, so the visitor makes no request to the service, and `track()` is silent. The script gets `data-domain` (the site's host name, for Plausible), `data-website-id` and `data-site-id` (the optional `NEXT_PUBLIC_ANALYTICS_SITE_ID`, for Umami and Rybbit) and `data-do-not-track="true"` (Umami's own switch). A script that cannot load (an ad blocker, offline) drops what waited and stops sending.
- **Dispatch** – `track(name, props?, revenue?)` hands the event to the first service that is there: `window.plausible(name, { props, revenue })`, `window.umami.track(name, props)`, `window.rybbit.event(name, props)` (the vendor's documented name, to be checked on install), then a `window.dataLayer` array if the page has one (it is never created). Up to 20 events wait for the script (the oldest go first) and are sent when it has loaded. Revenue (`{ currency: "USD", amount }`) goes to Plausible only (it needs a revenue goal in its dashboard) and, as the props `revenue` and `currency`, to Umami. It never throws.
- **What may be sent** – only the events and props below, nothing else: `sanitizeProps()` keeps a prop only if it is in the event's list, a string only if it is a short token (at most 32 characters; no `@`, no slash, no URL, no space, nothing that starts `jbs_` or `eyJ`) and, for the props with a fixed set of values (`PROP_VALUES`), only if it is one of them; numbers must be finite. Never an email, a receipt reference, a licence, a seed, an address or free text. The `purchase` event carries the plan's list price in USD as revenue and is sent once per receipt reference in a page (the reference is compared in memory, never sent or stored).
- **Page views** – the service's own script counts them (and the single-page navigation); no code of ours. The host's page-view beacon of Cloudflare Web Analytics, if it is switched on in the Cloudflare dashboard, is added by Cloudflare at the edge, is not part of this repository and does not follow the gates above.
- **Click events from markup** – a button or link carries `data-track="cta_click" data-track-id="<id>"` (or `data-track="download_click" data-track-kind="<kind>"`); one delegated listener on the document, bound by the loader, reads it, so server components stay server components. Only those two events can come from markup. The `id` is one of `CTA_IDS`.
- **Entry** – a click on the gallery's Try it notes `gallery` for the next `sim_start`; a decoded `?c=` link notes `share`; a daily-challenge link is `daily`; anything else is `direct`.

| Event | Props | Where emitted |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `cta_click` | `id` | `data-track` on the landing hero (studio, pricing link), the Windows button (`DownloadAppButton.tsx`), the header's links and button and its phone sheet (`Navbar.tsx`), the gallery's Try it, the TikTok guide's button, the footer's Install app |
| `sim_start` | `mode`, `entry` | `Simulator.tsx`: the first run of a page load |
| `clip_export` | `kind`, `mode`, `res`, `watermark`, `secs` | `Simulator.tsx` (a recording when it is saved, a fast export when it is saved) and `useBatchRender.ts` (one batch ZIP, `kind` batch without `mode` and `res`, `secs` = the clips' total) |
| `share_copy` | – | `Simulator.tsx`: Copy link |
| `share_open` | `kind` | `Simulator.tsx`: a `?c=` link that decoded (code), a `?daily=` link the visitor opened by address (daily) |
| `download_click` | `kind` | `download/page.tsx`: the installer, the portable EXE, the checksums |
| `pwa_install` | `outcome` | `installPrompt.ts`: the answer to the browser's install dialog |
| `pwa_installed` | – | `installPrompt.ts`: the browser's `appinstalled` |
| `unlock_open` | `feature` | `UnlockDialog.tsx`: the Unlock dialog opened, with the locked action |
| `checkout_start` | `plan`, `provider`, `from` | `PlanCards.tsx`: the backend gave a checkout address |
| `purchase` | `plan`, `provider` | `PricingClient.tsx`: the claim of a returned checkout installed a licence |
| `restore` | `via` | `PricingClient.tsx`: Restore purchase (email and reference) or Paste a licence key worked |
| `signup` | – | `lib/account/controller.ts`: an account was made |
| `channel_connected` | `platform` | `lib/publish/controller.ts`: a YouTube channel or a relay account was connected |
| `publish_sent` | `platform`, `via` | `lib/publish/controller.ts`: a clip was published through the relay or YouTube (api), or handed to the share sheet or the upload page (share) |

- **Values** – `cta_click.id`: `hero_studio`, `hero_pricing`, `hero_download`, `nav_studio`, `nav_gallery`, `nav_download`, `nav_pricing`, `nav_cta`, `gallery_try`, `tiktok_cta`, `footer_install`. `sim_start.entry`: `direct`, `share`, `daily`, `gallery`. `clip_export.kind`: `record`, `fast`, `batch`; `watermark`: `free`, `pro` (a fast export says what it drew, a recording and a batch say what the licence store says); `res` is the export size (`1080x1920`); `secs` the clip length (a recording: the setting). `share_open.kind`: `code`, `daily`. `download_click.kind`: `setup`, `portable`, `checksums`. `pwa_install.outcome`: `accepted`, `dismissed`. `unlock_open.feature`: `record`, `fastExport`, `batch`, `bot`, `publish`, `renderQueue`, `download` (the `ProFeature` ids). `checkout_start.plan` and `purchase.plan`: `monthly`, `yearly`; `provider`: `stripe`, `paypal`, `crypto`; `from`: `page`, `dialog`. `restore.via`: `email_ref`, `key`. `channel_connected.platform` and `publish_sent.platform`: `tiktok`, `instagram`, `youtube`; `publish_sent.via`: `api`, `share`.
- **Campaign names survive the studio** – an ad click lands on a localised address such as `/en/simulator/?utm_source=tiktok&gclid=…`. The studio mirrors its settings into the address bar, which used to rewrite the whole query (everything but the settings was gone within seconds, before a statistics script read it). `src/lib/analytics/attribution.ts` exports `ATTRIBUTION_PARAMS` (`utm_source`, `utm_medium`, `utm_campaign`, `utm_term`, `utm_content`, `utm_id`, `gclid`, `gbraid`, `wbraid`, `fbclid`, `ttclid`, `msclkid`, `twclid`, `rdt_cid`) and `withAttribution()`, which the mirror in `Simulator.tsx` calls: those names stay in the address bar, nothing else is kept and nothing is stored (no cookie, no `localStorage`). A seed in the link is read once, as before, and is still not written back. The share link a visitor copies never carries them (`withoutAttribution()` in the long-link fallback; the short `?c=` link is built from the settings alone).
- **Not measured** – by the action counter: the Windows app (it is offline by design) and a visitor who sends Do Not Track or Global Privacy Control. Cloudflare Web Analytics, which the edge adds to page views, looks at neither signal and counts those visitors' page views. Also not measured: a purchase whose checkout never returns to the pricing page. The app's downloads are counted by GitHub on the release assets of the public downloads repository.
- **Owner steps** – choose the service (Plausible or Umami; Cloudflare Web Analytics cannot count events), put its script address and site id in `NEXT_PUBLIC_ANALYTICS_SCRIPT_URL` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_ANALYTICS_SITE_ID` (the VPS deployer, `JBL_ANALYTICS_*`, and `deploy.yml`, `ANALYTICS_*`, pass them to the build), create the goals `clip_export`, `checkout_start` and `purchase` (a USD revenue goal for Plausible), and say the service's name in the privacy policy (it names it by the host of the script address).
- **Tests** – `tests/analytics.test.ts`: the gates (empty address, a non-http address, `app:` and `file:` pages, the Windows app, Do Not Track in three forms, Global Privacy Control), the sanitiser, the dispatch order and the revenue rules, the queue and its limit, the markup listener, the `EVENTS` table against this table, every event and id used in `src/` against `EVENTS` and `CTA_IDS`, and that every name of `ATTRIBUTION_PARAMS` survives the studio's mirror.

### Press page, ad kit and launch check
Feature press: what to hand a writer, what to say in an ad, and a check that the live site is fit for both before money is spent.

- **Press page** – `src/app/[locale]/press/page.tsx` (a server component wrapped in `ProseLayout`, no client component, so `PAGE_CLIENT_NAMESPACES` needs no entry; texts in the `Press` namespace of `messages/*.json`, held to docs/COPY-STYLE.md like the other pages). Sections: the fact sheet (what it is, platforms, `{count}` modes, export sizes, the free version with its watermark, the Pro prices from `faqPrices()` / `PLANS`, the first release from the changelog, the website build label from `src/lib/buildInfo.ts`, the Windows app's `DESKTOP_VERSION`, the operator's name and the support e-mail from `src/lib/operator.ts`, else the feedback page), the description in three lengths (a label with the word count measured from the text), the logo downloads (`public/icon.svg`, `public/icons/icon-512.png`, `icon-192.png` and the home page's social card `public/og/<locale>/home.png`: no new file ships, nothing is precached by the press page itself), the colours (`ACCENT`, `ACCENT_LIGHT`, `PWA_THEME_COLOR` and the ink of `globals.css`), usage rules, a "Not part of the press kit" box (Fight League names and footage) and the contact. `tests/pressPage.test.ts` holds every figure to the code.
- **Ad kit** – [`docs/ADS.md`](docs/ADS.md): the positioning and the one promise, the banned claims (viral, earnings, superlatives, PayPal, Publish, Fight League and every franchise name), audiences, destinations and the UTM naming table (`utm_source`, `utm_medium`, `utm_campaign` as `YYMM_goal_audience`, `utm_content` as `cNN_<recipe>_<ratio>_<secs>s_<locale>`; always a localised address, never the bare domain, never the studio for cold traffic), the copy of each platform in English, Polish and Spanish, creative specs and safe zones, the bot recipes that make the best clips with the exact commands (always `--family escape` or `--family rhythm`: the default family includes Fight League clips), the measurement plan without pixels, KPIs and a one-variable-at-a-time test plan, the launch-day and compliance checklists and the owner's steps. Platform limits are third-party figures except Google's: each is marked "confirm in the platform UI".
- **Copy as data** – [`docs/ads/copy.json`](docs/ads/copy.json) holds every line with its platform, set, field, language and limit (`reserved` is the part the platform adds: 24 for the link in an X post). `tests/adCopy.test.ts` holds every line to its limit and to the copy style, to the banned claims (also in Polish and Spanish), to the Fight League roster and the source franchises (no fighter, no franchise), to the billing config's prices and to the mode count; it checks that ADS.md carries every line with its count (`| text | 24 / 30 |`), that every address in it is a localised address with valid UTM names the studio keeps (`ATTRIBUTION_PARAMS`), and that every `viral-bot.mjs` command in it parses with the bot's own `parseArgs` and, on each recipe row, plans that recipe on that date (the bot's `daySlots`, recomputed).
- **Launch check** – `npm run launch:check` (`scripts/launch-check.mjs`, Node only; `--url`, `--json`, `--browser`, `--analytics <host>`, `--commit <sha|HEAD>`, `--billing`, `--relay`, `--downloads`): `A1` the build names itself (`/build.txt`, `/version.json`, the `jbl-build` meta and `X-Build` agree), `A2` to `A2g` every public page in every language (200, own canonical, the hreflang set, social tags, a reachable 1200×630 PNG card, title at most 60, description 70 to 160, no two pages alike), `A3` the privacy page has a mailto, `A4` no developer string is visible, `A5` the pricing page names only the payment methods the billing `/config` has on, `A6` Publish is not sold while the relay's `/api/health` has every platform off, `A7` the installer, the portable EXE and the checksums download and `latest.yml` is `DESKTOP_VERSION`, `A8` the statistics service is in the site's code, `A9` campaign names survive `/en/` and `/en/simulator/` (Playwright), `A10` `http` and `www` redirect, `A11` the security headers, and `B1` to `B6` the sitemap and robots, noindex, JSON-LD, the Fight League notice, the ad copy's price and mode count, `security.txt`. Every result is `PASS`, `FAIL`, `WARN` or `SKIP`; the exit code is 1 on any FAIL. It only sends GET and HEAD requests; behind a proxy set `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1`. The pure helpers are exported and tested against fixture HTML and a fake site (`tests/launchCheck.test.ts`: no network).
- **Owner steps** – the order, how to run the check and what must be green before spending are in [docs/GO-LIVE.md](docs/GO-LIVE.md), section 14.2; the accounts, the verification, the signing, the support mailbox and the VPS variables are in ADS.md section 12.

### Territory (oddplayground)
The oddplayground family's pong-wars mode (feature odd-territory): `src/lib/physics/modes/territory.ts` holds the settings, the pure board maths and the `TerritoryMode` plugin; `src/components/simulator/territoryRenderer.ts` draws it and `src/components/simulator/sections/TerritorySection.tsx` is its block of the Mode row; `tests/territory.test.ts` covers them (the board, the rule, its symmetry and order independence, every power, the percentages, the verdict, the rig, determinism, a resize, the finder, and the Ball Size past the old cap: the contact edge, the crowd, the ate-the-arena finish, the old sizes replayed bit for bit).

- **Board**: the mode owns its playfield (the `"none"` ring layout). `territoryField()` puts a grid of `cols` × `territoryRows(cols)` tiles (the rows follow the field's shape, rounded down to an even count) in the centred square the recorder crops to, under a HUD band (`TY_HUD_BAND` of the square). The tiles are a `Uint8Array` (row-major, the owner per tile) with running counts per team; `regionOwner()` / `fillRegions()` split it into halves (2 teams) or quadrants (4 teams, an odd middle column shared row by row), every team within one tile of the others.
- **Rule**: the engine moves the balls (no gravity, `ballsMayRest` – the mode keeps each ball at its cruising speed, a fraction of the square per second, so a run looks the same on any canvas) and resolves ball-to-ball collisions in its pair loop; `onBallStep()` bounces a ball off the frame (a seeded scatter, headings kept off the axes: `awayFromAxes()`), off the pegs (`TY_PEG_STEP`, through the obstacle layer's `resolveBallCircle()` with a reused peg) and runs the pong-wars probe: eight points on the ball's rim; every probe moving into a tile of another team converts it (`convert()`), and the ball reflects once about the sum of those probes' directions (a flat border sends it straight back, a concave corner back the way it came). A ball whose centre sits on an enemy tile (a blast engulfed it) converts that tile, so it eats its way out; `onPostSubStep()` keeps a ball pushed by a collision on the board. That is the test of every ball up to `TY_PROBE_RADIUS` (0.9 tiles, the old size cap); a bigger ball tests its contact edge instead (see Ball Size).
- **Fairness**: nothing depends on which side of the board a team plays or on the order the engine moves the balls in. The reflection is the same for every heading (reflecting at each hit in a fixed probe order favoured one side by a few tiles a run – enough to decide most crowded battles); the conversions of a sub-step are queued and applied in `onPostSubStep()` (`flush()`, `apply()`: counts, the flip log, a note), so every ball judges the board as it stood when the sub-step began; and the balls join in rounds – a ball of every team a round, the team that starts a round turning from a seeded first one –, so no team always moves first. The powers are tuned on the default board (24 columns, 2 balls a team): the vortex, the bomber and the painter win between about two and three fifths of their pairings; the ghost, which never fights at the borders, is the underdog of a two-team battle (it takes a fifth to two fifths of them, less on big boards, where its 3×3 blocks are a smaller share) and the favourite of a four-team battle of single balls, where most of the frame it bounces off is enemy ground.
- **Powers** (`TyPower`, one per team slot): timed ones fire every `powerEvery` seconds from a seeded phase per ball – the **vortex** (always curving at `TY_VORTEX_CURVE` rad/s) drags a whirl whose two spiral arms (`whirlPoint()`) sweep out to `radius` tiles over `TY_WHIRL_MS`, converting the tiles they land on; the **bomber** arms (`armedMs`) and blows on its next bounce off an enemy tile – or where it is when no such bounce comes within `TY_ARM_MAX_MS` –, converting every tile within `radius`, pushing nearby balls away, logging a shock (`shocks`) and queueing a `"gap"` sound (the wall-break sound, which is also the camera's screen-shake event); its next charge starts at the blast; the **painter** dashes `TY_DASH_TILES` tiles (the same trail on any board) without bouncing off enemy tiles, converting the tile under it. The **ghost** (a faster ball) never bounces off or converts enemy tiles and converts a whole 3×3 block on every frame bounce (the block around its tile, moved inside the board).
- **Sound**: every sound is an ordinary engine sound event, so melodies, instruments, hit samples, the beat lock and the music bed work: a flip is a `"hit"` pitched by team and row on a pentatonic ladder (`flipFrequency()`, at most one per step and `TY_NOTE_GAP_MS`), a dash or a ghost block an accented hit, a whirl the multiplier arpeggio, a blast the wall-break sound.
- **Verdict**: the last `TY_FINALE_MS` ramp the speed up (`territoryFinaleFactor()`) and a lead change there asks the camera for slow motion (`noteNearMiss`). When the countdown runs out the board freezes: `territoryLeaders()` names the teams with the most tiles, every team is credited with its tiles as "walls" and the leaders with an "escape" – so the teams banner, the finder's "winner" outcome and the scoreboard rank the battle by tiles, and a level top stays a DRAW. The mode is in `BATTLE_WINNER_MODES`: the rigged forced winner is enforced by the mode (`rigBlocksConversion()` absorbs a conversion that would put any rival ahead of the chosen team; a level verdict goes to it), and the finder follows every run to its end. Every run lasts its countdown whatever the seed (`fixedRunDurationSec()`), so the finder's useful search is the winner – and that search follows each run to the countdown's verdict however far past the clip and the search's horizon it lies (`simulateOutcomeRun()` stretches the horizon to the countdown + `FIXED_RUN_SLACK_MS`; the page's horizon, Find Duration + 30 s, used to cut a 61–120 s countdown short, and no seed could match).
- **Canvas**: `TerritoryLayer` keeps the tile map in an offscreen canvas (device pixels) and repaints only the tiles whose owner changed (a byte diff against the copy it painted), then draws the pops of fresh flips (a springy scale from the wobble field's `wobbleEnvelope()`), the dotted grid, the frame, the blasts (shock ring, seeded debris) and the whirls under the balls; soft halos and glossy bodies per team (a ghost translucent, a charge ring before a timed power, a flickering ring around an armed bomber) in the ball pass; a blast jolts the board (`blastJolt()`: the camera's shake envelope and wobble at `TY_BLAST_SHAKE`, on the simulation clock, applied by Canvas.tsx to the world view while the camera's own Screen Shake is off – with it on, the camera shakes the view on the blast's wall-break sound); and the HUD band (badge, PICK A SIDE, countdown, "VORTEX 52% VS BOMBER 48%" – a team's power or, without one, its name –, the eased percentage bar) plus, without a roster, the winner banner and confetti in screen space. `writeTerritoryDataset()` mirrors the state onto `data-ty-*` attributes (`data-ty-jolts` counts the blasts that jolted the board). With a team roster, `TeamLayer` pads it with the palette (PINK, CYAN, LIME, GOLD), hides the scoreboard while the HUD shows (without the HUD the scoreboard's columns read TILES – the live count – and WIN, and the badge moves to the top-right corner when the scoreboard sits top-left) and draws the winner banner. `drawOverlay()` returns the bottom of the HUD band (or of the badge), which the top captions start below. Tile bounces, frame bounces and peg hits are bounce math's "bounce" trigger (`ctx.noteBounce()`), ball-to-ball hits its "ball hit" and a bomber's blast its "wall break".
- **Settings**: `tyCols` (`tyc`), `tyTeams` (`tyt`), `tyBallsPerTeam` (`tyb`), `tyPowers` (`typ`, a comma list of the four slots), `tyPowerEvery` (`tye`), `tyRadius` (`tyr`), `tyDuration` (`tyd`), `tyPegs` (`typg`), `tyBadge` (`tybg`), `tyHud` (`tyh`); a change of the fight restarts it and drops a found seed, the badge and the HUD follow live. The mode's default clip covers the countdown and its verdict (`territoryClipSec()`), and the Countdown slider moves the clip with it. Every number is uncapped (see Uncapped everything): a value typed past its slider runs as typed – a reach of 50 tiles, a power every 30 s, a ten-minute countdown, whose clip follows it –, and the board's columns, the teams and the balls of a team stop at their memory-safety ceilings (`MEMORY_CEILINGS` in lib/uncap.ts: 1,000 columns, 4 teams – any count from 3 plays the quadrants –, 500 balls a team; the link keeps the typed value and the panel names the ceiling). A whirl or a blast reaching past the board takes what lies on it in work bounded by the board: `whirlReach()` walks (and the canvas draws) the arms only out to the board's diagonal, a blast's loop stops at the board's edges and its ring is drawn no wider than any canvas (`visualValue()`). With a duration search the finder says the run is the countdown (`Territory.finderFixed`) and points at the winner search.
- **Ball Size** (uncap-all, the review's last Territory cap): a ball's radius is `TY_BALL_SCALE` (0.42) of a tile per 8 px of Ball Size, at least `TY_MIN_RADIUS` (0.2) tiles and with no maximum (`territoryBallRadius()`). It used to stop at 0.9 tiles (`TY_MAX_RADIUS`), so every Ball Size from about 17 px – inside the slider – played the same ball (and a live Ball Size change, which scales the balls without the cap, disagreed with a restart); now the typed size is the size on any board, and the Power Reach past its slider acts as typed too (it never had a maximum: `typedNumber()` – once `clampNumber()` – only lifts a value onto the slider's minimum). Every size up to the old cap keeps the eight rim probes and replays exactly – `tests/territory.test.ts` fingerprints whole runs (the defaults to their verdict, four teams with every power and the grid, Ball Sizes 16 and 4, the bot's world) bit for bit against the code before the change. Past it:
  - **The contact edge, not the whole footprint** (what reads best): `edgeTiles()` tests the tiles the ball's disc overlaps within `TY_EDGE_DEPTH` (1) tile of its rim, the band deepened by how far the rim moved since the ball's last test – its moves, a collision's push, a growth – so every tile it newly overlaps is among them (`discSpans()`: row spans, about the band's length in work however big the ball; the whole disc on its first test). Every enemy tile there turns its team's, and the ball reflects once about the sum of the directions to the enemy tiles it moves into, each weighted by how deep the tile reaches into the disc (a flat border sends it straight back, a concave corner back the way it came, the same from either side of the board). A hit also takes the enemy tiles it would overlap a tile further along its way: a dent the shape of its front, a tile deep – as a pong-wars ball's hit takes the tile in front of it, so a 3-tile ball takes about four tiles a hit where a default ball takes one, and its hits carve room the size of the ball. On a hit the footprint and the edge are the same tiles (the ground under a moving ball is already its own); the edge costs the ball's perimeter, not its area. Enemy tiles deeper under a ball (a blast engulfed it) turn as they pass through its edge, so it never sits stuck in enemy ground; the ground another team's ball stands on is that ball's (`occupied()`, judged from where the balls stood when the sub-step began – `snapshot()`, filed in a grid –, so the order the engine moves the balls in still changes nothing): two big balls meet as balls in the engine's pair pass instead of flipping tiles to and fro.
  - **Movement**: the frame is a clamp of the centre to the board shrunk by the radius, so no ball leaves the board at any size or speed; any Ball Size past the slider engages the engine's planned sub-steps (no ball moves more than half its radius, at most 4 px, a sub-step; time dilation past 64), and on a board whose tiles are smaller than that move the mode asks for one tile a sub-step (`stepSpeedBound()`), so a hit dents the border a tile deep on any board. A ball at least `TY_PEG_FIT` (1.4) tiles in radius cannot pass between two dots of the grid: it rolls over them instead of jamming between them.
  - **Powers**: a big ball's whirl and blast reach `territoryReach()` – the Power Reach plus what the ball outgrew 0.9 tiles by – so they always reach past the ball (the old sizes keep the Power Reach exactly); a big ghost's frame bounce takes every tile its disc covers (and the 3×3 block), a big painter's dash paints a trail as wide as itself.
  - **A crowd**: big balls covering more than `TY_CROWD_FILL` (0.6) of the board together have no room to bounce apart – they jammed against each other and the frame, every ball bouncing off the frame at every sub-step –, so they pass through each other (`ballsPassThrough`, each keeping the ground it stands on) and the run says ARENA FULL (`noteArenaFull()`).
  - **Bigger than the arena**: a ball wider than the board's shorter side (`territoryBallFits()`) has eaten the arena when the next step begins (a typed size: the run's first step; a live change or a merge: the next one) – the engine's outgrow finish, as a ball bigger than Classic's arena: everything stops, the ball fills the board from its middle (every ball at most that big), confetti, THE BALL ATE THE ARENA with the ball's size and the gulp; the countdown gives no verdict. (Past half the canvas' diagonal the extreme runtime eats it itself as the step begins – the same finish.) The banner's size is the multipliers runtime's `outgrewRadius` – the ball's radius when `outgrow()` took it, before fitting it into the board –, which the runtime's gulp step reads when a mode, not the runtime itself, ate the arena.
  - **Ceilings** (work and memory safety, never a user's number): the big balls' tests visit at most `TY_TILE_VISITS` (2²⁰) tiles a sub-step together – the conversions they queue stay under it too – and respect the ground of at most `TY_NEAR_BUDGET` (8) other balls each; 2,000 big balls piled on 24 columns step in about 10 ms. `data-ty-ball-tiles` mirrors the biggest ball's radius in tiles, and `data-ty-in-field` now checks every ball's whole disc. The smoke test opens a Territory link with Ball Size 100 – more than three times the slider's end: balls of 5.25 tiles, one a team – and sees the size applied, several tiles flipped a hit, every disc in the field at 30+ fps; and a link with Ball Size 200 (balls wider than the board) end at its first step with THE BALL ATE THE ARENA and no verdict.

### Maze Escape (oddplayground)
Feature odd-maze: `src/lib/physics/mazeGrid.ts` holds the pure maze – generation, the BFS distance map, the field geometry, the cell collision and the brains' choices – and `src/lib/physics/modes/maze.ts` the settings (URL keys `mzc`, `mzn`, `mzb`, `mzh`, `mzg`, `mzs`, `mzt`, `mztc`, `mzto`, `mzf`, `mzwc`, `mzd`, `mzbg`, `mzhud`, read and written by `readMazeParams()` / `writeMazeParams()`), the notes and the `MazeMode` plugin; `src/components/simulator/mazeRenderer.ts` (`MazeLayer`) draws it and `sections/MazeSection.tsx` is the "Maze" block of the Mode row. `tests/maze.test.ts` covers the maze (n − 1 passages, connected, deterministic), the distance map, the collision, the brains, the settings and full engine runs in the page's worlds (800 × 450 and 450 × 450, `lib/simulation/world.ts` – containment at every speed, pull, ball size and canvas, corridors a ball fits up to the columns' ceiling, determinism, the notes, explorers that always get out – also climbing at the slowest Speed under the strongest Pull –, the verdicts, the rig, a winner past the six teams, a resize, the finder).

- **The maze** – `generateMaze()` carves a perfect maze (a spanning tree) with an iterative recursive backtracker on the engine's seeded `random()` (the entrance column, the exit column, the start cell, then one draw per step with a choice), so a seed replays the same maze; `mazeRowsFor()` derives the rows from the portrait field (`MZ_FIELD_WIDTH` × `MZ_FIELD_HEIGHT` of the square the recorder crops to) and `buildMazeField()` fits the cells into it – a cell is 252 / columns world px in the world's 450 px square. The walls are `MZ_WALL_FRACTION` of a cell thick (at least `MZ_MIN_WALL` px) and a ball's radius is the Ball Size up to `MZ_BALL_FRACTION` of a cell (at least `MZ_MIN_RADIUS` px); in a tiny cell the pixel minimums give way (a wall at most `MZ_MAX_WALL_FRACTION` of a cell, the smallest radius at most `MZ_MIN_RADIUS_FRACTION` of one: `mazeBallRadius()`), so the contact distance stays a fixed share of the cell and a ball fits every corridor up to the 200-column ceiling (with a 1 px wall and a 0.5 px ball it outgrew half a cell from about 116 columns on and no ball left the chute). Nothing changes up to about 50 columns. The distance map (`mazeDistances()`, BFS from the exit cell) is computed once at init: the pitch of every note, the HUD's bars, the rig's shortest way and the clip limit's verdict read it.
- **Collision** – a ball whose centre is in a cell can only touch that cell's four edges and the round posts at its corners (a wall of a neighbouring cell reaches in only through its end cap, the post), so `resolveMazeCell()` looks at nothing else: one-sided edge tests (a ball whose centre reached or crossed a closed edge is put back inside at the contact distance) and radial posts, rebounds mirrored (restitution 1). `onBallStep()` undoes the engine's move and moves the ball again in micro-steps of at most `MZ_MICRO_STEP` of its contact distance (more than `MZ_MAX_MICRO_STEPS` and it is slowed instead), following it from cell to cell through open passages only, so nothing tunnels at any speed. The entrance chute (row −1) is open toward the entrance until the ball is fully in; the exit gap is open to every ball – but, with a forced winner, only to it until it is out. The balls fly through each other (`ballsPassThrough`) and feel no engine gravity (`gravityScale` 0): the mode's own pull is `MZ_GRAVITY_BASE` field heights per s² × Pull, the cruising speed `MZ_SPEED_BASE` field widths per s × Speed.
- **Brains** – the steering decides once per cell, when the ball crosses the cell's centre line across the direction it came in, and nudges it toward the chosen passage with a seeded scatter (`MZ_STEER_SCATTER`: it ricochets down the corridor – every bounce a note), with the speed to climb a cell against the pull (`climbSpeed()`: `MZ_CLIMB` cells' worth of height; both speed caps – `MZ_MAX_SPEED` × the cruising speed in `onBallStep()` and in the nudge – admit it, which matters at a low Speed under a strong Pull: at Speed 0.25 and Pull 1 the climb in a 12-column maze takes 181 px/s against a cap of 156, and a capped ball bounced at the foot of every upward corridor). `wallFollowChoice()` (left / right hand; `alternate` gives the balls different hands), `explorerChoice()` (the exit when in sight, else a seeded pick among the unvisited passages, else back along its path – a depth-first search over the ball's own explored set; a cell counts as explored once the ball reached its centre line, so a corridor it only bounced into is tried again, and when `explorerExhausted()` finds it back at the root of its search with nothing left – the rig's seal turned it away from the exit – it starts a fresh search there instead of shuttling between two cells) and, for the rig's forced winner, `shortestChoice()`. `bounce` never steers (a small seeded scatter at every bounce keeps it out of periodic orbits).
- **Sound and scores** – a counted hit (`MZ_HIT_MIN` of the cruising speed along the normal – also one against a wall of the cell the ball just moved into) is a plain `hit` event at `mazeNoteFrequency()` – a C-major pentatonic ladder from C4 at the entrance's distance up to A6 at the exit – rate-limited per ball and overall, so melodies, instruments, hit samples, the beat lock and the music bed apply as everywhere; it credits a bounce in the team stats. The first ball out gets the run's only escape (`ctx.creditEscape`: the teams banner, the finder's winner outcome and the mode's verdict agree), a `gap` event (the wall-break sound, the camera's shake) and the banner; reaching the exit cell first triggers the camera's slow motion. The run ends when every ball is out or at the clip limit (the ball nearest the exit then takes the escape – the rig's forced winner whatever the distances) and finishes `MZ_FINISH_HOLD_MS` later, with the banner in the clip. The seventh and eighth balls play for no team: one of them winning credits no team (the teams banner then stays away and the maze's own banner shows the win).
- **Drawing** – `MazeLayer` caches the walls (merged runs, a soft glow, the red chute and slot, the exit chevron) and the trail (paint strokes the mode records only when a ball paints a cell or passage it had not painted, stamped incrementally) in layers of their own, flares the walls where they were just hit, draws the fog as one pixel per cell scaled up smoothly, and the halos, the badge, the HUD and the banner (with a roster the teams banner takes over at the end: `teamsRenderer.ts` pads the roster with the maze palette and hides its scoreboard under the HUD; with the HUD off and the scoreboard in the top-left corner the badge moves to the top-right one). The badge and the HUD shrink into the bands beside the maze column (`MIN_OVERLAY_FONT` at the smallest) so they never cover the maze or its chute – `data-mz-clear` says so. `drawOverlay()` returns the bottom of the badge and the HUD where they reach into the captions' column, so the top captions start below them. The canvas mirrors the state as `data-mz-*`.
- **Wiring** – `BATTLE_WINNER_MODES` (rigged.ts) gets the maze (forced winner, the finder's winner outcome judged at the run's end), `SLOW_VIEW_MODES` (camera.ts) too; `effectiveBallCount()` returns `mzBalls` (at most six teams), the finder's `ModeSettings.maze` / `createEngineForSettings()`, `simulateOutcomeRun()` (one team per ball, a seed dropped once another ball has won) and `MAZE_FINDER_BATCH`. The obstacle editor and the multiplier pickups stay with the ring modes (the maze owns its playfield); the Ball Speed slider does not apply (the maze has its own speed). Bounce math: every counted wall hit is a bounce (`ctx.creditBounce`), the winner's break-out through the exit a break (`BOUNCE_MATH_BREAK_MODES`), and a pitch rule shifts the ball's notes (`shiftedObstacleFrequency()`). The desktop AI studio tunes the `mz*` settings (`MODE_PREFIXES`). Every number is uncapped (see Uncapped everything): a value typed past its slider runs as typed – a pull of 50, a speed of 1000, a ten-minute clip limit, a trail or fog past 1 (drawn fully opaque) –, and the columns and the balls stop at their memory-safety ceilings (`MEMORY_CEILINGS` in lib/uncap.ts: 200 columns – 286 rows, 57,200 cells –, 32 balls – the paint masks hold a bit per ball –; the link keeps the typed value and the panel names the ceiling). An absurd speed or pull melts instead of tunnelling: a ball's travel per frame stays within `MZ_MAX_MICRO_STEPS` micro-steps (it slows down), and a jump that is not one cell through an open passage is put back into its cell.

### Land Claim (the weaponballarena family)
Feature land-claim. Research: the Instagram account weaponballarena posts land-claim battles – a square (or, in its #hexagon clips, a hexagonal) arena whose wall is lined with columns of blocks, country-flag balls bouncing around inside, every ball that hits a column knocking its top block off in its colour, every eighth block knocked off adding another ball ("1,144 blocks, 146 balls"), a title line asking "who will claim the most??", live bars per country, and a verdict banner – DOMINATION for a runaway winner, SUCH A CLOSE BATTLE for a photo finish. The mode keeps that format and makes every number a setting.

`src/lib/physics/modes/landClaim.ts` holds the settings (URL keys `lcc`, `lcr`, `lca`, `lct`, `lcb`, `lcm`, `lce`, `lcd`, `lcti`, `lch`, read and written by `readLandClaimParams()` / `writeLandClaimParams()`), the presets (`landClaimPresetPatch()`), the pure geometry and verdict maths and the `LandClaimMode` plugin; `src/lib/countries.ts` the countries; `src/lib/audio/landClaimTones.ts` the sounds; `src/components/simulator/landClaimRenderer.ts` (`LandClaimLayer`) draws it, `sections/LandClaimSection.tsx` is the "Land Claim" block of the Mode row and `sections/CountryPicker.tsx` the Country picker of the Teams section. `tests/landClaim.test.ts` covers them; the smoke test (section 35) checks the card, the URL and the controls, a four-country battle, the hexagon, a steal battle, the Country picker, the finder and the 1144-block preset's frame rates.

- **The arena** – the mode owns its playfield (the `"none"` ring layout). `landClaimField()` puts the arena in the centred square the recorder crops to, under a HUD band (`LC_HUD_BAND` of the square): a square, a flat-topped hexagon (fitted by its width) or a circle. A polygon's columns are spread over its sides as evenly as can be (`sideColumns()`), the circle's over its circumference; a column is the wedge between two rays from the centre through its edges on the wall, `LC_DEPTH` of the way to the centre deep, so the columns of a side tile it and the corners need no special case; the columns run clockwise from the top-left corner (the circle: from the top) – `columnAt()` finds a bearing's column by binary search. A block is a slice of its column (`blockCorners()`): on a polygon the side's wall shifted inwards, on the circle a ring sector; row 0 stands on the wall and the top block is the one nearest the centre.
