- **Physics** – the engine moves the balls (gravity as the Gravity slider sets it – 0 by default in this mode, `landClaimModeDefaults()` – and its slow-ball boost off: `ballsMayRest`); the mode keeps every ball at its cruising speed (`LC_SPEED` of the square a second at Ball Speed 400, so a run looks the same on any canvas; a ball's size is `LC_BALL_SCALE` of the square per 8 px of Ball Size, no maximum), and `onBallStep()` resolves the deepest overlap with the walls and the columns near the ball's bearing (`contacts()`: a polygon's sides, the circle, and the columns within asin(r/ρ) of the bearing, each the nearest of its top face and its two sides) by pushing the ball out and reflecting it with a small seeded scatter (`LC_SCATTER`: no periodic orbits). The ball-to-ball hits are the mode's own (`ballsPassThrough`): elastic, equal masses, the pairs from a spatial hash – O(n) a sub-step for the clip's 146 balls and the 2,000 of the ball ceiling; a ball pushed against the wall is put back inside.
- **The rules** – a hit on a column with land queues a knock; the knocks of a sub-step wait for its end (every ball judges the columns as they stood when the sub-step began; a block two balls reached together goes to the first) and its spawns for its knocks. **Knock**: the column's top block comes off (`heights` per column, a histogram of the heights keeps the tallest column in O(1), so the contact test skips the columns while a ball keeps clear of them), flies off inwards in the hitter's colour (a ring of `LC_FLY_LOG` flights with a hashed spread and spin, never the seeded RNG) and counts for the hitter; a column knocked bare is the wall. **Claim**: the column's top unclaimed block takes the hitter's colour where it stands (`owner` per block, two bytes each) – a column hands out its blocks from the top down, and one claimed to the wall is only a wall to bounce off. **Steal**: like claim, and a hit on a claimed column flips its topmost block that is not the hitter's (`stolen` / `lost` per competitor); the battle runs to its duration. Every `every`-th block a competitor knocks or claims (steals do not count) spawns one more ball of its colour at the knock point, heading off the wall within `LC_SPAWN_SPREAD` of its inward normal – past the ball ceiling (`LC_BALL_CEILING`, the board ceiling) a spawn is refused with ARENA FULL and the knock still counts.
- **Verdict** – the run ends when every block is knocked or claimed (a steal battle: never) or at the duration. `landClaimVerdict()`: the most land wins – on a level top the rig's pick, then the one with more balls, then the earlier slot; DOMINATION past `LC_DOMINATION` (60 %) of the land, SUCH A CLOSE BATTLE when the top two are within `LC_CLOSE` (5 %), else "[name] CLAIMS THE MOST". The first six competitors are credited with their land as "walls" and the winner with an "escape", so the teams banner, the scoreboard (its blocks column) and the finder's winner outcome rank the battle by land; a winner past the six teams has no team winner and the mode's own banner names it. The mode is in `BATTLE_WINNER_MODES`.
- **The rig** – the Forced Winner is honest steering, not a hard constraint: whenever a ball of the chosen competitor (one of the first six) bounces off a wall or a column, its rebound is aimed a little better – of `LC_RIG_SAMPLES` headings within `LC_RIG_TURN` of it (never back into the surface it left, never grazing along it), the one whose ray meets the nearest column top that still has land for it; when none does, it turns towards the nearest such column round the wall. Speed, size and the rules stay the same and nothing is taken from the others – the chosen colour simply wastes fewer flights. It wins about six default battles in ten where it would win one in four; the panel says "Rigged: [name] is favoured", and Find Simulation's winner outcome finds a run it actually wins.
- **Find Simulation** – the outcomes are the run length (a knock or claim battle ends when the seed lets the wall be taken; a steal battle's length is its duration, `fixedRunDurationSec()`), **[competitor] wins** and **Close battle** (`CLOSE_BATTLE_MODES` in `outcomes.ts`: the verdict's gap between the top two within `LC_CLOSE`; the miss and the progress show the closest gap in percent of the land). The finder's `ModeSettings.landClaim`, `createEngineForSettings()`, `simulateOutcomeRun()` (every battle judged at its end, at its duration at the latest; the first six competitors as teams and the verdict's margin) and `LAND_CLAIM_FINDER_BATCH`.
- **Sound** – `SoundEvent.lcSound`: a **knock** is a short wooden click (`scheduleKnock()`: a band-passed noise tick over two damped sine partials, the fundamental and an inharmonic 2.76× above) pitched by the column on a C-major pentatonic ladder round the wall (`knockFrequency()`, at most one a step and one per `LC_KNOCK_GAP_MS`; in the sample mode the hit sample), a **spawn** a bright chime rising a third and a fifth (`scheduleSpawnChime()`, pitched by competitor), a **KO** – a column's last block – a low thump falling an octave under a dark noise burst (`scheduleKo()`), the arena's last block and the verdict accented with a sparkle. `ToneGenerator.playLandClaim()` plays them on the beat grid (the chime and the KO duck the music bed); the page's sound loop, the fast export's `playSoundEvent()` and the split-screen arenas' `playArenaSound()` route them. Bounce math: every wall and column bounce is a bounce (`ctx.creditBounce`), every ball-to-ball hit a collision (`BOUNCE_MATH_COLLIDE_MODES`).
- **Countries** – `src/lib/countries.ts`: 47 countries `{ code, name, flag, colors: [primary, secondary] }` (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2, the English name, the flag emoji from the regional indicators, two flag colours), `flagOf()` / `flagCodeOf()`, `countryByCode()`, `countryOfFlag()` and `countryTeamEntry()`; the names are translated in every language (`Countries.<code>`, at most a team name's 16 characters). The String Circle style of the String Battle (feature string-circle) uses the same module and picker and added the five countries of its clips the list lacked (Afghanistan, Bangladesh, Iran, Pakistan, Sri Lanka). The Teams section's **Country** picker (a compact select on every roster row) fills the row with a country's translated name, its flag as the emoji and its primary colour.
- **Drawing** – `LandClaimLayer` keeps the arena (its floor and every block) in an offscreen canvas in device pixels and repaints only the columns whose count of changes (`colVersion`) moved, the blocks batched into one path per colour (a neutral or a competitor's, a shade for alternate rows); a block a few device pixels deep keeps its gaps, a finer one is drawn as runs of one colour sampled about once a device pixel down the column – a million blocks draw as a few thousand runs. Over it: the wall (flashing in the winner's colour at the verdict), the springy pops of claimed and stolen blocks and the knocked blocks flying off in the hitter's colour, on the simulation clock. The balls: a glow and a badge – a flag (or another roster emoji) inside a ring in the competitor's colour, its two-letter code on a disc where the fonts have no flag glyphs (`flagGlyphsSupported()`: measured once – a colour flag glyph against the fill-coloured letters a font without one draws), else a glossy disc; past 400 balls no glow, past 1,200 plain discs, one path per colour. The HUD band: the title line, the counters (blocks left or steals, balls, the clock) and a bar per competitor (past eight the leaders and "+N MORE"); the verdict banner in the arena's middle, naming the winner and throwing the confetti when no roster does it (with one the teams layer's banner and confetti take over, lower). `data-lc-*` mirrors the battle (teams, columns, rows, the land, balls, spawned, stolen and lost per competitor, knocks, spawns, steals, KOs, the leader, close, finished, the finish time, winner, verdict, share and margin, the rig, what was drawn, the badges and names, every ball inside the wall).
- **Settings and wiring** – `lcCols`, `lcRows`, `lcArena`, `lcTeams`, `lcBalls`, `lcRule`, `lcEvery`, `lcDuration`, `lcTitle`, `lcHud`; a change of the battle restarts it and drops a found seed, the title and the HUD follow live, and the Battle Duration moves the clip (`landClaimClipSec()`: the duration and the verdict's hold). Every number is uncapped: the sliders are comfort ranges and a typed value past them runs as typed; what a run builds stops at the memory-safety ceilings – 5,000 columns, 2,000 rows and a million blocks in all (a wall past it builds fewer rows), 1,000 competitors, 2,000 balls –, the page and the link keeping the typed values. `effectiveBallCount()` returns the competitors (six at most are teams), the Rigged forced winner, the finder (`ModeSettings.landClaim`), the bot's `modeSettingsOfSettings()` and the desktop assistant (`lc` prefix) carry the settings; a question caption's answer may say `[winner]` (`captionAnswerText()`), which the canvas fills in with the winner at the verdict – the block's "Who claims the most?" button adds one.
- **Pyramid layout** (feature land-claim-pyramid, the loop-clip account's "1,144 blocks, 146 balls, and the summit goes last") – `lcLayout` "pyramid" (URL `lcl`) swaps the wall for a G × G height map on a square floor: `src/lib/physics/landClaimPyramid.ts` holds its settings (`lcPyramidSize` / `lcg` 11, `lcSteps` / `lcs` 6, `lcBlocks` / `lcbk` 1,144 – 0 keeps the natural Σ of the steps –, `lcRebuild` / `lcrb`, `lcRebuildSec` / `lcrs` 2.5 s), the height map (`lcPyramidHeights()`: `steps` minus the Chebyshev distance to the centre, every ring's height scaled to exactly the blocks asked for, the remainder to whole rings from the summit outwards, so an inner ring never stands lower than an outer one and no height is negative), the top-down floor and its square-obstacle contacts (`lcPyramidContact()`), the start places, the pitch rule, the layer palette, the rebuild's maths and the run's view (`view.pyramid`); `LandClaimMode` steps it from its `// --- land-claim-pyramid ---` blocks with Land Claim's own knock and multiply rules (a hit knocks the top block, which flies off in the hitter's colour; every New Ball Every-th block adds a ball, so 3 + ⌊B / 8⌋ balls at the end). Every random choice of a pyramid run comes from its own generator (`LcpRandom`) seeded once from `ctx.random()`, so a seed replays exactly at 30, 60 and 120 fps. **Rebuild and loop**: a hold (`LCP_HOLD_SEC`) after the summit's fall, the rebuild (columns rising outer steps first, the added balls melting in, the starting balls gliding home), then the seam restores the first frame and the generator – `cycleSeconds()` / `loopSeams()` feed the loop contract. Sound (`SoundEvent.loop`, the loop library): a pentatonic pluck per block by its layer (`LcpStepNotes`: at most six new notes a tick, same-pitch hits merged), a chime per spawn, the summit's impact and C-major chord, the completion chord at the clear (minor when the duration ran out) and the reset glide, wash and cut. `components/simulator/landClaimPyramidRenderer.ts` (`LandClaimPyramidLayer`) draws it isometrically – painter's order by diagonal, hidden faces culled, the balls depth-sorted among the columns with floor shadows – and `sections/LandClaimPyramidSection.tsx` holds its controls; the loop HUD ("a pyramid, one block at a time", a grey "N blocks" counter) replaces Land Claim's own in the **Pyramid, 1,144 blocks** preset. Find Simulation adds **The summit falls last** (`summit-last`: the centre column the last one standing in the first cycle). `tests/landClaimPyramid.test.ts` covers it; the smoke block `land-claim-pyramid` runs the preset to its clear (0 blocks, 146 balls, the summit last), the URL round trip, the seam and the finder.

### Conveyor Belt and timed respawns
Feature gerald-conveyor: an escape-family mode after the character-ball account's "conveyor belt loading" and "Gerald respawns every 3 seconds" clips (`src/lib/physics/modes/conveyor.ts`, id `conveyor`), and a respawn timer for Classic and Multiply (`src/lib/physics/respawn.ts`).

- **The belt and the schedule** – `buildConveyorLayout()` lays a portrait field out in the centred square the recorder crops to (like Ball Drop and Bullseye): the belt along its top (`BELT_AT`), a hatch in the left wall, the drop point in the middle and the arena below. Ball k comes out of the hatch at k × the interval of the simulation clock (`conveyorLoadMs()`; a ball due part-way through a step waits in the hatch for the rest of it, so it drops exactly `conveyorRideSec()` later whatever the frame rate), rides the belt at `BELT_SPEED` field widths per second and drops (`conveyorDropMs()`), up to the Balls setting (its memory-safety ceiling is `BOARD_BALL_CEILING`). `init()` draws six numbers per ball up front from `ctx.random()` – size, colour, palette, drop jitter, launch angle, patience – so a seed replays exactly. Riding the belt, sliding down the tube and riding the bottom belt are scripted (the mode pins those balls again after the pair pass, in `onPostSubStep()`); everything else is the engine's physics.
- **Arenas** – **rings**: the engine's own ring walls (`ctx.setCircularWalls()`, centred on the canvas): as many of the Wall Count as fit between `RING_INNER` and `RING_OUTER` for the biggest ball (`conveyorRingRadii()`; the outer ring leaves room beside it for the escaped balls to fall past), gaps `RING_GAP_SCALE` × the Gap Size, never narrower than the biggest ball can pass (`conveyorRingWalls()`), turning at the Rotation speed. A dropped ball slides down the loading tube into the core (`CHUTE_SEC`) and is launched at the Ball Speed in its seeded direction; the rings never break (`onGapPass()` handles every pass), a ring passed plays an accented note (`conveyorPassPitch()`) and lights the ring up, and getting out (`ESCAPE_MARGIN` beyond the outer ring) plays the wall-break sound with confetti. Escaped balls skip the rings, roll off the outer ring as off a dome and land on the bottom belt, which carries them out under the right wall. After its patience inside (`PATIENCE_SEC` plus a seeded share of `PATIENCE_SPREAD_SEC`, so the escapes spread out and the seed moves the run's length) the director helps: a rebound off its ring is aimed at that ring's gap (`steerToGap()` flies every direction that leaves the wall inward through the engine's own motion – gravity and the cruising boost, the ring inside in the way, `steerFlight()` – and takes the one that comes back where the gap's middle will be, the ring turning step by step meanwhile; when none can, the one that leaves the gap a comfortable hop away), and in the gap the ball slips straight through (`onGapPass()`) – Gerald always escapes. Only the balls launched into the rings count for the engine's escape scan (`ConveyorMode.inRings()` → `scanEscapes()`): a ball at the hatch, on the belt or in the loading tube is outside the rings without having escaped them, so the first escape that question captions, split-screen races and Find Simulation read (`getFirstEscapeMs()`, `hasBallEscaped()`) is the first ball out of the rings – within a step or two of the counter's (the scan's margin is a few px wider). **bowl**: a U of obstacle segments (`BOWL_*`) sized by the balls, not the frame – its inside fits `BOWL_CAPACITY` balls of the average size (`conveyorBowlHalfWidth()`), heaped up about 30 before the first one rolls off, in every world shape (in a tall frame the rim moves up, at most `BOWL_FALL_DEPTHS` depths below the belt, so the drop never bounces a ball straight out) – where the pile settles (ball-to-ball hits are damped to `BALL_TO_BALL_RESTITUTION` in `onBallCollision()`, like Ball Drop's); a ball outside the bowl below its rim counts as overflow and rides the bottom belt out. **pegs**: a Galton board – staggered pegs with one under the drop point, each a note by its column (`conveyorPegPitch()`), a ramp from each wall onto the outermost peg of every full row (`RAMP_LIFT`) so no corner at a wall holds a ball, bins below; a ball in the bins has landed, one resting on a peg is nudged off (`PERCH_KICK`, at most `MAX_PERCHES` times).
- **Freeze and variety** – with the freeze on, a ball that lands freezes in place (bowl, pegs: at rest over a `REST_WINDOW_MS` window; rings: once its freedom of `FREEZE_SEC` has run out) and becomes an obstacle the mode resolves the others against (`resolveBallCircle()`; the engine's pair rebound against a frozen ball is turned into a static one). The variety spreads the balls' sizes ±`SIZE_SPREAD` around the Ball Size (`conveyorBallScale()`, through `radiusScale`, so a Ball Size change keeps the spread) and mixes Gerald's Ball Colour with a palette (`conveyorBallColor()`); the first ball is always Gerald.
- **The end** – once every ball has dropped and is out (rings: escaped or frozen, and carried away) or at rest (bowl, pegs) – at the latest `SETTLE_TIMEOUT_MS` after the last drop, when the banner says TIME'S UP (only at extreme settings: the narrowest gaps on the fastest rings) – the final banner holds for `FINAL_HOLD_MS` and the run finishes. The seed moves the run's length (the escapes, the bounces), so Find Simulation searches it (`ModeSettings.conveyor`).
- **Sound** – `src/lib/audio/conveyorTones.ts`: the belt's hum while it carries a ball (`scheduleConveyorHum()`: a low filtered sawtooth and its fifth with a slow wobble) and the click of a drop (`scheduleConveyorClick()`: a band-passed noise tick over a falling sine thunk), queued as `SoundEvent.conveyor` ("hum" with its length in `cvSec` – the first ball's with the run's first step, so a page plays nothing before its run starts – and "click") and played by `ToneGenerator.playConveyor()` – on the beat grid, the click ducking the music bed, never a melody note or a slice. The page's sound loop, the fast export's `playSoundEvent()` and the split-screen arenas' `playArenaSound()` route them. Every bounce is an ordinary hit – the rings' wall tones, the bowl's, the bins' and the floor's by the ball's size (`conveyorHitFrequency()`), the pegs' by their column, a frozen ball's tick, at most `MAX_NOTES_PER_STEP` a step – so melodies, instruments, hit samples, the beat lock and the slicer apply.
- **Respawn timer** – `respawnEvery` (seconds, 0 = off; URL `rse`, because `rs` is the Rotation Speed's) travels in `PhysicsConfig`, so the page's config effect, the seed finder (which copies the page engine's config), the fast export, the batch render and the split-screen arenas (`arenaPhysicsConfig()`, next to the moving exits and the splat barrier – a period set during a race reaches every arena) all run it. In Classic and Multiply (`RESPAWN_MODES`) the engine's `RespawnTimer` drops a new ball in from the top every N seconds of the simulation clock (`respawnDue()`), into the innermost ring still standing, falling at `RESPAWN_DROP_SPEED` × the Ball Speed in a seeded direction within `RESPAWN_SPREAD` of straight down, with the belt's click; the team slots take turns. Classic stops respawning once every ring is broken, and a run holds at most `RESPAWN_MAX_BALLS`. A new period set mid-run takes over at once: a shorter one drops one ball at once and never a backlog, a longer one goes on at its next multiple (1 s → 3 s at 10 s: 12, 15, 18 s) – the timer counts its respawns on the schedule of the period it last ran (`RespawnTimer.every`) and moves that count onto a new period's schedule, so it never waits for the new schedule to catch up with the old count. Nothing is drawn from the generator while the timer is off, so every run without it replays as before.
- **UI** – `components/simulator/conveyorRenderer.ts` (`ConveyorLayer`): the belts (the tread moving at the belt's speed, the rollers turning), the hatch (lighting up when a ball comes out), the drop lip, the loading tube, the bowl's glass and stand, the bins and the frost on the frozen balls; the title and the counter ("Loaded 17 / Escaped 4", "Overflow", "Landed", and the frozen count) at the top right of the exported square, where the belt leaves the field empty. The canvas adds the final banner (ALL OUT!, SETTLED!, LANDED!, or TIME'S UP) and mirrors `data-cv-*` (arena, balls, loaded, escaped, overflow, landed, frozen, carried, passes, notes, hums, clicks, the drop times, the end) through `ConveyorDataset` – and, with the respawn timer on, `data-respawns` and `data-respawn-balls`. The panel block is `sections/ConveyorSection.tsx` (arena, interval, balls, freeze, variety and a run summary; its keys are pushed into `SECTION_KEYS.ball`), and its `RespawnControl` is the Respawn Every slider of the Ball section in Classic and Multiply (the Ball section's reset turns it off, `sectionDefaults()`). Settings `cvInterval` (URL `cvi`), `cvMaxBalls` (`cvn`), `cvArena` (`cva`), `cvFreeze` (`cvf`), `cvVariety` (`cvv`) and `respawnEvery` (`rse`) are validated by `resolveConveyorFields()`; a change of the conveyor restarts the run and drops a found seed, the Sound section's scale and root follow live, and a new respawn period drops a found seed. The canvas words live in the `Conveyor` namespace of `messages/*.json`.
- **Tests** – `tests/conveyor.test.ts`: the settings, the URL and presets and the registration, the layout of every arena, the schedule (load and drop times on the simulation clock, at any frame rate), the counters (loaded, escaped, carried away; overflow; landed), freezing (the pile in the bowl, the rings' freedom running out, frozen balls as obstacles), the director's steering and its flight model, determinism, resizes, the end and the finder, the sounds and their synthesis, the escapes the engine counts (none while a ball rides the belt; a caption's answer and a split-screen race's mark at the first ball out of the rings), and the respawn timer (its schedule in Classic and Multiply, nothing while it is off, Classic stopping once every ring is broken, the config through the page's path, a longer or shorter period set mid-run); `tests/extras.test.ts` holds the fingerprint of the default run, and `tests/conveyorExitSplat.test.ts` runs the respawn timer together with the moving exits and the splat barrier (exact replays for a seed, the Conveyor Belt untouched by all three, the split-screen arenas following a new period – a longer one too –, the section resets putting all three back). The smoke test checks the card and the preview, the URL ↔ panel round trip and the search box, a short rings run with its counter and banner, the bowl with the freeze, the peg field, the finder, the respawn timer in Classic and a period lengthened mid-run in Multiply, a question caption that waits for the first ball out of the rings and a split-screen race decided by the arenas' first ring escapes.

### Bounce math
Feature bounce-math: "add more bounciness on every bounce – customisable, mathematical – for every parameter, some on the beat of a song".

- **Rules and maths** – `src/lib/simulation/bounceMath.ts` (pure, no DOM; `tests/bounceMath.test.ts`): a `BounceRule` is `{ param, trigger, every, op, amount, formula?, min?, max?, scope }` over `BOUNCE_PARAMS` (bounciness, speed, size, gravity, rotation, gap, thickness, damping, trail, hue, pitch, timeScale, wobble, balls), `BOUNCE_TRIGGERS` (bounce, pass, collide, break, beat, bar, second, start) and `BOUNCE_OPS` (add, subtract, multiply, divide, power, root, modulo, set, random, formula). `applyRule(value, rule, ctx)` runs the operation, clamps to the rule's own min / max (none by default – no upper limit, the owner's rule), rounds counts and then fits the parameter's domain (`BOUNCE_PARAM_DOMAINS`): NaN, ±Infinity or a value below what the parameter can mean (a negative or zero size, a zero speed…) is rejected and the value stays as it was; no ceiling clamps it any more (`FLOAT_CEILING` is the largest float, and the thickness, damping, time-scale and wobble domains are open above): only the gap stays within its ring (`MAX_GAP`, a gap wider than the ring means nothing) and a trail keeps at most `MAX_TRAIL_POINTS` = `TRAIL_POINT_CEILING` (200) points (memory: every ball draws its own every frame). A ball whose numbers overflow the float range anyway is put back at the centre at the Ball Speed (`SPEED_CEILING` is the float range itself). Formulas (`compileFormula()`): a recursive-descent parser for + − * / ^ ( ) (^ right-associative and above unary minus), v n t b r, pi and e, sin cos tan abs sqrt pow min max floor ceil round log exp clamp; it compiles once into a postfix program (`Int32Array` of op / argument pairs and a `Float64Array` of constants) evaluated per fire on its own preallocated stack – never `eval` or `Function`, no allocation. Errors come back with a code, a position and the name (`FormulaError`), which the panel translates. `serializeRules()` / `parseRules()` are the URL form (`bmr`: `param.trigger.every.op.amount[.min][.max][.scope]`, rules joined by `;`, at most `MAX_BOUNCE_RULES` = `BOUNCE_RULE_CEILING` (1,000) rules – the list's memory-safety ceiling – and any every-N from 1 up (no 1,000,000 cap); numbers – every N too – write their decimal point as `_` because `.` separates the fields, a formula is percent-encoded in the amount field, `all` is the only scope written; the parse is tolerant – an invalid rule is dropped, the rest stays). `bm` stays the beat markers' key. `BOUNCE_MATH_PRESETS` are the six presets, `addRule` / `updateRule` / `moveRule` / `removeRule` / `appendPreset` the panel's list edits, `rotateHue()` the colour shift.
- **Engine** – `src/lib/physics/bounceMathRuntime.ts` (`BounceMathRuntime`, owned by `PhysicsEngine`, wired into `engine.ts` in delimited `--- bounce-math ---` blocks). The rules travel in the physics config (`PhysicsConfig.bounceMath` = rules, the beat grid, the canvas values' starting points, Show values: `bounceMathConfigOf()`), so the seed finder, split-screen arenas, the batch renderer and the fast export – which all build their engines from `engine.config` – replay a rule run exactly. The hooks only queue a trigger (preallocated, allocation-free): the ring bounce and the obstacle / editor-obstacle hits (`BM_BOUNCE`), the gap pass (`notePass()`, once per pass), the ball pair and a merge (`BM_COLLIDE`, both balls), a mode's own bounces (`ctx.creditBounce()` – String Battle – and the new optional `ctx.noteBounce()` that Bouncing Shapes, Glass Smash and the Journey's glass stage call), and the step's "gap" sound events for wall breaks (paired with the balls `reportWallBreak()` / `ctx.creditWallBreak()` reported). The start trigger fires at the start of a run's first step; at the end of every step the beats and bars of the grid (`beatSchedule.ts`: the grid Beat Drop and Picture Paint follow – the page's `rhythmBeat`, i.e. the loaded song's beats, an imported video's or the hand-placed markers, else the Sound section's BPM; a bar is every beat whose index is a multiple of four) and the whole seconds that fell into the step are queued, and the queue applies in order – each trigger's rules in list order, every N-th time. Random numbers come from the engine's seeded `random()` (only "random" rules and formulas that read r draw). A run without rules never enters any of this and replays exactly as before (`tests/extras.test.ts` still reproduces every mode's recorded trajectory).
  - Per ball: bounciness is `ball.restitution` (a factor on the engine's ring rebounds; on the obstacles' restitution – applied after their 0.98 cap (`rebound(…, ballRestitution, liftSpeed)` in obstacles.ts), so it has no upper limit: a 2 turns a 0.7 peg into a 1.4 rebound, and what it adds is bounded by a lift speed – the ball's cruising speed × the restitution scale × the bounciness, what a ring rebound sets – so a ball bouncing between pegs settles there instead of gaining speed hit after hit (Ball Drop, Bullseye, the obstacle editor, Bullseye's stuck darts, a knock from below on Glass Smash's and the Journey's panes); on Glass Smash's and the Journey's pane landings it scales the hop (a 2 hops twice as fast, four times as high); and – at most once, never compounding – on a rebound a mode sets itself – Bouncing Shapes' mirror walls report theirs through `ctx.noteBounce(ball, true)` and each shape's own speed carries the bounciness exactly once; the step planner's rebound bound includes it); speed and size go through the multipliers (`MultiplierRuntime.apply()`: the speed scale sets the velocity at once and the cruising speed from then on; the size refits the ball into its corridor, bursting rings it no longer fits, and a ball bigger than the arena ends the run with the multipliers' OUTGREW THE ARENA finish – in a mode without rings it stops at 45 % of the canvas' smaller side), so the multipliers' planner sub-steps fast balls (up to 64 sub-steps, then time dilation) and nothing tunnels; hue turns `ball.color` (`hueShift`; white, black and greys – the default ball is white – have no hue of their own, so `rotateHue()` turns them from a saturated colour of a similar lightness and the shift always shows), pitch is `ball.pitchShift` – the ring and obstacle hits of the ball (and Bouncing Shapes' wall notes) carry the shifted pitch in the sound event's `frequency` (the existing pitch override), nothing else in the audio changes. Clones and split halves inherit them.
  - World: gravity, the ring spin and the gap size go into the engine's config (the gap in place, like a keyframed gap, in the modes whose rings are built from it); `engine.config` hands out the page's values meanwhile and the next run starts from them again; a page patch that merely re-sends the page's value keeps the rule's, a new value replaces it. Air drag, the clock scale (`clockScale()`: each step covers that much more simulated time, planned like a multiplier run – in the modes the engine integrates itself), the trail length (the engine's trail ring buffer, up to 200 points) and the canvas' wall thickness and wobble (read by `components/simulator/bounceMathRenderer.ts`, so the fast export draws them too) are held by the runtime; balls spawns copies of the ball (the multipliers' clone with a seeded turn) within Multiply's memory-safe ceiling of 200 balls (with No limits on the ceiling is lifted like Multiply's: the copies fill the run's full-physics balls and the rest join the crowd).
  - A parameter a mode has no use for is ignored there (`bounceParamApplies()`: bounciness, speed and size in the ten ring modes, Ball Drop, Bouncing Shapes, Glass Smash, Bullseye and Journey; the gap in Classic, Accumulation and Multiply; balls in the ring modes; the clock where the engine integrates the balls) and the panel marks the rule.
  - Every mode reports its bounces: the engine's rings and obstacles, and every mode that resolves its own contacts calls `ctx.noteBounce(ball)` – String Battle, Bouncing Shapes, Glass Smash and the Journey's panes, the Collision Playground's container, the Multipliers board's walls, pegs and bars, the arena games' and the race's walls and obstacles, Beat Drop's landings, Paddle Keep-Up's walls, ceiling and paddle, Power Layers' stack hits, Circle Illusion's contacts, Bullseye's stuck darts – and in the rhythm modes without contacts the ball's note counts as its bounce (a Pendulum Wave bob's swing note, a harp pluck, a Polyrhythm dot's turn, a Sound Vortex ring, Beat Runner's landings). Ball-to-ball hits a mode resolves itself go through `ctx.noteCollide(a, b)` (the Collision Playground's orbs and lollipops, the Multipliers board, the arena games' clashes, the sparring double pendulums), next to the engine's own pair pass. `bounceTriggerApplies()` says where a trigger can never fire – a gap pass outside the ring modes and the Journey, a ball hit where balls pass through each other, a wall break where nothing breaks – and the rule row shows `BounceMath.triggerNotInMode` (`data-applies="0"`).
- **Page** – `components/simulator/sections/BounceMathSection.tsx` (search keys `BOUNCE_MATH_KEYS` in `SECTION_KEYS.ball`): the rule rows (parameter, trigger, every N, operation, amount with a comfort slider or the formula with live validation and an example line, min / max, this ball / all balls, ↑ ↓ ✕), + Add rule, the preset picker, Clear, Show values and the live readout (polled from `engine.getBounceMathView()` ten times a second). Simulator.tsx sends `bounceMathConfigOf(settings, rhythmBeat)` in an effect (new rules – or a new beat while a rule may follow it – drop a found seed like new keyframes), carries the rules over to another mode and hands the arenas the page's config. `bounceMathRenderer.ts` draws the Show values badge in the recorded square (bottom-left above the page's buttons, the bottom text and the song bar; top-right below the scoreboard and SLOW-MO while bottom captions show) and mirrors `data-bm-*` (rules, fires, total, the last ball's bounciness / colour / speed / size, gravity, balls, clock, the badge's rectangle). Words live in `Controls.bounceMath*` and the `BounceMath` namespace of `messages/*.json`.
- **Add a parameter**: add it to `BOUNCE_PARAMS`, `BOUNCE_PARAM_KIND`, `BOUNCE_PARAM_DOMAINS` and `BOUNCE_PARAM_SCALE`, read and write it in `BounceMathRuntime.applyBall()` / `applyWorld()` (and in `bounceParamApplies()` if some modes ignore it), and add `BounceMath.param.<name>` to every messages file. **Add a trigger**: add it to `BOUNCE_TRIGGERS` (its code is its index), call `note()` where the engine or a mode sees it (or queue it in `endStep()` for a clock-driven one) and add `BounceMath.trigger.<name>` / `everyUnit.<name>`.
- **Tests and smoke** – `tests/bounceMath.test.ts`: the parser (precedence, functions, errors, no eval), every operation, min / max, the rejections and ceilings, the URL round trip (settings, presets, project files, share codes, next to `bm`), rule order, every trigger in the engine, a song's irregular grid and its offset, per-ball vs all, deterministic replays, world values per run, the finder's and the fast export's engines replaying a rule run, the outgrow finish, runaway values staying finite, sub-stepping, the ball ceiling, the pitch and the clock, Glass Smash hopping twice as fast with a bounciness of 2 (and the Journey's panes), Ball Drop and Bullseye bouncier than the obstacles' cap but never running away, the white ball turning on a colour shift, and the bounce trigger firing in every mode of `MODE_IDS` (ball hits where balls hit each other, `bounceTriggerApplies()` elsewhere); `tests/obstacles.test.ts` and `tests/obstacleEditor.test.ts` the bounciness after the cap and its lift. The smoke test loads a rule from a link, edits it and sees the link follow, plays "Bouncier every bounce" and sees the readout grow, fires a beat rule twice a second at 120 BPM, turns the default white ball's colour (`data-bm-color`), fires bounce and ball-hit rules in the Collision Playground and sees Ball Drop's panel mark a gap-pass rule.

### Publish (TikTok, Instagram, YouTube)
Feature social-publish: the owner asked to "add connections to tiktok and instagram and youtube so the user can upload and
send those videos quickly, be able to connect multiple users to those accounts and send them quickly". The site is a static
export, so there is no server of ours: the **Publish** block (`components/simulator/sections/PublishSection.tsx`, search keys
`PUBLISH_KEYS`, after the Viral video bot block of the Recording section) offers three delivery paths, and with nothing set up
it still offers the third and explains the other two in a line each.

- **The clip** – every finished video is handed to a small in-memory inbox (`lib/publish/clips.ts`, `offerPublishClip()`):
  Record Video and the fast export in `Simulator.tsx`, each clip of a batch or of the bot's render in `useBatchRender.ts`
  (a bot clip keeps its plan id, so its copy comes from the plan); **Choose a video…** adds a file. The newest 8 are kept; the
  block shows the name, length, size and a thumbnail (`lib/publish/thumbnail.ts`, a frame drawn from a detached `<video>`;
  MediaRecorder WebM files get their length by one seek past the end).
- **The words** – `lib/publish/copy.ts` writes the first draft from the viral bot's copy generator: a bot clip's hook, series
  line, question, keywords and hashtags; any other clip the copy of the bot recipe that plays in its mode (keywords, question,
  its hashtags) under a title built from the mode's name (`Publish.defaultTitle` / `defaultCaption`). `lib/publish/caption.ts`
  keeps one shared draft plus per-platform overrides (the tabs; a field set back to the shared text stops being an override)
  and composes each platform's post: TikTok and Instagram a caption ending in the hashtags, YouTube a title (the caption's
  first line when empty), a description and tags (the hashtags without "#", within 500 characters); every platform gets its
  own tag (#fyp, #reels, #Shorts – first on YouTube) and loses the others'. Counters per limit (TikTok 2,200 UTF-16 units;
  Instagram 2,200 characters and 30 hashtags; YouTube a 100-character title and a 5,000-byte description without `<` `>`,
  at most 60 hashtags); a platform over a limit blocks the send and says which.
- **Path A – YouTube straight from the browser** (`lib/publish/youtube.ts`): Google Identity Services
  (`https://accounts.google.com/gsi/client`, loaded when the block mounts with a client ID, so the click can open its popup
  at once) asks for an access token with `youtube.upload` (and `youtube.readonly`, for the channel's name and avatar from
  `youtube.channels?mine=true`); **Connect YouTube** opens Google's account picker, so any number of Google accounts /
  channels can be added. The upload is the resumable protocol: a POST to `upload/youtube/v3/videos?uploadType=resumable`
  with the video resource (title, description with hashtags, tags, `privacyStatus` public / unlisted / private,
  `selfDeclaredMadeForKids: false`, category Entertainment) opens a session, PUTs send 8 MiB chunks (a multiple of 256 KiB),
  a `308` with `Range` says where to go on, a dropped connection or a 5xx asks the session (`Content-Range: bytes */N`) and
  resumes after a back-off (5 retries); the result is the Short's link. Tokens (about an hour) live in localStorage per
  account; browser apps get no refresh token, so an expired one shows **Sign in again** (a popup, then that upload goes on).
  - **Set it up**: [Google Cloud console](https://console.cloud.google.com) → a project → *APIs & Services → Library* →
    enable **YouTube Data API v3**; *OAuth consent screen* → External, add the scopes `…/auth/youtube.upload` and
    `…/auth/youtube.readonly`, add yourself (and anyone who will connect) as **Test users**; *Credentials → Create
    credentials → OAuth client ID → Web application* → **Authorized JavaScript origins**: the site's origin without a path
    (`https://jumpingballslive.com` and `https://www.jumpingballslive.com` for this repository, `http://localhost:3000` for local runs) – no redirect URI is needed.
    Put the client ID (it is public) in the block's **YouTube app setup** or build with `NEXT_PUBLIC_YOUTUBE_CLIENT_ID` (on
    the live site: the repository variable `YOUTUBE_CLIENT_ID`, passed by `deploy.yml`). Until Google verifies the app it works
    for its test users, and videos uploaded by an unaudited API project are locked to private (request the YouTube API
    Services audit to upload publicly); an upload costs about 1,600 of the default 10,000 daily quota units.
- **Path B – the relay** (`relay/`, see [relay/README.md](relay/README.md)): TikTok's Login Kit and Content Posting API need
  a client secret at the token exchange (and an audited app to post publicly), and Instagram publishes Reels only from a
  public video URL – so a small self-hosted server does those parts. `relay/server.mjs` is one Node 22 ESM file without
  dependencies (not part of the site: excluded from `tsconfig.json` and ESLint; `relay/Dockerfile`, `relay/package.json`,
  `relay/.env.example`); it holds the app secrets in its environment, runs the OAuth sign-ins (TikTok Login Kit v2 with
  `user.info.basic` + `video.publish`; Instagram through Facebook Login for Business with `instagram_basic` +
  `instagram_content_publish` on the Instagram professional account linked to a Page – one sign-in adds every Page's account –
  or, with `IG_LOGIN=instagram`, the Instagram API with Instagram Login and `instagram_business_basic` +
  `instagram_business_content_publish`; Google for YouTube with offline access), keeps the accounts and refresh tokens in a
  JSON file (`RELAY_DATA_DIR`), and publishes: TikTok direct post (creator info, `video/init` with `FILE_UPLOAD` in chunks – or
  `PULL_FROM_URL` from the relay's `/media/` URL with `TIKTOK_SOURCE=pull` –, status polling; an unaudited app's public post is
  refused by TikTok, so it posts privately and says so), Instagram (the flow of `src/lib/bot/instagram.ts`: a `REELS` container
  from the clip's public relay URL, `status_code` polling, `media_publish`, the permalink – the relay's request builders are
  tested against that module's), YouTube (the resumable upload); retries on network errors, 429 and 5xx, refreshed tokens, and
  errors that say what to do ("this Meta app is not yet approved…", "connect the account again", quota, format). **Multi-user**:
  `RELAY_ADMIN_KEY` creates **access keys** (`POST /api/keys` with a label, or `node relay/server.mjs create-key <label>`);
  every key sees and posts only to the accounts it connected, so a team or several creators share one relay. The page's side
  is `lib/publish/relayClient.ts`: in the block's **Relay** settings you save **relay profiles** (URL + key + label; **Test**
  shows the key's label, its accounts and the relay's apps) and switch between them; **Connect TikTok / Instagram / via
  relay** opens a popup on a one-time sign-in link (`POST /api/connect/:platform` → `GET /connect/:platform` → the platform →
  `/oauth/:platform/callback`, which stores the account and `postMessage`s the page before closing; the new accounts come
  ticked, and the list also reloads when the page gets the focus back); **Send to selected**
  uploads the clip once (`POST /api/publish`, multipart with the per-platform words; XMLHttpRequest for the upload progress)
  and polls `GET /api/jobs/:id` for every account's status, progress, link or error. The relay never returns a token. Deploy it
  on Render, Fly.io, Railway or any Docker host – the steps, the TikTok developer, Meta app and Google Cloud settings, the
  scopes and the redirect URIs (`<relay>/oauth/<platform>/callback`) are in [relay/README.md](relay/README.md). In short:
  - **Deploy**: on [Render](https://render.com) a *Web Service* from this repository with Root Directory `relay`, Start
    Command `node server.mjs` and the variables of `relay/.env.example` – `RELAY_PUBLIC_URL` (its `https://…onrender.com`
    address), `RELAY_ALLOWED_ORIGINS=https://jumpingballslive.com,https://www.jumpingballslive.com`, `RELAY_ADMIN_KEY`, the app keys, and `RELAY_DATA_DIR` on a
    persistent disk (Fly.io / Railway / any Docker host: `relay/Dockerfile`, a volume at `/data`; locally `node relay/server.mjs`).
  - **Keys for several people**: `curl -X POST <relay>/api/keys -H "Authorization: Bearer $RELAY_ADMIN_KEY" -d '{"label":"Alice"}'`
    prints a key once (`GET /api/keys` lists them, `DELETE /api/keys/:id` revokes one and forgets its accounts); each person
    adds the relay URL + their key under **Relay** in the block and connects their own accounts.
  - **TikTok**: [developers.tiktok.com](https://developers.tiktok.com) → Manage apps → Connect an app; add **Login Kit** and
    the **Content Posting API** (Direct Post on); scopes `user.info.basic` + `video.publish`; redirect URI
    `<relay>/oauth/tiktok/callback`; client key / secret → `TIKTOK_CLIENT_KEY` / `TIKTOK_CLIENT_SECRET`. Until TikTok audits
    the app only its sandbox target users can connect and posts are private ("Only me").
  - **Instagram**: [developers.facebook.com](https://developers.facebook.com) → Create App → **Business**; add **Facebook
    Login for Business** with the redirect URI `<relay>/oauth/instagram/callback` and the permissions `instagram_basic`,
    `instagram_content_publish`, `pages_show_list`, `pages_read_engagement`, `business_management` (or, with
    `IG_LOGIN=instagram`, the Instagram product's business login with `instagram_business_basic` +
    `instagram_business_content_publish`); link each Instagram professional account to a Facebook Page; app ID / secret →
    `IG_APP_ID` / `IG_APP_SECRET`. In development mode only people with a role on the app can connect; App Review
    (Advanced Access for the publish permission) opens it to everyone.
  - **YouTube through the relay** (optional; refresh tokens keep channels signed in): a Google OAuth *Web application* client
    with the redirect URI `<relay>/oauth/youtube/callback` → `YT_CLIENT_ID` / `YT_CLIENT_SECRET`.
- **Path C – quick share** (`lib/publish/share.ts`): **Send to TikTok / Instagram / YouTube** – on a phone or tablet the Web
  Share API gets the video file itself (`navigator.canShare({ files })`; the system sheet lists the installed TikTok, Instagram
  and YouTube apps; the caption goes to the clipboard first, since most apps drop shared text); on a computer the clip
  downloads, the caption and hashtags go to the clipboard and the platform's upload page opens in a new tab
  (`https://www.tiktok.com/upload`, `https://www.instagram.com/`, `https://www.youtube.com/upload`), with a one-line hint.
- **Send to selected** – `lib/publish/controller.ts` holds the block's state outside React (read with
  `useSyncExternalStore`), so a send keeps going and its progress stays when the Recording section closes: every ticked
  account at once – each direct YouTube channel uploads on its own, the relay accounts of the relay in use go in one job –
  with per-account progress, result links, **Try again** and **Sign in again**; each finished account lands in the **recent
  sends** (localStorage, the last 50, with links). **Try again** re-sends what that account was sent with – its clip, words
  and visibility, kept per account until the next send – whichever clip is on show by then (a recording, a fast export or a
  batch may have offered new ones, or another may be picked); a clip that has left the list, or a relay account of another
  relay profile than the one in use, gets a message instead (`Publish.errors.page`). **Sign in again** carries on with an
  upload only while its send row is on show and waiting for it: a new send (which replaces the rows), removing the channel or
  removing the clip forgets it, so renewing a channel from its account row never uploads a clip of an earlier, abandoned
  send. **Visibility**: Instagram Reels are always public, so with *Unlisted* or *Private* chosen and Instagram accounts
  ticked the block holds the send back and says so (`Publish.instagramPublicOnly`: choose Public or untick them) – and the
  relay refuses such a Reel too (code `visibility`), for the CLI and any other client; TikTok has no unlisted, so *Unlisted*
  posts for mutual friends (Friends), which the block says before the send (`Publish.tiktokUnlisted`) and the relay's result
  note after it. The relay uploads a TikTok clip whole up to 64 MB and in 10 MB chunks above that (the remainder in the last
  one), as TikTok's Media Transfer Guide asks. What the browser remembers (`lib/publish/store.ts`, one localStorage key:
  the client ID, the YouTube accounts and their tokens, the relay profiles and keys, the visibility, the ticks, the log) is
  validated on load and never put in the URL, presets or project files; a client secret never reaches the page.
- **The bot CLI** – `node scripts/viral-bot.mjs --relay https://my-relay.example --relay-key $KEY --accounts a_1,a_2`
  (or `--accounts all`) posts every rendered clip through the relay (the hook as the title, the caption without its hashtag
  line, the hashtags for the relay to compose per platform) and prints each account's result; with `--post-only` it sends
  what `--out` holds. The relay client is bundled into the CLI's planner (`src/lib/bot/node.ts`).
- **Strings** – the `Publish` namespace of `messages/*.json` (plus `Controls.publish*` for the block's label and search, and
  `Privacy.publishing` for the privacy page's section on what is sent where and the YouTube API Services terms).
- **Tests and checks** – `tests/publish.test.ts` (hashtag formatting and the limits of every platform, overrides, the first
  draft in three languages, the relay client against a mocked fetch – info, accounts, connect link, publish form, job polling,
  errors –, the quick share with a fake `navigator.share` / `canShare` and a fake document, the YouTube token, channel and
  resumable upload – session, chunked PUTs, resume after a 308 and after a dropped connection or a 503 –, the stored state,
  the clip inbox, a whole Send to selected through the controller, a relay sign-in popup with a fake window – the accounts it
  added ticked, a reload when the page gets the focus back (a sign-in page whose Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy cuts the popup
  off never reports back), a failed sign-in's message kept – the thumbnail read only for the clip on show, **Try again**
  re-sending the failed account's own clip, words and visibility after a new clip arrived (and saying so when the clip has
  left the list or the relay profile changed), **Sign in again** from the account row never uploading a clip of an earlier
  send, the Instagram public-only rule and TikTok's unlisted note, the CLI's `--relay` options and posting);
  `relay/relay.test.mjs` (run by `npx vitest run relay`: the key / account store, the one-time OAuth states, the multipart
  parser, TikTok's chunking (whole up to 64 MB – a 7 MB clip is one chunk –, 10 MB chunks above, the guide's rules for every
  size), the words per platform, the Instagram request builders against `instagram.ts`, the callback page,
  CORS and keys over HTTP, the TikTok / Instagram / YouTube sign-ins, a publish to all three – TikTok's unaudited fallback,
  one Instagram account failing, a YouTube resume – with the clip served at its public URL, an unlisted / private send – the
  Reel refused, TikTok's Friends with its note –, an expired refresh token, WebM for Instagram and the upload limit). The smoke test renders the block with nothing configured (the three paths explained),
  shares a clip to a stubbed `navigator.share` on a phone, downloads it and opens the upload page on a computer, lists a
  relay profile's accounts from a stubbed relay, connects a YouTube channel through a stubbed token client and uploads to a
  stubbed endpoint with a 308 resume and progress, holds the send back while Unlisted is chosen with an Instagram account
  ticked, sends to relay and YouTube accounts in one click, re-sends a failed account's own clip after another clip arrived
  (Try again), and offers a fast export's clip to the block.

### User accounts
Feature user-accounts: the owner asked to "allow the user to make an account and connect all other accounts like YouTube, TikTok
and Instagram, allow them to connect to multiple of those, and build it into the exe file and website". The site is a static
export, so the account lives on the relay (`relay/server.mjs`, the same server that already holds the platform app secrets – the
steps to host it are in [relay/README.md](relay/README.md)); the page, the installed PWA and the Windows app all talk to it.

- **The relay** – `POST /api/auth/signup` / `login` / `logout` / `password` and `DELETE /api/auth/user`, plus `GET /api/me`
  returning the signed-in `user` and `GET /api/health` saying whether sign-up is open (`signup`). A user is a record with its own
  *owner key* (no secret of its own); a sign-up or sign-in returns a **session token** (`jbs_…`, 30 days, `RELAY_SESSION_DAYS`)
  that `requireKey()` resolves to that owner key, so every existing endpoint – accounts, one-time connect links, publishing, jobs –
  works for a session exactly as for an access key, and a user's TikTok, Instagram and YouTube accounts (any number each) follow them
  to every device. Passwords: scrypt (N 16384) with a random salt, an unknown email costs as much time as a wrong password; sessions
  are kept as SHA-256 only; at most 20 per user. Limits: 10 sign-ups an hour per address, 30 sign-ins per 15 minutes per address
  and 10 per 15 minutes per email, 10 password checks per 15 minutes per user (`RateLimiter`; the address is `clientIpOf()`: the
  header named by `RELAY_CLIENT_IP_HEADER` – on Render `CF-Connecting-IP` – else `X-Forwarded-For` counted from the right by
  `RELAY_PROXY_HOPS`, never the leftmost entry; `RELAY_TRUST_PROXY=0` uses the socket's address). `RELAY_SIGNUP=off` closes sign-up; the Windows app's origin
  `app://jumpingballslive` is always allowed (`RELAY_ALLOW_APP=0` turns that off), so the exe needs no extra relay setting.
- **The page's side** (`src/lib/account/`) – `client.ts` (`AccountClient`: health, sign up, sign in, me, sign out, change
  password, delete; relay errors become codes the page translates), `store.ts` (the session – relay address, token, user – in
  one localStorage key, validated on load, never the password), `controller.ts` (`AccountController`, outside React, read with
  `useSyncExternalStore` through `hooks.ts`: it signs in, checks a stored session against `/api/me` – a 401 signs out, a dropped
  connection keeps it –, and hands the session to the Publish feature as the relay profile `account` labelled with the email, selecting
  it, so the channels, the tick boxes, **Send to selected** and the desktop Library's Publish use it with no further set-up;
  signing out or deleting the account removes that profile) and `hint.ts` (the navbar's one-read peek at who is signed in,
  with nothing of the Publish feature loaded).
- **The UI** – `/account` (`app/[locale]/account/page.tsx`, `components/account/AccountPanel.tsx`, the `Account` namespace in
  English, Polish and Spanish; not indexed, not in the sitemap): **Create account** / **Sign in** tabs, **Server** (the relay's
  address – the build's `NEXT_PUBLIC_PUBLISH_RELAY_URL`, or one typed in for a self-hosted relay, opened by default when the
  build has none), then signed in: who, **Sign out**, **Connected channels** (TikTok, Instagram, YouTube cards with the accounts,
  **Connect** / **Add another** – a button is disabled with a note when the relay has no app keys for that platform –,
  Reconnect for an expired account, Disconnect), a pointer to the studio, and **Security** (change the password – ends the
  other devices' sessions – and delete the account, which forgets every connected channel). The navbar shows the signed-in
  name (or "Account"); the Publish block says who is signed in and opens the same panel in a dialog, so a clip waiting there is
  not lost to a page change.
- **The Windows app** – the same page (`app://jumpingballslive/<locale>/account/`); the platform sign-ins use the existing
  `jbl-relay-connect` popup (`windowOpenAction()`). `desktop.yml` passes the repository variable `PUBLISH_RELAY_URL` as
  `NEXT_PUBLIC_PUBLISH_RELAY_URL` to the app's site build (as `deploy.yml` does for the website); without it the Account page
  asks for the relay's address.
- **Set it up** – host the relay ([relay/README.md](relay/README.md): Render, Fly.io, Railway, any Docker host or your own VPS
  with `relay/deploy/install.sh` – Docker Compose and Caddy for HTTPS, one command, a nightly backup; with `--use-existing-caddy` it
  joins a Caddy that already serves other sites on the server, backed up, validated, checked before and after and rolled back on any
  difference – and in every case a persistent disk for `RELAY_DATA_DIR`), register the TikTok, Meta and Google apps' redirect URIs (`<relay>/oauth/<platform>/callback`), set
  `RELAY_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` to the site's origin and the repository variable `PUBLISH_RELAY_URL` to the relay's address, and
  redeploy the site and the app. Free play never needs an account; the privacy page says what an account stores.
- **Tests and checks** – `tests/account.test.ts` (the client against a scripted relay, the stored session, the controller – sign up,
  bad input never sent, a typed-in server, a stored session restored and checked, a dead session signed out and an offline one kept,
  sign out with the relay down, change password, delete – and the messages' keys and placeholders in all three languages) and the
  "user accounts" suite of `relay/relay.test.mjs` (password hashing, the limiter, sign-up and its refusals, sign-in and the
  guess limit, sign-out of one session, the password change ending the others, deleting the account with its connected channels,
  sessions expiring and surviving a restart). Run with `npx vitest run tests/account.test.ts relay`.

### Runtime budgets

`src/components/simulator/renderBudget.ts` keeps the canvas loop's cost flat over long runs and on any display: the loop
draws at most 60 frames a second on the rAF timestamps (`FrameGate`; a 75 / 90 / 144 Hz display draws 60, a late 60 Hz
callback is not dropped), and Record Video copies each drawn frame once (`captureStream(0)` + `requestFrame()`); ball sprites
are cached per colour and power-of-two size, one glow sprite per colour, at most 64 each; classic Paint strokes only the new
points into a trail layer (a finished run records no more), and the Lines / Grow strings go out in one path per colour.
Leaving the simulator (an in-app link) stops a recording and closes the audio. The pages hand the client only the message
namespaces their client components read (`src/i18n/clientMessages.ts`; the simulator page gets the whole catalogue).

### Uncapped
The owner's direction: *"make all parameters uncapped … so it goes so fast it can break the web app"*. Nothing a setting can hold is refused for being too big – only for being invalid – and whatever the values do to the physics, the tab keeps responding, the recording keeps rolling and the run keeps going, slower and cheaper to draw if it has to (feature uncap-all, built on No limits).

- **Every value, no maximum** (`src/lib/uncap.ts`, `src/lib/unlimited.ts`, wired into `settings.ts` in delimited blocks): `RANGES` are the sliders' **comfort ranges** only. Every numeric setting with a range is uncapped whether the Wide sliders switch is on or off – `SEMANTIC_MAX` is empty and `BOUNDED_KEYS` holds only `INDEX_KEYS`, the list indices (the Rigged forced winner's team slot: a slot past the team colours names nothing, so it is invalid, not capped). Values past the meaning of their slider act as far as physics allows: an air drag of 1 or more stops the balls each step (never reverses them), a camera zoom past 1 zooms further in, a squash past 1 flattens the ball (never flips it), a music or hit-sample volume past 1 amplifies (and may clip), walls breathing past 100 % shrink to a point. Parsing rejects only invalid values: NaN, ±Infinity, text, a negative or too small value where that means nothing (below the range's minimum, which a setting's own reader lifts onto the minimum as before; `SIGNED_KEYS` – the winds, the pendulum's start angles, arena seeds – take any sign). Every resolver of every mode floors at the minimum and never caps (`atLeastMin()`, `uncapped()`, `signedUncapped()` replace the clamp helpers). Links: `writeUnlimitedParams()` / `readUnlimitedParams()` run after the normal writers and readers whatever the switch – a value past its range goes under the setting's own URL key (the core `NUMERIC_URL_KEYS`, `nb`, and whatever key a feature writer puts a probe value under, `discoverUrlKeys()`), anything without one in `infx=key:value,…`, and a value a writer rounded to three decimals is written again exactly; presets (`restoreUnlimitedValues()`), project files (`resolveProjectSettings()`: any value from the slider's minimum up, a signed one and the Rigged forced winner as they are) and share codes (they carry the link) keep the typed value exactly. `uncappedEngaged(settings)` tells the page whether a value **its run's engine reads** is past its slider – `engineSettingKeys(mode)`: the core keys with the physics extras (`CORE_ENGINE_KEYS`), in the ring modes the obstacle editor's, the multiplier pickups' and On beat's (`RING_ENGINE_KEYS`) – an obstacle past its row sliders included –, and the mode's own `MODE_ENGINE_KEYS` (without the keys of a sub-rig the run does not build, `inactiveEngineKeys()`: the Double Pendulum's chaos-fan numbers on a harp run, the harp rig's on a fan run) – so a long clip, a big caption text or another mode's settings never engage the runtime or raise a badge.
- **The number field** (`src/components/simulator/NumberField.tsx`, its behaviour the pure `numberFieldReduce()` of uncap.ts): a text input (`inputMode="decimal"`, no `type` attribute, so selectors of a card's own text fields stay unique); `parseTypedNumber()` reads `1e6`, `.5`, `1,5` (decimal comma), `1 000 000` / `1_000_000` / `1,000,000` and the HUD's short forms (`2.5k`, `1.2M`, `3B`); typing only changes the draft, Enter and leaving the field commit a valid value, ↑/↓ step it (the slider's step inside the range, about a tenth of the value beyond it; Shift ×10, Alt ÷10), Escape drops the draft; invalid text is never committed – the old value stays and a small alert says why (`Uncap.err*`). The shared `Slider` (ControlPrimitives.tsx) renders it next to every slider of every section, always; the track keeps its comfort range and pins at its end for a value beyond it (a faint amber ring and the `Uncap.beyondTip` tooltip, the readout in short form: 1.2M), and a value past its memory-safety ceiling gets a note under it. The numeric controls that are not `Slider`s got the same field: the BPM (Sound and Picture Paint), the clip length, the overlay text size, the rotation speed (`TimelineRangeInput`), a keyframe's value and every keyframe row's time and value (↑/↓ step on past the slider, a moved row keeps the focus), Find Simulation's escape time, the split-screen arena overrides, the obstacle editor's rows (a position, an angle and a spin are signed), the Multipliers board's six gate weights, bounce math's every N, amount and min / max (an optional variant: an emptied field clears the bound – `onClear`, `NumberRules.optional`), the batch count and the sweep's ends and steps, the bot's clip count, the desktop queue's random-seed count, the fast export's frame rate. The field's `aria-label` is `Uncap.fieldLabel`, its error is announced (`role="alert"`, `aria-invalid`), and the `Searchable` wrapper still matches the control by its label.
- **Bounciness** (the uncapped Bouncier): the Bouncier switch is the numeric setting `bounciness` (URL `bnc`, default 1 = off; `bouncierEnabled` follows it, so every reader of the switch keeps working, and an old link's `bounce=1` or an old preset's switch reads as **1.03**, the step the old Bouncier added). Every wall bounce adds (Bounciness − 1) × the Ball Speed to the rebound multiplier with **no ceiling** (the old ×3 is gone; a gap pass or a wall break resets it, as always; Accumulation's frozen-ball bounces add the same gain). Up to the old ×3 the engine takes the sub-steps it always took (old runs replay exactly); past it the run engages the planned steps (`UnlimitedRuntime.engage()`): enough sub-steps that no ball moves more than half its radius, up to 64, and time dilation beyond – the run slows down instead of clamping, so collisions stay correct at any speed. The canvas shows the fastest ball's speed (**⚡ 12.3K PX/S**, `uncapRenderer.ts`, `data-uncap-speed`) climbing.
- **The engine** (`src/lib/physics/limits.ts`): there is no switch any more – the runtime engages by itself when the page's config says a value is past its slider (`PhysicsConfig.unlimited` = `uncappedEngaged()`), when a core value is past its comfort range in an engine built without the page (`coreBeyondComfort()`, `CORE_COMFORT` – a test keeps it equal to `RANGES`) or when a Bounciness grows the rebounds past ×3. At the defaults nothing engages and the engine takes its old code paths (`tests/extras.test.ts` still replays every mode's recorded trajectory). Speeds no mode clamps any more reach the step planner instead: the Collision Playground and the Journey report a ball past their old speed caps (`GameMode.stepSpeedBound()`), and the obstacle editor's kicks and flings bound `planStep()` once one went past the old obstacle limit – the planned sub-steps (time dilation past 64) keep such a ball from tunnelling, and runs that never got there replay exactly. The soft ceilings are gone: `ceilPatch()` only drops non-finite values and builds at most `LIVE_WALL_LIMIT` rings; speeds, sizes, gravity, rotation, thickness, the extras and the split limits run as typed; `MAX_SAFE_SPEED` is the float range itself, `MAX_EFFECTIVE_BOUNCE` is Infinity (a bounce multiplier scales every rebound), `MULTIPLIER_CEILING` is the largest float; a ring breathing past ±100 % shrinks to a point (radius 0), never below. A ball whose numbers overflow the float range is put back at the centre (the others of the same step on a golden-angle spiral around it) and counted: **NUMBERS OVERFLOWED ×n**.
- **Memory-safety ceilings** (`MEMORY_CEILINGS` = `ENGINE_CEILINGS`, the only ceilings left): what a value *allocates* stops at a value a tab holds comfortably – crowd balls `CROWD_BALL_CEILING` (1,000,000 in typed arrays), rings `RING_CEILING` (10,000), a mode's entities `ENTITY_CEILING` (5,000: pendulums, metronomes, shapes, orbs, panes, layers, spikes, Target numbers, race screens – every lap's together…), the race's racers `RACER_CEILING` (256, as for the other pairwise modes: its per-racer state and its roster of names and colours are sized for the grid at init, the colours past the palette golden-angle hues), the Circle Illusion's nested depth (`ilDepth`, `ENTITY_CEILING`), Color Match's colours (`ENTITY_CEILING`; past the palette's seven, golden-angle hues), the bodies of the pairwise modes `PAIRWISE_BODY_CEILING` (256: String Battle balls, arena-game squares), String Battle strings (1,024), Journey stages `JOURNEY_STAGE_CEILING` (500 – the auto count, a typed or linked list; the panel's add button stops there), Glass Smash rows / stages (1,000, at most 5,000 panes in all), pendulum chains (64 links, 2,048 bobs in all), split-screen arenas `ARENA_CEILING` (36, each a whole engine), keyframes `KEYFRAME_CEILING` (2,000), the obstacle editor's obstacles `OBSTACLE_CEILING` (1,000) and captions `CAPTION_CEILING` (1,000) – list settings, their length is what allocates (`LIST_CEILING_KEYS`) –, bounce math's rules `BOUNCE_RULE_CEILING` (1,000) and trail points `TRAIL_POINT_CEILING` (200 a ball), batch and bot clips `CLIP_CEILING` (50, held in memory), the desktop render queue's jobs `MAX_QUEUE_JOBS` (500: a random-seed count past it draws that many seeds and says so), frames of one export `EXPORT_FRAME_CEILING` (216,000). The typed value stays in the settings, the link and the panel (`memoryCeiling()` applies where a mode builds; `pastAnyMemoryCeiling()` – the mode's engine keys only – → `PhysicsConfig.memoryFull`), and the canvas says **ARENA FULL**. Three counts also have a lower soft ceiling of what a frame can draw (`src/lib/physics/softCeilings.ts`, a notice under the canvas): 10,000 rings (the same as `RING_CEILING`), 100 Target segments and 360 Accumulation spikes.
- **The crowd, the levels of detail and the ate-the-arena finish** (from No limits: `crowd.ts`, `unlimitedRenderer.ts`, `gulpTone.ts`): a Ball Count past the team balls, Multiply's children and x2 BALLS clones past the full-physics balls (`OBJECT_BALL_LIMIT`, fewer the more rings they bounce in: `objectLimitFor()`) are crowd balls in typed arrays (~23 bytes each) that move through their ring lanes (O(1) a ball, no tunnelling at any speed) and pour in `CROWD_POUR_PER_STEP` (50,000) a step; from `LOD_PLAIN_FROM` (2,000) balls the full-physics balls are plain discs batched per colour, from `LOD_POINTS_FROM` (20,000) the crowd is points in one image, wall-break effects are drawn `EFFECT_RENDER_CAP` (48) at a time and at most `MAX_SOUNDS_PER_FRAME` (24) sounds reach the synth a frame (`thinSounds()`: the ate-the-arena gulp always – it is queued after the step's bursts, so a ball eating a thousand rings would otherwise lose it –, then breaks, then bounces); the badges (ball count, **x0.4 REAL TIME**, **ARENA FULL**) sit bottom right of the recorded square and `data-unlimited-*` attributes mirror them; a ball bigger than the arena ends the run with **THE BALL ATE THE ARENA** and a gulp.
- **The modes past their sliders** (from the No limits review): where a mode sized its arrays for its slider – the Sound Vortex's balls and rings, Bullseye's shots and rings (a ring index past 127 too), String Battle's kill record past six balls – a run past it grows them once at init, before the seed's draws; caps that only mattered past the slider follow the setting (Glass Smash's 30 panes a stage and 7 hit points – a pane keeps its newest `MAX_PANE_CRACKS` cracks and is drawn at most as thick as the slider's thickest), Power Layers past the stack's pixel rows draws one band per row, the race track's screens and laps follow the settings (`resolveRaceTrackSettings()`, every lap's rows together at most `RACE_SCREEN_CEILING` screens: `raceLapsWithin()`), the obstacle editor's bumper boost runs as typed, and with the runtime engaged a Collision Playground pile crushed by absurd forces resolves at most `COLLIDE_PAIR_BUDGET` (40,000) contacts a sub-step. The modes' own scale helpers floor and never cap: Glass Smash's (and the Journey's glass) gravity, the race's tempo and gravity, the Beat Runner's gravity, Paddle Keep-Up's ball size and gravity (a ball too wide for the paddle's reach outgrows the arena), Territory's balls (`territoryBallRadius()`: past the old 0.9 tiles a ball tests its contact edge; wider than the board, it ate the arena), the arena games' squares (as big as typed while they fit, else as big as fits with ARENA FULL), the Circle Illusion's nested circles and painter (an innermost circle past the arena outgrows it), Glass Smash's size gates (a ball wider than the shaft outgrows it); the camera's shake and slow motion, the On beat range, the BPM grid, the hit-sample volume and Color Match's colour count follow their values too. The panel's run summaries (Glass Smash, Power Layers) resolve the settings the way the engine does.
- **Graceful degradation** (so the tab never hard-freezes): the frame budget (`frameBudget.ts`, whole steps only), the planned steps and their bounds (`STEP_WORK_CAP`, `STEP_RING_WORK`), the crowd, dense rings resolved one rebound a pass, the spatial hash's candidate budgets (`PAIR_CANDIDATE_BUDGET` a query, `PAIR_STEP_BUDGET` a planned step – a pile-up of thousands of discs skips the rest of its pairs for a sub-step), a Pendulum Wave bob's sound events per step (`EVENTS_PER_WINDOW`) and the Circle Illusion's rim touches per step (the last cycle's), the harp's notes read by index (a billion octaves allocate nothing), the ring pass drawing every k-th ring past `RING_DRAW_LIMIT` (1,500) and every width stroked no wider than `DRAW_EXTENT_PX` (the same picture on any canvas). All of it depends on the simulation state only, so a seed replays exactly.
- **Wide sliders** (`sections/UnlimitedSection.tsx`, `unlimitedSlider.tsx`): the old Unlimited switch, kept for its URL key `inf` – with it on the slider of an uncapped setting (found by its range object, `RANGES.<key>`) is linear across its comfort range and logarithmic above it up to `UNLIMITED_SLIDER_CEILING` (1B); off, the track pins at its end. It changes no value either way.
- **Find Simulation**: an engaged run's run-length search is time-sliced (`unlimitedFinder.ts`, fewer seeds on a heavy run – `limitedSeeds`; it keeps its own 60 s budget and never searches deeper than `maxSeeds` – the plain search's deeper pass, `FINDER_DEPTH_BUDGET_MS`, is for cheap runs), and the headless requests search the run the page renders – `physicsConfigOfSettings()` (the bot, the desktop AI's `find_simulation`) carries the page's `uncapConfigOf()` (engaged, the crowd, ARENA FULL) when a value is past its slider, and the desktop studio's search is the page's budgeted one; a search in which no tested seed ended first runs one seed over a long horizon (`confirmRunEnds()`: ten times the target, at least `NEVER_ENDS_CONFIRM_SEC` = 600 s of simulation, at most 20 s of wall time, time-sliced) – a Paint or Target run that merely outlasts the search's window is reported with its real length – and only a run that still never ends says so (`FinderResult.neverEnded`, the `Uncap.finderEndless` note) instead of quoting a length.
- **Fast export and split-screen**: the export's frame rate is any rate from 30 up (`resolveFastExportFps()`, the number field next to the 30 / 60 buttons; `exportFrameRange()` holds a simulation frame for several export frames past 60 and drops some between 30 and 60); the split-screen race takes any whole arena count (`ArenaCount` is a number; the buttons offer 1, 2 and 4, the grid layout any count; at most `ARENA_CEILING` are built).
- **Tests and smoke**: `tests/uncap.test.ts` – the number field (parsing, commit rules, arrow steps, beyond-range display, invalid input), a table test that walks every setting with a range and every core URL key and round-trips 10×, 1000× and 1e9× its slider maximum through links, presets, project files and share codes, exact decimals, invalid values, nothing engaged at the defaults, Bounciness (old links and presets, a Bounciness-3 ball faster every second for 60 s, deterministic replay, the old ×3 replayed exactly), no ball leaving a sealed ring at 1e6, 1e12 and 1e100 px/s, the ceilings that are left, ARENA FULL, Find Simulation on runs that never end and **the guard** – a scan of the sources that fails on any clamp against a slider's maximum outside a short allow-list of memory-safety ceilings; `tests/unlimited.test.ts` keeps the No limits machinery's tests (crowd, time-slicing, rendering, the budgeted finder) and its review fixes: every mode setting reaching its engine past its slider as typed or at its memory-safety ceiling with the switch off, a String Battle of twenty balls crediting every kill, the gulp kept behind a thousand bursts, a 100,000-ring link without `inf=1` that keeps the switch off and stays responsive. The smoke test types 100× the Ball Speed's maximum into its field and sees the link carry it, types a Bounciness of 3 and watches the speed readout pass the old cap within 10 s, types a 1e6 Ball Count and sees ARENA FULL instead of a crash, counts a number field next to every slider of every section and of every mode's own controls, types a gate weight of 40 (`mpgm=40,2,1,1,1,1`), steps a 1,000 keyframe value down to 990 (not the slider's 800) and a keyframe time with the focus kept, types a 1,000 rpm spinner, and sees settings a run never reads raise no badge while 2,000 Glass rows and a 1e9 nested depth say ARENA FULL.
- **Add a setting**: nothing to do for a numeric setting with a range – it is uncapped and gets its number field from `Slider`. Never `Math.min(…, range.max)` a value (the guard test fails); if the value sizes an allocation, give it a `MEMORY_CEILINGS` entry and apply `memoryCeiling(key, value)` where the mode builds (`tests/uncap.test.ts` fails on a whole-number setting without one unless it is listed as sizing nothing). If the run's engine reads it, add its key to its mode's `MODE_ENGINE_KEYS` in settings.ts (or to `CORE_ENGINE_KEYS` / `RING_ENGINE_KEYS`): only those engage the runtime and its badges.

### Moving exits and splat barriers
Feature gerald-exit-splat, after two formats of the character-ball account ("solving the case of the moving exit", "Splat
Barrier Demo"). Everything is off by default – a run without it takes the old code path – and lives in new files wired into
the shared ones in small `--- gerald-exit-splat ---` blocks.

- **Settings** – `src/lib/physics/exitSplat.ts`: `exitBehavior` (URL `exit`: rotate | jump | flee | shrink), `exitJumpSeconds`
  (`exj`), `exitSense` (`exs`, degrees round the ring, 0 = the exit never reacts), `exitFleeSpeed` (`exf`, degrees a second),
  `splatBarrier` (`splat`), `splatSize` (`sps`, × the ball's radius) and `splatMax` (`spm`); their defaults, comfort ranges
  and validation (`resolveExitSplatFields()`: an unknown behaviour is rotate, a number is lifted onto its minimum and never
  capped – only `splatMax` stops at its memory-safety ceiling `MEMORY_CEILINGS.splatMax` in the engine), the URL writer and
  reader, `exitSplatConfigOf()` (the physics config the page's effect, the seed finder, the fast export, the split-screen
  arenas and the bot carry) and `exitSplatCarryOver()` (they carry over to the next mode). `MOVING_EXIT_MODES` (Classic,
  Accumulation, Multiply) and `SPLAT_MODES` (the ring modes but Shatter and Color Match, whose walls colour themselves) say
  where they apply – and which engines read the numbers (`engineSettingKeys()`).
- **Moving exits** – `src/lib/physics/movingExits.ts`: the pure maths (`placeGap()`, `inAnyGap()`, `edgeDistance()`,
  `fleeVelocity()`, `shrinkWidth()`, `pickExitSpot()`) and `ExitController`, owned by the engine. `beginStep()` says at every
  fixed step whether the exits move (then `wallRotationRate()` is 0: the rings hold still) and `advance()` moves every intact
  ring's exit at the start of every sub-step, before the balls move and the rings resolve them, so the gap-pass check sees
  the exit exactly where it is. **Jump**: a timer per ring (spread over the rings so they never all jump at once), a sense
  that arms a reaction of `EXIT_REACTION_SEC` (the exit flickers; a ball that reaches the doorway in that time gets out),
  a cooldown of `JUMP_COOLDOWN_SEC`; a ball in the doorway makes it wait; the new spot is drawn with `ctx.random()` at least
  `MIN_JUMP_RAD` away and clear of the balls it dodged. **Flee**: a short first-order lag towards `fleeVelocity()` of the
  nearest ball, never faster than `exitFleeSpeed`, easing to a stop on the far side of the ring; a ball in the doorway
  catches it. **Shrink**: `full × (1 − phase³)` over `exitJumpSeconds`, shut, then re-opened over `REOPEN_SEC` at least
  `MIN_REOPEN_RAD` away; it follows the engine's resizes – a width it did not write is the new full width, kept round the
  centre the controller wrote last (`centres`: the engine's `resizeGaps()` keeps a gap's start, not its centre, so a
  half-shut exit used to slide along its ring with every gap-size keyframe, bounce-math gap rule or Gap Size / Ball Size
  change) – and gives the exits their full width back round their centres when the behaviour changes. Only the balls the
  rings resolve count (not Multiply's escaped ones). The rig (never escape, the forced winner) predicts the rings held still
  while the exits move (`RigDirector.beginStep(…, ringsHeld)` with the engine's `exitsHoldRings`), so it steers a forced
  winner at the exit where it is, not where a turning ring would have carried it.
- **Splats** – `src/lib/physics/splats.ts`: `SplatField` – pooled `Splat`s, each a circle obstacle resolved with
  `resolveBallCircle()` (obstacles.ts), bucketed by angle every sub-step. The engine adds one at every hit of a ring from the
  inside (`processWallCollisions()`; never in an exit, not for a Portal teleport or a refused pass; Grow's in the Ball Size
  and only paint), resolves the balls inside a splat's ring against it like the editor's obstacles (the restitution scale,
  the ball's bounciness and lift, a hit's sound, bounce math's bounce, `keepRingSide()`) and calls `endStep()`: past
  `splatMax` the oldest fade (`SPLAT_FADE_MS`), a splat an exit's span came over or whose ring broke falls, and after
