  `SPLAT_LEAK_MS` without a new splat the oldest falls – the barrier never shuts a run for good.
- **Sound** – a landing queues a `SoundEvent` with `splat: true` (`type: "hit"`, `melody: false`, `level` from how hard the
  ball landed; at most `MAX_SPLAT_SOUNDS_PER_STEP` a step). `ToneGenerator.playSplat()` schedules `scheduleSplatTone()`
  (`src/lib/audio/splatTone.ts`: the String Battle's seeded noise through a low-pass falling from 2600 to 320 Hz over a sine
  blop gliding from 260 to 95 Hz) on the beat grid and ducks the music bed – in sample mode the hit sample plays at half
  speed instead; the page's sound loop, `playSoundEvent()` (fast export) and `playArenaSound()` (split-screen) dispatch it.
- **Drawing** – `src/components/simulator/exitSplatRenderer.ts` (`ExitSplatLayer`, drawn by Canvas.tsx after the rings and
  under the balls): every splat a seeded blob with drips and droplets in the ball's colour (rainbow balls paint in rainbow
  colours), splashing in, squishing when a ball bounces off it, dripping down as it fades; the flicker of an exit about to
  jump, the flashes at the spot it left and the spot it took, the red glow of a shutting exit and the streaks behind a
  fleeing one. The canvas mirrors `data-splats`, `data-splats-created`, `data-splat-hits`, `data-splats-max`,
  `data-exit-behavior`, `data-exit-moves` and `data-exit-angle` (the innermost intact exit's centre, degrees).
- **Panel** – `components/simulator/sections/ExitSplatSection.tsx`: **Exit Behaviour** closes the Wall section (four buttons
  and the sliders the chosen behaviour uses), **Splat Barrier** follows Wobbly Walls in the Visual section (a switch, then
  Splat Size and Most Splats); both also show while the search box is in use, their keys are in `SECTION_KEYS`, and
  `panelKeys.ts` offers them in the command palette in the modes they apply to only. Strings: `Controls.exit*` and
  `Controls.splat*` in every messages file, and a sentence in the ring modes' descriptions.
- **Tests and smoke** – `tests/exitSplat.test.ts`: the settings (defaults, the link round trip, invalid values, presets, the
  engine keys), the maths, the controller (timed and sensed jumps, the reaction and the cooldown, the doorway, the flee speed
  and its stop, shrinking and re-opening, resizes), the splat field (placement, solidity, the cap, the fades, the leak, Grow),
  whole runs (rings held still, one move per sub-step, gap passes only through the exit, out-running a jump, splats in
  Classic and Grow, untouched defaults and other modes, determinism with the seed finder and the page's engine, a forced
  winner escaping first with every behaviour, a shrinking exit staying on its spot under gap-size keyframes and a Gap Size
  drag), the splat sound and its dispatch, and the panel blocks in three languages; `tests/rigged.test.ts` checks the rig's
  flights with the rings held. The smoke test opens a Classic link with a jumping exit and the splat barrier, checks the
  controls and the link, runs it until the exit has jumped and splats stand within Most Splats, then switches to Flee and
  Shrink and sees the exit move, lets a forced winner out with each behaviour and keeps a shrinking exit on its spot under
  gap-size keyframes.

### Pricing and licences
--- free-watermark --- Since the watermark gate (see [Free-video watermark](#free-video-watermark)) video creation is no
longer refused: everyone records and exports, a video made without a verified Pro licence carries the watermark, and Pro
removes it and keeps Publish. The guard below now refuses Publish only; what follows describes the paywall as it was built,
with the changed points marked.

Feature paywall-gate: the playground stays free and **Pro** – $6.99 a month or $49 a year (save 42 %) – unlocks video
creation. The payments run on a Cloudflare Worker under [`billing/`](billing/README.md) (feature billing-backend, see
[Billing backend](#billing-backend)), which takes them and issues
signed licences; the site only verifies them. Everything lives in `src/lib/billing/` and `src/components/billing/`, wired
into the shared files in small `--- paywall-gate ---` blocks.

- **Free and Pro** – free, with no account and no limits: everything that plays – every mode, setting and slider, sound,
  Find Simulation, presets, share links and project files, the gallery, split-screen races, the obstacle editor, captions,
  the timeline and the daily challenge. Pro: everything that creates a video file – Record Video, the fast export, the batch
  render, the viral bot's renders, Publish (send, quick share, retry and the desktop Library's publish targets), the desktop
  app's render queue and GPU encode, and the downloads of rendered videos. --- free-watermark --- Now: Free also records and
  exports – Record Video, the fast export, the batch render and its downloads, the viral bot's renders, the desktop render
  queue and GPU encode – with the watermark; Pro makes the same videos without it and keeps Publish.
- **Gating at the action, never by hiding** – the buttons stay visible with a lock badge (a padlock and a "Pro" tag, "Pro
  feature" as tooltip and in the button's accessible name; `LockBadge.tsx`, shown to free visitors only, so a Pro user's
  buttons look and are named as before) and their sections stay editable, so a free visitor can prepare everything. Pressing
  one asks THE guard, `requireEntitlement()` in `src/lib/billing/guard.ts` (it waits for a licence check still in flight),
  which returns a typed refusal (`free`, `expired`, `unverified`) that the caller turns into the Unlock dialog
  (`requestUnlock()` in `unlock.ts`; `UnlockDialog.tsx`, mounted once by the simulator: one line of why, the two plans with
  their pay buttons, links to the pricing page and to Restore purchase, the test-mode line, Escape and the close button).
  The programmatic entry points ask the same guard – `VideoRecorder.startRecording()` (it keeps the refusal in
  `lastRefusal()`), `renderFast()` (it throws `EntitlementRequiredError`), the batch runner (useBatchRender's start, which the
  viral bot and the desktop queue go through too), the bot's `renderAll()`, the queue's `add()` / `start()` – so a call that
  goes around the UI meets the refusal as well. The account row at the top of the Recording group (`AccountRow.tsx`) says
  "Free – unlock video creation" or "Pro – <plan> until <date>" ("renews / ends on <date>" once the licence's exp is within 7
  days, "payment pending" in the grace days, "Renewing your licence…" while a lapsed subscription's licence is being renewed,
  "has ended" after that; `accountView()` in `account.ts`), with Unlock and/or Manage, and Restore. Everything that leaves the
  studio – the dialog's checkout, its pricing and Restore links, the row's Manage and Restore – opens in a new tab on the
  website, so the setup the visitor prepared (and any uploaded media) stays as it is: the claimed licence reaches the studio
  tab through the storage event and the dialog says it is unlocked. The checkout's tab is opened inside the click (a popup
  blocker allows that) and pointed at the checkout once the backend answers; if the browser refuses it, the checkout leaves
  in the studio's tab after remembering the studio's address (`jbl.checkoutFrom` in sessionStorage, `returnTo.ts`), and the
  claim's or Restore's success offers "Back to your setup" – only ever this site's own `/<locale>/simulator/` page.
- **The licence contract** (`license.ts`, shared with `billing/`; pure WebCrypto, no JWT dependency) – a JWT (RFC 7519) signed
  with ES256, the signature being the raw 64-byte r‖s pair WebCrypto's ECDSA produces: `base64url(header).base64url(payload).
  base64url(signature)`, header `{"alg":"ES256","typ":"JWT"}`, payload `{ sub: email (lower-cased, trimmed), plan: "monthly" |
  "yearly", provider: "stripe" | "paypal" | "crypto", iat, exp: the paid period's end + 3 days of grace, jti }`. The site
  imports the SPKI public key (`importKey("spki", …, { name: "ECDSA", namedCurve: "P-256" })`), verifies the signature over
  `header.payload` and refuses a header that is not alg ES256 / typ JWT (or names critical extensions), a payload without
  sub, plan or exp, and an exp in the past (60 s of clock skew allowed).
- **The entitlement store** (`entitlement.ts`, a small live store, and its hook `useEntitlement()` →
  `{ status: "checking" | "free" | "pro", plan, provider, email, expiresAt, testMode, dropped, lapsed, renewing }`) – the raw
  token lives in localStorage under `jbl.license`; the store verifies it when the page starts, when the window gets the focus
  and when another tab changes it (the storage event), drops a token that is invalid or expired (the account row says
  which), turns Free at the licence's exp while the page is open, and installs a claimed or restored licence only after
  verifying it. An invalid token goes with its receipt; an EXPIRED one (genuine, its signature verified) keeps its receipt
  (`jbl.license.ref`) and leaves its email, plan and provider in `jbl.license.lapsed` – a subscription's licence runs out at the
  end of every paid period unless the page fetched the renewed one in time, so the next visit renews it instead of asking
  for a new payment. Only Remove from this browser, an invalid token, or the backend's `not_active` / `unknown_reference`
  forget the receipt.
- **The billing API** (`api.ts`; base = `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_API`, JSON bodies, CORS) – `GET /config` (prices, the providers
  that are on – only those get a pay button –, the backend's test mode), `POST /checkout/stripe | /checkout/paypal |
  /checkout/crypto` → `{ url }` (Stripe Checkout with Apple Pay and Google Pay; PayPal's approval page; a NOWPayments invoice
  for BTC, ETH, USDT, USDC, SOL, DOGE, LTC, XRP, BNB, ADA, TRX and 300+ more – crypto buys a prepaid period, 30 days for $6.99 or
  365 for $49, because it cannot renew by itself, and the UI says so), `POST /license/claim` → `{ token } | { pending } |
  { error, message }`, `POST /license/restore` and `POST /portal/stripe` with `{ email, ref, returnUrl }` (the customer
  portal: like Restore it needs the receipt reference of that card subscription – the one this browser keeps –, an email alone
  opens nothing; its return link comes back to the pricing page, sent as `returnUrl` – the Worker's fallback, the bare site
  origin, is not this site under a base path). Every checkout gets the
  pricing page of the buyer's language as `returnUrl` (without a query) and comes back with `?claim=<provider>&ref=<id>`,
  which the page claims (a pending crypto payment is asked again every 5 s for up to 10 minutes). Every answer is read
  defensively: a network error, a non-JSON body or an unknown shape is a message, never a throw; only an https address is
  followed. Without `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_API` nothing is sent anywhere and the pay buttons say that payments are not
  configured yet. A subscription's licence is renewed quietly through `/license/restore` with the receipt reference it was
  claimed with (`renewQuietly()`, kept under `jbl.license.ref`): near its end (at most every 6 hours) and, once it has run
  out, on every visit and whenever the tab gets the focus again (at most every 15 minutes) – the account row and the pricing
  page say "Renewing your licence…" meanwhile and the guard waits for the answer. Inside the desktop app a checkout opens in
  the system browser and the purchase is restored in the app afterwards (with the email and the receipt reference the
  public site shows after the payment, or by pasting the licence key): the app's page calls the Worker from its own origin,
  `app://jumpingballslive`, which the Worker grants CORS (no web page can claim that origin).
- **The pricing page** (`src/app/[locale]/pricing/page.tsx` with `PricingClient.tsx` and `PlanCards.tsx`; in every language,
  the sitemap, the navbar, the footer and a line under the landing's call to action) – the Free / Pro table, the two plan
  cards with "Card (Stripe)", "PayPal" and "Crypto – BTC, ETH, USDT and 300+ coins" and one email field (the card and crypto
  checkouts use it; PayPal brings its own), the claim's states (claiming, waiting for the network, "You are Pro until
  <date>", a failure with Restore) – each with the receipt reference the checkout came back with and a Copy button, since
  neither the Worker nor Stripe's receipts tell a card buyer their `cs_…` / `sub_…` id –, this browser's licence (or the one
  that ran out) with Manage subscription (Stripe → the customer portal, PayPal → paypal.com's automatic payments page,
  crypto → buy another period), its receipt reference with Copy, Copy licence key and Remove, Restore purchase (the checkout
  email and the receipt reference: the Stripe session or subscription id, the PayPal subscription id or the crypto order id)
  and Paste a licence key (a key copied on another device, or taken into the desktop app, is installed once it verifies).
  The privacy policy and the terms gained a Payments / Pro subscriptions section, and the copy that called the video export
  free (the FAQ, About, the hero, the meta descriptions of the landing, studio, TikTok and About pages, the Terms'
  description, the Disclaimer, the Windows app's download note and description) now says what is free and what is Pro.
- **Test mode** – a build without `NEXT_PUBLIC_LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY` verifies licences with the committed TEST key pair
  (`tests/fixtures/license-test-key.json`, whose public half is `LICENSE_TEST_PUBLIC_KEY` in `config.ts`; `src/` never imports
  the fixture). Anyone can sign licences with that key, so test mode is visible: a yellow "Licensing is in test mode –
  payments are not configured yet" line on the pricing page and in the Unlock dialog, and one `console.info` when the store
  starts. In test mode only, a `jbl.billingApi` localStorage key may point the page at another backend (a Worker under
  `wrangler dev`, the smoke test's mock); a production build ignores it.
- **Configuration** – `NEXT_PUBLIC_LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY` (base64url of the SPKI DER of the P-256 public key) and
  `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_API` (the Worker's origin) are public and baked in at build time; `deploy.yml` (the website) and
  `desktop.yml` (the Windows app's site build) pass them from the repository variables `LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY` and
  `BILLING_API_URL` (Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions → Variables; empty while unset). `node
  scripts/billing-keygen.mjs` makes a production key pair and prints two lines: `{"privateJwk":…}` for the Worker's secret
  (`wrangler secret put LICENSE_PRIVATE_JWK` – the Worker takes that line as printed, or the bare JWK inside it) and the
  public key for `LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY` (`billing/scripts/keygen.mjs` prints the same format). Set both variables together: a
  build with the API but without the key is in test mode and refuses the Worker's real licences.
- **Tools that record** – `scripts/lib/test-license.mjs` signs licences with the TEST key in Node (`crypto.sign("sha256",
  data, { key, dsaEncoding: "ieee-p1363" })` gives the raw r‖s form; `node scripts/lib/test-license.mjs [--plan monthly]
  [--expired]` prints one). The smoke test installs one in every browser context it opens (`browser.newContext()` is wrapped
  to add the init script; `license: null` opens a free one), so every recording, export, batch, bot and publish check runs as
  a Pro user. The viral bot puts `BOT_LICENSE` (your licence key: pricing page → Copy licence key) into the page before it
  loads, or – for a test-mode build – a test licence it signs itself, and stops with a clear message when the page refuses it.
  The desktop app's `--smoke` run does not record, so it needs none.
- **Honest limits** – the gate in the browser is advisory: the page's code runs on the visitor's machine, and a patched
  browser can skip any check (or capture the canvas itself). What cannot be forged is the licence – an ES256 signature only
  the Worker's private key makes, verified with the public key baked into the build. A licence is a bearer key bound to an
  email, not to a device. Only rendering on a server would enforce video creation fully. (--- free-watermark --- The same
  holds for the watermark; what DevTools can and cannot do about it is in [Free-video watermark](#free-video-watermark).)
- **Tests** – `tests/billingLicense.test.ts` (a good licence, expired, another key, a tampered payload or header, a foreign
  algorithm, malformed tokens, DER signatures, the bot's licence), `tests/billingEntitlement.test.ts` (the store's
  transitions, the focus and storage events, install / clear, the guard, the Unlock channel, the recorder starting without a
  licence (--- free-watermark --- it used to refuse; its frames now carry the watermark), a lapsed licence kept across reloads and forgotten only when told, the guard waiting for a renewal),
  `tests/billingPlans.test.ts` (the plan maths: 42 % saving, 30 / 365 prepaid days, 3 grace days; the backend's answers, the
  portal's reference, the claim URL, the account row's wording, the quiet renewal – an expired Stripe licence plus its receipt
  leads to one restore call and ends Pro, `not_active` / `unknown_reference` forget the receipt, a network failure keeps it –,
  the way back to the studio, the Billing namespace in three languages). The smoke test's paywall block opens a free visitor's
  studio (--- free-watermark --- Record, the fast export and Render batch carry the watermark tag and work – the clips carry
  the watermark –, the account row's Remove watermark opens the Unlock dialog; it used to be the lock and no recording), the
  pricing page in three languages and its links, a full checkout → claim with a mocked backend (`page.route()`) that ends Pro,
  shows the receipt reference and records, the Unlock dialog's checkout in a new tab that leaves the studio's setup in place
  (and the same-tab fallback's "Back to your setup"), an expired and a foreign licence being dropped, a lapsed subscription's
  licence renewed on the next visit (and one the backend no longer knows forgotten), Manage subscription sending the
  reference, Restore and Paste a licence key with the mock, and the desktop group's queue (--- free-watermark --- tagged and
  open to a free visitor; it was locked).

### Bouncing Orbs
The Instagram account particlesflow ("Particle flow", 124K followers, "Everything is simulated, not animated.") posts one format – "Satisfying physics simulation – N varied bouncing orbs", the count as the hook (484 to 4900 orbs; 1089 = 33 × 33 is the most liked) – with variants like "Corner to corner distribution" (44 × 43), "Centre outwards distribution" (22 × 20), "octagons dropping outside in", metallic orbs, "music composed by the bounces" and "sleep sound". Its clips show a perspective grid or disc of glossy orbs on a slab or a round plate in which every orb bounces straight up on its own spot with heights that vary smoothly across the field, so the whole set becomes a rising sheet, a funnel or rings that fall into phase and dissolve again, coloured by height and sounding a soft tone per landing.

In the clips every orb bounces on its own spot forever at a constant height – nothing ever comes to rest – and the heights and tempos vary smoothly across the field, so the surface travels, morphs and resolves (every orb landing at once) before it dissolves again. The mode's first version let its orbs lose energy and settle, stepped them at the physics rate and redrew thousands of sprites a frame, so the field juddered and died down; the owner's verdict – "bouncing orbs is clunky and not fluid like the instagram given, add metronome and polyrythms to it" – led to the rework (feature orb-rhythm): the **rhythm model** is the default, the settling field stays as the **decay model** (**Model**, `ogModel`).

- **The rhythm model** (feature orb-rhythm; the default, **Model** = Rhythm, `ogModel`) – `src/lib/physics/modes/orbRhythm.ts` (pure arithmetic on the simulation clock) with `initRhythm()` / `stepRhythm()` in `orbGrid.ts`. Every orb is an ideal bouncer on its own spot, forever: orb i bounces n_i times a cycle of L seconds (its frequency f_i = n_i / L, its period T_i = L / n_i) with a phase φ_i, and its height – the orb's bottom above the slab – is the parabola of an elastic bounce, **h(t) = A_i · 4u(1 − u)** with u = frac(t · f_i − φ_i) (`bounceHeight()`): 0 on every landing, the apex A_i half a bounce later. The apex is the free flight's own, **A_i = g · T_i² / 8** (`apexOf()`; a launch at g · T / 2 climbs exactly that high and lands T later), so the faster an orb bounces the lower it flies – as in the clips –, under a field gravity g that puts the slowest orb at the **Bounce Height** (`ogH`) times the Gravity slider's share of its default (g = 8 · H · gravity / T_max²: more gravity, higher bounces on the same rhythm); **Equal Heights** (`ogEq`) gives every orb the same apex instead. There is no integration and no step error: the engine evaluates the formula at its fixed 60 Hz steps for the landings (⌊t · f − φ⌋ so far, `landingsUpTo()`), the counters, the IN PHASE moments and the sound; the canvas at every frame's own simulation time; the fast export at its exact frame times – the same seed gives the same field at any frame rate and playback speed. A **Landing Squash** (`ogSquash`, on) flattens an orb by 10 % (`SQUASH_AMOUNT`) as it lands and eases it out over 60 ms (`SQUASH_SEC`; drawing only, standing on its spot). The only random numbers are the Varied preset's (one draw per orb from `ctx.random()`); a rhythm field never settles, so only the clip ends its run – on an IN PHASE moment (`data-og-finish` = `phase`, the banner IN PHASE) or between two (TIME! with "in phase every …s").
- **Polyrhythms** (**Polyrhythm**, `ogPoly`, on) – the field splits into **Groups** (`ogGroup`, `groupIndex()`: **rows**, **columns**, **rings** from the centre – a disc's or the octagons' own rings, a grid's circles one spacing apart –, **diagonals** corner to corner (row + column, the default), a **checkerboard** or **every orb** its own) and group k bounces n_k times a **Cycle** (`ogCycle`, 30 s): T_k = L / n_k. Every orb is on the slab together at t = 0 and at every multiple of L – the **IN PHASE** moment, which replaces ALL SETTLED: a banner under the top of the square (the lime title, "1089 orbs land together at 30.0s" in white, on the dark rounded backdrop of the end banner, fading out after 1.8 s – `IN_PHASE_HOLD_MS`), an accented chord of the field's sound (root, third, fifth and octave: `inPhaseDegrees()`) and `data-og-resolves` / `-resolve-at` / `-in-phase` – and between them the surface morphs through travelling waves, two, three, four bands… When the counts share no factor the field is in phase exactly then and never between (`inPhasePeriodSec()` = L / gcd of the counts); the ratio presets' voices also meet on every downbeat (a bar). With the polyrhythm off the whole field shares one tempo (the preset's slowest) and each group's position shifts its phase by up to the **Tempo Spread** (`ogS`) of a bounce: a steady travelling wave that never resolves (`data-og-period` = never). The presets (`ogRhythm`, `rhythmCount()`; K is the number of groups, "bars" the bars a cycle – the Bars setting while the metronome is on, else the cycle in bars of the tempo):

  | Rhythm preset (`ogRhythm`) | Groups | Bounces a cycle n_k (k = 0, 1, …) | In phase |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | **Pendulum wave** (`pendulum`, the default) | the Groups setting | 51 + k – the classic pendulum wave | at every cycle's end |
  | **3 against 2** (`3-2`) | the Groups setting, two voices taking turns | 3 · bars and 2 · bars (3 and 2 landings a bar) | on every downbeat |
  | **4 against 3** (`4-3`) | the same | 4 · bars and 3 · bars | on every downbeat |
  | **5 against 4** (`5-4`) | the same | 5 · bars and 4 · bars | on every downbeat |
  | **7 against 5** (`7-5`) | the same | 7 · bars and 5 · bars | on every downbeat |
  | **3:4:5** (`3-4-5`) | three voices taking turns | 3, 4 and 5 · bars | on every downbeat |
  | **Euclidean** (`euclid`) | the Groups setting | 51 + ⌊k · steps / K⌋ – the onsets of the Euclidean rhythm E(K, steps), **Euclidean Steps** `ogSteps` (16) | at every cycle's end |
  | **Corner to corner** (`corner`) | diagonals | round(51 · (1 + spread · k / (K − 1))): one corner slowest, the opposite one fastest | at every cycle's end |
  | **Centre outwards** (`centre`) | rings | the same ladder from the centre out | at every cycle's end |
  | **Varied** (`varied`) | every orb | 51 … 51 + round(51 · spread), drawn per orb from the seed | at every cycle's end |

- **The metronome** – a **Tempo** (`ogBpm`, 120 BPM; while the Sound section's beat lock is on the metronome follows its tempo and the slider says so), **Beats per Bar** (`ogBeats`, 4) and a **Visual Metronome** (`ogMetro`, `orbRhythmRenderer.ts`, timed by the frame's own simulation time): **bar** – a pendulum hanging from the top of the exported square swings from end to end, reaching an end exactly on every beat (θ = −θmax · cos(π t / beat)); the end it reaches flashes, lime on the downbeat, and a dot per beat of the bar under it shows where the bar is; **ring** – a ring on the floor around the field pulses on every beat; **dot** – a strip of beat cells at the bottom of the square with a dot hopping from cell to cell, landing on every beat. A **Click** (`ogClick`, a volume; 0 = silent) plays a woodblock tick on every beat – a sine "tok" falling a fifth in 20 ms with a brighter partial on top, the downbeat higher (1900 Hz against 1250 Hz) and louder (`orbRhythmTones.ts`) – through `ToneGenerator.playOrb("click")` from the page's sound loop, the fast export's `playSoundEvent()` and the split-screen arenas' `playArenaSound()`: at once, never moved onto the beat lock's grid (the lock would delay a beat by a grid step), never a sample, no ducking, and ahead of the frame's voice cap (no beat is ever dropped). While the metronome is on (a look or a click) the cycle is whole bars – L = **Bars per Cycle** (`ogBars`, 16) × beats × 60 / BPM (`beatClock()`; 32 s at the defaults) – so every IN PHASE moment lands on a downbeat and the ratio voices land on the bar's own subdivisions. **Melody** (`ogMelody`) gives every group its own degree of the Sound section's scale – the slowest tempo lowest, the fastest 14 degrees up (`groupDegree`) – so the landings play the polyrhythm as a tune: a group's landings in a step are one note (the music variant sings the loudest landing's group); `data-og-melody-notes` counts them.
- **Fluid drawing** – every frame is drawn at its own simulation time: `orbRenderTimeMs()` = the engine's time + the part of a step it holds (`PhysicsEngine.getStepRemainderMs()`) + the page's leftover − one step (the frame shows the step that made its sound), so it moves on by exactly the frame's own time every frame – a new height on every refresh of a 120 Hz display (a rhythm field whose frame costs under 5 ms takes every refresh past the canvas' 60 fps budget: `OrbFrameCost`), the exact frame times in the fast export – and never past a finished run's end. The decay model draws its closed-form flights between the steps the same way (`sampleOrbHeights()`), the camera's auto-orbit turns on the same clock. The orbs are drawn back to front once a frame with no per-frame allocation: each a cached sprite (colour bucket × size bucket × material) at its sub-pixel position – the gloss glint painted into the full-quality sprites (one `drawImage` an orb instead of two) –, the shadows (from the analytic height: smaller and fainter the higher the orb) resampled once to their drawn sizes and blitted unscaled on whole device pixels (`nativeShadow()`: about half the cost of a scaled sub-pixel draw, and a shadow lies still on the floor – under the auto-orbit's turning camera the shadows keep their sub-pixel places), and every colour's sprites painted ahead for the sizes on screen (`warm()`: under the height palette an orb walks every colour within a bounce, which used to paint a few dozen sprites in the middle of the first seconds' motion). Measured with the page's renderer in headless Chromium on 4 busy cores (load ~5, the 800 × 450 world at 1.13 device px a world px): 1089 orbs at full quality ~17 ms a frame against ~23 ms before the rework, 4900 orbs ~26 ms as before (sprites only).
- **The field and the decay model** – `src/lib/physics/modes/orbGrid.ts` (id `orbGrid`, feature orb-grid, the rhythm family). `buildOrbLayout()` stands `columns × rows` orbs in a unit field – a grid, a hex grid (odd rows shifted half a spacing), a disc of concentric rings (round(2πk) orbs on ring k) or octagons (8k orbs, k on each edge, axis-aligned) – and in the **decay model** (`ogModel` = `decay`: the mode's first version, a seed replaying exactly as it did before the rework) every orb is an independent 1-D bounce solved in closed form between landings (h(τ) = v·τ − ½·g·τ², the next landing 2v/g later, then v ← e·v), evaluated at every fixed 60 Hz step of the simulation clock: a seed replays exactly at any frame rate and playback speed. An orb whose next apex would stay under `REST_FRACTION` (0.3 %) of its drop settles (a run of landings shorter than a step – the Zeno tail – settles too); the run finishes `SETTLE_HOLD_MS` (1.5 s) after the last orb settled, or at the clip at the latest – as settled when every orb is already at rest (a clip ending during the hold), as `time` only while an orb still bounces. The seed draws the field's tempo (±`TEMPO_JITTER`, 12 %: the default ~27 s run spans 23.8–30.3 s, so Find Simulation lands a 30 s run and most seeds settle inside a 30 s clip), the varied distribution's values and the random release slots – every draw from `ctx.random()`. The engine's own ball is a placeholder pinned in the centre (never drawn); `planOrbGrid()` is pure, so the panel's run line ("1,089 orbs · every orb at rest after about 27.0s · in phase at about 5.0s") and the tests plan the same field.
- **Distributions** (the decay model's variation) – `distributionValue()` gives every orb a value 0–1: **varied** (seeded random), **corner** to corner ((u + v) / 2), **centre** outwards (the radius), **rows**, **columns**, **spiral** (the angle plus 1.25 turns over the radius), **ripple** (2.5 concentric bands) and **checker**; the **spread** scales how far the varied property moves with it: bounciness e₀ ± 0.1 × spread, drop height ×2^±spread, a release delay of up to 4 fall times, size (a bigger orb starts lower and falls faster), gravity ×2^±spread, or the **period** (perfectly elastic orbs with periods up to 50 % apart – a field that never settles). **Release**: together, **outside-in** / **inside-out** ring by ring (`releaseStep()`; a grid's rings are its square shells), **row by row** or random slots, `stagger` seconds apart.
- **The resolve moment** (the decay model's; a rhythm field is in phase at its cycle's ends) – the clip's payoff: with Resolve Moment on, every orb's time scale is tuned by at most `TUNE_TIME_RANGE` (±20 %, through its gravity; its bounciness, drop and heights stay its own, so the variety survives) so that one of its landings falls exactly on the field's pattern clock (`resolveTargetSec()`: half the base orb's Zeno time after half the release span, at most 16 fall times; 60 fall times for an elastic field): every orb still bouncing slams the slab at once, then the field dissolves again. The detector (`addPhaseCoverage()`, `coverageShare()`) measures the share of the moving orbs within a ±2/64 window of their bounce phase every step (the phases each orb swept, on a circular difference array – no sort); with hysteresis (armed below 0.45, an in-phase moment at 0.8) it counts the moments (`data-og-resolves`) and keeps the first (`data-og-resolve-at`) – the default field resolves within a step of its plan, ~5 s in. A step counts only while at least half the field is in flight (`resolveQuorum()`, `RESOLVE_FIELD_SHARE`): the last rows or rings of a decaying field bouncing in step are no resolve (they once added late "in phase" moments with 4–27 % of the orbs moving – Metallic 525 at 24 and 27 s, an untuned rows field from 14 s); every preset has its whole field in flight at its plan.
- **The projection** – `src/components/simulator/orbGridRenderer.ts` (`OrbGridLayer`), drawn by `Canvas.tsx`: the floor in the world pass, the orbs in the ball pass, the HUD line over the run. The camera looks at the field's centre from `elevation` degrees above the slab (`ogE`, 30° by default) at `CAMERA_DISTANCE` (2.6 field widths), the field turned by `rotation` (`ogRot`) plus 6° a second of the simulation clock with the auto-orbit (`ogO`; deterministic, so a found run and its export turn alike). `orbCamera()` fits the field's bounding cylinder – not the turned field – into 95 % of the centred square the recorder crops, so the centre and the framing stay fixed while the camera turns. Each orb's floor spot is projected and lifted by its height along the screen's up axis; the orbs are drawn back to front after one counting sort by projected depth a frame (`depthOrder()`, O(n + buckets), no `Array.prototype.sort`), each a cached sprite (one per colour bucket × size bucket × material, painted once: glossy – a bright specular dot and a darker rim; metallic – a chrome sky over a dark horizon band; matte; glass), with a soft shadow ellipse on the floor that shrinks and fades with the height (8 cached shadow sprites, resampled to the sizes drawn – see Fluid drawing). Palettes: by current height (24 buckets from green through orange and pink to purple), by ring, by row, the Ball Colour, a rainbow by position. The floor: a slab with a cell grid over a blue grid floor, a round dark plate, the grid alone or nothing; the background is the theme's. The "1089 bouncing orbs" line (`ogHud`) sits at the top-left of the exported square – on the right while the teams scoreboard holds that corner, and in split-screen arenas, whose letter badge does – and the top captions start below it; the end banner says ALL SETTLED (also when the clip ends during the hold) or TIME! (the clip ended while an orb still bounced) – a rhythm field's says IN PHASE (the clip ended on a cycle's end) or TIME! with "in phase every …s". It sits right over the resting orbs – bright green in the height palette – and is the clip's end frame, so `OrbGridLayer.drawBanner()` draws it opaque (the screen-space overlays otherwise inherit the world pass's 0.6) on a rounded 60 % black backdrop, the title in lime and the subline in white: at least 3:1 (title) and 4.5:1 (subline) over every palette colour.
- **Performance ladder** – the orb state lives in typed arrays (positions, heights, launch times and speeds, next landings, states; the renderer's projections and depth order in grown-once buffers – nothing allocated a frame), drawing is `drawImage` of sprites, and `orbQuality()` picks a level by the orb count – deterministic, so the fast export draws what the page drew: **full** (shadow + sprite + a gloss glint – painted into the sprite since the rework –, up to `QUALITY_GLOSS_MAX` = 1,400 orbs), **no gloss** (up to `QUALITY_SHADOW_MAX` = 2,200), **no shadows** (sprites only, up to `QUALITY_SPRITE_MAX` = 5,000 – the 70 × 70 field keeps its glossy orbs), **flat discs** (one path per colour and depth slice, up to `QUALITY_DISC_MAX` = 8,000), then **points** in one image. The thresholds are measured on the simulator page (headless Chromium, 4 cores, the 1400 × 900 page, load ~3): a frame costs ~5.8 ms of page work plus ~11.5 µs an orb at full quality, ~6.5 µs with shadows, ~3.4 µs sprites only, ~2.5 µs as discs. Measured: **1089 orbs (33 × 33) 56.8 fps on the page, 36.8 fps while recording 1080×1920; 1936 (44 × 44) 54.5 / 34.8 fps; 4900 (70 × 70) 45.3 / 30.7 fps** (the smoke test's floors: 30 on the page, 20 recording); 12,100 orbs as points held 60 fps. Since the rework (the rhythm model, measured the same way while other builds kept the machine busy, load ~2): **1089 orbs 54.7 fps on the page and 29.2 fps while recording 1080×1920, 4900 orbs 34.8 fps**; the renderer alone (a frame's draw in the scratch benchmark, load ~5) takes 17 ms for 1089 orbs against 23 ms before the rework, 29 ms against 35 ms at the recording's 2.4 device px a world px, and 26 ms for 4900 as before. The physics costs 0.02–0.05 ms a step for 1089–4900 orbs, a plan of 250,000 orbs (`ORB_CEILING`: ~24 MB with the renderer's buffers; past it the field keeps its aspect and says so in the panel) builds in well under a second. Split-screen arenas pick the level for all of their orbs together (`orbArenas`: four arenas of 1089 draw like 4356): 2 arenas held 60 fps, 4 arenas 59.7 fps. The fast export draws the same levels at its own scale (1920 × 1080 device px for a 1080 × 1920 frame) and mixes the grouped voices offline: the default 1089-orb run (a 27.7 s clip) exported at 1080 × 1920 and 60 fps in 56 s silent and 88 s with the notes on the same machine.
- **Sound** – `src/lib/audio/orbTones.ts`: a field lands thousands of times a second, so `groupOrbLandings()` turns a step's landings into voices – one per row of a grid (ring of a disc or octagons), a chord of its columns' pitches (up to 3, spread from the lowest to the highest when more landed) – and keeps the loudest `MAX_NOTES_PER_STEP` (2; loudness from the landing speed and how near the camera the orb is); the mode passes on at most `MAX_ORB_EVENTS_PER_FRAME` (6) a frame. Pitches are degrees of the Sound section's scale on its root over 14 degrees (`orbPitchHz()`; the chromatic default plays a C major pentatonic so thousands of landings never clash). **Notes**: ordinary wall hits (`SoundEvent.chord`), so the instrument, the scale snap, the beat lock, the hit sample, a melody and the song slicer apply; **sleep**: `scheduleOrbSleep()` – low-passed soft sine pairs with a slow attack and a 1.8 s release, at most one voice every 0.32 s; **music**: the earliest landing of a step picks the melody's next step through the scale (`orbMusicNext()`), at most one note every 0.14 s; **metal**: `scheduleOrbClink()` – four inharmonic partials a pitch, an octave up, at most one voice every 0.06 s; **silent**. Sleep and metal are `SoundEvent.orb` events played by `ToneGenerator.playOrb()` (on the beat grid, never a melody note or a slice; the hit sample at the voice's pitch in sample mode) through the page's sound loop, the fast export's `playSoundEvent()` and the arenas' `playArenaSound()`. The rhythm model adds the metronome's click (`playOrb("click")`, ahead of the frame's cap) and the IN PHASE chord, and with **Melody** on every group's landings sound its own degree of the scale (see The metronome).
- **Find Simulation** – `ModeSettings.orbGrid` (the page leaves the clip out: `maxSec` 0): the run length (the classic search; a field that never settles – the period property, a bounciness of 1 or more, no gravity – has none), **Never settles** (the field still bouncing when the clip ends: judged by the moment its last orb comes to rest, `settledMs` in the run summary, not by the run's end – that comes `SETTLE_HOLD_MS` later, so a field at rest in the clip's last 1.5 s ends past the clip although the page already shows ALL SETTLED) and **In phase at** (the first resolve moment within ±0.5 s of a chosen second, `firstResolveMs` in the run summary; outcomes.ts). A rhythm field never settles (no run length and no Never settles – those are the decay model's): **In phase at** is answered at once by the cycle maths (`orbRhythmFinderAnswer()`) – every seed is in phase at every multiple of the cycle, so a moment k · L within ±0.5 s of the target is found with any seed and no search; otherwise the panel names the nearest moment and the cycle to set it (with the polyrhythm off: never in phase). A caption's question is answered at a rhythm field's first IN PHASE moment (`PhysicsEngine.isOrbInPhase()`; the decay model's at its end, as before).
- **UI and presets** – `sections/OrbGridSection.tsx` in the Mode row (Field: columns, rows, arrangement; Variation: property, distribution, spread, release, stagger, drop height, orb size, bounciness, resolve moment; Look: material, palette, camera elevation and rotation, auto-orbit, floor, the count line; Sound: the variant), every number with its number field and no maximum (the rotation is signed – `ogRotation` is in `SIGNED_KEYS`, so −45° from a link, a preset, a project file or the number field turns the camera the other way); a change of the field or the variation (or the gravity) restarts the run and drops a found seed with its promise (the Found! line, "Ready to start simulation for …" and the warning), the look and the sound follow live. The Presets group starts with eight presets after the account's clips: Varied orbs 1089, Corner to corner 44x43, Centre outwards 22x20, Octagons outside-in 1352, Metallic 525 (metal clinks on a dark plate), Sleep sound 484, Music 484 and 70 x 70 (4900). Since the rework the **Model** switch tops the block, and the rhythm model shows its Rhythm group (the preset, Polyrhythm, Groups, Euclidean Steps, Cycle – while the metronome is off –, Tempo Spread, Equal Heights, Bounce Height, Landing Squash) and the Metronome group (Visual Metronome, Tempo, Beats per Bar, Bars per Cycle, Click, Melody) in place of the Variation group (`sections/OrbRhythmSection.tsx`); the URL keys are the field names (`ogModel`, `ogEq`, `ogSquash`, `ogPoly`, `ogGroup`, `ogCycle`, `ogRhythm`, `ogSteps`, `ogBpm`, `ogBeats`, `ogMetro`, `ogClick`, `ogBars`, `ogMelody`; the defaults write none), every number uncapped. A change of the model, the rhythm or the metronome's clock restarts the run and drops a found seed; the squash, the look, the click volume and the melody follow live. The run line under the block (`orbGridRunLine()`) is planned again whenever something its summaries read changes – the memos are keyed on exactly that (`orbGridSummaryKey()`, `orbRhythmSummaryKey()`), the model included: without it a switch from Rhythm to Decay kept the rhythm field's plan ("144 orbs · never settles – the clip ends the run · in phase at about 30.0s" for a 12 × 12 field that comes to rest after about 26.9 s and is in phase at 5.0 s). The presets play the rhythm of their clip – Varied, Corner to corner and Centre outwards their own, the octagons a pendulum wave in rings, Metallic 3:4:5 in rings, Sleep a slow 60 s centre-outwards wave, Music a pendulum wave in rows that plays its tune, 70 × 70 corner to corner – and a ninth joins them: **Metronome 3:2** (24 × 24 = 576 orbs, 3 against 2 on a checkerboard under the swinging bar with the click and the melody).
- **Data attributes** – the canvas mirrors `data-og-orbs`, `-requested`, `-full`, `-arrangement`, `-released`, `-released-rings`, `-bounces`, `-landed` (this step), `-settled`, `-moving`, `-resolve` (the detector's share this step), `-resolve-at`, `-resolves`, `-resolve-plan`, `-tuned`, `-quality`, `-sound`, `-voices`, `-notes`, `-pitches`, `-finished`, `-finished-ms`, `-finish`, `-time`, `-hud`, `-tempo` and `-banner` (the end banner's backdrop, `x,y,w,h` in world px, while it is drawn). Since the rework: `data-og-model`, `-cycle` and `-period` (ms, "never" with the polyrhythm off), `-groups`, `-tempos`, `-bpm`, `-beats`, `-beat` (beats so far), `-clicks`, `-metro`, `-metro-box` (the visual metronome's box, `x,y,w,h` in world px), `-melody-notes` and `-in-phase`, and for every drawn frame `-frame` (a counter), `-frame-ts` (its rAF time), `-frame-t` (its simulation time) and `-sample` (the drawn heights of four orbs).
- **Tests** – `tests/orbGrid.test.ts`: the registration and the messages, the layouts and distributions (corner, centre, rows, spiral, ripple and checker ranges and symmetry), the release orders (and octagon rings released outside in by the engine), exact replay fingerprints for three seeds at 60 and 30 fps, the fall and the apex, settle detection and the finish (settled or clipped – a clip ending during the hold ends a field at rest as settled), elastic fields, the resolve detector on constructed fields and in the engine (its quorum: every preset's planned moment kept, no moments once most of the field rests), the tuning, the projection (depth order, a higher orb drawn higher, the centre and the framing fixed while the camera turns, the ladder), the end banner (its contrast over every palette colour, the backdrop's size, the draw order and alpha), the sound grouping (the cap, chords), the voices a frame and the variants, the settings / URL / preset round trip, the uncapped fields and the signed rotation (a link, a preset, a project file, the number field), the finder's outcomes (never-settles matching exactly the seeds whose clip ends on a field still bouncing – none at rest within the clip's last 1.5 s; no In phase at a moment when most of the field rests) and the page dropping a found seed's promise with the seed. The smoke test checks the card and the preview, the URL and the panel, a 1089-orb run with its counters and HUD line, the corner preset's resolve moment, the octagons' release, the sleep and music sounds, the finder (a 30 s run, and a Never settles search passing over a seed at rest in the clip's last 1.5 s), that seed's run ending with the clip as settled under a banner on a dark backdrop with a white subline (canvas pixels), a change of the field dropping a found seed's promise, in-phase moments counted only while most of the field bounces (Metallic 525: once; an untuned rows field: never), a negative camera rotation from a link and the number field, and the frame rates of 1089 orbs (page and 1080×1920 recording) and 4900 orbs. The rework's tests (the end of `tests/orbGrid.test.ts`): the height formula and its period / apex relation (A = g T² / 8, the curvature −g), the squash, the plan (f = n / L, the slowest orb at the bounce height, the Gravity slider, equal heights) and the engine playing it, every rhythm preset's counts and in-phase period, every orb on the slab at every multiple of L and never between (the ratio voices on every downbeat), the engine's IN PHASE moments, banner and chord, a clip ending on or between them, the metronome's snapping (whole bars, the beat lock's tempo) and its clicks (every beat, the downbeat accented, counted), the melody's pitches, replay fingerprints at 30, 60 and 120 fps (the run, its sounds and the heights drawn), the fast export's exact frame times, the fluidity bound at 120 fps (and the stepped heights it catches), the decay model drawn between its steps, the decay model's runs replaying exactly as before the rework (fingerprints and sound hashes), the outcomes and Find Simulation's instant answer (kept by the seed's run), a caption answered at the IN PHASE moment, the links and presets of the new keys, the visual metronome's timing and the panel's run line (the two memos replayed over a change of every setting their summaries read, on five fields of both models: the line always reads what a fresh load of the new settings reads – a Rhythm → Decay switch included –, and the block shows it). The smoke test plays the decay model in the checks above that are its own, and adds: a 12 × 12 field's fluidity (every frame at its own time; the drawn heights off a smooth arc by under 2 % of the apex), the IN PHASE banner at the end of a 3 s cycle on its dark backdrop, the metronome bar and a click on every beat, the melody's tones, the new keys' URL round trip, the instant In phase at answer, the decay model still ending ALL SETTLED and the run line following the Model switch (after Rhythm → Decay it reads what a fresh decay load reads, and back); the corner preset falls into phase at the end of its cycle, and the frame-rate checks play the rhythm model.

### String Circle (the weaponballarena family)
Feature string-circle. Research: the Instagram account weaponballarena – the account of the land-claim battles (see "Land Claim") – also posts STRING CIRCLE fights: "INDIA USA STRING FIGHT" (a duel), "BATTLE OF COUNTRIES" (Turkey, India, the USA and Iran on its thumbnails), "MEGA COUNTRY FIGHT" (a dozen flags), five-flag "STRING ARENA" clips whose fans paint a star, and #hexagon variants. Country-flag balls bounce inside a thin white circle on black, every ball trailing a dense fan of thin lines in its flag's colour from itself to the rim; a ball that flies through a rival's lines slices them, a ball that loses its strings is knocked out, the last flag standing wins, and a title line names the format. The String Battle already had the balls, the lives, the shield, the eliminations, the verdict, the rig and the finder, so the format is a third style of it (next to web and neon) rather than a new mode, with the clips' numbers as settings.

`src/lib/physics/modes/stringCircle.ts` holds what the style adds – the settings (`sbArena`, `sbRate`, `sbCut`, `sbTitle`; URL `sba`, `sbrt`, `sbc`, `sbti`, read and written by `readStringCircleParams()` / `writeStringCircleParams()`, validated by `resolveStringCircleInto()`), the pure geometry, the rim's coverage, the line-up, the sounds' pitches and the presets (`stringCirclePresetPatch()`); `StringBattleMode` (`stringBattle.ts`) calls it from `// --- string-circle ---` blocks (`initCircle()`, `circleBallStep()`, `circleCrossings()`, `circleStep()`, `anchorString()`, `circleSounds()`). `src/lib/audio/stringCircleTones.ts` has the sounds, `src/components/simulator/stringCircleRenderer.ts` (`StringCircleLayer`) draws it and `sections/StringCircleSection.tsx` is its part of the String Battle block. The countries are Land Claim's `src/lib/countries.ts` and its Teams-section Country picker (`sections/CountryPicker.tsx`), reused as they are; this feature added the five countries of the clips the list lacked (Afghanistan, Bangladesh, Iran, Pakistan, Sri Lanka – 47 in all, named in every language). `tests/stringCircle.test.ts` covers it; the smoke test (section 36) checks the URL and the controls, a four-flag battle to its end with its sounds, the hexagon, a preset's flags, the finder and the frame rates.

- **Flight** – the battle's balls, cruising speeds and seeded rebound scatter (`reboundHeading()`, shared with the web style), with a **curl**: a ball's heading turns at `curl × speed / R` a second (R the arena's radius), so its path bends with a radius of about R / curl at any Ball Speed and the rim point straight ahead of it sweeps along the rim – what makes a fan (a straight flight would anchor every string at one point). Each ball draws its curl (`SC_CURL` 0.9 ± 40 %, either way round) at the start and afresh at every wall bounce. The wall is the battle's ring – a circle of radius `arenaRadius()`, or the flat-topped hexagon inscribed in it (apothem R·cos 30°): `wallOverlap()` and `circleBounce()` keep every ball inside either, and a resize scales the arena, the balls and the anchors together.
- **Strings** – every ball still in the battle anchors `rate` strings a second (`anchorsDue()`; the first ones staggered by slot), each at its due time from where the ball was then (interpolated between the step's ends) to the rim point straight ahead (`rimHit()`: the ray against the circle or the hexagon's sides), so a battle plays the same at 30, 60 and 120 frames a second (pinned fingerprints). A ball keeps at most `SC_STRING_CEILING` live strings, the oldest fading past it: 1,024, the String Battle's own thread ceiling (`STRING_CEILING`) – a memory-safety ceiling, not a design count (a string is ~80 bytes; what it bounds is work – every frame strokes the strings, every sub-step re-indexes the fans a rival moved past). The winner keeps anchoring through its victory lap, painting the rim.
- **Cuts and lives** – after every sub-step `circleCrossings()` tests every rival's move against a fan: the fan's strings are bucketed by their direction from its ball (`FanIndex`: 128 angular buckets by a division-only pseudo-angle), a move can only cross the strings whose direction lies between its two ends, so only those buckets' strings get the full crossing test – the same strings cut as testing all of them (a unit test compares the two on random fans), a few percent of them tested. The test (`scCuts()`) is the web style's crossing on the anchor-side half of a string (`SC_CUT_SPAN` 0.5) and never the stub next to its ball: all of a fan's strings meet at its ball, so whole-thread cuts let any pass near a ball wipe its fan (measured: the fans never grew past a few dozen strings) – here a rival cuts what it slices through out toward the rim, the fan's claim. Every `cut` strings of one owner cut take a life (`damage()`, so the Forced Winner's `rigAbsorbs()` holds: the chosen flag never loses its last life) and shield its fan for `SB_INVULN_MS.cut` (2.5 s: nothing of it can be cut while it grows back); a ball out of lives is out and its fan dissolves (at most `SC_DISSOLVE_GHOSTS` strings drawn dissolving). The last flag standing wins; at a clip limit the survivors with the most lives, then the one holding the most rim (`coverageLeaders()`). The style always plays the cut rule (no collision damage) and draws no warning badge.
- **The rim** – the score: `updateCoverage()` splits the rim into `SC_RIM_BINS` (720) bins; two strings a ball anchored one after the other (both still up, at most `SC_ARC_JOIN` = 30° apart) claim the arc between their anchors, a lone string its bin, and where fans overlap the newer claim wins – a sweeping fan claims the arc it swept, a cut breaks it. `data-sb-coverage` mirrors every flag's share in percent.
- **Defaults, measured** – nearly every string is cut sooner or later, so a fan's density is the rate times a string's life and a battle lasts about lives × cut ÷ (balls × rate): with 30 strings a second and 200 per life, four balls of four lives fight for about 34 s with fans of 130–330 strings, a duel of five lives for about 45 s with fans of 300–700, twelve balls of three lives for about 24 s, the hexagon's six for about 25 s and five balls of four lives for about 32 s. A lower rate makes thin fans, a lower cut a slaughter paced by the shield alone. Both are uncapped (the sliders' 1–120 and 1–1,000 are comfort ranges).
- **The line-up** – the Teams roster names, colours and flags the first balls (a roster emoji that is a flag reads as its country's code); the balls past it play `SC_LINEUP` – the clips' countries (Turkey, India, the USA, Iran, Germany, Canada, Japan, China, Vietnam, Sri Lanka, Pakistan, Bangladesh, Afghanistan), then the rest of the list – each named by its two-letter code and wearing a flag colour no earlier fan is near (`colorsClash()`: an RGB distance under 80; else a golden-angle hue at three lightnesses), `circleLineup()`. The first six balls are teams (the scoreboard, the teams banner, the finder's winner and the Forced Winner, named as on the canvas by `teamChoiceNames()`); a mega fight's winner past them takes the battle's own banner.
- **Sound** – `SoundEvent.scSound`: a **twang** for the strings the balls anchored – a Karplus–Strong pluck with a soft triangle on top that starts a fifth above the note and glides onto it (`scheduleTwang()`), one C-major pentatonic note per team from C4 (`twangFrequency()`), the anchors of a step grouped into one event of at most `SC_TWANG_NOTES` (3) team notes at most every `SC_TWANG_GAP_STEPS` steps (twenty a second: a shimmer, not a buzz), the teams taking turns; a **snap** for strings cut – a short band-passed noise crack over a sine ping an octave above the twang of the fan that lost the most, louder with more strings (`scheduleSnap()`, `snapLevel()`), at most every `SC_SNAP_GAP_STEPS`, ducking the music bed; a KO is the battle's shatter. All accompaniment (`melody: false`); `ToneGenerator.playStringCircle()` plays them (snapped to the scale; the twang plays the hit sample in the sample mode) from the page's sound loop, the fast export's `playSoundEvent()` and the split-screen arenas' `playArenaSound()`.
- **Drawing** – `StringCircleLayer`, called where the battle's own layer is: the thin white wall (flashing in the winner's colour at the end), the strings leaving the battle (snapping at the cut – at most `SC_SNAP_GHOSTS` drawn per slice –, fading past the ceiling, dissolving with their ball; batched by team and alpha tier, not a stroke each), every fan batched into one path per team (thin lines at 0.42 alpha that add up to a dense sector; past 3,000 strings in all every k-th string of each fan by its serial – the same strings frame after frame, the stride dropping again only 10 % below its step –, a little stronger: `data-sb-stride`), dark flag colours lifted for the black stage (`fanColor()`) and the rim arcs just outside the wall; the balls – a glow, the flag inside a ring of the ball's lives whose current life drains as its strings are cut (the two-letter code on a disc where the fonts have no flag glyphs, `flagGlyphsSupported()`), the shield's blink, names and shatter bursts; over the square, the title line (drawn once into a sprite: its glow is a blur), the standings strip (every flag still in with its share of the rim, the leader first, past six "+N"; captions keep above it) and – unless the teams banner crowns a roster's winner – the winner banner with confetti. A question caption's `[winner]` names the winning flag. --- low-findings --- With the strip off and a roster's scoreboard on (the teams' board in a top corner of the square) the title line keeps clear of it (`titleSpot()`: beside the board, shrunk to the room there, or – with less than 30 % of the square beside it – under it; `data-sb-title-box` / `-title-avoid`); the battle's banner shows exactly when the teams banner crowns nobody (`TeamLayer.crownsBattleWinner()`, one rule for the web, neon and circle styles: a web mega fight won past the six teams used to end with no banner at all); a new battle in another arena puts its balls inside the new wall (`initCircle()` sets the new state before it places them – a hexagon battle after a circle battle kept oversized balls inside the old circle). A free video's watermark badge keeps clear of the standings strip (the strip is a bottom band of the HUD the canvas reports; see [Watermark on every free simulation](#watermark-on-every-free-simulation)).
- **Settings and panel** – `sbArena`, `sbRate`, `sbCut`, `sbTitle` next to the battle's fields (the strip is `sbHud`); `StringCircleSection.tsx` in the String Battle block while the style is circle (and in the settings search): the five built-in presets (Country fight: India vs USA – two balls of five lives; Battle of countries: Turkey / India / USA / Iran; Mega country fight (12) – the roster's six flags and six of the line-up; Hexagon arena (6); String arena classic (5) – each the circle style with its roster of countries, arena, lives and a clip long enough for its battle and banner), the arena, the two sliders, a note naming the line-up's flags past the roster, the title line, the strip and a "Which flag wins?" caption button; the rule, threads and badge controls belong to the other styles and are hidden. A change of the style, the arena, the rate or the cut restarts the battle and drops a found seed; the title and the strip follow live. The finder (`ModeSettings.stringBattle`), the bot's `modeSettingsOfSettings()`, presets, project files, share codes and the desktop assistant (`sb` prefix) carry the fields.
- **Data attributes** – the canvas adds `data-sb-arena`, `-coverage`, `-fans`, `-anchors`, `-twangs`, `-snaps`, `-circle-lives`, `-title`, `-flag-glyphs`, `-badges` (f a flag glyph, c its code, e another emoji, d a disc), `-names`, `-segments`, `-stride`, `-rim-arcs`, `-in-arena` and `-strip-drawn` to the battle's own `data-sb-*` (removed in the other styles).
- **Performance** – the physics is linear in the strings (the fan index keeps the cut pass to a few percent of them): measured in Node, 5 balls whose fans hold ~420 strings each cost ~25 ms of CPU per simulated second, 12 balls with ~420 each ~70 ms. The drawing is what a dense frame costs: in headless Chromium the rasterisation of 12 fans of ~430 strings took ~10 ms of a ~16 ms raster, so past 3,000 strings in all the fans are thinned (by serial, see Drawing; ~11 ms in all), the leaving strings are batched (the battle's two hundred cost ~5 ms as single strokes, ~2.5 ms batched) and the title's glow is a sprite. The smoke test (section 36) measures, on a shared 4-core machine with software rendering (load 4–6; the same on the obfuscated build): the four-flag default at 60 fps; the mega preset (12 flags) at 60 fps on the page and 46–55 fps recorded at 1080×1920; 5 balls with fans of ~390 strings at 60 fps on the page and 43–50 fps recorded; 12 balls with fans of ~360–400 strings (every other one stroked) at 60 fps on the page and 29–40 fps recorded. A dense frame's recording is bounded by the software video encoder sharing the CPU, so those two recordings are held to a sanity floor (12 fps) and reported; the mega preset's keeps the presets' 20 fps floor.
- **Tests** – `tests/stringCircle.test.ts`: the settings, URL and presets; the geometry (the rim ahead on the circle and the hexagon, the cut test against the web style's on 4,000 random cases, the fan index against testing every string); the cadence and the coverage; the rules on a hand-made battle (the outer half cut, a life per `cut` strings, the shield, the dissolve, the rig); whole battles in the engine (the cadence, the ceiling, a battle to its end, the hexagon, a clip-limit tie broken by the rim, the forced winner, the sounds, pinned fingerprints at 30/60/120 fps, a resize, style switching); the finder's winner outcome played back, the teams banner of a mega fight, the drawing and the canvas data, the thinning of dense fans, the countries, the line-up, the finder's names and the messages; and the tones on a fake audio graph.

### Free-video watermark
Feature free-watermark – the owner's direction (2026-10-04): "put a watermark in all videos that dont have thr pro mode, make
sure they can bypass it with inspect element", built as the only reading a paywall allows: they can **not** bypass it with
inspect element. It changes the paywall's model: everyone records and exports – Record Video, the fast export, the batch
render, the viral bot and the Windows app's render queue run for free visitors too – and every video made without a
verified, unexpired Pro licence carries the watermark; a Pro licence removes it. Publish stays Pro (the one action the guard
still refuses) and nothing else about billing changed.

- **The mark** (`src/lib/watermark/layout.ts`, `paint.ts`) – two layers drawn into the frame's pixels: the **badge** – the logo
  mark (the open ring and ball of `public/icon.svg`), `SITE_NAME` and `SITE_DOMAIN` on a dark pill with the accent rim, at 70 %
  opacity with a soft shadow – in a bottom corner of the exported square inside the safe zone (portrait frames: 6 % left,
  12.5 % right for the action buttons, 8 % top, 20 % bottom for the caption, music line and navigation; other frames 5 % all
  round), changing between bottom-left and bottom-right every 6 s of the clip (`badgeCorner()`), so cropping one corner away
  leaves the other – --- low-findings --- above the HUD the source canvas reported for the frame where that HUD holds the corner
  (a String Circle standings strip, Fight League's ability boxes, the bottom captions: `placeVideoBadge()`, see
  [Watermark on every free simulation](#watermark-on-every-free-simulation)); and the **tiles** – the domain written diagonally (−30°) across the whole frame at 4 % opacity in staggered
  rows, a white fill on a dark rim so they show on light and dark backgrounds alike (`tileLayout()`). Both scale with the
  exported square, so 500×500, 1280×720, 1920×1080, 1080×1920 and the desktop app's 4K upscale carry the same mark. Cheap: the
  badge is drawn once into a sprite and the tiles once, from one tile pattern, into a layer of the frame's size, both with
  their opacity in their pixels – a frame costs two `drawImage()` calls. A layer that comes out blank throws
  `WatermarkUnavailableError` and the recording or export stops rather than produce a clean video.
- **Where it is drawn** – `drawRecordingFrame()` (`src/lib/recording/recorder.ts`), the compositor the page recorder and the
  fast export share, ends with `stampFrame(ctx, mark.seal, …)`: over the background, the copied arena and the Top / Bottom
  Text, in the compositor's own canvas – the recorder's off-screen canvas its MediaRecorder stream is captured from, the fast
  export's frame canvas its VideoFrames are made of – which never enters the document. The batch render, the viral bot's
  renders and the desktop render queue all export through the fast export, split-screen races record through the page
  recorder, and the desktop app's ffmpeg only scales and pads the rendered file, so every output path carries it. The live
  canvas on the page stays clean (the recorder captures its own composed frames, not the page's canvas: drawing the mark there
  too would put it into the clip twice); the line under the stage says that a free recording carries the mark. --- watermark-everywhere ---
  No longer: the live canvas carries the mark too, and the page recorder adds no badge of its own to a frame that already
  carries it – see [Watermark on every free simulation](#watermark-on-every-free-simulation).
- **The gate** (`src/lib/watermark/seal.ts`) – when a recording or export starts, `sealWatermark()` waits for a licence check
  or a renewal in flight (at most `ENTITLEMENT_WAIT_MS`), reads the stored token (`jbl.license`), verifies it again –
  signature with the build's public key, header, payload and expiry (`watermarkDecision()`) – and returns a frozen
  `WatermarkSeal`; its verdict lives in the module's private WeakMap and the compositor holds the seal in its frame loop's
  closure. No setter, option, flag, DOM attribute, class, `data-*` attribute, CSS variable, global, URL parameter or
  localStorage flag reaches it, a look-alike object is marked, and editing the token makes it invalid (= marked). The built-ins
  the verdict path calls are captured when the bundle loads – WebCrypto's `importKey` / `verify`, the clock, the verdict
  `WeakMap`'s `get` / `set`, and the licence decoding's `atob`, `JSON.parse` and `TextDecoder` / `TextEncoder` – so
  `crypto.subtle.verify = async () => true`, `Date.now = () => 0`, `WeakMap.prototype.get = () => true` or an `atob` /
  `JSON.parse` patched to revive an expired licence, typed into the console afterwards, changes nothing (and so flips no
  recording already under way); a verification patched before the page loaded meets a canary: the
  same signed bytes with one signature bit flipped must not verify. The build's test mode (no `NEXT_PUBLIC_LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY`)
  is the only switch: there the committed TEST key's licences – the smoke test's – remove the mark as they make a visitor Pro;
  no debug flag, query parameter or other environment variable does.
- **The UI** – the video-making buttons carry a **Watermark** tag (a drop, `WatermarkBadge.tsx`) for a free visitor instead of
  the old lock, the account row says "Free – your videos carry a watermark" with **Remove watermark**, a line under the stage
  (`FreeWatermarkNote.tsx`) says it too – while recording: this video carries it – with **Remove the watermark**, and the Unlock
  dialog is titled "Remove the watermark". The pricing page's table shows Free's videos "With watermark", Pro's "No watermark"
  and Publish as Pro; the landing, FAQ, studio, TikTok, About, download, terms and disclaimer copy and the page metadata say
  the same in English, Polish and Spanish (namespace `Watermark`, the `Billing` keys). All of it is display only.
- **The viral bot** – without `BOT_LICENSE` (on a production build, which refuses the CLI's own test licence) it renders and
  says the clips carry the watermark; a `BOT_LICENSE` the site refuses still stops it.
- **Honest limits** – the page's code runs on the visitor's machine. What DevTools' Elements and Application panels reach –
  the DOM, classes, attributes, storage – changes nothing, and neither do the console patches the tests cover: the built-ins
  the verdict path calls are captured at load (WebCrypto's `verify`, the clock, the verdict `WeakMap`'s `get` / `set`, the
  licence decoding's `atob`, `JSON.parse` and `TextDecoder` / `TextEncoder`), so redefining them from the console reaches
  nothing. This is not console-proof in general – the verdict path still calls a few other built-ins (its `String` methods,
  `await`), and redefining one of those, or any code that changes the page's JavaScript itself (the Sources panel's local
  overrides or breakpoints, an extension or a userscript, a rewriting proxy), can still remove the stamp; so can capturing the
  live canvas or the screen. The obfuscation of the shipped JavaScript ([Obfuscation and the licence](#obfuscation-and-the-licence))
  raises the bar; only rendering the videos on a server would be a guarantee.
- **Tests** – `tests/freeWatermark.test.ts`: the decision (no licence, Pro, monthly, expired, another key, an edited exp or
  plan, a flipped signature, `alg: none`, garbage), storage flags, globals and the DOM ignored, the module's exports (no
  setter), frozen seals and look-alikes, WebCrypto and `Date.now` patched after load, the verdict `WeakMap`'s `get` / `set`
  and the licence decoding's `atob` / `JSON.parse` / `TextDecoder` / `TextEncoder` patched after load (an earlier seal's mark
  unchanged, a real Pro licence still clean), the canary for a patch before load; the
  6 s corner schedule; the badge inside the safe zone and the exported square of every export size, one corner each; the tiles
  covering every part of the frame, staggered and turned about the centre; the painter's two draws a frame from one sprite and
  one layer with 70 % and 4 % baked in, and blank layers refused; `drawRecordingFrame()` with a free, null, look-alike and Pro
  seal; the page recorder and the fast export (stand-in WebCodecs, the digest still reproducible), free and Pro; and a source
  check that only those two make videos and that the batch, the bot and the desktop queue go through the fast export
  (`tests/fakeCanvas.ts` is the stand-in canvas). The smoke test's free-watermark block decodes the downloaded videos in the
  browser: a free recording's frames carry the badge (bottom-left before 6 s, bottom-right after) and the faint tiles; a free
  recording during which the page is tampered with – every element whose id, class or data attribute names a watermark, Pro or
  a licence removed, `data-pro="true"` and Pro classes on `<html>` and `<body>`, Pro flags and an expired-then-revived licence
  in localStorage, `crypto.subtle.verify`, `WeakMap.prototype.get` / `set` and `atob` / `JSON.parse` patched – still carries
  them (both the recording under way and the next); a Pro recording (the test licence) carries neither; a
  free fast export and a free batch render carry them; the pricing page and the Unlock dialog show the new copy; and a free
  1080×1920 recording with the mark keeps its frame-rate floor.

### Mode thumbnails
Feature mode-thumbnails – the owner's "change the thumbnails": every mode card (the modes wall, the studio's mode picker, the
daily card) shows its mode at its **hero moment** – mid-action, with the payoff on screen – framed the same way for all of them.

- **The hero table** – `HERO_MOMENTS` in `src/lib/thumbnails/heroMoments.ts`, an entry per id of `MODE_IDS`: the settings of a
  link on top of glow, trails and wall glow (`HERO_BASE_QUERY`: `glow=1&trails=1&wglow=1`), a pinned seed, the second of the
  run, a camera – the subject square's centre (`x`, `y`, fractions of the world) and `zoom` (1, the default, is the centred
  square a clip records) – and the mode's colour for the edge glow. Bigger balls (`r`), thicker rings (`wt`) and trails (`tt`)
  keep a 240 px card legible; the modes' own HUD switches (`tsb=0`, `tn=0`, `lch=0`, `sbh=0`, `mzhud=0`, `mzbg=0`, `tybg=0`,
  `rcst=0`, `rcmm=0`, `ogHud=0`, `plb=none`, `plp=0`, `lh=0`) leave only the element that is the point – a battle's score, the
  fighters' names, the HOME count –, and the camera frames out a HUD without a switch (the String Circle title, the Sound
  Vortex and Conveyor Belt counters, the race commentary, the Multipliers badges, Fight League's ability boxes – --- low-findings
  --- and its names' stat lines and clock, Bullseye's corner counters, Journey's stage label, Territory's PICK A SIDE and clock:
  Fight League's card is the clash up close). No captions or
  top / bottom text, no watermark: the stills come from the arena canvas, never the video compositor, and the generator runs
  with a test Pro licence (`PREVIEW_LICENSE`, a real key, against a production build) – the still camera stamps a free
  visitor's stills like a video's frames (see the still camera below). The entries are below, one line per mode.
- **The frame** – `src/lib/thumbnails/heroFrame.ts`: a square like every card (`aspect-square`), `THUMB_SIZE` 480 × 480 (2× the
  ~240 px card), the subject with the same `THUMB_INSET` margin (4 %) on every side – past the world's edge the stage's darkest
  corner colour fills it –, the same radial vignette and a subtle inner glow along the edges in the mode's colour, encoded as
  WebP under `THUMB_MAX_BYTES` (60 KB): the quality steps down from 0.9 until it fits (`encodeUnderBudget()`).
- **The still camera** – `window.__jumpingBallsStill` (`src/components/simulator/useHeroStill.ts`, `src/lib/thumbnails/stillRender.ts`):
  the fast export's hidden offline canvas with its seeded `Math.random` and clock, stepped frame by frame to the hero second and
  drawn at 2–6 device px per world px (`heroRenderScale()`), then filtered down – an entry gives the same picture on any
  machine, however busy. The moments are tuned for the 800 × 450 world of a 1400 × 900 window (`HERO_WORLD`); the generator
  refuses another. --- watermark-everywhere --- A still is a picture of the run like a video's frame: each capture is sealed
  when it starts (free-watermark's `sealWatermark()`) and every picture it hands back – the framed card and the raw world – is
  stamped (`stampFrame()`) unless that seal is a verified Pro licence's, so the still camera, reachable from the console, gives a
  visitor without Pro no clean frame; the result says so (`watermarked`), and the generator refuses to write such a picture.
- **Regenerating** – build and serve the site, then run the generator (`npm run previews`, `scripts/generate-mode-previews.mjs`):

  ```bash
  NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH=/Balls npm run build
  node scripts/serve-static.mjs --port 3000 --base /Balls &
  BASE_URL=http://localhost:3000/Balls npm run previews                       # every card (and the gallery cards)
  BASE_URL=http://localhost:3000/Balls MODES=classic,drop npm run previews    # those cards only
  BASE_URL=http://localhost:3000/Balls MODES=classic SWEEP=2:6:0.25 SEEDS=1,2,3 npm run previews   # pick a moment
  ```

  It stops before writing anything when a mode of `MODE_IDS` – or a card on the served site – has no hero moment
  (`missingHeroModes()`), and prints every file's size, WebP quality and the seed and second it shows, then the total and the
  largest. `SWEEP=from:to:step` writes a contact sheet per mode (`<mode>-sweep.jpg`, a row per seed of `SEEDS`, framed like the
  cards) to `OUT_DIR` (default: the system's temp folder) instead of `public/modes`; `RAW=1` adds the whole world of each second
  (to place a camera), `EXTRA=<query>` tries settings on top of the entry's (a key the entry sets keeps its value) and
  `CAMERA=x,y,zoom` another camera. A new mode adds its line to the table and runs `MODES=<mode> npm run previews`.
- **The viral bot's covers** – the same idea for a clip (`heroMomentSec()`, `src/lib/bot/cover.ts`): the payoff on screen (its
  second + 0.25 s); the clip's last moment when it is cut before the payoff; else the mode's hero second when the clip reaches
  it; else 85 % of the clip – never the first frame. The manifest's `cover` (`atSec`, `ms`, `source`), a "Cover: 12.4 s – the
  payoff on screen" line in every caption `.txt` and a Covers list in `posting-schedule.md` carry it (translated, `ViralBot.cover`;
  the manual posting steps say to set that frame as the cover); the CLI posts it as Instagram's `thumb_offset`, and the publish
  relay takes it as `coverMs` – TikTok's `video_cover_timestamp_ms` (1 s without it) and Instagram's `thumb_offset`.
- **Tests** – `tests/modeThumbnails.test.ts`: a hero moment for every `MODE_IDS` id and nothing else, complete entries, links
  that open their mode with glow, trails and their seed and no caption, text or watermark, every own key changing the run (no
  typo, no no-op), the frame's geometry and budget, the still schedule, the pictures on disk (WebP, 480 × 480, under 60 KB), the
  table below against `HERO_MOMENTS`, a clip's hero second and the bot's covers (manifest, caption files, schedule, the three
  languages); `relay/relay.test.mjs` the relay's `coverMs`. The smoke test's mode-thumbnails block fetches every card picture
  as served (200, `image/webp`, under 60 KB, 480 × 480 in its header), decodes each on the modes wall and samples its pixels
  (colours, contrast, something bright), and checks that the wall and the studio's mode picker show a card for every mode.

### Watermark on every free simulation
Feature watermark-everywhere – the owner's request (2026-10-04): "add the watermark to all simulations made without pro". The
free-video watermark (above) marked the videos; now every frame of a live simulation a visitor without a verified Pro licence
sees carries it too: the studio's canvas in every mode – share links (`?c=`), the gallery's presets, the daily challenge and
the Windows app run that same canvas –, a split-screen race's composed frame and the landing page's live preview.

- **The mark** – the free-watermark one, reused, not a second implementation: the badge sprite (`buildBadgeSprite()`) and the
  diagonal domain tiles at 4 % (`buildTileLayer()`) of `src/lib/watermark/paint.ts`, and its 6 s corner schedule
  (`badgeCorner()`). On the live canvas (`src/lib/watermark/liveLayout.ts`) the badge is 4.5 % of the exported square's side tall
  (`LIVE_BADGE_HEIGHT`, at least 18 px), 1.5 % of it from the edges (`LIVE_MARGIN`), in a corner of the exported square (the
  centred square the page recorder crops), on the left for 0–6 s, on the right for 6–12 s and so on. The landing page's preview
  places it in its whole phone frame.
- **Clear of the HUD** – after drawing, the canvas reports the HUD of the frame (`LiveHud`, in world px, no allocation): bands
  along the top and the bottom of the square – the modes' top HUD (`modeTopHud`: Fight League's names, Land Claim's, Territory's
  and the Maze's HUD bands, the Bouncing Orbs line, the String Battle's badge, Power Layers' pills, the multiplier badges), Fight
  League's ability boxes, the String Circle's standings strip, the arena games' scoreboard band, the captions' stacks, the song
  bar – and blocks: the teams' scoreboard, the bounce-math values, the No limits and speed readouts, Journey's clock and
  mini-map, a race's standings and mini-map, the title blocks of the Sound Vortex, Beat Drop, Bullseye and Conveyor Belt, the
  Top / Bottom Text, a split-screen race's arena labels (`src/components/simulator/liveMarkHud.ts` holds the geometry of the
  ones whose renderers do not report it). The badge takes the bottom corner of its side inside the free band between the bands;
  when a block holds it, the top corner of the same side (so it still changes side every 6 s); when both are held, the other
