  side's; only when the HUD holds every corner does it go over it, in its bottom corner (`placeLiveBadge()`).
- **Where it is drawn** – into the canvas' own pixels as the frame's last pass (`stampLiveFrame()`): `Canvas.tsx` calls it at
  the end of its draw routine, after everything else, for its live canvas only (`if (!offline)`: the fast export's hidden canvas
  and a split-screen race's arena canvases are composed elsewhere); `splitScreenCanvas.tsx` once per composed frame, after the
  labels, banner, text and captions; `LivePreview.tsx` after each preview frame. Never a DOM overlay, a CSS pseudo-element or a
  second canvas: deleting elements, classes or styles in DevTools leaves the pixels as they are.
- **The gate** – free-watermark's, not a second one: the live gate (`live.ts`) holds a `WatermarkSeal` from `sealWatermark()`
  (the stored licence verified again, with the built-ins captured at load) in module-private state and asks `sealVerdict()`
  every frame; until the first verification answers there is no seal, which is marked. It seals again whenever the licence can
  have changed – the entitlement store reports a change (a licence pasted, restored or removed, another tab's storage event, the
  focus, a licence that ran out), the stored licence is not the one verified (checked every second: marked at once until the new
  one verifies) – and every minute. So a Pro licence removes the mark from the next frames, mid-run too, and removing it brings
  the mark back on the very next frame. No setter, option, flag, DOM or `data-*` attribute, class, CSS variable, global or storage
  flag reaches it, and the canvas' `data-*` attributes say nothing about it.
- **One badge per video frame** – the page recorder copies the live canvas, which already carries the mark, so its compositor
  adds no badge (and no tiles) of its own to a frame the live pass marked (`liveMarkCovers()` in `drawRecordingFrame()`); while it
  records, the live pass puts the badge where the video layout puts it in the export frame (inside the platforms' safe zone, 5.2 %
  of the exported square), so the clip looks as before. A frame the live pass did not mark – the fast export's, the batch
  render's, the viral bot's and the desktop queue's offline canvases, a canvas resized since, a free recording during which a Pro
  licence was activated – is stamped by the compositor as before.
  --- low-findings --- **The recorder's tap**: the studio's canvas and a split-screen race's stage hand every frame they draw to
  the page recorder just before their live pass (`setRecordingTap()`, `RecordingOptions.registerSourceTap`), so the recorder
  copies it without the live mark and stamps the video's own: the badge at export resolution (the canvas' badge scaled up 2× from
  a 509 px square was soft) and the tiles over the whole export frame, the letterbox bars included (the live tiles cover only the
  canvas) – still one badge a frame (the stream's first frame, copied when the recording starts, carries the canvas' own). The
  compositor places it as the live pass does while it records: in the video layout's zone, clear of the HUD the source canvas
  reported for that frame (`placeVideoBadge()`, the HUD mapped with `mapLiveHud()` in `liveLayout.ts`); the fast export's
  offline canvas reports its HUD too (`OfflineFrameRenderer.hud()`), so a free String Circle export's badge sits above the
  standings strip instead of on its end chips – with no HUD the badge is exactly where it always was.
- **Cost** – one badge sprite (per badge height: the live one and a recording's) and one tile layer per canvas size, cached per
  canvas: a frame costs two `drawImage()` calls, and the live and recording frame-rate floors of the smoke test hold. A mark that
  cannot be built (a canvas that draws nothing) never breaks the frame loop: it is tried again a second later, and meanwhile the
  frame counts as unmarked, so a recording gets the compositor's mark.
- **The copy** – the pricing page (under its lede) and the Unlock dialog (under its reason) say that free simulations and free
  videos carry a small watermark and Pro removes it from both (`Billing.liveMark`), the video buttons' tag says so in its tooltip
  (`Watermark.liveTagTip`) and the line under the stage too (`Watermark.liveNote`), in English, Polish and Spanish.
- **The site's own images** – `scripts/generate-mode-previews.mjs` and `scripts/generate-og.mjs` render as Pro (a licence signed
  with the committed TEST key, which a test-mode build accepts; `PREVIEW_LICENSE` takes a real key for a production build), so
  the mode cards, the gallery's pictures and the social preview stay clean. The mode cards come from mode-thumbnails' still
  camera (`window.__jumpingBallsStill`, `useHeroStill.ts`), which renders on the fast export's offline canvas – never stamped
  live – and so stamps its pictures itself, like a compositor: sealed per capture, a free visitor's stills (the framed card and
  the raw world) carry the badge and the tiles, and a capture that carries them says so (`watermarked`) – the generator then
  stops rather than write a marked card.
- **Extending** – a new canvas that plays a simulation calls `stampLiveFrame()` as its last pass (and never on a canvas a
  compositor exports from) – and, while the page recorder records it, its tap just before that (--- low-findings ---); a mode that
  draws HUD text in a corner of the square or along its top or bottom edge reports it to the frame's `LiveHud` (a band for a
  full-width strip, a block otherwise) before the pass – live and offline, where the export's badge keeps clear of it too.
- **Honest limits** – as for the videos: code that changes the page's JavaScript (an override, an extension, a userscript) or a
  console that draws over the canvas after the live pass can hide the badge from the screen – and so from a page recording, which
  copies the canvas – and capturing the screen records whatever it shows; only rendering on a server would be a guarantee.
- **Tests** – `tests/watermarkEverywhere.test.ts`: the sizes and margins, the 6 s side change, the HUD bands and blocks (a held
  corner, both held, all held, a band with no room), the HUD geometry of the modes, the recording's placement against the video
  layout of four export sizes; the gate (no licence, Pro, monthly, expired, another key, garbage; marked from the first frame;
  a licence activated and removed mid-run; storage flags, globals and the DOM ignored; the module's exports); the painter (two
  draws a frame, last, state reset; caches per canvas and size; a mark that cannot be built); one badge per video frame (the
  compositor over a marked canvas, the fast export's, a resized one, a licence activated mid-recording, the page recorder end to
  end; --- low-findings --- the tap's frames stamped at export resolution with tiles over the bars, the badge clear of a reported
  HUD, the tap taken back when the recording stops); the wiring (the canvas' last pass, never offline; the split-screen stage once; the landing preview; the Windows app; the
  still camera's seal and stamps, and the generator refusing a marked still); the copy in three languages. The smoke test's
  watermark-everywhere block runs a free visitor in ten modes, a split-screen race, a share link, the daily challenge and the
  landing preview (the badge within 1 s of the start, on the other side within 7 s), tampers with the DOM during a live run,
  toggles the test licence mid-run (no mark for Pro in the same modes), counts the badge regions of a free recording on a light
  background (exactly one, one layer deep), measures the live and recording frame rates with the mark, and takes the same still
  with the still camera as a free visitor (badge and tiles, card and raw world, reported `watermarked`) and as Pro (neither; two
  Pro stills give the same pixels).

### Grow: fill and loop
Feature loop-foundation – Grow's upgrade after the growing-ball clips of the loop family. Two options on top of the classic
mode, both off by default (every old link, preset and found seed replays exactly – `tests/grow.test.ts` keeps the classic
fingerprint):

- **Growth law** (`growLaw`, URL `gLaw`): **approach** – the classic rule, each bounce closes a share of the gap to the cap
  (Growth Rate) –, **multiply** – r ← min(cap, r · (1 + step / 100)), so r_n = r0 (1 + p)^n: slow at first, explosive at the
  end, the bounces crowding together as the free chord shortens – or **add** – r ← min(cap, r + step px). The new laws start
  the ball at **Start size** (`growStart`, % of the ring, URL `gStart`) in the centre and launch it at the Ball Speed in a
  seeded direction; every bounce grows it by **Growth per bounce** (`growStep`, % or px, URL `gStep`) and rebounds it in a
  clean chord – specular, never closer than a per-run chord angle (18–36°, from the seed) to the diameter – so every cycle of
  a run plays the same pattern turned by its launch direction. Only a real bounce (the ball moving out) grows the ball.
- **When it fills** (`growOnFill`, URL `gFill`): **stay** (the classic: the full ball keeps buzzing), **loop** – within half
  a pixel of the cap the ball snaps to it in the centre and holds for **Fill hold** (`growHold`, 1.6 s, URL `gHold`), shrinks
  back to the start size over **Shrink** (`growShrink`, 1 s, eased, URL `gShrink`) and relaunches in a seeded direction –
  a seamless cycle: the hold, the shrink and the relaunch land on exact simulation times (fill + hold, fill + hold + shrink),
  and the mode reports the cycle and its seams to the loop contract – or **finish**: the run ends at the fill, so Find
  Simulation searches it: its length, **Fills within** a limit or **Fill on a bar line** (within a 60 fps frame of a bar of
  four beats at the Sound section's BPM).
- **The look** (render-only): **Colour by size** (`growHue`, URL `gHue`) runs the ball through the **colour stops**
  (`growRamp`, 2–6 colours, cyan → green → yellow → orange by default, URL `gRamp`) by r / cap; **Contact markers**
  (`growMarkers`, URL `gMark`) leave a small ring where the ball hit, fading over **Marker lifetime** (`growMarkerLife`,
  0.3 s, URL `gMarkT`; a ring buffer of 64); the hold and the shrink draw the ball as a solid disc
  (`components/simulator/growRenderer.ts`).
- **Pitch by size** (`growPitch`, URL `gPitch`): every bounce plays a pentatonic pluck pitched by the size the ball hit with
  (`degreeFromScalar()` over the log of the size, inverted – bigger is lower, about two bounces a degree at ×1.11); the fill
  plays the completion chord on G2 (root, fifth, octave, twelfth and a soft tenth, with a ding and a sub thump), the shrink
  cuts the loop voices and glides from the chord's root to an octave under the next cycle's first pluck.
- **Presets** (the Grow block of the Mode row): **Fill and loop** (multiply 11 %, start 5 %, loop, colour by size, markers,
  pitch, on a black page with a thin slate ring, no gravity or trails, no HUD), **Slow burn** (4 % a bounce), **Instant**
  (60 % a bounce) and **Classic grow** (back to the defaults).
- Every number is uncapped (comfort ranges in `GROW_FILL_RANGES`: Growth per bounce 0–1000, Start size 0.1–100, hold and shrink
  0–60 s, marker lifetime 0–5 s; any value from the minimum up is typed in). Settings and URL keys live in
  `src/lib/physics/growFill.ts`; the mode in `src/lib/physics/modes/grow.ts`.

### Loop foundation: sounds, whole loops and the loop HUD
The shared pieces every loop-family simulation builds on (feature loop-foundation):

- **The loop sounds** (`src/lib/audio/loopTones.ts`, all original and synthesized – no audio file is copied): pentatonic
  plucks (three partials, a decay that shortens as the hits come faster), tuned bars, chimes, dings, a progress ladder that
  climbs a step per event and resets, a groove bed (pad, sub and arpeggio over a seeded progression), impact accents, the
  completion chord, risers, reset glides, noise washes, a ratchet and a field drone whose energy and speed follow the run.
  `ToneGenerator.playLoop(kind, frequency, level)` plays them live and in the fast export's offline twin, under a voice budget
  (at most 6 new notes a tick, 18 oscillators, one thump and one noise voice, 24 sounding; notes of the same pitch within
  30 ms merge at 1/√n; the bed at most 10 nodes; the drone retuned at 30 Hz). The pure pitch rules are in
  `src/lib/audio/loopPitch.ts` (`degreeFromScalar()`), the clip slots in `src/lib/audio/loopClips.ts` (your own sounds for a
  family's voice, kept in the page only).
- **Loudness** (`src/lib/audio/loudness.ts`): the fast export's and the batch render's mix is measured with BS.1770-4
  (K-weighting, 400 ms gated blocks, a 4× true-peak estimate) and given one static gain to −14 LUFS, the true peak at most
  −1 dBTP (a silent mix is left alone); the live page keeps its limiter.
- **Whole loops** (`src/lib/loop/loopContract.ts`): a mode with a seamless cycle reports it (`GameMode.cycleSeconds()`,
  `loopSeams()`; the canvas mirrors it in `data-loop-cycle` and `data-loop-seams`). With **Export whole loops** on (the
  Recording section, `exportWholeLoops`, URL `wl`, on by default) the page recorder, the fast export and the batch render end
  the clip just before the seam that closes its last whole cycle – the largest whole number of cycles that fits in the clip
  (a frame of slack; the recording may run a frame or two past the clip to reach that seam), whose last frame flows into its
  first. A recording of a looping run starts at the run's start; a mode that does not loop records as before. --- low-findings ---
  Record Video holds the restarted run at that start (`holdRun()` on the canvas: no physics steps, the frame still drawn) until
  the canvas has drawn it and the recorder has copied it as the clip's first frame (`waitForDrawnFrame()`), so the clip starts on
  the seam it ends on – the engine used to run on through the audio's and the recorder's start-up, a few frames into its cycle
  (the canvas' `data-loop-record-start-ms` is the run's time at that first frame: 0). The fast export's done line says what it
  did besides: "Cut to 3 whole loops of 12.85 s, so it loops seamlessly." and the normaliser's gain, "Sound levelled to −14 LUFS
  (+1.1 dB)." (`FastExport.doneLoops` / `doneGain` in en / pl / es, `fastExportDoneDetail()`; a gain that rounds to nothing is
  left out).
- **The loop HUD** (`src/lib/loop/hud.ts`, the Recording section's **Loop HUD**, `loopHud`, URL `lh`, off by default): a bold
  lowercase title at about 16 % of the frame, an optional grey subtitle under a thin rule, and one amber counter (`#f2c46a`,
  grey on a light page) at about 85 % – drawn into every export frame by the compositor (`drawRecordingFrame()`), so the
  recordings carry it, and previewed live around the ring. The words default to the mode's own (Grow: its bounces) and can be
  typed (`loopHudTitle`, `loopHudSubtitle`, URL `lht`, `lhs`). In a portrait clip the title and the counter sit in the bars
  above and below the arena; on a square or landscape frame the counter moves up just above the watermark's badge. --- low-findings
  --- The live preview leaves a line out where its margin is thinner than the smallest line (`LIVE_HUD_MIN_SIZE`, 10 px): on a
  phone's square canvas the ring reaches up under the page's buttons, so the title is not drawn on the ring there (the counter
  below it stays; the clips carry every line – the recorder lays out its own frame). `data-loop-hud-live` says where the lines
  went against the ring.
- **Loop style captions** (`src/lib/publish/loopCaption.ts`, the Publish caption editor's **Loop style** button): a
  lowercase hook led by a number and ending in " 🔊", a plain-English fact under it, and four or five hashtags,
  #satisfying #oddlysatisfying first, then the topic's – no mentions, no call to action. --- low-findings --- The words are the
  clip's: the page takes `loopCaptionContext()` of the settings a clip was made with when it offers the clip to Publish
  (`PublishClip.loopCaption`: Record Video, the fast export, the batch render), and `clipLoopCaptionContext()` uses them – the
  page's settings of the moment only for a clip of its mode or of none (a picked file), another mode's clip its own seconds –
  and a "{count} seconds" hook counts the clip's own length when it is known.
- **The Windows app's assistant** (--- low-findings ---): on a Grow page it may change Grow's settings – the classic ones and the
  fill-and-loop ones (`growLaw`, `growOnFill` as enums, `growStep`… uncapped like every slider) – and on any page Export whole
  loops and the loop HUD (`MODE_PREFIXES` / `SETTING_OPTIONS` / `CATALOG` in `src/lib/desktop/ai/settingsPatch.ts`).
- **Tests**: `tests/loopTones.test.ts`, `tests/loudness.test.ts`, `tests/loopClips.test.ts`, `tests/loopContract.test.ts`,
  `tests/loopHud.test.ts`, `tests/loopCaption.test.ts` and `tests/grow.test.ts`; the smoke test (section 38, block
  `loop-foundation`) runs the Fill and loop preset at 2× (two fills or more, every cycle within a frame of the others), checks
  the HUD's pixels, exports a whole number of cycles and lists the Grow outcomes of Find Simulation.

### Chord Stars
Feature chord-stars – the loop family's star-polygon clip ("five stars, all closing at once"), a rhythm-family mode
(`starChords`) built on the loop foundation:

- **The geometry** (`src/lib/physics/starChords.ts`, pure): a ball bouncing inside a circle meets the wall at the same angle
  every time, so ball i moves its contact point by the central angle 2π·kᵢ/nᵢ every bounce: vertex j sits at θ0 + 2π·((j·k)
  mod n)/n, every chord is 2R·sin(πk/n) long and lies R·cos(πk/n) from the centre – the inner circle every chord of the star
  touches. A step that shares a factor with n traces the reduced star gcd(n, k) times (10/4 draws 5/2 twice).
- **The timing** (`src/lib/physics/modes/starChords.ts`): the mode owns its playfield (ring layout "none") and pins ordinary
  engine balls to the simulation clock like the Pendulum Wave: at cycle time t ball i has made bᵢ = ⌊t·nᵢ/T⌋ bounces and sits on
  the chord from vertex bᵢ to bᵢ + 1, its speed nᵢ·chordᵢ/T, so every star closes on the same frame t = T whatever the frame
  rate (`tests/starChords.test.ts` checks 30, 60 and 120 fps). The bounces of a step are counted from the clock, never
  detected, so none is missed or doubled and a star of a million points costs what a pentagram costs. Then the drawing holds
  (`scHold`), fades (`scFade`) and the next cycle starts exactly like the first: a loop of T + hold + fade that the mode reports
  to the loop contract (`cycleSeconds()`, `loopSeams()`), so **Export whole loops** cuts every export at a seam. Nothing is
  random: every seed plays the same run.
- **Settings** (the Chord Stars block of the Mode row; every number uncapped from its minimum): **Balls** (`scBalls`, 1–12,
  URL `scn`; a run builds at most `ENTITY_CEILING` = 5,000), **Stars** (`scStars`, "5/2,7/3,8/3,9/4,12/5", URL `scs`; balls
  past the list take the next free point counts with the step nearest 0.4·n; the **Random** button draws a coprime set – a
  different n per ball, never 6), **Drawing Time** (`scCycle`, 1–30 s, default 12, URL `sct`), **Hold** (`scHold`, 0–3 s,
  1.2, `sch`), **Fade** (`scFade`, 0–3 s, 1.8, `scf`), **Start Spread** (`scSpread`, 0–1, `scsp`), **Line Width**
  (`scLineWidth`, 0.5–4 px, `scw`), **Inner Circles** (`scEnvelope`: when closed / always / off, `sce`), **Colors**
  (`scPalette`: the pastels pink, cyan, yellow, green and lavender – then more –, a rainbow or the Ball Color, `scp`),
  **Bounce Sound** (`scVoice`: pluck, chime, bar or silent, `scv`) and **Closing Chord** (`scChord`, `scc`). A fresh Chord
  Stars page starts on the navy page with the lavender circle and the loop HUD on. Presets: **Five stars** (the defaults),
  **Seven-point bloom** (seven heptagrams 7/3, each a seventh of a point further round: a 49-point bloom over one inner circle)
  and **Heptagram duel** (7/2 against 7/3 from opposite sides, 10 s).
- **The canvas** (`src/components/simulator/starChordsRenderer.ts`): the thin circle, the inner circles, the chords drawn so
  far in an offscreen layer that only adds the chords completed since the last frame and is cleared with every cycle (a frame
  draws at most 20,000 new chords; past `SC_CHORD_CEILING` = 200,000 chords in a cycle the oldest fade by half every quarter of
  the ceiling – nothing is stored per chord), the chord each ball is drawing, a glint at every bounce, the balls with a small
  glow and a soft flash as the stars close. The loop HUD reads "stars that close together", a two-line grey subtitle (a
  subtitle splits into two lines at " / ") and the amber counter "stars closed 3/5" (`{count}` and `{total}`; back at 0/5
  halfway through the fade, so a clip's last frames read like its first). The canvas
  mirrors the run in `data-sc-*` (cycles, phase, stars closed, chords this cycle, per cycle, last cycle and in all) and what
  its chord layer drew (this cycle, and of the last cycle it saw end: `data-sc-layer-last`, `data-sc-layer-last-cycle`).
- **Sound** (`loopTones.ts` through `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`, original synthesis): every bounce is one note on its ball's
  pitch – the balls ranked by speed, two degrees apart on A major pentatonic from A3 (five balls: A, C♯, F♯, B, E; a crowd
  closer together, never past B5, so every pluck stays inside the C3–C6 register) – at the
  narrow velocity 0.8 + 0.2 × its speed (the fast balls with the bright third partial), 1/√n when n balls bounce in one step,
  at most one note a ball a step and 20 a step; a star that closes before the others (its step shares a factor with n, so it
  closes its reduced star early) rings a soft ding an octave over its note; when the stars close, the completion chord on A2
  with its ding and sub; when the fade starts, the loop voices are cut (after a hold of 0.3 s or more) and a reset glide falls
  from the chord's root to an octave under the next cycle's first note, silent at the seam. Every bounce is also bounce math's
  "bounce" trigger.
- **Find Simulation** offers **Best star set**: every star closes on time by construction, so the search draws random
  coprime star sets (`randomStarSet()`), keeps the best-looking one (`starSetScore()`: inner circles evenly spread, none a dot
  in the centre or a ring at the rim, about eight chords a ball) and fits the drawing time to the clip in whole loops
  (`cycleForClip()`), so the found run's export is an exact loop of the clip (`src/lib/simulation/starChordsFinder.ts`).
- **Tests**: `tests/starChords.test.ts` (the vertex sequence of 5/2 back at the start after exactly 5 bounces, the chord's
  distance R·cos(πk/n) within 1e-9, every ball closing on the same frame for any T, the fingerprints at 30/60/120 fps, the URL
  round trip, the finder, the sound scheduling through `tests/fakeAudio.ts`); the smoke test (section 39, block `chord-stars`) runs three cycles
  at 4× and checks that every cycle drew Σn chords – by the run's clock and on the canvas' chord layer –, the URL round trip
  of the block, the HUD's counter, the plucks' pitches, the finder's outcome and a fast export of whole loops.

### Spinning Hoops (the loop-clip family)
Feature bead-hoops – the loop-clip account's bifurcation reel ("the biggest hoop's bead lifts first 🔊") as a rhythm-family
mode without rings (`hoops`: the physics in `src/lib/physics/modes/hoops.ts`, the settings, URL keys and presets in
`src/lib/physics/hoopsFields.ts`, the picture in `src/components/simulator/hoopsRenderer.ts`, the panel block in
`src/components/simulator/sections/HoopsSection.tsx`):

- **The physics**: **Hoops** (`hpCount`, 1–16 on the slider, URL `hpn`; any count typed, built up to the memory ceiling)
  with radii evenly spaced from **Outer hoop** to **Inner hoop** (`hpRadiusMax` 0.75 and `hpRadiusMin` 0.25 of the half
  field, which is one metre – the rig leaves the loop HUD its margins; URL `hprx`, `hprn`) spin together about their vertical
  diameter, one bead sliding on each:
  θ″ = ω² sin θ cos θ − (g/R) sin(θ − α) − c θ′ (with α = 0 the textbook sin θ (ω² cos θ − g/R) − c θ′), integrated with
  classic RK4 in at least 8 sub-steps a 60 Hz step – more for stiff hoops, counted from the simulation time alone, so 30, 60
  and 120 fps replay the same run; a bead past the sub-steps' reach rests on its balance point instead of blowing up. Below a
  hoop's critical spin ω_c = √(g/R) its bottom is stable; past it the bead climbs to θ* = arccos(g/(Rω²)), higher the faster
  the spin (a pitchfork bifurcation): bigger hoops lift first, and every bead that is up sits the same g/ω² under the centre,
  so the beads line up level across the hoops and rise together as the spin climbs. **Hoop gravity** (`hpGravity`, 9.81 m/s², URL `hpg`),
  **Bead damping** (`hpDamping`, 1/s, URL `hpd`) and the **Axis tilt** α (`hpJitter`, 0.004 rad, URL `hpj`) – the
  imperfection that keeps the symmetric bottom from being a fixed point, so a bead climbs promptly once past ω_c (at 0 it may
  balance on its bottom: an extreme, not a bug). The only random draws, in this order, from the run's seed: the side the axis
  tilts to, then each hoop's tilt factor in [0.9, 1.1], outermost first.
- **The spin**: **Start spin** (`hpOmegaStart`, 0.13 turns a second, URL `hpw0`) to **Top spin** (`hpOmegaEnd`, 1.05, URL
  `hpw1`) over **Ramp up** (`hpRamp`, 20.5 s, URL `hpr`) in a **Ramp shape** (`hpRampShape`, URL `hps`: linear, ease – a cubic
  in and out – or steps, one equal step a hoop with eased risers), a **Top hold** (`hpHold`, 1 s, URL `hph`), and with
  **Return and loop** (`hpReturn`, on, URL `hprt`) the ramp mirrored over the **Return ramp** (`hpReturnSec`, 4 s, URL
  `hprs`), then 3 s at the start spin while the beads settle (a 28.6 s cycle: the chord as the last bead lifts comes at
  about 68 % of it and the top hold ends at about 76 %, the loop family's 65–85 %). The schedule is closed-form (the spin and its turns at any
  time); the hold is stretched by less than a turn so a cycle holds whole turns, and the last second before the seam eases
  every bead onto its exact rest state, so the clip's last frame flows into its first: the mode reports its cycle and seams to
  the loop contract (**Export whole loops**, `data-loop-cycle`). Without the return the run ends after the hold. The block
  names the spins the outer and the inner bead lift at, and warns when the top spin leaves beads at the bottom.
- **The picture**: every hoop an ellipse 2R |cos φ| wide and 2R tall (φ = 2π × the schedule's turns at the frame's own
  simulation time), rainbow by hoop (Rainbow Walls; else the wall colour) in the Wall Thickness with the Wall Glow, the half
  turned away dimmer; every bead at x = R sin θ cos φ, y = R cos θ, its size and light following its depth, glowing in its
  hoop's colour; a faint vertical axis and a pivot disc under the hoops. With the loop HUD on (the presets turn it on; off
  by default, as in every mode) the mode's words are "spin faster, and the beads climb", the grey subtitle "the bigger the
  hoop, the sooner its bead lifts" and the amber counter "0.13 turns a second · 0 of 8 beads up" (in the page's language and decimal style; "… of 1 bead up" with one
  hoop), laid out around the hoops and clear of the watermark. The canvas reports the run in `data-hoops-*` (count, beads
  up, lifts and their order, settles, ticks, spin, phase, cycle, the all-up moment).
- **The sounds** (the loop family's voices through `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`, so the page, the fast export, the batch render
  and the bot sound alike): a glock chime on every turn, higher as the spin climbs – six pentatonic degrees from A5 up to A6,
  the glock register (**Spin chime**, `hpTick`, on, URL `hpt`); a tuned-bar strike when a bead passes 5° above its bottom while the spin is past its critical speed, pitched by its
  hoop on the G pentatonic (bigger is lower); the completion chord on G2 when every bead is up; a soft pluck an octave under
  its bar as each bead settles on the return; at the reset a hard cut and a glide from G2 up to the outer hoop's bar that ends
  exactly on the seam; and with **Groove bed** (`hpBed`, off, URL `hpb`) a pad and sub from the first lift to the reset
  (or to the end of a run without the return; switched off mid-run, it stops at once).
- **Loop-style caption** (the Publish caption editor's **Loop style**): the hoop count leads the hook – "8 hoops spinning
  faster and faster: the beads climb in order, the biggest hoop first 🔊" ("1 hoop spinning faster and faster, until its bead
  starts to climb" with one hoop) –, a plain-English fact under it with checkable numbers (the critical spin √(g/R): about
  0.58 turns a second for a 75 cm radius, almost a full turn for 25 cm) and #satisfying #oddlysatisfying #physics #math
  #creativecoding (`LoopCaption.hoops*`, `loopCaptionContext()`).
- **Presets** (the top of the block; each brings the clip's navy page, rainbow hoops, the HUD and a clip of one whole
  cycle): **8 rainbow hoops** (the defaults), **One hoop, slow** (one hoop at 0.75, an eased 20 s ramp to 0.9 turns a
  second) and **Twin hoops** (0.75 and 0.4, the spin in two steps: one bead a step).
- **Find Simulation**: a looping run has no length to find, but **Lifts in size order** (the first cycle's beads leave
  their bottoms in strict size order – true by physics unless the tilt or the damping is extreme; close twin hoops on a steep
  tilt split the seeds) and **All up within** (every bead up within a time limit) are searched, and a found run records one
  whole cycle. Without the return a run's length is fixed (the ramp and the hold).
- **Tests**: `tests/hoops.test.ts` – the stable bottom (within 1e-6) below ω_c and the balance point (within 1e-3) above
  it, the lift order on every seed, the schedule's whole turns and mirrored return, the fingerprints pinned at 30, 60 and 120
  fps, the sound events and their scheduling on the fake Web Audio graph, the chime's register and the bed's stops, the
  seamless cycle and the HUD counter, the finder outcomes, the URL keys, the presets and the loop-style caption; the smoke test (section 40, block `bead-hoops`) runs the mode for 20 s and sees its eight lifts in
  order, round-trips the URL and the panel, reads the HUD, finds both outcomes, fast-exports whole loops and checks that a
  free visitor's watermark stays clear of the HUD.

### Chaos fan (the Double Pendulum's palindrome)
Feature chaos-fan – the loop-clip account's "they start less than a thousandth of a degree apart" reel (catalogued from its poster
and caption) as an option of the Double Pendulum (`doublePendulum`) rather than a mode of its own: the physics and the run in
`src/lib/physics/chaosFan.ts`, delegated to by `src/lib/physics/modes/doublePendulum.ts` while the fan is on (`ChaosFanRun`), the
picture in `src/components/simulator/chaosFanRenderer.ts` (drawn through `drawDoublePendulumBodies()`), the panel block in
`src/components/simulator/sections/ChaosFanSection.tsx` (at the top of the Double Pendulum block):

- **The fan**: **Chaos fan** (`dpFan`, URL `dpf`) swings **Pendulums** (`dpFanCount`, 1–500 on the slider, any count typed, URL
  `dpfn`; a run builds at most `ENTITY_CEILING` = 5,000 – the memory-safety ceiling the step budget sets, ARENA FULL past it)
  double pendulums from one pivot with the rig's first two rod lengths and masses, its gravity, friction and start (the set
  angles, or the seeded start: the same four draws per try as the harp rig). Pendulum i of n starts i/(n − 1) of the **Start
  spread** (`dpSpreadDeg`, a total of 0.001° by default, 0–1° on the slider with quick picks from a millionth to a tenth of a
  degree, any value typed, URL `dpfs` written exactly) further on its lower rod. With a spread of 0 every pendulum stays
  identical to the last bit; the differences otherwise roughly double every fraction of a second, so the fan holds together for a
  few seconds and then bursts apart – sooner the bigger the spread. The harp, sparring and the trail rest while the fan is on.
  Only the rig a run builds counts for No limits and ARENA FULL (`inactiveEngineKeys()` in `settings.ts`): a harp run ignores
  the fan's numbers past their sliders (the 1,000 threads preset switched back to the harp raises no badge), and a fan run the
  harp rig's own – the pendulum count, the arm count and the third arm, the trail, the strings and their octaves.
- **The batch** (`FanBatch`): every pendulum's θ₁, θ₂, ω₁, ω₂ in a Float64Array of its own, advanced together by classic RK4 on
  the closed-form double pendulum equations (`fanAccel()`; friction −kθ̇ like the harp rig's), the sub-steps planned for the whole
  batch from its fastest rod (at least 8 a 60 Hz step, a rod turning at most 0.02 rad in one) and bounded by the runtime step
  budget `FAN_STEP_BUDGET` (160,000 derivative evaluations a step: 5,000 pendulums at the fewest sub-steps) – 500 pendulums cost
  about a millisecond a step. Nothing allocates in the step, a pendulum whose numbers overflow is put back at rest, and only the
  state decides, so a seed replays exactly at any frame rate (`tests/chaosFan.test.ts` pins the fingerprint at 30, 60 and 120 fps).
  One engine ball rides the leader's bob (bounce math's bounce at its swing through the bottom, the faces); the fan itself is drawn
  from the view.
- **The palindrome**: **Run backwards** (`dpPalindrome`, on, URL `dpfp`) records the **Forward run** (`dpForwardSec`, 10 s, 1–30 on
  the slider, URL `dpff`) on a tape (`FanTape`: both angles of every pendulum each step in a Float32Array – past `FAN_TAPE_BUDGET`
  floats it keeps every k-th step and interpolates) and then plays it backwards for as long; at the end of the way back the seam
  restores the exact start (not the tape's copy) and the next cycle integrates the same run again. The cycle (twice the forward
  run) and its seams go to the loop contract (`cycleSeconds()`, `loopSeams()`, `data-loop-cycle`), so **Export whole loops** cuts
  every export on the seam and the clip's last frame flows into its first. Reversing works only because the way out was recorded:
  run backwards from the end, the rounding errors would grow just as the hair did. Without the palindrome the fan runs forwards to
  the clip's end and its finale, like the harp rig.
- **The picture**: a faint circle marks the reach (`FAN_REACH_SHARE` = 0.42 of the recorded square), every pendulum is a thin
  two-segment line with a small bob (the Ball Size: about 6 px on a 1080 clip) – white lines and the leader's pink bob while they
  are one, each taking its **Colours** (`dpColourBy`: a rainbow by index from the leader's pink, or the Ball Color; URL `dpfc`) as it
  leaves the leader. The pendulums are batched into 36 hues × 5 separation levels, one stroke a bucket, so thousands draw in a few
  hundred strokes. With the loop HUD on the words are "200 pendulums, a hair apart" (the count and its plural in the page's
  language), the grey subtitle "tiny differences keep doubling / until no two swing alike" and, instead of the amber counter, a
  grey state label "running forwards" / "running backwards" (`LoopHudState`, `LoopHudSpec.states`). The canvas mirrors the run
  in `data-dp-fan-*` (count, spread, forwards/backwards, the cycle's step and cycles, the split and homecoming seconds and counts,
  swings, sub-steps, the step's evaluations and the budget, the tape's stride, the colours).
- **The split** (`dpDivergePx`, 30, URL `dpfd`): the fan counts as split once a bob is this far from the leader's – in px of a
  1080-px-wide frame, whatever the canvas. It times the split cue, the colours' separation and Find Simulation's **Splits at**
  (outcome `diverges-at`: the first split within ±0.5 s of a chosen second, so a found seed puts the burst on the beat you want;
  offered whenever the fan is on, and the only search of a looping fan). A found palindrome is recorded for one whole loop.
- **The sounds** (the loop family's voices through `ToneGenerator.playLoop()` – the page, the fast export, the batch render and the
  bot alike): a pentatonic pluck on the A major pentatonic whenever a bob swings through the bottom, its degree from its distance
  to the leader's bob (A3 while within a quarter of the split – one voice while the fan is together –, up nine degrees to F♯5 at
  400 px, logarithmically), one note a degree a step at the narrow velocity 0.8 + 0.2 × its speed, 1/√n for n notes, at most six a
  step; a soft ding (A5) at the split, a noise wash as the film turns, the completion chord on A2 as the fan comes home under the
  split on the way back – the payoff, around 70 % of the loop –, and a reset glide from A1 up to A2 that lands on the seam, the
  swings falling silent for its last 1.5 s so nothing rings across the loop point.
- **Presets** (the top of the block; each brings the fan's rig – equal rods and bobs, no friction, a seeded start –, the navy page,
  the loop HUD and a clip of one whole loop): **200 pendulums, a hair apart** (0.001°, 10 s each way), **1,000 threads** (0.0001°,
  12 s) and **Two that drift apart** (two pendulums 0.01° apart, 12 s).
- **Loop-style caption**: the pendulum count leads the hook ("200 pendulums start a hair apart, then the film runs backwards 🔊"),
  the butterfly-effect fact and why the way back needs the recording under it, and #satisfying #oddlysatisfying #physics #pendulum
  #creativecoding.
- **Tests**: `tests/chaosFan.test.ts` – the integrator (the small-angle normal modes, energy over 30 s, friction, the sub-step plan
  and the step budget, overflow), identical pendulums at a spread of 0 through the whole palindrome, the split coming later the
  smaller the spread, the fingerprints at 30/60/120 fps, the tape and the seam (the last frame equal to the first step within float
  error, the seam the start bit for bit, a second cycle replaying the first), the sounds and their scheduling on the fake Web Audio
  graph, the grey HUD label, the finder outcome, the URL keys, presets, uncapped values and the memory ceiling, the renderer's
  buckets and data attributes, the caption and the translations; the smoke test (block `chaos-fan`) runs the 200-pendulum preset
  in the page (the split, the turn, the way home and the seam, the grey label, the plucks), a 500-pendulum run under the step budget
  at 30+ fps, the URL round trip and the panel, Find Simulation's Splits at, and a fast export of one whole loop.

### Phase Spiral (the loop-clip family)
The loop-clip account's phase-spiral reel ("each ring gains one lap on the next", "in line 0%" over a thin bar, "watch the spiral
straighten into one line 🔊"), built as an extension of Metronomes & Polyrhythms (feature phase-spiral; the pure parts in
`src/lib/physics/phaseSpiral.ts`, the mode in `src/lib/physics/modes/polyrhythm.ts` in delimited blocks, the drawing in
`src/components/simulator/phaseSpiralRenderer.ts`, the controls in `sections/PhaseSpiralSection.tsx` at the end of the Mode row's
polyrhythm block). Only the poster and the caption were seen, so every quantity is a setting and the default look is the poster's.

- **Arms** (`prArms`, URL `prar`, 1–8 on the slider, any whole number from 1 typed; the dots a run builds stop at `DOT_CEILING`,
  rings × arms): k dots a ring on the rings and spiral layouts, 2π / k apart. A dot steps **one spoke** (2π / k) a tick, its
  angle `armAngle()` from its exact tick phase modulo k (`tickPhaseModArms()`, a remainder of whole numbers like the classic
  placement), so ring i still ticks i + 1 times a cycle and the polyrhythm – the ticks, the alignment chord, the accents, the
  cycles and the finish – is the classic one. The k-fold picture, which repeats every 1/k of a turn, makes i + 1 of its laps a
  cycle (each ring gains one lap of the picture on the next), winds into a k-armed spiral and straightens into the k spokes
  **exactly once a cycle**, on the alignment – never at T / k. With one arm the rings are placed exactly as before (the
  fingerprints in tests/extras.test.ts hold); the arcs and metronomes keep one dot a voice, and k arms ride plain circles (no
  polygons). Every dot is an engine ball (faces, bounce math), built again live when the arms or the layout change the count.
- **The in-line meter** (`prAlignMeter`, URL `prm`): "in line N%" over a thin amber bar, N = |mean of e^{i·k·θ}| × 100 – the
  arms-fold resultant of the dot angles, so the k spokes count as 100 %. A dot's k·θ is its tick phase times 2π plus a
  constant, so the meter (`inLineOf()`) is the order parameter of the tick phases, exact from the step counter: 100 % at t = 0
  and t = T, under 20 % at T / 2 for 20 rings (0 for any even count), near 0 between. With the meter on the rings shrink to 0.40
  of the field to leave it room under the disc; with the loop HUD on the HUD's counter shows it instead (a bar under the amber
  counter, `LoopHudFrame.bar`), so a clip carries it either way, and the rings keep the whole field (`polyOuterShare()`: the
  page's loop HUD reaches the engine as `meterInHud`). The HUD's words "each ring gains one lap on the next" / "watch the spiral
  straighten into one line" are the phase spiral's own: harmonic rings only (`isPhaseSpiralPage()`, the caption's test too);
  another layout or tempo series shows the mode's name over the meter's counter (`loopHudModeText()`). Its upward crossings of 50, 75 and 90 % are the
  **alignProgress** events (`progressCrossings()`, counted in `data-poly-crossings`).
- **Comet tails** (`prTrail`, URL `prtl`, seconds of motion, 0–1 on the slider, the classic 0.18 s by default) behind every dot of
  the orbit layouts, in four bands of falling opacity and width (`tailArc()`: at most 1.2 rad per 0.18 s and 90 % of the way back
  to the previous arm; up to 160 rings and 480 dots), and the **spiral palette** (`prPalette` "spiral", URL `prco`): blue inside,
  through cyan, green and yellow, to orange and red outside, on a dark disc with faint ring guides and the k spokes.
- **Loop sounds** (`prLoopSound`, URL `prls`; the loop foundation's voices through `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`, so the live page,
  the fast export, the batch render and the bot sound alike): a pentatonic pluck every tick, the ring's degree of A major
  pentatonic from A3 over 12 degrees (`phaseSpiralPitch()`: the inner, slow rings low, the outer, fast ones high – A3 to B5, inside
  C3–C6), velocity 0.8–1 by tempo rank, bright (the 3f partial) for the fast half or an accented tick, at most 6 a step spread
  over the degrees that ticked (`pickPluckDegrees()`) at 1/√n; every alignment the completion chord on A2 with its ding and sub;
  and every alignProgress crossing a riser from A2 × 1, 1.5, 2 gliding two octaves up that stops exactly when the alignment's
  chord starts (`stepsToNextAlignment()`; none when the alignment is more than 3 s away). The instrument's notes are the default.
- **The loop** (`cycleSeconds()`, `loopSeams()`): an endless run (Cycles 0) is seamless – the alignment period for the orbit
  layouts, the cycle with the polygons turning, twice the alignment period for the arcs and metronomes, times Accent Every when it
  is on (`polyLoopPeriod()`) – so **Export whole loops** cuts a clip of whole cycles that starts and ends on the spokes; a run with
  cycles finishes as before.
- **The preset** "Phase spiral, 20 rings × 3 arms" (`PHASE_SPIRAL_PRESET`): 20 harmonic rings, 3 arms, a 20 s endless loop, the
  meter, 0.6 s tails, the loop sounds and the spiral colours on the clip's navy page with the loop HUD ("each ring gains one lap
  on the next" / "watch the spiral straighten into one line" / "in line N%"). The loop-style caption leads with the rings ("20
  rings, each gaining one lap on the next: watch the spiral straighten into one line 🔊") and explains the arithmetic.
- **Tests**: `tests/phaseSpiral.test.ts` (the meter at 0, T / 2 and T and once a cycle, the arms-fold resultant from the balls'
  positions, arms tripling the dots, the classic rhythm with any arms, live changes, one cycle's alignment, crossings and
  risers, the plucks' key, register and budget, their scheduling on the fake Web Audio graph, the loop and a whole-loop cut,
  fingerprints at 30, 60 and 120 frames a second, the canvas, the HUD's counter and bar, the caption, the settings and the
  translations); the smoke test's block `phase-spiral` loads the preset from a link, runs a cycle (one alignment after the
  start's, the meter back at 100 %, three crossings), checks the URL round trip and a live arms change.

### Colour Sort (the loop family)
Feature colour-sort – the loop family's colour-sorting rings ("360 mixed balls, sorted by four rings 🔊"): one-way colour gates on
turning rings sort a packed mix into a bullseye, then a remix pulls it back into the first frame, so the clip loops forever.
`src/lib/physics/modes/colourSort.ts` holds the pure maths (`gatePasses()`, `targetBand()`, `inGate()`, `reflectInMovingFrame()`,
`remixOffset()`, the pitch and the palette) and the `ColourSortMode` plugin; `src/lib/physics/colourSortSettings.ts` the settings
(URL keys `csc`, `csn`, `csg`, `csw`, `css`, `csf`, `csa`, `csv`, `csr`, `csx`, `csm`, `csu`, `csl`, `csh`), the presets and the
HUD's words; `src/components/simulator/colourSortRenderer.ts` draws it and `sections/ColourSortSection.tsx` is the "Colour Sort"
block of the Mode row (its labels in the `ColourSort` namespace of `messages/*.json`).

- **The arena** – K colours make K rings at (k + 1) / K of the outer radius (304 px of a 720-wide frame, the same look at every
  size): K − 1 gated rings and the closed outer wall. A gated ring is G arcs and G gates of the chosen width, its gates at seeded
  phases; it turns at spin × falloff^k degrees a second, odd rings the other way with Alternate Directions. Balls of colour k end
  in band k (`targetBand()`: outward through every ring that lets them pass).
- **The gate test** – every sub-step, a ball whose centre crossed ring k is tested where it crossed, in the ring's turning frame:
  inside a gate the rule decides (`gatePasses()`: outward = v · r̂ > 0; the reference rule passes outward and never the ring's own
  colour); otherwise – an arc, a refused ball – it is reflected off the ring in the ring's frame (`reflectInMovingFrame()`: the
  normal part of the relative velocity reversed, the speed in the ring's frame kept, a quarter of the tangential slip taken by the
  arc's grip – without it a ball can lock into an orbit that never meets a gate). The pins at the arc ends reflect like small discs,
  the balls keep their speed (a gentle governor), and with Ball Contacts on – the default, the spec's norm – the balls collide
  elastically (a spatial hash, two passes). The reds, which never leave, crowd the core and every other ball has to diffuse out
  through them, so the default speed is 300 px/s: the reference 360 sort in 11.5–17 s (median about 14.7 s over 20 seeds) against
  the clip's 13.5 s, and the chord lands at about 80 % of the cycle (at 220 px/s with contacts the sort takes 15–30 s). Off, the
  balls are a ghost gas – each meets only the rings: 12–18 s at 220 px/s – and the cheap way to run thousands (the Ten thousand
  preset). The step that crosses the seam is planned for the frame-0 launch (`stepSpeedBound()`), so a run with contacts replays
  its first cycle exactly too.
- **Both ways settles, it never sorts** – the reference rule and Reverse are one-way: a ball in its own band can never leave it,
  so the census only climbs and every ball home is the end state. Both ways (`csSortSettles()`) lets colour c in through every
  ring but its own, so a sorted ball wanders back into any band inside its own and "every ball home at once" is a fluctuation
  that never comes for more than a handful of balls: the census settles at an equilibrium (about 250 / 360 with the default contacts,
  ~200 with the ghost gas). The run ends that sort once it has settled (`settledCensus()`: the census averaged over the last 6 s –
  three 2 s blocks of the cycle's clock – no higher than over the 6 s before; 18–36 s in for the default 360, median ~26 s) on
  the minor chord, so the cycle loops at ~21–40 s instead of running the whole Time Limit, and Find Simulation offers nothing
  for it (no first sort to search; the finder is hidden). A tiny run (a few balls a colour) may still sort completely before it
  settles – then it ends sorted, on the major chord.
- **The census and the loop** – a ball is sorted while its centre is in its own band; the counter is that census (the loop HUD's
  "[count] / [total] sorted", lowercase, in amber). When every ball is sorted – or the sort has settled (Both ways), or at the
  Time Limit – the completion chord plays, the bullseye holds for half a second (every gate shut: no pass, no pluck, the census
  still), and the remix starts ("mixing again"): the gates
  stop filtering and a critically damped spring (`remixOffset()`, ω0·T = 8, the rest faded out by a smoothstep) brings every ball
  to its exact place and launch velocity of frame 0 at the remix's end, while each ring is eased onto an angle its gates repeat
  at. The seam – the first step at or after the remix's end – resets the run to its first frame, so every cycle replays the first
  exactly (`cycleSeconds()`, `loopSeams()`; the default cycle is about 18.2 s). Every random choice is `ctx.random()` at init, in
  a documented order. The canvas mirrors how the cycle's sort ended in `data-cs-end` (sorted / settled / limit, empty while it
  runs) and the first cycle's in `data-cs-first-end` and `data-cs-first-end-sec`.
- **Sound** (the loop sound library, `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`): every gate pass a pentatonic pluck at its colour's degree of A
  major pentatonic (the core high, the outer colour ten degrees lower; at most six colours a tick, a colour's passes of a tick in
  one note at √n), the completion chord on A2 held with its 1.7 Hz tremolo (minor when the time limit or the equilibrium ended
  the sort), the remix a noise wash over its length and the seam the loop voices' hard cut – identical live, in the fast export
  and in the batch render.
- **The look** – a very dark tint of each band's colour, the rings as neon arcs (red #ff5a5a, amber #f7b23b, green #57e07a, blue
  #5b8cff by default; the rainbow for more colours) with a soft glow (the Wall Glow switch) and white pins, the balls as glowing
  dots through the page's shared ball pass (glow on and trails off in this mode's defaults – switched on, in each ball's own
  colour), Refusal Flashes on demand; the canvas mirrors the run in `data-cs-*` attributes. The loop HUD is on and the drama layer
  off in this mode's defaults (its moods – a tension ring around every ball, the glow's swell – run on a clock no seam matches).
- **Presets** – Bullseye sort (4 × 90, the reference), Rainbow 7 (7 colours × 50), One-gate torture (one 10° gate a ring), Ten
  thousand (4 × 2,500, ghost gas, 2 px balls) and Reverse rule (a gate lets out only its own colour); each also sets the navy page
  (#050812), glowing balls and the loop HUD.
- **Find Simulation** – the run loops forever, so the outcomes are its first sort's: **All sorted within** a time limit, **Last
  colour** (the final ball sorted is of the chosen colour, the colours named by their palette names) and **Sorted on a bar line**
  (the final ball within ±20 ms of a bar of four beats at the Sound section's BPM). A found run's clip is its first whole cycle.
  Under Both ways there is no first sort, so none of them is offered (`availableOutcomes()` with `colourSortSettles`) and the
  finder is hidden.
- **Uncapped** – the sliders are comfort ranges (Colours 2–8, Balls per Colour 1–500, Gates 1–12, Gate Width 5–180°, Spin 0–360°/s,
  Falloff 0–2, Speed 20–2000 px/s, Size 1–30 px, Remix 0–10 s, Time Limit 1–300 s); every value from the minimum up is typed in. The
  memory-safety ceilings: 5,000 colours, 5,000 gates a ring, 10,000 balls a colour and 20,000 balls a run (past it every colour
  holds its share – ARENA FULL).
- **Tests** – `tests/colourSort.test.ts`: the gate truth table, 10,000 random steps that never move a ball to an inner band, a
  single ball of every colour ending in its own band, the counter against the band census, the remix ending exactly on the seeded
  frame-0 packing (and the next cycle replaying the first), the speed in a spinning arc's frame kept by a reflection, the settings
  and URL round trip, the presets, the pitch, the sound events and their playback, the finder's outcomes and fingerprints at 30, 60
  and 120 fps; Both ways settling (the settle test on its ring of block means, sorted balls leaving their band inward, the end
  "settled" well before the limit on the minor chord and the same second in the next cycle and at 30 / 60 / 120 fps, a tiny run
  still ending sorted, a stalled one-way sort still running to its limit, no finder outcomes and a seed followed only to its
  settle). The smoke test (section 39, block `colour-sort`) runs `?mode=colourSort` at 8× to 360 / 360 sorted, sees the remix
  and the seam, finds no ball past the outer wall and checks the HUD, the block and the outcomes (none under Both ways, whose run
  it watches settle on the minor chord and loop on); it also fast-exports a quick
  sort cut to whole loops (the HUD in its frames, the plucks and the chord in its audio) and checks that a free visitor's
  watermark badge stays clear of the HUD's title and counter.

### Add a smoke-test block

--- smoke-sharding --- A feature's browser checks go into a block of their own, appended just above `smoke.endBlocks()` in
`scripts/smoke-test.mjs` (see [Smoke test shards](#smoke-test-shards)):

```js
await smokeBlock("my-feature", async () => {
// --- my-feature --- what the block checks
{
  await page.goto(`${BASE}/en/simulator/?mode=classic&…`, { waitUntil: "networkidle" });
  check("…", ok, extra);
}
});
```

- **The title** is the block's key – unique, kebab-case (the feature key) and a plain double-quoted string: the split, the timing
  baseline and `SMOKE_ONLY` know a block by it. A renamed block weighs the median until the baseline is refreshed.
- **Self-contained**: start with your own `page.goto()` (or `browser.newContext()`) and count on nothing an earlier block set
  up – a page, localStorage, a permission, a helper declared inside another block (a helper two blocks share goes above the
  first of them, outside both). Put back what you change for the blocks after yours (Show Advanced Options, the viewport, a
  listener). `SMOKE_ONLY=my-feature npm run smoke` runs the block alone, the way a shard may run it.
- **Nothing outside the blocks**: code between two blocks would run in every shard; `tests/smokeShards.test.ts` fails on a
  statement there that does work (an `await`, a check).
- A new block weighs the median of the baseline; a long one (minutes) is worth a refresh of `scripts/smoke-timing.json`, so the
  four shards stay even.

### Focus Shot
Feature ellipse-foci – the loop-clip account's breakout reel, rebuilt as a rhythm-family mode (`ellipseFoci`):
`src/lib/physics/ellipseFoci.ts` holds the maths, the settings, the presets and the URL keys, `src/lib/physics/modes/ellipseFoci.ts`
the mode, `src/components/simulator/ellipseFociRenderer.ts` the picture, `sections/EllipseFociSection.tsx` the controls and
`src/lib/simulation/ellipseFociFinder.ts` Find Simulation.

- **The geometry** – an elliptical pool, standing (vertical) or lying down, with the major semi-axis a = Size × 0.425 × the
  world's shorter side (100 % is the ring modes' arena, inside the recorder's centred square) and b = a·√(1 − e²); its foci at
  ±e·a are marked. A volley leaves the launch focus at once – the start angle plus i · spread / n, or a fan of less than 360°
  from −spread/2 to +spread/2 around the direction of the other focus – at the speed v = 2a / T. The motion is analytic: the
  next wall hit is the positive root of the ray–ellipse quadratic (in its stable form), the reflection is about the normal
  (x/b², y/a²), and a transit ends exactly at the other focus. Every focus–wall–focus path is 2a long, so the whole volley
  arrives together at t = k·T, alternating foci (the arrival error the reflection leaves is checked against 1e-6 a,
  `data-ef-conv-error`). Nothing is integrated: the balls are placed from the simulation clock every sub-step (no gravity,
  `ballsMayRest`, `ballsPassThrough`), so a seed replays exactly at any frame rate and in the fast export.
- **The collapse** – ψ, the angle between a ball's chord through a focus and the long axis on the side away from the other
  focus, follows tan(ψ'/2) = (1 − e) / (1 + e) · tan(ψ/2) every pass: the swarm narrows onto the axis until it slides along it as
  a comet (the ball fired straight at the other focus stays on the axis the other way). A circle (e = 0) has both foci at the
  centre: every ball is back there at 2R/v, and nothing collapses.
- **At a convergence** (`efR`) – **Refire** (the default) fires a fresh volley from the launch focus every Passes per loop, under
  the convergence's flash: the frame at passes × T is the run's first, the loop contract's seamless cycle (`cycleSeconds()`,
  `loopSeams()`), and Export whole loops cuts the recordings, the fast export and the batch render to whole loops. **Collapse**
  lets one volley collapse for the whole run (no loop); **Every pass** fires a fresh volley from the focus the swarm landed on (a
  loop of two passes).
- **Settings** (the Mode row; uncapped number fields, the slider in brackets) – Balls `efN` 72 [1–720; a run builds at most
  2,000, `MEMORY_CEILINGS.efCount`], Eccentricity `efE` 0.65 [0–0.99; 1 and up has no inside, so the run uses 0.9999], Pool size
  `efS` 100 % [50–140], Transit time `efT` 4 s [0.5–20] (T = bars × 4 × 60 / BPM puts the convergences on the beat), Passes per
  loop `efP` 4 [1–16], Spread angle `efA` 360° [1–360], Start angle `efAim` 0° [0–360], Launch focus `efF` top / bottom,
  Orientation `efO` vertical / horizontal, At a convergence `efR` refire / collapse / every, Pool look `efK` table / neon /
  minimal, Trail length `efTr` 0.2 s [0–2], Paths `efPa`, Groove bed `efB` (on) and Pass counter `efC` (off). A change of the
  volley, the pool or the timing restarts the run; the look, the trails, the paths, the bed and the counter follow live.
  Presets: Whispering gallery (the clip), Billiard focus shot (one ball at 50°, a 2 s trail, its path), Thousand-ball volley
  (720 balls, 0.3 s trails), Circle control (e 0) and Fan 90°; each also sets the clip's look – the near-black navy page
  (`#060912`) and the loop HUD.
- **The picture** – the pool table (a wide dark wooden rim `#2b1d16`, a thin copper rail `#b07a4a`, green felt `#15452f`), a
  neon outline in the Wall Color or a thin grey line; the foci as small hollow circles; the balls around the hue wheel by launch
  angle (turned by a seeded offset) with comet trails cut from each ball's last corners – a preallocated ring of its launch and
  wall hits, so a trail is the exact flight at any frame rate – in six tapering, fading bands, batched by hue (at most 72 paths a
  pass whatever the volley); a white radial bloom on the focus at every convergence (a 120 ms swell, a 400 ms decay; on both foci
  when a refire leaves from the other one); and the paths overlay, every ball's chords since its volley drawn faintly within the
  Lines mode's stroke budget (1,000 points: a big volley draws its newest corners, past 500 balls every k-th ball's path).
- **Sound** (original synthesis through loop-foundation's `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`, on the simulation clock: the live page, the
  fast export's offline twin and the batch render play the same events) – every wall hit is a pluck of G major pentatonic (D4 to
  A6) running down the ladder in hit order within a transit (the share of an even volley that has hit by then: the first hits
  at the top, the last at the bottom); a step's hits on one degree are one note at √(their share) of the level, at most 6 notes a
  step (the voice budget merges a pitch within 30 ms); plucks in the last min(0.4 s, T / 10) before a convergence are 12 dB
  softer (the pre-drop); every convergence plays the impact – kick, noise crack and the tonic chord on G3 – and a loop's seam the
  completion chord on G2; the groove bed (pad, sub and a kick on every beat at 8 · 60 / T BPM, two bars a pass, a chord a pass
  from a seeded progression) drops in at the loop's first convergence. The seeded choices, in this order: the hue offset, then
  the bed's progression.
- **The HUD** – the loop HUD is on in Focus Shot: "fire from one dot, hit the other" and its two-line grey subtitle (the
  `EllipseFoci` namespace, `loopHudMessageKey()` reads a key that names its namespace), and with the Pass counter the passes of the current loop in amber ("1 pass", "3 passes", 0 again at a seam – a counter template may carry ICU's plural argument,
  `{count, plural, one {…} other {…}}`, which `hudCounterText()` resolves per language through `src/lib/loop/hudPlural.ts`); the live HUD
  keeps clear of the pool and its rim.
- **Find Simulation** – **Converges on the beat**: every volley converges on time, so the transit becomes the whole number of bars
  at the BPM set under Sound nearest it, and the clip the whole loops that fit the target (at least one); **Longest collapse**:
  the start angle within one step of the volley whose swarm takes the most passes to narrow under two ball radii (at most 64; a
  circle finds nothing) – the run found is left to collapse, its clip those passes and the comet's. No seed is simulated: the
  seed only colours the run. The Publish caption editor's **Loop style** has the mode's hook ("72 balls fired from one focus,
  all landing on the other together"), its fact and #math #geometry #creativecoding.
- **For tools** – the canvas mirrors `data-ef-balls`, `-time`, `-transit`, `-convergences`, `-volleys`, `-seams`, `-hits`,
  `-sounds`, `-conv-sounds` (the sound events up to each convergence), `-spread`, `-spreads`, `-conv-error`, `-nan`, `-bed`,
  `-chord`, `-bpm`, `-skin`, `-trail`, `-trail-points`, `-path-points`, `-bloom`, `-loop`, `-focus` and `-hue`; the section
  `data-ef-reset`, `-skin`, `-loop` and `-bpm`.
- **Tests** – `tests/ellipseFoci.test.ts`: the focal property for random eccentricities (every ball within 1e-6 a of the other
  focus at T), the hit order and the mirrored pairs, ψ never growing, the refire loop's state at passes × T equal to t = 0, the
  circle, the resolver past the slider ranges, the sounds (the descending ladder, at most 6 notes a step at 1/√n, the impacts and
  the pre-drop, the bed, a real ToneGenerator on `tests/fakeAudio.ts`), the replay at 30, 60 and 120 fps (pinned), the seeds,
  the finder, the links and presets, and the renderer's budgets. The smoke test (section 41, block `ellipse-foci`) runs 20 s at
  4× (no NaN, at least four convergences, the HUD, the bloom), compares a whole-loop fast export's sound events with live play,
  and checks a preset's link and the on-the-beat search.

### Beat Machines
Feature beat-machine – the loop-clip account's two music machines ("every note is a marble", "eight steps. eight notes. one
phrase"), rebuilt as one rhythm-family mode (`beatMachine`) on the pattern of the [Beat Drop](#beat-drop) planner
(`src/lib/simulation/beatDropPlan.ts`): every impact is solved backwards from its note time and the balls are placed from the
plan, never integrated. `src/lib/simulation/beatMachinePlan.ts` is the planner, `src/lib/physics/beatMachine.ts` the settings,
links and presets, `src/lib/physics/modes/beatMachine.ts` the mode, `src/components/simulator/beatMachineRenderer.ts` the picture,
`sections/BeatMachineSection.tsx` the controls and `src/lib/simulation/beatMachineFinder.ts` Find Simulation.

- **The planner** – pure, in machine units (about one panel, y down; the mode fits the plan's box into the page's centred square).
  `buildPhrase()` writes the notes – a seeded original phrase (`originalMarblePhrase()`: two motifs of 8 beats, a quarter, two
  eighths or a rest a beat and a walk over the bars, laid out A, A transposed, B, A coming home to a tonic bar; the staircase's
  `originalStairsDegrees()`), a scale run, the loaded melody (one note a beat, folded onto the nearest bar) or a typed list
  (`parseNoteList()`: bar numbers, note names like C4 or F#5, `-` for a rest). `planBeatMachine()` turns them into piecewise
  trajectories (`BmSegment`: parabolic arcs, constant-speed arc-length paths, holds) and the loop's events (`BmEvent`: notes,
  releases, rung pick-ups, the hook's catch and the tip, the glide, the bass).
  - **Marbles**: a fall of h = Drop Height from the tube's exit lands on the bar at the note time (release at t − √(2h/g)); the
    rebound (Rebound × the impact speed, a sideways kick that clears the bar's edge) ends on the floor; the floor roll, a wait for
    the next free rung (first come, first served on the periodic schedule; the rungs pass the lift's foot a whole number of times
    a loop), the lift, the rail and the drop into the tube follow at constant speeds. `tubeNeeds()` counts, at every release, the
    tube's marbles still out on the endless periodic schedule: a tube holds max(Marbles per tube – one under auto –, what its tune
    needs), the difference its spares (`plan.spares`, `plan.conflicts`); `marblePosition()` places marble k of a tube at any time
    (note g of the endless schedule is marble g mod count – first in, first out – and a release lets the stack above fall a slot).
  - **Stairs**: step k at (k·dx, k·dy); every hop lasts T = 60 / BPM with the gravity solved from T, the drop and an apex of
    1.2 × the drop (`stairsGravity()`); with two or more return beats the ball drops into the hook, rolls the U-shaped track
    (down, along the bottom, up the left side, over the tipping hook) for returnBeats − 1 beats and is tipped onto step 1 – the
    loop is (steps + returnBeats) · T; with fewer it flies straight back.
- **The mode** – the "none" ring layout, `ballsMayRest` + `ballsPassThrough`, engine balls with no gravity placed from the plan at
  every sub-step's time (the stairs' ball, or every marble up to the board's 2,000); the plan's events are emitted over
  (last step, now] – sounds in the step that holds their time, so live play, the fast export, the batch render and the finder
  replay them exactly; every note is bounce math's "bounce" trigger. The loop contract reports the plan's loop (`cycleSeconds()`,
  `loopSeams()`), and at every seam the mode checks the machine against its state at t = 0 (`data-bm-seam-error`). The seeded
  choices: the original phrase's draws, nothing else.
- **Settings** (the Mode row; uncapped number fields, the slider in brackets; labels in the `BeatMachine` namespace) – Machine
  `bmV` marbles / stairs, Tempo `bmBpm` 100 [1–1000] (a switch of the machine moves a default tempo and look to the other's; a link naming the stairs without `bmBpm` plays
  their own 80, and the link leaves the tempo out only when it is the named machine's own),
  Notes from `bmSrc` original / scale / melody / custom with the list `bmN`; stairs: Steps `bmSt` 8 [1–64], Step width / drop
  `bmDx` / `bmDy` 7.5 / 7.9 % [0–20], Return beats `bmRt` 4 [0–64]; marbles: Bars `bmB` 10 [2–48], Drop height `bmDh` 28 %
  [1–90], Rebound `bmRb` 0.3 [0–1.5], Marbles per tube `bmMt` auto (0) [0–50], Loop beats `bmL` 32 [1–1024], Phrase bass `bmBs`;
  and the HUD's Note counter `bmC`. Memory-safety ceilings: `bmSteps`, `bmBars`, `bmPerTube`, `bmLoopBeats`. A change re-plans
  and restarts the run (the counter follows live), as does a new scale, root or loaded melody – while Beat Machines is the mode:
  those three are every mode's, so elsewhere a change of them leaves a found seed and its "Found!" line alone. Far past the sliders the machine
  still plans and plays without stalling: a tube holds at most 200 marbles (`BM_TUBE_CEILING`; a longer trip reuses them), a
  hop's gravity stops at `BM_G_CEILING`, and a loop shorter than a step plays its last four loops' events a step and counts the
  rest (`BM_LOOPS_PER_STEP`; past 4,096 marbles the in-flight count reads the trips and the seams go unchecked).
- **The picture** – the staircase on the clip's navy page: a rounded panel (#0f1a28, a 1 px #1f2c3a border, a faint dot grid),
  steps on a mint-to-sky ramp that glow when landed on, the hook and the grey-teal return track, the cream ball with its glow
  and a trail of its exact flight, the progress dots under the panel; the marble machine on a warm dark-brown gradient: brass
  floor, ladder lift with rungs moving up, top rail, pale tubes with base caps and stacked marbles, rainbow bars that brighten,
  glow and dip 2 px when struck, and glossy marbles with a white specular dot and a soft glow. Drawn from the plan at the frame's
  own time, with no gradient or array allocated per frame.
- **Sound** (original synthesis, no audio copied: loop-foundation's `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`) – the marbles' strikes are tuned
  bars (`"bar"`: the additive f / 4f / 10f strike with its click) at the bar's pitch, with the phrase bass (`"bass"`, the new loop
  kind playing loopTones' `schedulePhraseBass()`: a triangle 55–220 Hz, 300 ms attack, 2 s decay) every two bars on the window's
  lowest note; the staircase's landings are pentatonic plucks (`"pluck"`) and its return a reset glide (`"glide"`) from the first
  note's pitch two octaves down to one octave down, ending exactly at the loop point. At most six notes a step; the voice budget
  merges the rest.
- **The HUD** – the loop HUD is on in Beat Machines: "every note is a marble" or "each step plays a note" (the reel's two-line
  title on one line) with the rule in grey, and with the Note counter "12 / 32 notes" in amber (`loopHudCount()`).
- **Find Simulation** (the marble machine) – **No tube conflict**: the first seeded original tune whose tubes never need a spare;
  **Most marbles in flight**: the seeded tune with the most marbles out at once. A plan per seed (the headless engine's), nothing
  simulated; the clip is the whole loops that fit the target. The Publish caption editor's **Loop style** has the machine's hook
  ("10 tuned bars, and every note is a marble", "8 steps. 8 notes. one phrase"), its fact and #marbles #animation #creativecoding.
- **For tools** – the canvas mirrors `data-bm-variant`, `-time`, `-loop`, `-loop-index`, `-bpm`, `-notes`, `-played`, `-sounds`,
  `-seams`, `-seam-notes`, `-seam-sounds`, `-seam-error`, `-balls`, `-marbles`, `-spares`, `-conflicts`, `-in-flight`,
  `-in-flight-max`, `-releases`, `-lifts`, `-glides`, `-basses`, `-source`, `-nan`, `-lit`, `-rungs`, `-glowing` and `-drawn`; the
  section `data-bm-variant`, `-source`, `-loop` and `-beats`.
- **Tests** – `tests/beatMachinePlan.test.ts`: every strike on its note time within 1e-9 s, every release √(2h/g) before it, no
  marble needed before it is back (or a spare), the staircase's beat-long flights and its (steps + return) · 60 / BPM loop,
  continuous trajectories at every join, the same plan for the same seed, the engine's notes × loops and seam state, 30 / 60 / 120
  fps replays, the sounds through a real ToneGenerator on `tests/fakeAudio.ts`, the finder, the links and presets and the renderer.
  The smoke test (section 43, block `beat-machine`) runs every preset for two loops at 2×, checks a link, the two outcomes, that a
  scale change drops the found tune, a whole-loop fast export and that a found Classic seed survives a scale and root change.

### Ring Escape (Classic, the loop family)
--- ring-escape --- The loop family's oldest reel, a ball working its way out through a spiral of rainbow rings ("it takes three
seconds to get through the first one 🔊"), built from **Classic's own rings** – no new mode (the pure parts in
`src/lib/physics/ringEscape.ts`, the timer and the sounds in `modes/classic.ts` in delimited blocks, the drawing in
`src/components/simulator/ringEscapeRenderer.ts`, the controls in `sections/RingEscapeSection.tsx`, under Wall
Settings in Classic). Only the poster and the caption were seen, so every quantity is a setting and the preset is the poster's look.

- **Gap layout** (`gapLayout`, URL `regl`): `even` – gap k at 2π·k / n, the classic rings (the default: every old run, link and
  fingerprint replays exactly; the engine keeps its own formula) –, `random` – gap k anywhere, one draw a ring from the run's
  seeded RNG (`engine.random()`, inner to outer, right after the seed is reset and before the ball's launch angle, so a seed
  replays its layout) –, `aligned` – every gap at θ0 – or `spiral` – gap k at θ0 + k · **Spiral step** (`gapSpiralStep`, URL
  `ress`, degrees: 0–90 on the slider by halves, any angle from 0 typed; it is an angle, so it engages no extreme-values runtime).
  θ0 = 0 (`RING_GAP_THETA0`), every start taken modulo 2π. A new layout or step lays Classic's rings out anew live (the rotations
  from 0, the broken rings stay broken) and drops a found seed. Only Classic reads it; Shatter and the other ring modes keep theirs.
- **Ring spin** (`ringSpin`, URL `resp`): `alternate` (neighbouring rings opposite ways, as always) or `together` (every ring the
  same way at the same speed, so the spiral turns as one). The rig's flight prediction turns its rings the same way (never escape
  and the forced winner keep their promise).
- **Rainbow by ring** (`ringRainbow`, URL `rerb`): the rings red outside, through orange, yellow and green, to cyan inside
  (`ringRainbowHue()`), the wall glow in the same colours; the canvas only.
- **Time in ring** (`ringTimer`, URL `retm`): ClassicMode keeps a `RingTimer` from the broken rings and the simulation clock (the
  ring the run is in, when it entered it, the first escape, the way out; `engine.getRingEscapeView()`), and the canvas draws "ring
  k: t s" – one decimal, the page's separator, lowercase – in the loop HUD's amber counter style under the rings, "out in t s"
  once the last ring is behind the ball. It is inside the recorded square, so every clip carries it, and the live watermark keeps
  its badge clear of it (a bottom band). With the loop HUD on, the HUD's own counter shows it instead (`LoopHudSpec.ringTimer`,
  `loopHudCount()`'s ring time, `ringTimeText()`: the same words, live and in every export at the export's resolution) and the
  line under the rings gives way. The canvas reports the run in `data-ring-*` (layout, spin, rings, escaped, current ring,
  first escape, the way out, the gaps' starts in degrees, the line's text).
- **Ring sounds** (`ringSound`, URL `resn`; original synthesis through the loop foundation's `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`, so the
  live page, the fast export, the batch render and the bot sound alike): every wall bounce is a pentatonic pluck on its ring's
  fixed degree of C major pentatonic (`ringPluckHz()`: 12 degrees over the rings, the outer, bigger rings low – C3 – the inner ones
  high – E5 –, the inner half bright), level 0.8–1 by the radial impact speed, instead of the instrument's tone; every ring
  escaped is a progress-ladder step one degree higher (`ringStepHz()`, G5 up, wrapping after 13 degrees), the last one accented,
  and the way out the completion chord on G2 with its ding. The voice budget (6 new notes a tick, 24 sounding, same-pitch merges)
  is the ToneGenerator's. The wall break keeps its own sound.
- **Find Simulation** (`ring-at`, offered in Classic while one of these options is on – the classic list is unchanged otherwise):
  the first ring escaped within ±0.5 s of the chosen second (the target field of "Escapes at"), "Found! Through the first ring at
  2.87s"; a run that misses is dropped at once, a match is followed to its end, so the clip is the whole escape.
- **The preset** "Rainbow spiral, 11 rings" (`RING_ESCAPE_PRESET`): a pure black page, eleven thin rainbow rings, the spiral at
  30° turning together, the gaps 0.8 rad and the ball at 500 (the first ring takes about 1.7 s at the median, one seed in seven
  gets through it at 3 ± 0.5 s; the way out about 30 s), a white ball with a short white trail, the ring sounds; no title and no
  counter, like the poster. A Classic run ends with its escape, as ever: it reports no seamless cycle, so Export whole loops
  leaves its clips as they are.
- **Tests**: `tests/ringEscape.test.ts` (the spiral puts gap k at θ0 + k · step, the aligned, even and random layouts, the classic
  rings untouched by default, live relayouts, the rings turning together and never escape with them, the timer, the line's
  words in en / pl / es, the plucks' key and register, the ladder and the chord, their scheduling on the fake Web Audio graph,
  ring-at's predicates and a found seed whose first ring a fresh engine replays within the tolerance, the run at 30, 60 and 120
  fps and a pinned fingerprint, the rainbow, the settings, links and presets, the translations); the smoke test's block
  `ring-escape` applies the preset from Wall Settings and from a link, checks the spiral's gaps, the rainbow, the timer line and its pixels, the URL round
  trip, finds a first ring at 3 s and records the clip with the free watermark.

### Wave Tank (ripple tank)
--- wave-tank --- The loop family's ripple tank ("the double slit, in water 🔊": a wave squeezing through one gap, then two,
then seven), rebuilt as a rhythm-family mode (`waveTank`) that owns its picture like Bouncing Orbs (the "none" ring layout; the
engine's one ball is a pinned placeholder that is never drawn). `src/lib/simulation/waveField.ts` is the water (pure: no
randomness, no clock, no DOM), `src/lib/physics/waveTank.ts` the settings, links, sections, presets and pitches,
`src/lib/physics/modes/waveTank.ts` the mode, `src/components/simulator/waveTankRenderer.ts` the picture and
`sections/WaveTankSection.tsx` the controls (the Mode row; labels in the `WaveTank` namespace).

- **The grid model** (the default) – u_tt = c²∇²u − γu_t on a Float32 grid of `cols` × 4/3 · `cols` cells (192 × 256), solved
  by leapfrog steps with the 5-point Laplacian. **Stability**: the explicit scheme is stable for a Courant number ν = c·dt/h up to
  1/√2 in 2D; every 60 Hz step is cut into the fewest equal sub-steps that keep ν ≤ 0.5 (`substepsFor()`, `WT_COURANT`), so any
  wave speed and any grid stay stable – a faster wave or a finer grid only costs more sub-steps (the default: two a frame). The
  damping and the sponge are centred in time, which keeps the scheme stable for any γ. The sides, the front and the strip
  behind the source are a sponge of at least 12 cells (¾ of a wavelength on a fine grid) whose σ rises quadratically, so the
  waves leave instead of bouncing back. The wave-maker is a soft line source (it adds to the field and lets reflections pass, so
  the strip between it and the barrier never rings); the barrier's cells are clamped to 0 except the gaps' columns; **Point
  sources** (no barrier) put a soft source where each gap would be. A mirror-symmetric barrier gives a mirror-symmetric field to
  the last bit (the Laplacian is summed in a symmetric order).
- **The Huygens model** – the far field of every gap as a sum of cylindrical wavelets (⌈4w/λ⌉ points a gap, the obliquity
  (1 + cos χ)/2, 1/√r), plus the incident plane wave before the barrier: exact far from the gaps, periodic by construction and
  cheap (a phasor per sample point, cached per section).
- **Sections and the loop** – **Gaps per section** (`wtG`, "1, 2, 7"; an entry may name its own seconds, "1@11, 2@9, 7@10";
  each count from 1 up to 4,096, at most 256 sections), **Section length** (`wtS`, 10 s) and **Wrap** (`wtWr`, 5 s: seconds of
  the first section moved to the end of the loop, so it runs across the loop point as in the reel). Every section is whole 60 Hz
