  frames and switches on its exact frame; the source's frequency is snapped to a whole number of cycles a loop (150 in 30 s at
  the defaults), so the forcing repeats exactly. The grid is pre-rolled through the loop's end before t = 0 (1.6 tank transits,
  12.4 s at the defaults, at most 2·10⁸ cell sub-steps; a restart of the same run – Start after a preset, the fast export's
  own engine – copies the kept start bit for bit instead, grids up to 1 M cells), so the run starts in its periodic state and the closing section rebuilds
  the frame-0 field: `data-wt-seam-error` measures the difference at each seam (about 10⁻² of the wave's amplitude on the grid, 0
  with the Huygens sum), and the mode reports its loop to **Export whole loops** (`cycleSeconds()`, `loopSeams()`). At the
  defaults the label changes at 5, 15 and 25 s; the Double slit preset's 11 / 9 / 10 s sections put the changes at the reel's 6,
  15 and 25 s.
- **Settings** (uncapped number fields, the slider in brackets) – Water model `wtM` grid / huygens, Source `wtSrc` line / points,
  Gap width `wtW` 0.6 λ [0.1–5] and Widen to `wtWe` (the last section's width; 0 off), Gap spacing `wtD` 2 λ [0.5–20],
  Wavelength `wtL` 6 % of the tank [1–30], Wave speed `wtV` 1 (0.3 tank widths a second) [0.1–5], Damping `wtY` 0.002 per
  radian [0–0.1], Grid columns `wtN` 192 [32–1024], Bells `wtB` 11 [1–64], Scan rate `wtSr` 8 a second [0.5–32], Ridge lines
  `wtR` 40 [8–256], Tilt `wtT` 30° [0–80], Root bass `wtBs` and Section label `wtLb`. A change of the water, the sections or the
  grid rebuilds and restarts the run (`WT_RESTART_FIELDS`); the bells, the scan, the ridges, the tilt, the bass and the label
  follow live. Memory-safety ceilings (`MEMORY_CEILINGS`): `wtCols` 1,700 and the solver's ~4 M cells (`WT_CELL_CEILING`: a grid
  that would need more is made smaller, never refused), `wtBells`, `wtRidges`. Far past the sliders the run degrades instead of
  stalling: a step takes at most 512 sub-steps and 3.2·10⁷ cell updates (the water then lags the clock, `data-wt-lag`).
- **The picture** – the tank's front edge spans up to 94 % of the page's centred square: the field sampled along each ridge row (up to 512 samples),
  projected with a simple perspective (the depth sets the line's height and its width), drawn back to front, each line's outline
  filled with the page's navy (#070b17) so the lines behind a crest are hidden; 1 px pale blue-white strokes (#c8d6ff) in four
  brightness steps by height, the golden source line (#f2c879), the slate barrier (#5a6488) with dark notches, the bells as dim
  brass dots with amber halos (#f2c46a) whose alpha follows the smoothed amplitude, flashing on a strike. Nothing is allocated per
  frame.
- **Sound** (original synthesis, no audio copied: loop-foundation's `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`) – the field sonification: a
  cursor ping-pongs along the bells at Scan rate; each strike is a scanned tuned bar (`"scan"`: the bar with a ~1 s ring) at the
  bell's pitch – A major pentatonic over two octaves, A3 to A5, low to high left to right – as loud as (|u| / max)^1.5 of the
  smoothed amplitude under it (the reference is the loudest bell, held and let down over 2 s), a strike under −40 dB skipped, at
  most two a step. Under them a section root (`"root"`: a sine + 2f about 13 dB under the bells on A1, D2, E2…) crossfades over
  1 s at every section change (`"rootStop"` fades it out). The owner's clips: the bar-strike clip rings for a scanned bell, the
  drone clip loops in place of the root. Every strike is bounce math's "bounce".
- **The HUD** – the loop HUD is on in Wave Tank, on the clip's navy page: "the double slit, in water" with the rule in grey on
  two lines and the amber section label ("1 gap", "2 gaps", "7 gaps"; with point sources "ripples that cross" and "2 sources").
  The Publish caption editor's **Loop style** has the reel's hook, Young's 1803 fact and #physics #waves #creativecoding.
- **Find Simulation** – not offered: the tank has no randomness, so every seed is the same run; the fast export and the batch
  render build the same water through `initEngineForMode()` / `createEngineForSettings()`.
- **For tools** – the canvas mirrors `data-wt-model`, `-source`, `-time`, `-loop`, `-loop-index`, `-section`, `-gaps`, `-changes`
  (the frames of the section changes), `-section-changes`, `-seams`, `-seam-error`, `-nan`, `-max`, `-grid`, `-substeps`,
  `-clamped`, `-preroll`, `-freq`, `-lambda`, `-cycles`, `-bells`, `-levels`, `-reference`, `-strikes`, `-skipped`, `-cursor`,
  `-sounds`, `-roots`, `-root-hz`, `-ridges`, `-samples`, `-drawn`, `-strokes`, `-lit`, `-flashing`, `-lag` and `-tilt`; the
  section `data-wt-model`, `-source`, `-loop`, `-sections` and `-changes`.
- **Tests** – `tests/waveField.test.ts`: bounded energy over 10,000 steps without damping, a mirror-symmetric field for a
  symmetric barrier (to 1e-6), the two-gap far-field minima at sin θ = (m + ½) λ / d within a cell, sections on their exact frames
  at 30, 60 and 120 fps, bell gains rising with |u|, a grid past the ceiling lowered instead of refused, the seam, the same water
  for every seed and frame rate, the strikes and the roots through a real ToneGenerator on `tests/fakeAudio.ts` (the clips in
  their place too), the HUD, the links and presets, the translations and the renderer. The smoke test (section 48, block
  `wave-tank`) runs the Double slit preset for a whole 30 s loop at 2× (the label changes at 6, 15 and 25 s, no NaN, the frame
  rate at the default grid), checks a link and its round trip, the finder hidden, a whole-loop fast export and a free visitor's
  watermark.

### Brick Smash
Feature brick-rebuild – the loop family's Breakout picture smash and rebuild ("we smashed our own logo 🔊"), with its gold-capsule
("from 3 balls to 280") and kaleidoscope ("the other fourteen are reflections") variants. `src/lib/physics/brickRebuild.ts` holds the
pure maths (the playfield `brickField()`, the swept collision `sweepBody()`, the spawn rule `spawnDue()`, the symmetry group
`symmetryImages()` / `applyImage()` / `foldIntoWedge()`, the rebuild's order and timetable `rebuildOrder()` / `rebuildLandMs()` /
`rebuildFlight()`, the plucks' pitch), `src/lib/physics/brickPicture.ts` the pictures (the emblem, the mandala, text-to-pixels and
`quantisePixels()`), `src/lib/physics/modes/brickRebuild.ts` the `BrickRebuildMode` plugin, `src/lib/physics/brickRebuildSettings.ts`
the settings (URL keys `brC`, `brR`, `brW`, `brB`, `brE`, `brV`, `brS`, `brH`, `brT`, `brSt`, `brRb`, `brO`, `brP`, `brTx`, `brD`, `brBd`,
`brCp`, `brCc`, `brSp`, `brK`, `brKm`, `brHo`, `brOl`), the presets and the HUD's words; `src/components/simulator/brickRebuildRenderer.ts`
draws it, `brickUpload.ts` turns an uploaded picture into bricks, `sections/BrickRebuildSection.tsx` is the "Brick Smash" block of the
Mode row (its labels in the `BrickRebuild` namespace of `messages/*.json`) and `src/lib/simulation/brickRebuildFinder.ts` searches it.

- **The picture** – a W × H grid of bricks (30 × 30 by default; any size from 4 × 2 up, at most 40,000 bricks – past that the grid
  keeps its aspect at that many) fills the top **Wall height** (78 %) of a box of the reference clip's 645 × 817 shape. Its colours
  come from the built-in emblem (an original mark: a navy field, a teal ring and an amber square – no third-party artwork is
  bundled), from **your own picture** – your logo: cover-fitted into the grid like Picture Paint and quantised to at most 8 colours
  by a fixed-seed k-means (a picture of 8 colours or fewer keeps them exactly), kept in the page only (never in a link, a preset, a
  project file or a share code; without one loaded the emblem plays) – or from typed text in a built-in 5 × 7 pixel font. The
  picture only colours the bricks: the physics never reads a colour, so the finder and a link replay the same run with any picture.
- **The smash** – the start balls (3) leave the bottom centre upward at a constant speed (450 px/s at a 720-wide clip; Smash the
  emblem plays the reference clip's pace at 600: 0 bricks at about 15 s, a loop of about 20 s) with no gravity; they pass through
  each other and reflect off the box and the bricks. Every sub-step the mode takes the engine's move back and sweeps it through the
  grid (`sweepBody()`: a DDA over the cells the centre crosses, the bricks grown by the ball's radius, the first face met bounces
  it), so no ball tunnels at any speed or size – 1 px balls at 5,000 px/s included. A brick loses one of its **Brick hits** a
  contact and breaks at 0 (it greys to lilac and shrinks away in 0.2 s); every **New ball every** (4) breaks a ball spawns at the
  brick in a seeded direction – Land Claim's rule; past the board's 2,000 balls a break still counts but spawns nothing. The count
  falls slowly, then exponentially, then slowly again for the last corner bricks.
- **The settle, the rebuild and the seam** – at 0 bricks (or the **Smash time limit**) gravity drops the balls to the floor for the
  **Drop** (1 s), then the broken bricks fly back for the **Rebuild** (4 s) from seeded off-screen points along eased, slightly bent
  curves, enlarged in flight – by **colour layer** (the colour with the most bricks first: the field, the ring, the accent – each
  from the middle out), in a seeded **random** order or by **rows** from the bottom –, each landing adding one to the counter, while
  the balls fade and the start balls fade in at their places. The seam – the step after the rebuild's end – puts the run back at its
  first frame (every brick, the start balls), and every cycle replays the first exactly: the in-cycle random choices (the spawn
  directions, the capsule draws) come from a stream re-seeded every cycle (`cycleSeconds()`, `loopSeams()`; Export whole loops cuts a
  clip to whole cycles). Every random choice is `ctx.random()` at init, in a documented order.
- **Gold capsules** (`brCp`) – a broken brick drops a gold capsule with the **Capsule chance**; an auto-paddle above the floor runs
  for the lowest one (else under the lowest ball coming down) and balls coming down bounce off it, aimed by where they hit; a caught
  capsule splits every ball in play into **Split into** (3) – the copies fanned from its heading – up to the board's ceiling. A chime
  marks a catch, a riser the split; "N balls" shows under the box.
- **Kaleidoscope** (`brK` above 1) – the bricks form a round mosaic with a **Hole** (a mandala picture with the run's own symmetry).
  The real balls move in one wedge of 360° / images with **mirror** walls (an odd count doubles: the dihedral group) or **rotations**,
  are drawn with all their images, and every brick hit is applied to its images too – so the mosaic breaks symmetrically ("only 2 of
  these balls are real"; Kaleidoscope: 8 mirror images of 2 balls). The settle pulls the balls out to the rim.
- **Sound** (the loop sound library, `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`) – every break a pentatonic pluck pitched by its row (A major
  pentatonic, the top row eleven degrees up; at most six notes a 60 Hz tick, a degree's breaks of a tick in one note at √n); the
  groove bed (pad, sub, arpeggio over a seeded progression) drops on the first bar line after **Groove drop** (4 s at the Sound
  section's BPM) with an impact accent, kicks every beat and a chord every bar until 0 bricks, where an impact lands the payoff; the
  rebuild a noise wash with the pad, the last brick a ding, a glide toward the first note ending at the seam, which cuts the loop
  voices – identical live, in the fast export and in the batch render; the clip slots of the loop family take your own sounds.
- **The look** – the reference clip's cream page (#ecebe3), the box's thin grey border, navy (#1b2350), teal (#2fb7b4) and amber
  (#f4c55c) bricks with 1 px grout and a subtle vignette, hollow white balls with a dark rim (**Hollow balls** off: your ball look,
  the kaleidoscope's images as discs in its colours), the loop HUD on with its title ("smash the logo, then fix it"), the rule as
  its subtitle and "N bricks" counting down and back up. The bricks are a cached layer redrawn only for the cells that change, the
  breaks a pool of ghost squares; the canvas mirrors the run in `data-br-*` attributes.
- **Presets** – Smash the emblem (30 × 30, 3 balls, a spawn every 4 – the reference), Avalanche (a spawn every break), Big picture
  (100 × 100 from your upload; it opens the file picker without one), Classic breakout (1 ball, the paddle, no multiplying),
  Gold capsules (no spawns, 5 % capsules that triple every ball) and Kaleidoscope.
- **Find Simulation** – the run loops forever, so the outcomes are its first smash's, each seed's smash stepped headlessly across
  frames: **Cleared by** a time, **Last brick on the beat** (±20 ms of a bar line at the Sound section's BPM) and **Final ball
  count** (at least N balls in play when the smash ends – the capsules' luckiest catches). A found run's clip is its whole first
  loop.
- **Uncapped** – the sliders are comfort ranges (Columns 4–200, Rows 2–200, Wall 10–95 %, Start balls 1–1,000, New ball every 0–1,000,
  Speed 20–5,000 px/s, Size 1–40 px, Hits 1–99, Limit 5–300 s, Drop 0–10 s, Rebuild 0–60 s, Groove drop 0–30 s, Capsule chance
  0–100 %, Split 2–5, Kaleidoscope 1–12, Hole 0–80 %); every value from the minimum up is typed in. The memory-safety ceilings:
  40,000 bricks, the board's 2,000 balls and 256 kaleidoscope images.
- **Tests** – `tests/brickRebuild.test.ts`: the quantiser reproducing a 2-colour picture exactly, the swept collision never
  tunnelling at 5,000 px/s with 1 px balls, the spawns equal to ⌊broken / every⌋ + the start balls up to the ceiling, the counter
  reaching 0 and then W·H after the rebuild, the state after the rebuild equal to the first frame (and the next cycle replaying the
  first), the layer order, the kaleidoscope's symmetric breaks, the capsules' splits, the settings and URL round trip, the presets,
  the sounds, the finder's outcomes and fingerprints at 30, 60 and 120 fps. The smoke test (block `brick-rebuild`) runs the
  reference preset at 4× through the rebuild – the counter reaching 0 and then 900, the seam, the plucks and the HUD – and measures
  its frame rate at 1× with about 200 balls; it also checks the block, the link, an uploaded picture, the outcomes (a found run played
  back to its second) and a whole-loop fast export, with the free watermark clear of the HUD.

### Drawing Machines
Feature drawing-machine – the loop-clip account's two drawing machines ("three pendulums, one pen" and "earth and venus draw this
every 8 years"), rebuilt as one rhythm-family mode (`drawingMachine`) whose motion is analytic in the simulation clock, like the
[Pendulum Wave](#pendulum-wave): `src/lib/physics/drawingMachine.ts` holds the maths and the engine's settings,
`src/lib/physics/drawingMachineFields.ts` the page's fields, links, presets and HUD words, `src/lib/physics/modes/drawingMachine.ts`
the mode, `src/components/simulator/drawingMachineRenderer.ts` the picture and `sections/DrawingMachineSection.tsx` the controls.

- **The harmonograph** – three damped pendulums drive one pen (in units of the figure's radius, 1 = the paper's edge):
  x(t) = A1·e^(−t/τ)·sin(2π f1 t + φ1) + A3·e^(−t/τ)·sin(2π f3 t + φ3), y(t) = A2·e^(−t/τ)·sin(2π f2 t + φ2) + A3·e^(−t/τ)·cos(2π f3 t + φ3)
  (`penAt()`, which also gives the pen's velocity), with f2 = f1 · (1 + detune) – the caption's 2 % that makes the figure drift in
  and out of step and twist – and f3 = f1 · the rotary ratio. Friction (τ = 18 s) leaves the drawing at about 30 % after 22 s. The
  pen is sampled at `traceRateHz()` – 240 Hz, or 64 samples a swing of the fastest pendulum – sample k at exactly k / rate whatever
  the steps, into `DmTrace`, a typed-array ring buffer sized at init to what a sheet holds (an engine of another mode allocates
  nothing) with a memory-safety ceiling of 400,000 points (`DM_TRACE_CEILING`: past it the oldest are dropped; the drawing already
  inked stays on the sheet). After `drawSec` (22.5 s) the paper scrolls for `scrollSec` (1.5 s): the drawing slides up and off the
  rollers behind a perforation line, the pen glides back to its first point and the next cycle starts exactly like the first (the
  trace empty, the pendulums at t = 0).
- **The orbits** – two bodies on circular orbits round a sun, the inner at `radiusRatio` (0.723) of the outer. With **Exact ratio**
  the inner goes round p times while the outer goes round q in `years` (13 : 8 in 8: the pattern closes on the cycle's last frame);
  without it they keep their real sidereal periods (365.256 d for the Earth, 224.701 d for Venus…), so the pattern creeps round a
  little every cycle. A chord joins them every `lineDays` days (8) and stays; a conjunction – the relative angle crossing 0, once a
  synodic period S = 1 / |1/P_inner − 1/P_outer| (1.6 years for 13 : 8: five a cycle, each 144° further round) – puts a white mark on
  the outer orbit, and the marks are joined in order into the cream pentagram. The year runs from 0 to `years` at `secPerYear`
  (1.8 s), the drawing holds `holdSec` (1.5 s) and rewinds over `rewindSec` (3 s): the counter counts back to 0.0 (eased) and the
  chords and marks un-draw newest first. Everything is worked out from the clock (`orbitAngle()`, `conjunctionsUpTo()`,
  `chordsBy()`), so nothing is stored per chord.
- **The cycle and the loop contract** – the harmonograph's cycle is drawSec + scrollSec (24 s), the orbits' years · secPerYear +
  holdSec + rewindSec (18.9 s); the mode reports it (`cycleSeconds()`, `loopSeams()`), so **Export whole loops** cuts every clip
  on a seam, and its last frame flows into its first. The run never finishes (Find Simulation is hidden: the drawing is the same
  for every seed and the presets carry the variety; `runNeverFinishes()` says so).
- **Settings** (the Mode row; uncapped number fields from their minimum, the slider in brackets; labels in the `DrawingMachine`
  namespace) – Machine `dmm` harmonograph / orbits (a switch moves an untouched page to the machine's: the dark wood or the
  near-black sky); harmonograph: Frequency `dmf` 1.5 Hz [0.01–20], Detune `dmdt` 2 % [0–50], Rotary ratio `dmrr` 2 [0–8],
  Friction τ `dmtau` 18 s [0.5–600], the swings `dmax` / `dmay` / `dmar` 50 / 50 / 35 % [0–150] and their phases `dmpx` / `dmpy` /
  `dmpr` 0 / 90 / 0° [0–360], Drawing time `dmds` 22.5 s [1–600], Paper scroll `dmss` 1.5 s [0–10]; orbits: the planets `dmbi` /
  `dmbo` (Venus, Earth; a new planet sets the real radius ratio), Exact ratio `dmx`, p and q `dmp` / `dmq` 13 / 8 [1–1000], Radius
  ratio `dmrad` 0.723 [0.05–1], Line every `dmld` 8 d [0.1–365], Years a cycle `dmy` 8 [0.1–1000], Seconds a year `dmspy` 1.8
  [0.05–60], Hold `dmh` 1.5 s [0–10], Rewind `dmrw` 3 s [0–30], Join the marks `dmpg`; and Ink `dmi` (auto: violet → crimson on paper,
  blue → violet → magenta in space; ink, nebula, ember, ocean, rainbow) and Machine sounds `dms`. A number or the machine restarts
  the run; the ink, the marks' joins and the sound follow live. Far past the sliders nothing stalls: a step samples at most 20,000
  trace points (a stride past it), a frame draws at most 20,000 chords and 2,000 marks of a cycle (every n-th past it,
  `dmStride()`), a step plays at most 16 conjunctions or bar changes, and cycles far shorter than a step skip to the step's own.
- **Presets** – Three pendulums, one pen (the reference); Lissajous 3:2 (no rotary swing, a 2 : 3 figure 1 % off so it turns);
  Decay rose (a strong rotary swing at three times the rate, quick friction, ember ink); Earth and Venus (13 : 8, 8 years, the
  pentagram); Earth and Mars (their real periods over 15 years); Jupiter and Saturn (5 : 2 over 59.6 years at 0.25 s a year: three
  conjunctions, a triangle). Each sets the machine's page, the loop HUD and a clip of one whole cycle.
- **The picture** – the harmonograph on dark wood (#2a1c15) with a faint grain: cream paper (#f3ecdc) between two grey metal
  rollers, the trace at 1 px of a 720 px frame and alpha 0.7 drifting from blue-violet (#5b55c9) to crimson (#c93a5c) over the
  cycle, the brass rod from its pivot in the bottom-right corner to the pen and the dark ink dot (r ≈ 5 px); the orbits on
  near-black (#05060c) with faint star specks (a fixed sequence, never the run's random numbers): the 1 px orbit circles (#26304a),
  the chords with "lighter" compositing shifting blue → violet → magenta over the years, the amber sun with its glow, the white
  conjunction marks, the 2 px cream pentagram (#fff2c0), the string between the planets and the planets (Earth #4aa8ff, Venus
  #f6dc9a). The trace and the chords accumulate in an offscreen layer in device pixels, like the Picture Paint mask: a frame strokes
  only the new segments or chords, one path per step of a 64-colour palette made when the ink changes; a new sheet, a new run or a
  resize starts the layer over, and the rewind replays the chords still visible.
- **Sound** (original synthesis, no audio copied: loop-foundation's `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`) – the harmonograph's **drone**
  (sines at G2 × 1, 2, 4 with soft 3rd and 6th partials) at √(E/E0) of the pendulums' energy, rippled by the pen's speed, with the
  pen's **scratch** (noise band-passed 2–7 kHz at 0.06 × the speed), both updated at 30 Hz of simulation time with
  `setTargetAtTime` (no new nodes), swelling back to full while the paper scrolls under the scroll's **ratchet** (2 ms ticks at 6
  Hz); the orbits' **groove bed** (pad, sub and arpeggio over a seeded progression – the mode's one random draw, at init – changing
  chord every bar of four beats, whole bars a cycle near 110 BPM), a **pluck** at every conjunction one degree higher up the major
  pentatonic from A4, and a **wash** as the rewind opens. The held voices stop while the run is paused and come back where it is.
  (--- drawing-machine --- `startDrone()`'s `setSpeed()` now drives the scratch on a 30 Hz clock of its own: an energy update at the
  same moment used to swallow it.) The custom clip slots of the loop family apply (the drone, the bed, the pluck, the reset).
- **The HUD** – the loop HUD is on in Drawing Machines: "three pendulums, one pen" in a cream serif title with the rule in an
  italic serif subtitle (`LoopHudSpec.serif`), or "earth and venus draw a flower" (the bodies' names filled in, the Earth first)
  with the amber "year 8.0" – one decimal (`LoopHudCounter.decimals`) in tabular digits (`LoopHudFrame.tabular`: every digit drawn
  in a cell as wide as the widest, so the ticking counter never jitters). The Publish caption editor's **Loop style**
  has "3 pendulums, one pen", "earth and venus draw this every 8 years" (the 13 : 8 fact) or "two planets, one line every few days,
  {count} years", with #mathart #generativeart #physics.
- **For tools** – the canvas mirrors `data-dm-machine`, `-time`, `-cycle`, `-cycle-index`, `-phase`, `-year`, `-peak-year`,
  `-last-peak-year`, `-rewound-to`, `-rewinds`, `-scrolls`, `-sheets`, `-scroll`, `-trace`, `-trace-held`, `-rate`, `-chords`,
  `-chords-cycle`, `-marks`, `-conj-cycle`, `-conjunctions`, `-energy`, `-pen-speed`, `-drones`, `-ratchets`, `-plucks`, `-beds`,
  `-washes`, `-turns`, `-layer-points`, `-layer-chords`, `-marks-drawn`, `-redraws` and `-nan`; the section `data-dm-machine` and
  `-cycle`.
- **Tests** – `tests/drawingMachine.test.ts`: the pen on the closed form at random times and the e^(−t/τ) decay, the figure
  closing with 0 % detune, the 13 : 8 orbits back at the start after 8 years (1e-9), five conjunctions 144° apart, the rewind
  un-drawing newest first down to none, the trace's ceiling; then the cycle and the seams, 30 / 60 / 120 fps replays, the sounds
  through a real ToneGenerator on `tests/fakeAudio.ts`, the links, presets, HUD, captions and the renderer. The smoke test (block
  `drawing-machine`) runs one cycle of each machine at 4× – the scroll fires once, the year counter reaches 8.0 and then 0.0 – and
  checks the controls, a link and a whole-loop fast export.
- **Extending it** – a machine is a value of `DM_MACHINES` with its branch in the mode's `segment()` / `updateView()` (events and
  view at the clock's time), its cycle in `dmTimeline()`, its skin in the renderer's `drawStage()` / `drawBodies()` and its words in
  `drawingMachineHudKeys()`; a body is a row of `DM_BODY_SPECS` and a `body…` key in the `DrawingMachine` namespace.
### Perfect Fit
Feature perfect-fit – the loop-clip account's packing reels ("same 12 pieces, three perfect rectangles", "70 pieces, not one out of
place"), rebuilt as one rhythm-family mode (`perfectFit`): pieces dropped straight down into a container that a solver guarantees
ends exactly full. `src/lib/simulation/polyominoPlan.ts` is the planner, `src/lib/physics/perfectFit.ts` the settings, links and
presets, `src/lib/physics/modes/perfectFit.ts` the mode, `src/components/simulator/perfectFitRenderer.ts` the picture,
`sections/PerfectFitSection.tsx` the controls and `src/lib/simulation/perfectFitFinder.ts` Find Simulation.

- **Pieces** – the 12 free pentominoes (F I L N P T U V W X Y Z, each its own vivid colour, 30° of hue apart) and the 7 one-sided
  tetrominoes in the guideline colours; `fixedOrientations()` gives 63 fixed orientations for the pentominoes (rotations and
  mirror images) and 19 for the tetrominoes (rotations only: S and Z, J and L are different pieces). Any polyomino works there,
  so hexominoes are a `PieceSet` away.
- **Exact cover** – `DancingLinks` is Knuth's Algorithm X on typed arrays: a column per box cell and per piece, a row per
  placement. The column taken at each level has the fewest rows, ties broken by a seeded column order, and each column's rows are
  tried in a seeded order, so every seed picks a different tiling (`searchTilings()`, `tileBox()`; memoised per box and seed,
  `PF_MEMO_SIZE` plans). The search is exact: 10 × 6 has 9,356 covers – 2,339 up to the box's four symmetries.
- **Drop order** – a piece can fall straight down when every cell above its cells in its columns is still empty and it rests on
  the floor or a placed cell; for a whole tiling that holds exactly when "is directly below" between its pieces has no cycle, and
  every topological order is then a valid drop order (`dropOrder()`: lowest first, then leftmost). The check runs inside the
  search (`DropGuard`): a placement that closes a cycle is rejected at once and the search moves on – a cycle never opens again.
  3 × 20 has 2 tilings (8 with their mirror images) and every one has a cycle, so it yields "no droppable solution"; 2 × 30 and
  1 × 60 have none at all, and a box that is not 60 cells is refused at once (`status` "none" / "size": the box is shown empty
  with its words, then the next one). X can never land last: its arms would hide two cells nothing could fill afterwards.
  `simulateDrops()` drops a plan's pieces from above the box one by one – the tests' proof that each lands exactly on its target.
- **Perfect clears** – `planPerfectClear()` fills a section of L lines of a W-wide well by hole-free tetromino drops: a seeded
  search that always covers the lowest, leftmost empty cell with a drop that fits the stack exactly (an exact cover whose every
  solution is droppable by construction). A four-line section keeps a seeded column for a vertical I that lands last, so no row
  is full before it (the classic four-line clear); a taller section (the held finale) fills the whole width and holds its rows
  until its last piece. Wider or taller chunks are blocks of at most 10 × 12 side by side and bands stacked, each one of its
  size's 16 solved blocks (the "pre-solved library", memoised), the whole chunk mirrored by the seed. Sections whose W × L is not a
  multiple of 4 are left out; the well is drawn at least as tall as its tallest section.
- **The timeline** – rectangles: a piece every Seconds per piece (0.55 s), lifting out of the tray 0.945 slot before it lands
  (lift 0.12, travel 0.15, fall 0.25 of the reel's 0.55 s; the fall's curve u^(1 + Fall feel): 0 a constant speed, 1 gravity
  from rest), a 0.08 slot squash; then the flash (0.15 s), the scatter of the 60 unit squares (seeded velocities and gravity,
  0.8 s) and the label's crossfade into the next box (0.5 s): 3 × 8.05 = 24.15 s a loop. Tetris: a piece spawns just above the
  well as the last one lands and falls through its slot at a constant speed, each section 0.82 × the last's slot (0.3 s first);
  a clear's banner 0.55 s, then the hard cut and 0.3 s of silence; the finale's cream flash 1 s, then 0.5 s of empty well: about
  18.1 s. With **Land on the beat** a piece takes the power of two of beats nearest Seconds per piece (… ¼, ½, 1, 2 … beats), every
  completion and every start whole beats and the loop whole bars, so every landing is on the Sound section's beat grid.
- **The mode** – the "none" ring layout; the pieces are not engine balls: the plan is a pure function of the simulation clock, the
  canvas draws every piece at the frame's own time, and one anchor ball stays pinned in the middle (no gravity, `ballsMayRest`,
  `ballsPassThrough`, not drawn) – bounce math's "bounce" fires on every landing. Events are emitted over (last step, now], so the
  live page, the fast export, the batch render and the finder replay a seed exactly at any frame rate. The seeded choices: one
  `ctx.random()` draw at init, the solution seed when Solution seed is 0 (auto); everything else comes from the solution seed.
  The loop contract reports the plan's loop and every seam is an empty box with a full tray (`data-pf-seams-empty`).
- **Settings** (the Mode row; uncapped number fields, the slider in brackets; labels in the `PerfectFit` namespace) – Puzzle `pfV`
  pento / tetris (a switch moves a default Seconds per piece and page to the other's), Seconds per piece `pfS` 0.55 [0.05–5],
  Fall feel `pfF` 1 [0–10], Land on the beat `pfB`, Solution seed `pfSd` auto (0) [0–99999]; rectangles: Boxes `pfBx`
  "10×6, 12×5, 15×4" (W × H, at most 64; the panel names each box's count or why it stays empty); Tetris: Lines per section `pfSc`
  "4,4,4,4,12", Well width / height `pfW` / `pfH` 10 × 16 [4–100 × 4–200; memory-safety ceilings 1,000 × 2,000], Hold the last
  clears `pfHd`. A run plans at most `PF_PIECE_CEILING` (10,000) pieces. A change re-plans and restarts the run (the tempo too
  while the landings follow the beat).
- **The picture** – the rectangles on an indigo gradient (#1c1f4a → #15173a): the tray of 12 pieces in their final orientations
  (two rows of six, at most 45 % of a box cell), the box (#14163a, a lighter 1 px border, a faint grid) 90 % of the square wide or
  as tall as fits, glossy rounded cells (20 % corners, a lighter top bevel, a soft offset shadow; one cached `Path2D` per
  orientation), the white bold "6 × 10" (rows × columns, as the account writes it) and the grey "one of 2,339 ways" under the box;
  Tetris on near-black (#0a0b14): the well with a 2 px slate border (#5a5f8a) and a faint grid, the guideline colours with a darker
  bevel, a landed piece's brief glow, a translucent khaki band with the gold (#e5b04a) "four lines" over a clear, the finale's
  rows flashing cream-white under "twelve lines" and the grey "N lines cleared" under the well. The words under the box are one
  centred block of the watermark's HUD report (`labelRect`), live and in the exports, and they end above the bottom 9 % of the
  square, so the badge keeps its bottom corners under the box instead of covering the pieces; a piece lifted out of the tray's
  top row never leaves the square (`PF_LIFT_TOP`).
- **Sound** (original synthesis, no audio copied: loop-foundation's `ToneGenerator.playLoop()`) – the progress ladder: each piece
  leaving the tray plays the next degree of the major pentatonic up from C5 (`"step"`, sine + 3f, the last piece +3 dB), wrapping
  after 13 and starting again with every box, and its landing the soft thump (`"land"`); a completed rectangle plays the
  completion chord (`"chord"` on G2) with a noise crack (`"impact"`). Tetris pieces play the ladder as they land on the chip voice
  (`"step"` with the new `SoundEvent.loopChip`, a square) over the thump; a clear plays the chord under its banner, then
  `"cut"` silences every loop voice for the 0.3 s before the next section; the finale plays the chord and the crack.
- **Find Simulation** – **Most pieces on the floor** (the rectangles): of 24 solution seeds, the one whose rectangles rest the
  most pieces on the box's floor – the page keeps it as the Solution seed; **Every landing on the beat**: turns Land on the beat
  on at the page's tempo. A plan per seed, nothing simulated; the clip is the whole loops that fit the target. The Publish caption
  editor's **Loop style** has "same 12 pieces, three perfect rectangles 🔊" with the 2,339 / 1,010 / 368 fact, or "70 pieces, not
  one out of place 🔊", and #puzzle #creativecoding #animation.
- **Extending** – a new piece set is a `PieceSet` (its pieces and `fixedOrientations()`); a box's classic count goes into
  `PENTOMINO_WAYS`; a new completion effect into `drawPento()` / `drawTetris()` of the renderer, timed by the puzzle's phases.
- **For tools** – the canvas mirrors `data-pf-variant`, `-time`, `-loop`, `-loop-index`, `-seams`, `-seams-empty`,
  `-seam-cells`, `-puzzle`, `-puzzles`, `-placed`, `-landed`, `-lifts`, `-rects`, `-rects-loop`, `-rects-max`, `-lines`,
  `-lines-max`, `-lines-total`, `-clears`, `-sounds`, `-occupied`, `-pieces`, `-floor`, `-seed`, `-status`, `-beat`,
  `-beat-error`, `-drawn`, `-scatter`, `-banner`, `-label` and `-balls`; the section `data-pf-variant`, `-boxes`, `-fits`, `-lines`
  and `-pieces`.
- **Tests** – `tests/polyominoPlan.test.ts`: the 63 orientations, the 10 × 6 exact cover, simulated vertical drops landing every
  piece on its target, 3 × 20's 8 tilings all refused, every Tetris chunk clearing exactly 4 rows (an I last) and the finale 12,
  the same plan for the same seed, the timelines (24.15 s, about 18 s, every landing on the beat grid), the engine's rectangles
  and lines per loop with an empty box at every seam, 30 / 60 / 120 fps replays, the sounds through a real ToneGenerator on
  `tests/fakeAudio.ts`, the finder, the links and presets and the renderer. The smoke test (section 47, block `perfect-fit`) runs
  both presets to the end at 4× (3 rectangles, 28 lines, an empty box at the loop point), checks a link and the panel, the two
  outcomes, a whole-loop fast export, the watermark tag on Record Video and Fast export for a free visitor and the live
  watermark against the words under the box.

## Windows app

Feature desktop-exe: the owner asked for "a EXE file that is downloadable and the exe is the project but i can use my gpu to
create videos and use a AI build into the exe for video creation and other bits". `desktop/` is that app – an Electron shell
around the site's own static export, with its own `package.json`, `tsconfig.json`, tests and electron-builder configuration.
It is **not** a dependency of the site: `npm ci` / `npm run build` at the root and the GitHub Pages workflow never install or
touch it (the root `tsconfig.json`, ESLint and Vitest ignore `desktop/`).

### Architecture

```
desktop/
  package.json                 its own dependencies: electron, electron-builder, electron-store, electron-updater,
                               ffmpeg-static, node-llama-cpp, @anthropic-ai/sdk (+ esbuild, typescript, vitest)
  electron-builder.config.cjs  NSIS installer (per user) + portable EXE, icons from public/icons/icon-512.png,
                               the site export (../out → resources/site) and docs/virality-playbook.md
  scripts/build-site.mjs       builds the site for the app: NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH="" (share links on the public site)
  scripts/build.mjs            esbuild: src/main.ts → dist/main.js (ESM), src/preload.ts → dist/preload.cjs
  src/main.ts                  app lifecycle, window (state remembered), menu, tray, single instance, IPC, smoke run
  src/preload.ts               window.desktop – built from the contract's method table, exposed with contextBridge
  src/protocol.ts              app:// – the export served like GitHub Pages (index.html, foo.html, 404.html)
  src/gpu.ts                   GPU switches and the GPU panel's read-out (app.getGPUInfo / getGPUFeatureStatus)
  src/ffmpeg/                  encoders.ts (probe + choice), args.ts (command lines per preset), run.ts (spawn, progress)
  src/render.ts, library.ts    saving renders (+ ffmpeg pass), thumbnails, the Library index
  src/journal.ts               crash-safe JSON files (temporary file + fsync + rename, .bak fallback)
  src/models/                  catalog.ts (GGUF models, sizes, SHA-256, licences), manager.ts (resumable downloads)
  src/ai/                      local.ts (node-llama-cpp), cloud.ts (Anthropic SDK / OpenAI-compatible SSE),
                               gbnf.ts (reply schema → grammar), secrets.ts (safeStorage), service.ts (routing)
  src/updater.ts, menu.ts, media.ts, prefs.ts, logger.ts, handlers.ts
  src/about.ts                 window title, the site export's version.json, Help → About's buttons, the smoke run's identity check
src/lib/desktop/               shared with the page (pure): contract.ts (every IPC channel, event and payload),
                               bridge.ts, gpuEncode.ts, presets.ts, renderQueue.ts, queueSources.ts, publish.ts,
                               release.ts, ai/ (agent loop, JSON schema checks, settings patches, studio tasks, playbook)
src/components/simulator/sections/DesktopSection.tsx + desktop/*  the Desktop group (GPU, Render queue, AI, Library)
src/app/[locale]/download/     the download page (en / pl / es, in the sitemap, navbar and footer); the navbar / footer link
                               and the landing button read the small DesktopLink namespace, which every page hands its client
```

- **Renderer** – the page is the site's export for an empty base path, served by the privileged, secure `app://` scheme
  (`app://jumpingballslive/<locale>/simulator/`), so WebCodecs, fetch, storage and every relative URL work as on GitHub
  Pages. Context isolation on, Node integration off, the renderer sandboxed; navigation outside `app://` opens in the system
  browser; the service worker is never registered in the app (`PwaRegister` checks `isDesktopApp()`, and `app://` does not
  allow workers). The page detects `window.desktop` (`getDesktop()`, API version checked) and only then renders the Desktop
  group – on the website nothing changes.
- **IPC contract** – `src/lib/desktop/contract.ts` lists every channel (`IPC`), event (`DESKTOP_EVENTS`) and payload type and
  the `window.desktop` method table (`BRIDGE_METHODS`); the preload builds the bridge from it, the main process registers a
  handler for every channel (a `Record` over the channels, so a missing one does not compile) and `guarded()` refuses calls
  that do not come from the app's page or whose arguments fail `checkIpcArgs()`.
- **GPU video** – the app launches with `ignore-gpu-blocklist`, GPU rasterisation, zero-copy, the high-performance GPU,
  WebGPU and hardware video features (`gpuSwitches()`; `--safe-mode` turns the GPU off for a broken driver). In the app the
  fast export asks WebCodecs for the GPU first (`resolveVideoConfig()` in `gpuEncode.ts`: every probed and configured video
  config carries `hardwareAcceleration: "prefer-hardware"`, the plain config is the fallback) – H.264, and HEVC / AV1 MP4
  when the render queue asks for them (`pickDesktopFormat()`, mp4-muxer `hevc` / `av1` tracks), through Chromium's Media
  Foundation encoders on Windows. ffmpeg (ffmpeg-static, unpacked from the asar; or a full build the user points to in the
  GPU panel) does the rest: `ffmpeg -encoders` plus a one-second test encode per listed hardware encoder, then the running
  GPU's encoder first – NVENC on NVIDIA, AMF on AMD, QSV on Intel – any other working hardware encoder, else libx264 /
  libx265 / SVT-AV1 (`chooseEncoders()`). Transcodes (WebM → MP4, H.264 / HEVC / AV1), the platform presets (TikTok 1080×1920
  60 fps 10 Mbit/s AAC 192k; Reels 30 fps 8 Mbit/s AAC 128k; Shorts 60 fps 12 Mbit/s AAC 384k) and the 4K 60 upscale
  (2160×3840, Lanczos) are `buildTranscodeArgs()`; the benchmark encodes 5 s of a 1080×1920 60 fps pattern per encoder.
- **Render queue** – `renderQueue.ts` is a pure state machine (queued → rendering → encoding → done / failed / cancelled,
  retry, reorder, batch expansion over resolutions × fps × codecs × presets, journal (de)serialisation); the page
  (`useRenderQueue.ts`) runs one job at a time through the batch renderer's `runJobs()` – the page's own fast export, so a
  job renders exactly what the page would – and hands the bytes to the app, which writes them to the output folder (default
  Videos\JumpingBallsLive) and runs ffmpeg when the preset or codec asks for it (a transcode that is cancelled, times out
  or cannot start removes its half-written MP4, so nothing that looks like a finished clip is left and a retry keeps the name). The journal is written after every change
  (`JsonFileStore`: temporary file, fsync, rename, `.bak`); after a crash or restart the interrupted job goes back to the
  queue (a render cannot resume half-way – it starts over, at most `MAX_ATTEMPTS` times) and the panel offers **Resume**.
- **The site deployer for the VPS (vps-site-deployer)** – `deploy/vps/`: a systemd timer on the server (`install.sh`, once) runs
  `jbl-deploy.sh` every ten minutes (`install.sh --every MINUTES` changes it); when the watched branch has a new commit whose `build` job on GitHub (the site workflow, read through the Actions API) is green it fetches that
  commit, runs the production guard, builds the static site for the domain's root, stamps `out/build.txt`, runs `wrangler deploy`
  and only then calls it deployed (the live `/build.txt` names the commit). Outbound calls only, no port, an unprivileged user with
  systemd's sandboxing, the Cloudflare token in a file only that user reads; a failed commit is not retried for six hours, a rollback is
  `jbl-deploy.sh --sha <hash>`. `deploy/vps/README.md` has the trust model and the day-to-day commands; `tests/vpsDeploy.test.ts` runs
  the whole script against stand-ins.
- **My fighters and modes (custom-content)** – two more AI jobs, **New fighter** and **New mode**, make content that is kept on
  the player's own computer (`custom-content.json` in the app's data folder, a crash-safe `JsonFileStore` through the bridge's
  `custom.load` / `custom.save`; nothing is sent anywhere) and ships in no installer and in no source. No code is generated or run:
  a **fighter** is a data row like the 147 built-in ones. The model only picks from lists (a role, two colours, a weapon kind and a
  few optional numbers, an ability primitive and its numbers – `ai/customFighter.ts`; the local model is held to the schema by a
  grammar); `fightLeagueCustom.ts` builds the row and clamps everything: stats inside the role's template, weapon numbers in a band
  around the kind's defaults with the damage per second capped at 1.2× the kind's, ability numbers inside the ranges the built-in
  roster uses (`PRIMITIVE_PARAMS`, inside `FL_PRIMITIVE_LIMITS`) with a damage budget per primitive (`EFFECT_BUDGET`). Then the app
  **balances it by playing fights** (`simulation/flBalance.ts`, the headless engine the page already runs for Find Simulation): 48
  duels against a panel of twelve built-in fighters, each pair on both sides of the arena, and one strength scale – damage 0.45–2.4,
  attack speed 0.85–1.25, cast speed 0.7–1.3, the three power stats the built-in roster is tuned with – is bisected until the fighter
  wins 30–58 % of them (the built-ins win 6–83 %, 40 % in the middle). Measured with every ability primitive at its extreme
  a new fighter won 19–73 % on seeds the balancer never played, and with every weapon kind at its extreme 0–56 %: only a slow tank
  with one of the weakest weapons (bomb, whip, web) stays weak even at full strength, and nothing ends up unbeatable. Custom fighters join the **Wildcard** division at start-up under ids `my-…` (`setCustomFighters`, a live view
  of `FL_ROSTER` / `FL_BY_ID` the pickers subscribe to), are excluded from the roster's tuning hash and README table, and are
  limited to 24. A **mode** is built from the mechanics the app already has: a base mode plus a settings patch the settings
  assistant's checker validates (`ai/customMode.ts`), applied with **Undo**, listed under **My creations** to play again or delete
  (limited to 24). Tests: `tests/customContent.test.ts` (clamps, every primitive and weapon kind played through the real engine,
  the registry, the stored document, the balancer on seeds it never played, the bridge).
- **AI studio** – one tool-call loop (`ai/agent.ts`) for every job: each model reply must be one JSON object – a tool call or
  the final answer – and is validated (`jsonSchema.ts`: the envelope, the tool's argument schema and checks, the answer's
  schema and the task's checks) before anything runs or is applied; invalid replies go back to the model with the errors,
  at most three times in a row, then the run fails and nothing changes. **Make videos** gives the model the viral bot's
  planner (`plan_clips`), Find Simulation (`find_simulation`) and checked settings changes (`set_clip_settings`) as tools; its
  answer (clips with name, title, hook, caption and 5–15 hashtags per platform, only for plans it really made) goes into the
  render queue with the platform's preset. **Captions & hashtags** writes per-platform copy for the Publish block (copy
  buttons, "use hook as top text"). **Settings assistant** returns a patch checked by `validateSettingsPatch()` (known
  settings, types, `RANGES` – every uncapped setting takes any valid value from its minimum up, whatever the Wide sliders
  switch, as links and presets do (`liftedKey()`); the catalog, the schema and the prompt say "from N, no upper limit" with
  the slider as a comfort range, and a relative change ("twice as fast") is never capped at a slider –, options, colours,
  and the settings loader's final word)
  and applies it with **Undo**. **Ideas**
  is grounded in `docs/virality-playbook.md` (bundled; `selectPlaybookContext()` picks the recipe section and the chunks
  closest to the request). The model: node-llama-cpp in the main process (`getLlama({ gpu: "auto" })` – CUDA, Vulkan or the
  CPU; `gpuLayers: "auto"`; every reply constrained by a grammar built from the reply's JSON schema, `toGrammarSchema()`), or
  the cloud provider: Anthropic through the official SDK (streaming Messages API, `claude-opus-5-5` by default with the
  server-side refusal fallback) or any OpenAI-compatible endpoint (also a local Ollama / LM Studio). The key is typed in
  the AI panel, sealed with `safeStorage` (DPAPI) in the app's config, never returned to the page and never bundled.
- **Models** – the model manager downloads into the data folder (`%APPDATA%\JumpingBallsLive\models`, next to the EXE for
  the portable build) with an HTTP Range resume, progress events, and a size + SHA-256 check before the file is used; a
  GGUF file you pick is used in place. The catalog (sizes and checksums are the files' published LFS ids):

  | Model | Size | Licence |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Llama 3.2 3B Instruct Q4_K_M (default) | 2.0 GB | [Llama 3.2 Community License](https://www.llama.com/llama3_2/license/) – commercial use allowed under its terms ("Built with Llama") |
  | Qwen3 4B Instruct 2507 Q4_K_M | 2.5 GB | Apache-2.0 |
  | Qwen2.5 3B Instruct Q4_K_M | 2.1 GB | Qwen Research License – **non-commercial** |
  | Qwen2.5 1.5B Instruct Q4_K_M (small PCs) | 1.1 GB | Apache-2.0 |

  The packaged app carries llama.cpp's CPU and Vulkan builds (Vulkan runs on NVIDIA, AMD and Intel GPUs); the CUDA builds add
  ~180 MB (+370 MB of CUDA runtime) and are packed only with `JBL_CUDA=1` (the workflow's `cuda` input). node-llama-cpp's own
  `llama/` folder ships without its source-build parts (`llama.cpp/`, `gitRelease.bundle`, `localBuilds/`, `xpack/`) but with
  its JSON files and `grammars/` – its index reads `llama/binariesGithubRelease.json` while it loads, so without them the
  local AI cannot start at all. The smoke run checks exactly that in the package (`checkLocalAi()`: the module imports, its
  CPU backend starts with `build: "never"` and builds the JSON grammar).
- **Other bits** – window size and position remembered (electron-store), a native menu in English / Polish / Spanish with
  shortcuts (Ctrl+Enter start/pause, Ctrl+E fast export, Ctrl+1–4 the Desktop tabs, Ctrl+O / Ctrl+Shift+O open a song /
  video, F11 full screen), a tray icon (closing the window keeps renders going), native dialogs for the output folder and
  for songs / videos / projects, drag and drop of media anywhere on the window (a song becomes the music bed, a video the
  "Beats from a video" source, a MIDI file the melody, a picture the ball, a project file is opened), a single instance
  (a second start focuses the window and opens the files it was given), logs in the data folder (Help → Open logs), and
  auto-update from the downloads repository (electron-updater's generic provider; installed builds only, never an error when offline).
- **Which build is it** – the title bar reads `JumpingBallsLive 1.3.0` (`· portable` or `· safe mode` when they apply; `page-title-updated` is
  stopped so the page's own `<title>` does not replace it) and the tray tooltip carries the version. **Help → About** (`showAbout()` in
  `main.ts`, text from `src/lib/desktop/aboutText.ts`, pure helpers in `desktop/src/about.ts`) lists the app version and whether it is the
  installer or the portable EXE, the **website build** inside the app (`<resources>/site/version.json`, shown like the footer's
  `v1.10.0 · 8fa8dbb · 2026-10-14`), Electron and Chromium, how updates work (the portable EXE never updates itself: new versions come
  from the Download page) and the data folder and log; its buttons are **Copy details** (English text, the Windows user name replaced by
  `~`, the button then reads "Copied"), **What's new** and Close. **Help → What's new** opens the public site's changelog in the menu's
  language. The Desktop group's GPU tab (App card) shows the same rows (Website build, Install) with Copy details and a What's new link
  (`DesktopInfo.portable` and `.safeMode` are additive; `DESKTOP_API_VERSION` and the channel list are unchanged). The first log line, the
  AI diagnostics report (`app.site`) and the `--smoke` result (`appVersion`, `windowTitle`, `siteBuild`) name the build as well, and a
  **packaged** `--smoke` run fails when the bundled site has no readable `version.json` (`smokeIdentity()`), so the pipeline can never ship
  an EXE whose website build cannot be told. The bundled site is built from the same checkout as the app, so its footer shows the same stamp
  as the website would; its `/changelog` page works offline. See [Build identity](#build-identity).
- **Publish** – `src/lib/desktop/publish.ts` is the extension point; the Publish feature registers its paths there while the
  Desktop group is shown (`src/lib/publish/desktopTargets.ts`): **Publish to your accounts** puts the Library clip into the
  Publish block with its post copy as the draft and sends it in one click to every account ticked there (the relay's TikTok,
  Instagram and YouTube accounts – with the block's limits, visibility and per-account results; with nothing ticked the clip
  waits there and the Library says so; a click while the block is still sending leaves that send alone and says so), and **Publish to TikTok / Instagram / YouTube (quick share)** – the one for the
  platform the clip was rendered for – copies the caption, opens the platform's upload page in the browser and shows the
  file in its folder. The AI studio's "Copy for Publish" result goes into the Publish block's draft with **Use in Publish**
  (per platform). The block's sign-in popups work in the app: `window.open()` of the relay's sign-in window
  (`jbl-relay-connect`) and of Google's sign-in page open as small sandboxed child windows (`windowOpenAction()` in
  `src/protocol.ts`; they never reach `app://` and never get `window.desktop` – the preload only serves `app:` pages), every
  other link opens in the system browser. Google's sign-in does not accept `app://` as a web origin, so inside the app the
  block hides the direct YouTube sign-in and its set-up: YouTube channels connect through the relay (the relay allows
  `app://jumpingballslive` by itself) or the quick share. --- user-accounts --- The app has the website's **Account** page too (create
  an account, sign in, connect any number of TikTok, Instagram and YouTube accounts; the navbar's Account link): the session
  becomes the Publish block's relay profile, so the Library's one-click Publish reaches every connected channel. The app's build
  gets the relay's address from the repository variable `PUBLISH_RELAY_URL` (see [User accounts](#user-accounts)).

### Build it locally

```bash
npm ci                               # the site
cd desktop && npm ci                 # the app (Electron downloads on first run: npx install-electron)
npm run site                         # the site's export for the app (NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH="") into ../out
npm run typecheck && npm test        # desktop TypeScript + unit tests
npm start                            # build and run the app from the checkout
npm run smoke                        # electron . --smoke: load the simulator, check the bridge and that the local AI starts, print the GPU status, exit 0
npm run pack                         # electron-builder --dir (unpacked app for this OS)
npm run dist                         # on Windows: release/JumpingBallsLive-Setup-<version>.exe + JumpingBallsLive-<version>-portable.exe
```

Start the app with `--safe-mode` (or `JBL_SAFE_MODE=1`) to turn GPU acceleration off; `JBL_VERBOSE=1` mirrors the log
to the console.

### Release workflow

`.github/workflows/desktop.yml` runs for pull requests that touch the app, for `desktop-v*` tags, by hand (inputs `version`,
`publish`, `cuda`) **and for a push to the product branch that changes the version in `desktop/package.json`**
(--- desktop-autorelease ---: `npm run release:desktop -- 1.2.3`, commit, push). The `decide` job
(`scripts/release/desktop-version.mjs --decide`) compares the version with the one before the push and checks that the tag
`desktop-v<version>` does not exist yet; `build` (on `windows-latest`) builds the site with an empty base path for the
address `.github/actions/site-url` resolves (`CUSTOM_DOMAIN`, else jumpingballslive.com), installs the app, type-checks and tests
it, packages the NSIS installer and the portable EXE (with the CUDA build when the input `cuda` or the variable
`DESKTOP_CUDA=1` asks; the upload takes 300 MiB per file at most, so not yet with it), smoke-runs the packaged
`JumpingBallsLive.exe --smoke` and uploads the EXEs (plus blockmaps, `latest.yml` and `SHA256SUMS.txt`) as a workflow
artifact; `release` – the only job with `contents: write` – first checks the `DOWNLOADS_REPO_TOKEN` secret, then publishes to the
downloads repository (`scripts/release/publish-downloads.mjs`: one draft release with the versioned files, the fixed-name copies
`JumpingBallsLive-Setup.exe` / `JumpingBallsLive-portable.exe` that the download page links and `latest.yml`, published in one step), checks
that `latest.yml` there names the version and the installer has the built size, and only then creates the tag and the GitHub
Release `desktop-v<version>` with the same files and the changelog's notes for the version (--- changelog ---: `scripts/changelog.mjs --notes`, the same text as the public release) plus GitHub's generated ones (the archive; while the repository is public
it also checks that `…/releases/latest/download/latest.yml` names the version, because apps older than 1.1.0 read it). A
release build is never cancelled by a newer push. electron-updater (the generic provider, `JBL_UPDATE_URL` at build time)
reads `latest.yml` from the downloads repository's latest release. The app checks 8 s after launch and every 4 hours, downloads in the background,
shows a banner (Restart and update / Later) and a Windows notification, installs on quit; the GPU tab keeps the manual check.
The download page shows the build-time version (`DESKTOP_VERSION`, bumped by `npm run release:desktop` in the push that
deploys the site). The owner's step-by-step guide, with the one-time downloads-repository setup and the order for making the repository private:
[docs/RELEASING.md](docs/RELEASING.md).

**Code signing**: the EXEs are unsigned until a certificate is configured – Windows SmartScreen then shows "Windows protected
your PC" (**More info → Run anyway**), which the download page explains. To sign, add the repository secrets `WIN_CSC_LINK`
(the .pfx certificate, base64-encoded, or an HTTPS URL to it) and `WIN_CSC_KEY_PASSWORD`; the workflow passes them to
electron-builder, which signs both EXEs. Once a certificate is in use it must stay in use – signed installs refuse unsigned
updates. Never commit certificates, keys, models or built binaries (`.gitignore` covers `desktop/node_modules`, `dist`,
`release` and `*.gguf`).

### Tests and what is verified where

- `desktop/tests/` (Vitest, node environment, `npm test` in `desktop/`): encoder selection from a mocked `ffmpeg -encoders`
  output and a scripted runner, the ffmpeg arguments per preset, the GPU switches and read-out, `app://` resolution, the
  model manager against a fake download server (progress, Range resume, a server that ignores ranges, checksum mismatch,
  cancel, picked GGUF files, the ready hook before the "ready" event), the render queue with the crash-safe journal across
  a simulated restart, the AI loop through
  the app's AI service with a mocked node-llama-cpp (plan validation, retries, settings patches, the reply grammar), the
  Anthropic and OpenAI-compatible adapters and the sealed key, the IPC contract (the preload's bridge against a mocked
  Electron, every channel registered, origin and argument checks), the updater (offline and failing checks never throw),
  preferences, window state, menu, media and the render saver with a stand-in ffmpeg (a cancelled, timed-out or failed
  transcode leaves no half-written MP4), the packaging rules for node-llama-cpp's `llama/` files and the smoke run's
  local-AI check (`checkLocalAi()`, the plain-JSON grammar fallback), which `window.open()` calls open as popups and that
  only `app:` pages get the bridge, and (`about.test.ts`) the window title, the version.json reader, Help → About's text and
  buttons, the What's new command and the smoke run's identity check.
- `tests/desktop*.test.ts` (the site's Vitest): the render queue state machine and journal, queue sources, the AI loop with a
  scripted model, JSON-schema checks, settings patches, playbook grounding, prefer-hardware configs, HEVC / AV1 formats,
  output presets and the IPC argument checks; `tests/desktopPublish.test.ts` the Library's Publish targets (one click to the
  ticked relay accounts, a click during a send left alone, the quick share of the clip's platform, the AI's copy into the Publish draft).
- The site's smoke test checks the download page and its links, the navbar / footer / landing button, and the Desktop group
  with a stand-in `window.desktop` (GPU panel, queue, AI panel, Library with its Publish buttons, the Publish block's
  app-only YouTube note) – and that the group is absent on the website.
- **Verified on Linux** (the development machine – no Windows, no GPU): `tsc` and the app tests (56, then 75 with desktop-ai-fix, 78 with its review fixes), the site's type check,
  lint, unit tests and smoke test; `npm run pack` (electron-builder `--dir`, Linux x64) and `--smoke` under Xvfb both from the
  checkout and from the packaged `release/linux-unpacked` app – the export loads over `app://` (a secure context,
  WebCodecs present, missing paths answer 404, no service worker), `window.desktop` answers, the Desktop group renders
  under the simulator, the GPU read-out and the bundled ffmpeg probe work (ffmpeg-static 7.0.2 unpacked from the asar:
  libx264, libx265 and libaom-av1 pass the test encode; that static Linux build has no hardware encoders), the AI status
  reports the default model, the local AI starts from the package (node-llama-cpp imports from the asar with its `llama/`
  files, its CPU backend loads and both reply grammars build: `localAi.ok` in the smoke result) and the updater stays off. Also run by hand: real ffmpeg transcodes for every preset (TikTok
  1080×1920 + AAC, 4K 60 2160×3840, HEVC, thumbnails, the benchmark), a real resumable Hugging Face download through the
  model manager with its SHA-256 check, and node-llama-cpp loading a small GGUF model (CPU backend) and answering through
  the reply grammar – replies that failed the checks were retried and never applied.
- **Not verifiable on Linux** – covered by the Windows workflow or to be checked on a Windows PC: building the NSIS installer
  and the portable EXE (`npm run dist` needs Windows or Wine; the workflow builds and smoke-runs them on `windows-latest`);
  installing per user and the shortcuts; NVENC / AMF / Quick Sync in ffmpeg and Chromium's Media Foundation encoders behind
  `prefer-hardware` (need the GPU and its driver – on a PC without one the app falls back to software and says so in the GPU
  panel); CUDA / Vulkan offload in llama.cpp; DPAPI behind `safeStorage` (on this Linux box without a keyring the app
  correctly refuses to store a key); electron-updater against a real `desktop-v*` release; code signing and SmartScreen.

### Windows app: AI fix and status panel

Feature desktop-ai-fix, release **1.0.3** – the owner's "the exe ai doesnt work". A diagnosis of the shipped 1.0.2 (its own
packaged code, real GGUF models on CPU) found 11 causes; what each one changed:

- **Reply protocol (small local models)** – `src/lib/desktop/ai/normalise.ts` fixes each job's answer before it is checked
  (`AgentTask.normaliseFinal`): hashtags split / given their "#" / cleaned / de-duplicated / clamped to 3–15 (topped up from
  the plan's own), clip names slugified, platform names mapped, placeholders a model copied filled from the plan, Make
  videos' "1", "2", "3" mapped to the run's plans, a copy item for a platform nobody asked for moved to a missing one. The
  grammar gets every bound it can hold (`toGrammarSchema()` keeps lengths and enums, not patterns): a hashtag ≤ 61
  characters, a clip name ≤ 60, the copy's `platform` an enum of the requested platforms with exactly one item each. With
  Qwen2.5-1.5B on a 4-core CPU, Captions went from "failed after 227 s" to valid on the first reply (20–45 s in our runs).
- **Per-turn envelope** – `runAgent()` asks the task for the envelope of every turn (`AgentTask.turn`): Make videos offers
  only tool calls until `plan_clips` has returned plans, then its answer with `planId` an enum of those plans (and the find /
  settings tools with the same enum); the prompt itself never changes, so the local model reuses what it read. A retry is the
  app's note (a `system` message every transport folds into the next user turn, `conversation.ts`) with the turn's hint,
  and the temperature rises 0.1 per invalid reply in a row.
- **Shorter prompts** – the settings assistant lists the core settings and those the request points at
  (`settingsFilter.ts`: names, description words and English / Polish / Spanish concept stems) plus the page mode's own
  block, always – review fix: it was offered only to a request that named the mode, and as its settings are described only
  as "<mode> setting", "make the panes break on the second hit" on Glass Smash or "longer rounds" in Fight League reached
  none of them (the reply grammar is built from the same list, so the model could not change them). At most
  `MAX_RELEVANT_SETTINGS` (64: the core 18, the biggest block – Bouncing Orbs, 34 – and what the request points at besides;
  past it the block's settings the request does not point at give way first). The answer's shape is one line
  (`AgentTask.finalHint`, `AgentTool.argsHint` for set_clip_settings) instead of a 2,000-token schema, and recipe hooks reach
  the prompt without `{placeholders}` (`withoutPlaceholders()`). The Settings prompt fell from ~3,800 to under 1,000 tokens
  (976 for the default clip); a mode page adds its block (6 settings on Glass Smash: ~1,100 tokens; 26 in Fight League:
  ~1,700), about 2,200 at the very most.
- **Local runner** (`desktop/src/ai/local.ts`) – one chat session on one context sequence while the model stays loaded
  (node-llama-cpp keeps the evaluated prefix); `contextSizeFor()`: a context of at least 4,096 tokens and room for prompt +
  reply (`{ min, max: 8192 }` unless a request needs more), grown for a longer conversation; on `InsufficientMemoryError`
  the model loads again with half its GPU layers, then on the CPU, then the request fails with "Not enough free memory for
  this model: close other apps or pick a smaller model (Qwen2.5 1.5B is the smallest)"; `getLlama({ logger })` writes
  llama.cpp's messages into main.log, every load logs the backend, the GPU, its free VRAM, the layers and the context;
  `unload()` queues behind the reply in progress, and `before-quit` stops the AI, waits (≤ 8 s) for the unload, then quits
  (1.0.2 aborted with a Napi::Error).
- **Network** (`desktop/src/net.ts`) – the model manager, the OpenAI-compatible adapter and `new Anthropic({ fetch })` all
  use Electron's **net.fetch**: Chromium's stack with the system proxy / PAC and the Windows certificate store. Node's fetch
  failed with a bare "fetch failed" behind antivirus HTTPS scanning or a company proxy; it stays as the fallback for a
  request net.fetch gets no answer for (`withFallback()`: a CA only Node knows – this Linux development box's re-signing
  proxy, for one – still works, and the log says which path answered). `src/lib/desktop/errors.ts`
  explains the cause codes in every message ("fetch failed (SELF_SIGNED_CERT_IN_CHAIN: the TLS certificate is not trusted –
  an antivirus HTTPS scan or a proxy is intercepting the connection)") and strips Electron's IPC prefix wherever it sits.
- **Logs** – every IPC handler that rejects is logged with its channel, message, cause chain and stack (`registerHandlers`),
  AiService logs every request (job, provider, model, size, time to the first token and in all) and every failure, and
  node-llama-cpp's console lines (its backend choice and fallbacks) are mirrored into main.log (`consoleMirror.ts`).
- **AI status panel** (top of the AI tab, `AiStatusPanel.tsx`, IPC `ai:diagnose` → `desktop/src/ai/diagnostics.ts`) – rows
  Runtime (node-llama-cpp loads; the GPU types this PC supports; the backend, GPU, free VRAM), Model (the selected file, its
  checksum note; deep: loaded with the real context and an 8-token grammar test with tokens/s), Cloud provider (the endpoint
  through the app's connection with the stored key: the HTTP status or the error; key stored; secure storage), Network
  (huggingface.co through net.fetch and through Node's fetch – Node's failing on its certificate is TLS interception the
  app gets past; net.fetch failing where Node's works means the fallback carries the downloads) and Last error
  (`AiService` keeps the last failure of each path and returns it in `ai:status`). A quick look runs when the tab opens and
  whenever the setup changes – the selected model, whether it is ready, the provider, CPU / GPU – and then replaces the full
  checks' rows, which tested the old setup (review fix: "not downloaded yet" stayed up after the download finished); a run
  that fails refreshes only the Last error row. **Run checks** runs everything. **Copy report** copies JSON with the app /
  Electron / OS versions, RAM, GPU, backend, model, provider (never the key – it is scrubbed from the report and the log
  tail), the last errors and main.log's last 200 lines; **Open logs**; **Run the model on: Auto (GPU) / CPU only**
  (`prefs.aiGpu`). Strings: the `DesktopAiFix` namespace.
- **First run** – a card while no model is ready and no key stored ("Download the 2 GB model" / "Use a cloud key"); a
  finished download is selected when the selected model is not ready (also at start, for a 1.0.2 install whose download was
  never "used") – inside the download, before its "ready" event (`ModelManager`'s `onReady` hook, wired with the AI service
  in `desktop/src/ai/modelServices.ts`, which main.ts uses): the page refreshes its AI status once, on that event, and the
  review found 1.0.3's first cut selecting only after the download had returned, so the page kept Run disabled ("Download or
  pick a model first."). On that event the page also reloads the app's preferences; `desktop/tests/aiFix.test.ts` drives a
  real download through the same wiring and answers the page's refresh the moment the event arrives. A run shows "Reading
  the request… N s" until the first token, then "Writing… N s", and a timing line per step.
- **1.0.3 and updates** – `signtoolOptions.publisherName` is set only when `WIN_CSC_LINK` is present: electron-updater
  demands a signature from that publisher, which is why the unsigned 1.0.2 cannot update itself to 1.0.3 (its users install
  1.0.3 by hand once – the release notes said so; the download page's note for them was removed with `legal-copy`); from 1.0.3, unsigned builds update each other. The
  download page shows the version (`DESKTOP_VERSION` in `src/lib/desktop/release.ts`, tested against desktop/package.json
  and its lock). files / asarUnpack / extraResources are unchanged.
- **CI** – `desktop.yml` caches the smallest catalog model (Qwen2.5-1.5B Q4_K_M, checked against the catalog's SHA-256)
  and runs the packaged `--smoke` with `JBL_SMOKE_MODEL`: the smoke selects that file, opens the AI panel and runs one
  Settings and one Captions job the way a user does, through the bridge, AiService and llama.cpp with GPU auto, within
  `JBL_SMOKE_AI_TIMEOUT_MS`; it also runs a quick `ai:diagnose`. Locally: `JBL_SMOKE_MODEL=/path/model.gguf npm run smoke`
  (or the packed app with `--smoke`), and `node desktop/scripts/e2e-ai-panel.mjs` drives the packed app's AI panel with
  Playwright (status rows, Copy report, a Captions run on `JBL_SMOKE_MODEL`). On this Linux box (4 CPU cores, no GPU) the
  packed app's smoke with the cached 1.5B model ran Settings in 37–45 s and Captions in 43–59 s (10–12 hashtags) over three
  runs, and the Playwright run passed 7/7.

To add a studio job: give its task a `name`, bound its strings in the answer schema (`minLength` / `maxLength`, enums), a
`normaliseFinal` for what a small model gets wrong, and a `finalHint` when its schema is long. To add a status check: a row
in `runAiDiagnostics()` with its codes, and their texts under `DesktopAiFix.check` in every messages file (tested).

### Windows app: Postiz (social autopilot)

Feature social-postiz (docs/SOCIAL-AUTOPILOT.md). The app posts through **Postiz** (postiz.com, cloud or self-hosted), where the
creator has already connected TikTok, Instagram, YouTube and the rest; the app never holds a platform token. Phase 1 is the
plumbing and the Accounts tab:

- `desktop/src/social/postiz.ts` – `PostizClient` over Node's fetch and FormData: `<base>/public/v1/...` whatever the user typed
  (`https://api.postiz.com`, with or without `/public/v1`, or a self-hosted backend address), the raw key in `Authorization`
  (no "Bearer"), every endpoint the later phases use (`is-connected`, `me`, `integrations`, `integration-settings`, `upload`,
  `posts`, status / settings / delete, `find-slot`, `analytics`, `notifications`), typed results and a `PostizError` with a
  code (`unauthorized`, `rateLimited` with Retry-After, `tooLarge`, `network`, …) and a message the user may read. Uploads
  are checked before anything is sent (MP4 up to 1 GB, images up to 10 MB). The key never appears in a message or a log line.
- `desktop/src/social/keys.ts` – the key sealed with the same `SecretBox` (safeStorage) as the cloud AI key, in the config's
  `postiz` block; a new key is checked with Postiz before it replaces the old one; `forget` removes it.
- the `social` bridge namespace (`key.status/set/forget`, `channels.list(refresh?)`, `notices.list(page?)`), every argument
  checked in `checkIpcArgs`; the channel list is cached for a minute.
- the Desktop group's **Accounts** tab (`AccountsPanel.tsx`): paste the address and the key (Postiz → Settings → Developers →
  Public API), the channels with picture, name, platform and a "disabled" badge, Postiz's notices, Refresh, Forget key.
  Saving a key is Pro (`publish`), like the Library's Publish targets.

Tests: `desktop/tests/postiz.test.ts` (the client against a stand-in server, the key store, the cache, the bridge channels) and
`tests/desktopSocial.test.ts` (the tab in every state, the Pro gate, the strings in three languages).

Phase 2 posts a Library clip through Postiz, after the user approves it on a card. Nothing reaches Postiz any other way:

- `desktop/src/social/posting.ts` – `ClipPoster`: `prepareClip(libraryItemId)` hands an MP4 through as it is and turns a WebM
  (the fast export) into an MP4 with the bundled ffmpeg (H.264 on the render queue's encoder choice, AAC, a hidden temporary
  file next to the clip, removed after the upload), both within Postiz's 1 GB; `postClip(request)` uploads once and makes ONE
  `POST /posts` with an entry per channel (text, the full provider `settings`, `now` / `schedule` with the UTC date / `draft`),
  reporting `social:progress` (transcoding, uploading, creating, done). Guards: one clip on its way at a time, at most one
  created post per channel every 20 minutes and under 60 create calls an hour (both read from the audit log, so they hold
  across a restart), a 429 waited once for Postiz's Retry-After.
- `desktop/src/social/outcomes.ts` – `GET /posts` for `days` back and forward (scheduled posts show too), mapped to
  `{ id, channel, state QUEUE | PUBLISHED | ERROR | DRAFT, publishDate, releaseUrl, error, content }`; `social/audit.ts` –
  `social-audit.json` in the data folder (every create and delete with the time, channels, post ids, clip and `by: "user"`).
- the `social` bridge gains `prepare`, `post`, `posts(days?)`, `postDelete`, `audit(limit?)`, `channelSettings(id)`, `slot(id)`
  and the `social:progress` event; `checkIpcArgs` bounds every argument (`checkSocialPostRequest`: 1–20 channels, text ≤ 5000,
  plain-object settings with `__type` ≤ 20 000 JSON characters, `when` in the future).
- the page: `src/lib/desktop/socialCard.ts` (the card's rules, pure) and `PostizPostCard.tsx` – opened by the Library's
  **Post with Postiz** target (`lib/publish/desktopTargets.ts`, registered only while a key is stored, Pro-gated like the
  others, no bytes read until Approve) and by the **Posts** tab's Retry. Channels to tick (disabled ones greyed), the text
  pre-filled from the clip's caption and hashtags within `lib/publish/platforms.ts`' limits, and the provider forms: TikTok's
  audience and comments / duet / stitch as choices with no default (Approve waits for them), content disclosure (branded
  content cannot be SELF_ONLY), DIRECT_POST or UPLOAD (the TikTok inbox, finished in the app within 24 h), "made with AI" on by
  default, and the note that TikTok posts privately until it has audited the Postiz app in use; Instagram post (a Reel, public)
  or story, collaborators; YouTube title (2–100), visibility and made-for-kids with no default, tags; Postiz's
  `/integration-settings` schema for other platforms. When: now, a schedule in local time (must be in the future, "Next free
  slot" asks Postiz) or a draft in Postiz. Approve says what will happen, a progress line follows, and the outcome per channel is
  polled every 5 s for up to 3 minutes. `PostsPanel.tsx` lists the last two weeks (and the next) as Scheduled / Posted / Failed /
  Drafts with the link or Postiz's error, Retry and Delete (a confirmation says every channel of the post goes), plus the notices.

Tests: `desktop/tests/postizPosting.test.ts` (pass-through, transcode and size refusal with a fake ffmpeg and file system; one
upload then one create with the exact body; progress; the three guards; the 429 retry; the audit; the outcomes; delete; the
argument checks; `social:slot` through the guard), `tests/desktopSocial.test.ts` (the card in every state in three languages,
the Posts tab) and `tests/desktopPublish.test.ts` (the target registers only when configured and is Pro-gated).

Phases 3–4 (feature social-autopilot) add the proposal queue and the autopilot. The autopilot only proposes; nothing is
posted until the user approves it on the card:

- `desktop/src/social/queue.ts` – `SocialQueue` in `social-queue.json` (crash-safe, restored on start): items
  `{ id, clipId, channels: [{ integrationId, platform, text, settings }], when, status proposed | approved | skipped | posted |
  failed, by ai | user, reason, createdAt, postIds, error, log }`. A `proposed` item never reaches Postiz; `approve(id, request)`
  runs `ClipPoster.postClip` with the request the approval card produced (the only approval path) and marks the item posted or
  failed; `edit` / `skip` / `remove`; at most 200 items, proposals older than 14 days dropped, duplicates for the same clip
  and channel merged; TikTok's privacy and interaction keys are never stored from a proposal; `social:queue` event on every
  change. `desktop/src/social/autopilot.ts` keeps the settings block (`enabled` off by default, `channels`, `perDay` 1–5,
  quiet hours "HH:MM" 22:00–08:00, `language`, `proposeHours` 6, `lastPlannedAt`), sanitised by `src/lib/desktop/autopilot.ts`.
- the `social` bridge gains `queue.list/propose/approve/edit/skip/remove` and `autopilot.get/set`; `checkIpcArgs` bounds the
  proposals (1–5 a call, channels like the post request, no TikTok user-only settings) and the edit.
- `src/lib/desktop/ai/plan.ts` – the AI studio's **Plan posts** task (the agent framework, pure): unposted clips, the
  autopilot's channels, the day's budget, the quiet hours, the local time, a playbook excerpt and the recent outcomes →
  proposals with ids as enums, texts within the platform limits, `when` outside the quiet hours and within 48 h; `checkFinal`
  refuses unknown ids and TikTok's settings. `planRun.ts` gathers the inputs over the bridge; the AI panel shows the result
  with **Send to Posts tab**; `useAutopilot.ts` (mounted by the Desktop group's section) checks every 15 minutes with
  `shouldPlanNow` and runs the planner while the autopilot is on, the AI can answer and today's budget has room.
- the Posts tab: the **Proposed** group on top (thumbnail, channels, editable text, when, the reason, Review and approve →
  the card prefilled, the user completes TikTok's choices and approves; Skip; Remove) and the **Autopilot** card (toggle,
  channel checkboxes, per day, quiet hours, language, Propose now, "The autopilot only proposes. Nothing is posted until you
  approve it here.").

Phase 5 (copy, legal, release, 2026-10-19): channels are connected in **Postiz**, not on the website's Account page. The
Account page (`Account.*`) now says it serves the browser studio's relay, and a callout on top of its panel (`Account.postiz.*`)
sends Windows app users to postiz.com and the download page; the relay sign-in and channel UI stay for the browser studio
(tests/account.test.ts). The privacy policy has a "Postiz:" item under the Windows app and a paragraph under publishing (the
key stays encrypted on the PC, a clip and its text go to the user's own Postiz only on Approve, Postiz posts under its own terms
and privacy policy, we receive nothing), the terms a clause under Acceptable Use (Postiz's terms and the platforms' rules
apply, Postiz is not ours, the user answers for what they approve), and the download and pricing pages name posting through
Postiz as a Pro feature with the autopilot that only proposes. Windows app 1.4.0 and website 1.11.0 carry it (the changelog,
docs/RELEASING.md). Nothing is scheduled by the app itself and TikTok posts stay private until TikTok audits the Postiz app.

Tests: `desktop/tests/socialQueue.test.ts` (persist and restore, merge, the cap and the 14-day drop, approve runs the poster
exactly once and only with the card's request, skip / remove / edit, events, the settings' sanitising and quiet hours, the
argument checks through the guard) and `tests/desktopAutopilot.test.ts` (the plan task's schema and checks, a scripted model,
`shouldPlanNow`, the quiet hours, `runPlanner` over a fake bridge, the Proposed group and Autopilot card in three languages,
the card prefilled from a proposal, the strings).

## Browser support

Chrome, Edge and Safari export MP4; Firefox exports WebM. Recording uses `canvas.captureStream()` and
`MediaRecorder`, so it needs a recent desktop browser. The fast export uses WebCodecs (`VideoEncoder`, `AudioEncoder`) and an
`OfflineAudioContext`: MP4 (H.264 + AAC) in Chrome, Edge and Safari, WebM (VP9 + Opus) in Firefox and in Chromium builds without
an H.264 / AAC encoder; where it is missing, the button falls back to the real-time recorder. The simulator itself runs anywhere `<canvas>` and
Web Audio are available.

## Notes

- The tool is free of external runtime dependencies beyond Next.js, React, next-intl and the two MIT muxers of the fast export (mp4-muxer, webm-muxer).
- Uploaded images, MIDI files and sounds never leave the browser.
- Melodies shipped in `public/notes` are short public-domain themes generated from note lists in `scripts/generate-midi.py`.

<!-- --- billing-backend --- -->
## Billing backend

The playground is free and so are the videos, with a watermark; **Pro** ($6.99/month or $49/year, with cards,
PayPal and crypto — BTC, ETH and the other popular coins) removes the watermark and adds Publish (--- free-watermark ---). Because the site is a static export with no
server of its own, payments and licence signing live in a small **Cloudflare Worker** in
[`billing/`](billing/) (Stripe, PayPal and NOWPayments through their REST APIs, issuing short signed
ES256 licences the browser verifies with a public key). It has its own package, tests and deploy and is
kept out of the site's `tsc`/ESLint/Vitest/build. A non-developer, step-by-step setup — the accounts,
the secrets, the webhook event lists, the two GitHub repository variables (`BILLING_API_URL`,
`LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY`) the site reads, test/sandbox testing and the trust model — is in
[`billing/README.md`](billing/README.md); deploy it from the **Actions → "Deploy billing worker"**
workflow (and, with the repository variable `BILLING_AUTODEPLOY=1`, automatically on every push that changes `billing/`; the
workflow checks the secrets, the KV id and the custom domain first and probes `/health` or `/config` afterwards); it
type-checks and runs the Worker's own suite first (`cd billing && npm ci && npm run check && npm test`).
--- go-live-kit --- **To launch for real, follow [docs/GO-LIVE.md](docs/GO-LIVE.md)** – the plain-language guide (accounts,
keys, payouts, legal pages, checklists); `npm run go-live` pastes the keys for you and `npm run go-live:check` proves the live
setup.
The site's half of the contract – the Unlock dialog, the licence store, the guard, test mode – is described in
[Pricing and licences](#pricing-and-licences); `src/lib/billing/api.ts` and `src/lib/billing/license.ts` document the
endpoints and the licence format both sides share.
